Loading faces in sorted ID order into an empty database makes those ties repeatable from a fresh start. fontdb returns the first of equal candidates in database order, and system faces load first, so without the replacement rule an installed copy of a packaged font could win over the manifest's file.

**Consequences:**
- Extends `D-026`; it stands.
- A hot reload that adds or replaces a face appends it, so ties between faces of equal weight, stretch and style may resolve differently until the next start; visual tests and replays start fresh. Rebuilding the database in canonical order would restart fontdb IDs, which glyphon's atlas and the swash cache key glyphs by, so it is not done; the GPU half would have to drop its glyph caches with it.
- A chain change rebuilds the font system from its database (`into_locale_and_db`), keeping fontdb IDs. Family names reach the `Fallback` as `&'static str` through an interner the engine owns, which leaks each distinct name once.
- `RenderConfig` gains `system_fonts`: a public-API break recorded in the break ledger. `TextPipeline::new` keeps its signature and packaged fonts; `TextPipeline::with_font_source` takes a `FontSource`.
- Two registered faces with one fontdb family, weight and style draw as one: the engine warns, since `Attrs` cannot tell them apart.
- `gpu-visual.png` moves on purpose. The reference machine has JetBrains Mono 2.304 installed, and while system fonts loaded it shadowed the packaged 2.211, so the old fixture showed the installed face. Packaged-only fonts redraw the two HUD lines (2155 pixels, same strings and advances); a copy of the tree with the 2.304 file at the packaged path matched the old fixture, so nothing else moved. The owner approved the regenerated fixture (M34 Q12).
- Every shipped string draws from its source face under packaged fonts (`fc-query` on the three faces); a GPU-free test shapes printable ASCII plus `·—←→` in each with no missing glyph. Tests also pin the database contents, the replacement rule, the chain order in both directions over every load order, weight resolution and the epoch.

## D-117 — The text engine split: device-free `TextEngine`, neutral text types in core, retained nodes that layout measures
**Amended by D-125:** the seam clause only; core adds `TextNodeStore: TextMeasure` for the node lifecycle, which `TextEngine` and `TextNodes` implement by forwarding; `TextMeasure` and the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-04
**Decision:** `tungsten-render`'s text code splits into a device-free half and a GPU half. `text/engine.rs` holds `TextEngine`: the font system, faces, families, chain and `FontEpoch` (`D-115`, `D-116`), the bounded section cache (`D-085`) and retained text nodes (`text/nodes.rs`). `text/gpu.rs` holds `TextPipeline`, which owns the engine and glyphon's atlas, renderer, viewport and swash cache, with its public methods unchanged. `text.rs` stays the module root with `TextSection`. Core gains `tungsten_core::text`: the neutral types layout, the engine and the paint list share (`TextAlign`, `TextWrap`, `TextOverflow`, `EllipsisAt`, `TextHinting`, `FontFeatures`, `TextLayout`, `TextStyle`, `TextSpan`, `StyledText`, `MeasureWidth`, `TextMetrics`, `TextNodeId`, `FontEpoch`) and the `TextMeasure` trait; it never calls render (`D-007`), and the engine mints node IDs (`D-048`'s pattern). A node holds styled text and a style. `set_text` compares both before any work and resolves each span's face through the style's family. `measure` takes a width layout has fixed (`known_width`, which wins) or a `MeasureWidth`. Min- and max-content ignore overflow and are cached per text, style and epoch. Max-content is the unwrapped width. Min-content is the widest word under `Word` and `WordOrGlyph`, the widest glyph under `Glyph` (from a relayout at width 0 under `Word` or `Glyph`) and the unwrapped width under `None`. A definite width applies the node's overflow with unbounded height. Probes relayout the retained buffer and never reshape. `commit_layout` lays the buffer out in its final box and records it, and `committed` returns that layout while the buffer still holds it under the current epoch. A node shaped under an older epoch reshapes at its next measure or commit. A stale or removed ID measures zero and logs once per ID. `TextSection` gains `layout: TextLayout` and `Default`, whose default lays out as sections always did; the layout joins the section cache's key. `Renderer::text_engine` lends the engine read-only and `Renderer::text_nodes` lends a `TextNodes` facade with the node methods and `TextMeasure`. No public function returns `&mut TextEngine`.

**Why:** W1 M1's layout needs min- and max-content sizes that match what the renderer later draws (w01 §4), so measuring must use the same fonts, shaping and wrapping as drawing. Before the split, shaping was private to `TextPipeline` and every font load cleared every layout. Retained nodes let layout probe several widths on one shaped buffer: cosmic-text's `set_size` and `set_wrap` mark a relayout, not a reshape. The facade exists because the GPU half's atlas and swash cache key glyphs by the engine's fontdb IDs, so a caller that could swap the engine (`std::mem::replace`) could make them draw stale glyph images. `TextSection.layout` lands now as one field with `Default`, so later layout fields are additive and the break lands before the API freeze.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-085` as its marker line says: the section cache lives in the engine, its key gains the layout, and any font change (`FontEpoch`) still drops every section layout. The cache's bounds, spare list and unchanged-frame skip stand, and its eight tests pass unedited. A removed node's buffer joins the spare list.
- An ellipsis line limit counts lines per paragraph, since cosmic-text resets it at each newline (plan Q10); `lines: None` takes its height from a section's bounds or the committed size, and a measurement keeps every line.
- Under `Wrap::Glyph`, cosmic-text counts a trailing blank in a line's width, so min-content takes its width from the width-0 probe and its lines from the layout at that width.
- `TextSection` literals need `..Default::default()`: a public-API break recorded in the break ledger. New public enums and growable structs are `#[non_exhaustive]`.
- Drawing nodes, the paint list and carets are later work (W1 M2, the glyph gate, editing); `TextNodes` is what W1 M1 lends layout.
- Tests: the section-layout tests of each layout field, the node tests (each wrap mode's min-content, max-content across modes, definite widths, `known_width`, empty text, no repeated work, reshaping after a font reload, the committed width after probes, removed IDs, a node and a section laying out the same glyphs, a bold span, an ellipsis that fills the box).

## D-118 — The roadmap catalog carries each stop's stages and a mode, model and effort for each session stage
**Amended by D-136:** the tier clause only; a level A or B release takes Opus 5.5 at max in place of Fable 5.1; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-150:** the stages only; no stage keeps another stage's session (no `approve`, `run-c1` or `run-c-rest`, no `keeps` or `if_new`), every session stage runs in auto mode, one Release row serves every level, and `game-spec` and `graduate` are gone; the rest stands.
**Superseded by D-151:** the tier's Opus 5.5 max clause only; max is left for level C plans and gates at complexity 2–4, and every other session stage takes xhigh; the Fable and high clauses and the rest stand.
**Superseded by D-215:** the per-stop stages, modes and tiers; plans follow `D-140`'s routes as amended and tiers `D-151`'s rule as amended.
**Date:** 2026-10-04
**Decision:** `scripts/roadmap.py catalog` gives each stop in `docs/plans/1.0/roadmap.json` its `stages`, read from the Flow table in `.claude/skills/tungsten-next/prompts.md`. Level C releases take the level C row; other releases, at level A, B or no level yet, take the A-or-B row. `qa`, `gate`, `spike` and `rc` stops take their own rows, `custom` stops get none, and a step noted "the <name> gate only" joins only that gate. Each stage is `{key, title, where, at, note}`, and a session stage carries a `rec` of `{mode, model, effort}` from `recommend`. A stop's tier comes from its `complexity` and `effort`: Fable 5.1 at xhigh for complexity 4, or 3 with effort XL; Opus 5.5 at max for complexity 2 or 3; Opus 5.5 at high for complexity 1 with effort S; Opus 5.5 at xhigh otherwise. `plan`, `qa-plan`, `gate`, `run`, `run-c1`, `experiment` and `rc-fix` take the tier. `game-spec` is always Opus 5.5 at max, and `graduate` and `rc-checklist` Opus 5.5 at xhigh. `gate` and `game-spec` run in plan mode, every other session stage in auto mode. A same-session stage keeps the settings of the stage that opened its session: `approve` keeps `plan`'s or `qa-plan`'s, and `run-c-rest` keeps `run-c1`'s. Its `if_new` gives the settings for when it has to start a new session: auto mode with Opus 5.5 at high for `approve`, `run-c1`'s for `run-c-rest`. `catalog.sessions` holds the two session prompts outside every flow: `release` (Opus 5.5 at xhigh) and `verify` (Opus 5.5 at high). The payload is catalog schema 2, and its hash covers the derived fields. `meta/now` drops `flow`, because the page reads the stop's stages from the catalog. It gains `next.rec` and `recovery[].rec`, copied from the payload rather than worked out, and `tree`: `state.sh`'s upstream, `origin/main`, fetch, tag, uncommitted, session, capture and decision lines at sync time. `/tungsten-next`'s new-session prompt label shows the recommendation.

**Why:** The owner asked for this on 2026-10-04 and approved the session plan with its defaults, after reviewing the resulting values for all 47 stops. Only the next stop's flow reached the page, copied into `meta/now` by hand at each sync, so the route showed no stages, and nothing said which mode, model and effort a session should start with. Reading the stages from the Flow table keeps `prompts.md` the one home of flows (`D-109`). Computing recommendations from the catalog's existing ratings needs no hand edits per stop. Plan mode fits only the stages that ask before they write: a plan session writes the plan file, the register row and the README "Now" lines and runs the critique before approval, all of which plan mode blocks. Switching models mid-session drops the prompt cache, and the context the session already holds is what a same-session stage needs, so those stages keep their session's settings.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-109` as its marker line says.
- Editing the Flow table or re-rating a stop changes the catalog hash, so the next sync rewrites `meta/catalog`. If a stop's kind matches no Flow row, `scripts/roadmap.py catalog` fails, and so does `just repo-check`, because `check_roadmap` builds the payload.
- Recommendations are advice, and the owner may start a session another way. Re-rating a stop at a gate changes its recommendation, and the rule itself lives only in `recommend`.
- The payload grows from 30,251 to 55,392 bytes; one `db` document holds at most 256 KiB.
- The page reads catalog schemas 1 and 2, so it is republished before the sync that writes schema 2. `roadmap.md` keeps that order for later schema changes.
- Tests in `scripts/test-roadmap.py`: the Flow parse, stages by kind and level, every tier and stage rule, same-session `keeps` and `if_new`, the schema-2 payload and its hash, and every stop of the repository's catalog deriving its stages.

## D-119 — A game names itself in `game.id`; `App::new` installs the one engine logger, a log file per run and a crash-file panic hook in the game's user folder
**Amended by D-159:** the resolution order only; `game.user_folder`, set in code, follows smoke mode and the id check: `Off` gives no folder and `At(dir)` the three folders under `dir`, both before `TUNGSTEN_USER_DIR`; the settings and bindings create the `settings` folder on their first save; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-219:** the `game.id` rule only; an id may not end in `.`; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-05
**Decision:** `tungsten.json` gains a `game` section (`GameConfig`, `#[non_exhaustive]`): `game.id`, 1–64 ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `_` or `-`, starting with a letter or digit and not a Windows device name (`CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM1`–`COM9`, `LPT1`–`LPT9`, any case, any extension), and `game.version`, free text that crash files print. `GameConfig::validate` rejects a bad id as `ConfigError::InvalidValue { field: "game.id" }`, and `Config::load` and `App::new` both call it, since a config built in code skips the load. `logging.level` must name a `log::LevelFilter` (`off` … `trace`, any case). The user folder (`settings`, `saves`, `logs`) resolves in order: none in smoke mode (`TUNGSTEN_SMOKE_FRAMES` > 0); `<dir>/settings`, `<dir>/saves` and `<dir>/logs` under `TUNGSTEN_USER_DIR`, an empty value meaning none; none without a valid id; else `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/<id>`, `$XDG_DATA_HOME/<id>/saves` and `$XDG_STATE_HOME/<id>/logs` on Linux and other Unix (an unset, empty or relative variable falls back to `~/.config`, `~/.local/share` or `~/.local/state`; no usable `HOME`, no folder), `%APPDATA%\<id>`, `%APPDATA%\<id>\saves` and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\<id>\logs` on Windows, and `~/Library/Application Support/<id>`, `…/<id>/saves` and `~/Library/Logs/<id>` on macOS. Only the logs folder is created here; settings and saves create theirs. `App::new` first validates the id, resolves the folder and installs the engine logger, `env_logger` through `Builder::try_init`: `RUST_LOG`, when set, is the whole filter, else `logging.level`; stderr in debug builds, and in release builds only while `RUST_LOG` is set; a file whenever a folder exists, `<logs>/<stem>-<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS.mmmZ>-<pid>.log` (the executable's file stem, UTC), unbuffered, keeping the 10 newest log files of that stem; no logger with neither sink. A logger already set, the game's or an earlier `App`'s, stays, and the engine opens no file. One `Tee` writer takes each whole record and writes it to the file and to stderr; W1 M5's console adds a bounded `sync_channel` sink to it, fed with `try_send`, drops counted and drained on the main thread, rather than a second logger. `Config::load` keeps its warnings for `App::new` to log once the logger is up (`Config::take_load_warnings`), and `App::new` and `App::run` log the errors they return. With a folder, `App::new` also installs a panic hook once per process: it writes `<logs>/<stem>-<stamp>-<pid>-crash.txt` (game, game and engine versions, target, executable, time, thread, message, location, an anchor address, the executable's GNU build-id or PDB GUID, age and name, the backtrace and, on Linux, the file-backed mappings), names it in the run's log, then calls the previous hook. `TUNGSTEN_TEST_PANIC`, set and not empty, registers a system that panics in the first frame, to check a shipped build. `env_logger` 0.11, already locked as the examples' backend, becomes a dependency of `tungsten`. Satisfies D-015 rule 3.

**Why:** Owner question Q14 was answered on 2026-10-05 with M35's default, on by default: an opt-in call would also need `Config::load`'s warnings handled before it, and every game would have to remember it. `logging.level` was parsed and applied nowhere, each example installed its own `env_logger`, and a Windows release build without a console has nowhere to print, so its errors and panics need a file. Smoke mode has no folder, so captures, smoke runs and the pixel tests write no user files without a script change, and a release build writes stderr only under `RUST_LOG`, so a capture logs exactly as before: release build, filter from `RUST_LOG`, stderr only, default format. `log` holds one logger per process and std one panic hook: `try_init` leaves a game's logger in place, and the once-guard keeps a second `App` from chaining a second report. The id becomes a folder name, so it is checked wherever a config can come from, and no separator, traversal or device name reaches `Path::join`.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-008` as its marker line says.
- The hook's guard, a `static std::sync::Once`, is the only process-wide state the engine adds, an exception to the no-global-state rule: it guards std's hook slot, which is process-wide already, as `log`'s logger slot is. The log file is an `Arc<OnceLock<File>>` that the `Tee` and the hook share.
- A game opts out by installing its own logger before `App::new` and its own hook after it. A hook set later replaces the engine's unless it chains through `std::panic::take_hook`.
- Only panics leave a crash file: the hook runs before `panic = "abort"` ends a release process. Signals and aborts below Rust stay 1.x (w11).
- The hook never logs through `log`, since the panicking thread may hold `env_logger`'s lock, and ignores every I/O error. A folder or file that cannot be created costs one stderr line, never an error.
- `Config` gains a private field, so it can no longer be built by literal outside core (break ledger).
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/config.rs` (ids, levels, load warnings), `crates/tungsten/src/tests/{user_dir,logging,crash}.rs` and `tools/crash-probe/tests/probe.rs`.

## D-120 — Release builds keep their debug info in a separate debug archive, and the release run probes a crash report
**Amended by D-196:** the debug archive only; no release ships one: packaging strips each Linux binary and leaves the PDBs behind, and the build-id, the crash-report tool and the release run's probe stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-05
**Decision:** `release.yml` drops `CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_STRIP`, so release binaries keep the tuned profile's line tables (`debug = 1`), and the Linux build adds `-C link-arg=-Wl,--build-id`, so every binary carries a GNU build-id whatever the runner's compiler defaults to. The Windows artifact adds each example's `.pdb`. In the publish job `scripts/release.py package` splits each placed Linux binary (`objcopy --only-keep-debug --compress-debug-sections=zlib`, then `objcopy --strip-debug --remove-section=.debug_gdb_scripts --add-gnu-debuglink`; rustc's `.debug_gdb_scripts` is an allocated pretty-printer hint that `--strip-debug` keeps, and removing its header leaves the loaded segments as they were). The binary's build-id must equal its debug file's, read from the ELF notes in Python, and the debug file must hold a `.debug_line` section, else packaging fails; the launcher copies are stripped and their debug info dropped. Each Windows example's PDB, found under the file name its CodeView record names, must match the record's GUID and age (the PDB info stream, read from the MSF file), else packaging fails. The player archive keeps its layout and holds no `.debug` or `.pdb` file. `tungsten-debug-<tag>-<target>.tar.gz` (Linux) or `.zip` (Windows) holds `bin/<level>/<example>.debug` or `bin/<level>/<record name>.pdb` for both levels, under the player archive's top folder, so it extracts over the player archive and puts each debug file beside its binary. `SHA256SUMS` covers both archives, and `docs/releases.md` verifies a player-only download with `sha256sum -c --ignore-missing`. The splitter is a parameter of `package`, so tests inject a fake; `release.py split-debug <binary> <debug-out>` runs the same split. `README.txt` says where logs and crash files go (`D-119`), and its console line is Linux-only. `scripts/crash-report.py symbolize` resolves a crash file against a player archive with its debug archive extracted over it (`--root`), or a named binary and debug file, after checking the crash file's `build_id` against the debug file's (Linux) or the PDB's GUID and age (Windows): a mismatch or a missing identity exits 1 before anything is resolved. `crash-report.py probe` copies `tools/crash-probe` and its PDB into an archive-like layout away from their build paths, splits the Linux copy, runs it and symbolizes its report. The release build job builds the probe once at the portable level, outside the level loop, and runs `probe` on both runners with `continue-on-error: true`.

**Why:** A crash file from a player's machine holds addresses, and a stripped build leaves nothing to resolve them with, while debug info inside the player archive would multiply its size for players who never need it: the probe's release build is 16,084,112 bytes, its split binary 2,618,040 and its compressed debug file 3,800,816. Only the release run's own debug files fit its binaries: local builds use `target-cpu=native` and the runner's linker defaults are unchecked, so packaging pins the build-id (and the PDB's GUID and age) and the symbolizer refuses a debug file of another build. The probe needs no GPU, since `App::new` opens no window, so running it on hosted runners stays within `D-070`'s CPU-only, informational CI.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-071` as its marker line says.
- The publish job needs binutils `objcopy` (on the `ubuntu-24.04` image); Linux symbolization needs `addr2line`, Windows symbolization `llvm-symbolizer`. The probe step reports and blocks nothing; the owner reads its two verdicts on a rehearsal tag before the merge (M35 Q8, Q9).
- Downloads that take only `tungsten-examples-*` stay player-only; `SHA256SUMS` then lists files that are not present, hence `--ignore-missing`, which still fails when no listed file is.
- Tests: `scripts/test-release.py` (the ELF build-id and `.debug_line` readers and their failures, the PE CodeView and MSF info-stream readers, a fake splitter's player and debug archives, the Windows debug zip under the record's names, the README's log folders) and `scripts/test-crash-report.py`.

## D-121 — CI adds a CPU-only Windows test job, informational like the rest (W7a)
**Date:** 2026-10-05
**Decision:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gains a `windows-tests` job on `windows-2025`, the image the release build already uses. It runs `git config --global core.autocrlf false` before the checkout, so the tree's files keep their committed line endings, then the Linux job's pinned checkout and toolchain actions, then `cargo test --workspace --locked` in the dev profile, under the workflow's generic `RUSTFLAGS` (`-C target-cpu=x86-64`) and a 90-minute timeout. Like the rest of CI it reports and never blocks: no branch protection and no required status check. It covers the CPU test suite on Windows, the user folder, logger, crash hook and crash probe of `D-119` among it; rendering and audio on Windows stay with hosts or a lower tier (W7b).
**Why:** Linux is the only tested host. M35's Windows paths (`%APPDATA%` and `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, the PE CodeView reader, the windowless subsystem, the PDB check in packaging) are otherwise exercised only by the release build, which runs no tests. A hosted runner needs no GPU for them. The image's git converts line endings on checkout by default, and tests that read checked-in text byte for byte would then see CRLF.
**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-070` as its marker line says.
- The job's first run is on M35's release pull request; the owner reads it, and each failure becomes a known-issue row rather than a fix in M35.
- The job calls `cargo test` directly, so it needs neither `just` nor the Linux job's tool installs; lint, docs and script checks stay in the Linux job.

## D-122 — Release archives carry third-party license notices, collected in the build jobs (W9a)
**Amended by D-196:** the two-files clause only; `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` is the one notices file, and the standard library's `COPYRIGHT-library.html`, converted to plain text, is its last section; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-05
**Decision:** Each `release.yml` build job runs `scripts/release.py licenses <target> --out target/licenses.json` after the build and uploads the file at its artifact's root. The collector runs `cargo fetch --locked --target <target>`, takes the shipped crates from `cargo tree --locked -e normal --target <target>` rooted at the `examples/*` members and `tungsten-launcher` (normal edges only, proc-macro crates included; build-only and dev-only crates and the workspace's own crates, MIT under `LICENSE`, left out), and reads each crate's details from `cargo metadata --locked --filter-platform <target>`: name, version, `license`, `license_file`, `repository`, the text of each root-level file whose name starts with `LICENSE`, `LICENCE`, `COPYING`, `COPYRIGHT`, `UNLICENSE` or `NOTICE` (any case), and the font files (`.ttf`, `.otf`, `.ttc`, `.woff`, `.woff2`) anywhere in the package. It records `rustc --version` and the toolchain's `share/doc/rust/COPYRIGHT-library.html`; a missing crate source or standard-library notice fails it. `release.py package` reads `<bin-dir>/licenses.json`, failing without it or when it names another target, and writes `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES-rust-std.html` at the archive root, which `README.txt` names; `release.py notices <licenses.json> --out <dir>` writes the same two files without an archive, and `just notices` collects and writes both targets into `target/notices/<target>/`. Every crate needs an entry. Its expression's alternatives (`/` read as `OR`, `AND` binding tighter, `WITH` kept with its license) are tried in the order MIT, Apache-2.0, Zlib, BSD-3-Clause, BSD-2-Clause, ISC, Unlicense, 0BSD, CC0-1.0, Unicode-3.0, MPL-2.0, Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception, and the first whose licenses each match one of the crate's files, by phrases of the license's standard text, is used, one text per license; other alternatives' texts, such as `self_cell`'s GPL, are left out. `NOTICE` files always ship with their crate. `licenses/clarifications.json` holds, per crate version, the texts a package lacks (fetched upstream at the crate's tag or packaged commit, stored under `licenses/` with the URL), the works a crate compiles in under another license than its own (`bundled`), and a `fonts_reviewed` note for a package whose font files never compile in. An entry checked at another version than the graph's fails, as does a crate with no entry (every such crate named) and a package carrying a font file with neither a `bundled` entry nor a `fonts_reviewed` note. The engine's own embedded third-party works are the `EMBEDDED_WORKS` table in `release.py`, each with its license file (SMAA's `ATTRIBUTION.md`); a missing file fails. `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` holds a header, the embedded works with their texts, an index of crates (name, version, expression, the licenses used, the crates.io page, the repository, bundled works), then each distinct text once under the crates that use it.

**Why:** A release archive links about 200 third-party crates per target and the Rust standard library, and nearly all of their licenses (MIT, Apache-2.0, the BSDs, Zlib, MPL-2.0's notice duty) ask for their text beside binary copies; `D-071` shipped Tungsten's `LICENSE` only. The publish job has Python but no toolchain or crate sources, so the build jobs collect what only they can read. `cargo tree` resolves features as the build does; `cargo metadata`'s resolve also keeps edges that the build never activates (at `0a1f32f` `flate2` to `zlib-rs`, `jiff` to `defmt`, `codespan-reporting` to `termcolor`, `rustix` to `errno`: seven extra crates on Linux, six on Windows), so it supplies package details only. Standard-library Python, as `D-120` reads ELF and PDB data, needs no tool installed and pinned in the release run, so no `D-015` rule applies. Four shipped crates (`dasp_sample`, `harfrust`, `profiling`, `spirv`) package no license file, and crate metadata cannot show content compiled in under another license: `sctk-adwaita` 0.10.1, Linux only, embeds Cantarell Regular, byte-equal to upstream Cantarell 0.0.5 and under the SIL Open Font License 1.1, while its metadata and its one license file say MIT.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-071` and `D-072` as their marker lines say.
- A dependency change that moves a clarified crate to another version, or adds a crate whose files match no alternative, fails `just notices` and `package` until someone checks upstream and edits `licenses/`. The font-file guard covers fonts only; other data a crate compiles in under another license stays a manual check after a dependency change (`docs/releases.md`).
- `just notices` needs every crate source for both targets, so its `cargo fetch` may need the network.
- The standard library's notices are the building toolchain's file, unedited. The Windows build's static MSVC runtime is not covered here (M36 follow-up).
- Tests: `scripts/test-release.py` (the expression forms, the preferred alternative, the GPL text left out, `NOTICE` kept, clarifications and their version check, a bundled work under an MIT crate, the font guard, normal edges only, a missing source, both files in both archives, and failures for a crate with no entry or a missing `licenses.json`).

## D-123 — Standalone games: a game's folder holds its config and assets, and the engine owns its font (W12a)
**Amended by D-168:** the engine font clause only; `engine_mono` gains JetBrains Mono SemiBold, ID `engine_mono_semibold`, a copy of the root file with the same OFL text, compiled in and registered beside the Regular face, so the family's text at weight 600 picks it; a manifest face under `engine_mono` still replaces the Regular face alone, and `docs/assets.md` reserves both IDs; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-181:** the template clause only; `templates/basic` is a shell copied unchanged into each game (`src/shell/`) and a sample game a new game deletes (`src/sample/`), joined through six names in `game.rs` and `save.rs`, within a budget of 2,000 lines of code for `src/shell/` and 2,600 for `src/`, as `tokei` counts them, in place of "a few hundred lines"; it stays the supported layout and `just template-check` still copies it outside the workspace; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-183:** the outside-copy clause only; `just template-check` makes the copy with `tungsten new`, points its pinned engine lines at this checkout and runs `tungsten check` and `tungsten package` on it as well as the build, tests and smoke run; the template stays the supported layout; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-197:** the examples' working folder only; example 01 holds its own `tungsten.json`, `input.json` and `assets/` and runs from its folder, as the template does, and the workspace root stays the folder of `02_bench` and the fixtures crate; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-05
**Decision:** A game's `tungsten.json`, `input.json` and `assets/` sit in the game's own folder, its working directory; the workspace root is that folder for the examples. The engine owns what its own features draw with: `tungsten::ENGINE_FONT_ID`, `engine_mono`, names JetBrains Mono Regular, a copy of the root font kept with its OFL text in `crates/tungsten/assets/fonts/`, compiled in and registered through `Renderer::load_font` once the renderer starts, before any manifest loads. The HUD, the systems overlay and the inspector default to it, and their `font_id` fields stay public. A manifest face under `engine_mono` replaces it for engine text: `TextEngine::load_font` on a registered ID drops that ID's earlier faces once the new data has registered a face, and data with no face leaves the old registration in place. `TextEngine::register` no longer warns about another face of the same family, weight and style when its bytes are identical; other bytes still warn. `docs/assets.md` reserves `engine_mono`, which manifests are not checked against. No engine feature reads the shared root manifest. `templates/basic` is the supported game layout: built, tested and smoke-run in the workspace, and from a copy outside it in every release's checks (`just template-check`). The action map reloads only when the watcher reports its own canonical path. The sprite and lit shaders, which the renderer compiles in from `assets/shaders/`, and both stock-shader copies (`D-057`, `D-059`) stay until the crates' publication is settled (implementation plan amendment 14).

**Why:** A game copied out of the repository runs from its own folder (criteria §11, question 18), and the HUD's default font was the root manifest's `mono`, so a game without one drew HUD text in its own face and warned at every shaping (`D-116`). The root `mono` and the embedded face are byte-identical, so every example start would have warned about two faces of one family, weight and style. A face loaded under a registered ID left the ID's earlier faces in the font database, so an override could go on drawing the embedded face without a warning. The watcher pins `input.json` by its canonical path, but the reload dispatch matched any changed file of that name, such as a level's `input.json` under `assets/`. A copy in the umbrella crate, tested equal to the root file, keeps the font with the code that uses it; amendment 14 allows compiling in from the root `assets/` for the shaders only.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-008`, `D-013` and `D-045` as their marker lines say.
- One public item, `ENGINE_FONT_ID`, and no break. Core gains nothing and render learns no engine font, so the seam stands (`D-007`, `D-016`).
- Text picks a face by family, weight and style, and fontdb returns the first of equal candidates (`D-116`), so an `engine_mono` override with other bytes is ambiguous beside another manifest face of its family, weight and style, such as the root `mono`: the earlier face draws, and the clash warning names both. A manifest face under another ID that keeps the engine face's family, weight and style may likewise draw with the engine face.
- The examples keep their manifest `mono` for their own text until W12b; the template declares none. The embedded font's OFL text joins `release.py`'s `EMBEDDED_WORKS` (`D-122`).
- Tests: `crates/tungsten/src/tests/engine_font.rs` (the copy equals the root file; with the HUD, the systems overlay and the inspector shown, every engine section names `ENGINE_FONT_ID`), `crates/tungsten/src/tests/app.rs` (the action-map path check), `crates/tungsten-render/src/tests/text_fonts.rs` (identical bytes raise no clash and other bytes do; the engine face alone shapes text; an override replaces the embedded face, data with no face keeps it, and beside a byte-identical `mono` the override's clash names both).

## D-124 — Layout solver: Taffy 0.14 behind Tungsten-owned layout style types (W1 M1)
**Date:** 2026-10-05
**Decision:** `tungsten-core` depends on `taffy` 0.14 with `default-features = false` and the features `std`, `taffy_tree`, `flexbox` and `block_layout` (no `grid`, `float_layout`, `calc`, `content_size`, `detailed_layout_info` or `serde`). Satisfies `D-015` rule 3: the CSS flexbox and block layout algorithms are a solved primitive. Taffy never shows in the public API. A game writes `tungsten_core::ui::LayoutStyle` (`Sizing`, `Edges`, `Direction`, `Align`, `Justify`, `Anchor`, `Placement`) and `crates/tungsten-core/src/ui/layout/taffy.rs` converts it: on the main axis `Sizing::Auto` is content-sized and never shrinks (`flex_basis: auto`, grow and shrink 0), `Fill` takes the remaining space (`flex_basis: 0`, grow and shrink 1) and `Px` and `Percent` are fixed with shrink 0; on the cross axis `Auto` follows the parent's `align_items` (`Stretch` by default), `Fill` stretches and `Px` and `Percent` are fixed; `Placement::Anchored` is an absolute item with insets of its offset on the anchor's `Start` and `End` edges, a centred axis shifted by half the free space after the solve; `Visibility::Collapsed` is `Display::None` and `Hidden` leaves the solver's style unchanged. One Taffy tree per root inside a synthetic viewport-sized parent with the default `LayoutStyle`, so a dirty root relayouts alone. Rounding is off: boxes are fractional, and the rounding rule is defined once at extraction (w01 §6). Text nodes are leaves whose measure closure maps `known_dimensions.width` and the width's `AvailableSpace` onto `TextNodeStore::measure` (`D-125`).

**Why:** The precedent of owning in-scope code (`D-005`, `D-029`) put the burden on Taffy to show that the layout the first screens need is larger than what we would write. M37's spike (`perf-runs/20261005-taffy-layout-spike/`, 2026-10-05) ran w01 §12's three cases, a wrapped label in a row inside a column, hidden against collapsed and min and max constraints, plus anchoring, a fractional percentage width and a wrap boundary at 32.4 against 32.3 px, through a fixed-advance measure closure: every case passed, and a second solve with nothing changed called the closure zero times, so a clean root costs no measurement. Flexbox with min and max sizes, percentages, gaps, absolute positioning, content measurement and a per-node cache is more than a stack and anchor layout, and it is what the screens in w01 §14 need. The cost is one crate over the lock (`arrayvec` 0.7.8 and `slotmap` 1.1.1 were already locked; `version_check` is slotmap's build dependency), MIT, 1.8 s to build in release. Rounding after the solve could wrap a line that fit the measured width (a 32.4 px box rounds to 32 and wraps two 16.2 px glyphs), so the solver's fractional boxes stand and the extraction rounds once.

**Consequences:**
- Core gains a dependency and no `wgpu` type; the seam stands (`D-007`, `D-016`). `cargo tree -e normal -p tungsten-core` grows from 42 to 45 crates. `just deps` covers advisories, licences and bans.
- `LayoutStyle` is the public type, so the solver is internal after M1; a widget that needs a grid turns the `grid` feature on with its own decision.
- Under the default `align_items: Stretch`, a one-line label in a row takes the row's height with its text at the top; `Align::Start` on the row keeps content height.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/ui/layout.rs` reproduces the spike's cases through `UiTree` as `dump_layout()` snapshots.

## D-125 — Core UI model: one `UiTree` resource with generational IDs, a theme-token style model, the `TextNodeStore` seam and the headless `UiHarness` (W1 M1)
**Amended by D-149:** the hit-testing, focus and state-variant clauses only; hit testing follows the paint clip stack and modal roots, the `focused` variant applies only while focus is visible and layers under `hovered` and `pressed` (`disabled` alone), and hiding or collapsing a focus scope restores the focus recorded on entering it; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-162:** the relayout clause only; a `Toggle` or a `Slider` holds no text node, so its resolved text change relayouts its root only when it measures differently and a colour-only change marks paint; labels, buttons and the rest stand.
**Amended by D-163:** the colour and commit clauses only; a resolved text style that changed in colour alone marks paint and re-sets no text node, since the paint command carries the colour; a label measures and commits at its content box, its box less Taffy's border and padding; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-198:** the text-inheritance clause only; letter spacing joins the inherited text properties as `TextOverrides::letter_spacing`, in em, and a non-finite value is ignored; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-05
**Decision:** The UI is one `World` resource, `tungsten_core::ui::UiTree` (`crates/tungsten-core/src/ui/`): roots on `UiLayer`s (`Game`, `GamePopup`, `Debug`, `DebugPopup`, low to high) ordered by layer then insertion; nodes behind generational `WidgetId`s that a removed node never revives; a closed `WidgetKind` (`Panel`, `Label`, `Button`; `Image` at M2, `Toggle` and `Slider` at BC) and `Role` enum, an accessible name and the `interactive`, `focusable`, `focus_scope` and `hit_testable` flags in node data from the start; `Visibility` with `Hidden` (keeps its box, measured and laid out, neither painted nor hit) and `Collapsed` (leaves layout, no rect). Every mutation is a method that marks only what it changes layout- or paint-dirty, and `set_text` is compare-on-write. A `Button` is a node with one `Label` child. The style model is theme tokens (`Theme { tokens, kinds }`, plain data; a game edits a copy and calls `set_theme`, which bumps a `ThemeEpoch` the tree keeps), per-kind defaults with `focused`, `disabled`, `hovered` and `pressed` variants (the last two resolved from M3), per-node `StyleOverrides`, and text-only inheritance: non-text properties are the kind's normal visual, then the state variant, then the node's own, and never inherit; text properties start from the theme's base and pass down the tree, each ancestor's kind and own overrides in order. No selectors. Resolution runs inside `layout(viewport, &mut dyn TextNodeStore)`, cached per node behind the theme epoch, the `WidgetState`, the override version and the parent's resolved text; a change of theme, overrides, parent text or state marks the node style-dirty, and when its resolved text style changed in any property, colour included, the node is re-set and its root relayouts in the same pass, since the engine drops a commit on any style change (`D-117`); background, border and radius changes are paint-only. `layout()` reads the store's `font_epoch()` first and relayouts every root with text when it moved. Hit testing walks layers high to low, roots and siblings latest first, the deepest visible hit-testable node inside every ancestor's box, disabled nodes blocking. Focus is tree order inside the nearest `focus_scope`, wrapping, skipping disabled, hidden and collapsed nodes and their descendants; removing, hiding, collapsing or disabling the focused node or an ancestor clears it. The seam: core's `TextNodeStore: TextMeasure` (`font_epoch`, `create_node`, `remove_node`, `set_text`, `commit_layout`, `committed`), which `TextEngine` and `TextNodes<'_>` implement by forwarding; `TextMeasure` is unchanged. Tests run headless through `tungsten_core::ui::FixedAdvanceMeasure` (a glyph advances `size × advance_em`, greedy wrapping per `TextWrap`, `lines × line_height` tall, nothing rounded) and `tungsten::testing::UiHarness`, which wraps `Harness`, inserts a `UiTree` when the world has none and lays out at the `WindowSize` viewport after each frame. Nothing draws: `App::new` inserts the tree and `run_frame` gains the layout stage at M2.

**Why:** M1 is the data model every later rung paints (M2), routes input into (M3), builds screens on (M4) and moves the debug views onto (M5), so its invariants are set here: one resource with explicit mutation keeps invalidation a dirty flag per root rather than a diff; generational IDs keep a handle from naming a later occupant (`A2`'s shape, as `TextNodeId`); roles, labels and stable IDs in node data from M1 keep accessibility from becoming a rewrite (w01 §12); per-kind defaults with text-only inheritance keep style from growing into a cascade (w01 §14). The seam extends `D-117` rather than widening `TextMeasure`, so nothing breaks, and the engine's own node lifecycle is what the tree drives. A text colour change relayouting its root keeps one rule in M1; the paint-only colour path is M2's follow-up, measured against w01 §10. `UiHarness` on `D-110`'s harness gives games the same headless layout tests the engine runs.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-110` and `D-117` as their marker lines say. Core never calls render (`D-007`); no `wgpu` or `winit` type in `ui/`.
- New public surface in `tungsten-core` (`ui::*`, `text::TextNodeStore`), `tungsten-render` (two trait impls) and `tungsten` (`testing::UiHarness`); no break (`D-107`, the w04 new-surface list).
- Per-frame layout, the paint list, clips, the theme asset, images, input routing, `UiEvent`, hover and pressed states, scroll containers, state-owned roots, the scale factor, closure builders and consumer migration are M0b–M6's.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/ui/{measure,tree,style,layout,hit,focus}.rs`, `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/text.rs`, `crates/tungsten-render/src/tests/text_nodes.rs` (the facade forwards), `crates/tungsten/src/tests/testing_ui.rs`.

## D-126 — Glyph path for 1.0: several glyphon renderers on one atlas with a deferred trim (T1b); the owned glyph path waits (W1 glyph gate)
**Date:** 2026-10-06
**Decision:** UI text keeps drawing through glyphon (`D-026`), in the shape the UI draft calls T1b (`docs/plans/1.0/w01-ui-text-suite.md` §6). M2's paint list carries a text command that names a text run, never a glyphon type. Each text batch that the paint list's partition rule opens owns one `TextRenderer`; every renderer shares one `TextAtlas` and one viewport. `trim` runs only before a frame that re-prepares every batch; such a full frame is forced every K frames and whenever a batch's `prepare` returns `AtlasFull`; between full frames only changed batches re-prepare, and a batch whose `prepare` failed is not drawn that frame. K starts at 16, and the HUD's text telemetry reports atlas bytes, batches prepared per frame and growth frames so M2 and M4 tune it on real screens. The owned glyph renderer (T2) is not built for 1.0: it is a row in `docs/plans/1.0/backlog-1.x.md` with the criteria that bring it back, which are per-glyph transforms and colour animation (wave, pop), in-shader outline, shadow and glow, bitmap fonts, premultiplied alpha, an initial atlas size, per-batch glyph release and text preparation off the main thread (W2 R3, which leaves 1.0 with it). World-anchored labels (W1 WL, placed by the frame-loop gate) draw through the same atlas as one paint-list batch in the overlay's lowest layer and animate per label, through `TextArea.scale` and position; the acceptance game's damage numbers therefore pop and scale per label, with bold crits from a family's bold face (`D-115`).

**Why:** Settled at the glyph gate (implementation plan §11) on the prototype of 2026-10-05 (`perf-runs/20261005-glyph-path-prototype/`, machine-local), with the owner taking each item's default. Four overlapping windows of panels and text draw in paint order from one renderer per window whatever the prepare order (0 pixels differ against a fresh render; one collection drawn last puts 2,912 px of rear text over front panels), a batch re-prepared with the others retained matches a fresh render, and growth under retained batches is safe (0 px). Retaining batches across today's per-frame `trim` is the hazard the draft feared: on a pinned 256² atlas the retained HUD draws other glyphs from frame 4, and under churn every oracle check fails; the deferred trim never does, in 18 sweep cells, and degrades to a forced full frame under pressure. The K sweep over 16 static windows and one live label puts T1 at 56 µs p50 for 820 glyphs and T1b at 5 µs for the live label alone with K ≥ 4, the full frame at p95 until K ≥ 32; under churn K ≥ 16 grows the atlas to 1024² (1.3 MB) with 3–5.7 ms growth frames while K 2–8 stay at 512² and keep evicting. The typewriter reveal needs no T2: transparent spans move no glyph in 170 steps of either font. The one 1.0 criterion T2 would serve, the damage numbers' per-glyph pop, is not worth a second glyph path, an owned atlas and an amended `D-026`; a per-label pop reads the same at the sizes a damage number has.

**Consequences:**
- `D-026` stands and is extended; T2 would amend it. The run-reference text command keeps that reversal local to the paint list.
- M2 carries the prototype's three findings: a draw after `AtlasFull` fails wgpu validation because `prepare` returns from the middle with the new instance count over the old vertex buffer (today's `TextPipeline::prepare` logs and `render` still draws, the same hole); etagere frees a bucket only when every glyph in it is gone, so a nearly full atlas evicts far more than it needs and K must be finite; glyphon evicts before it grows, so the atlas tracks K frames' working set and never shrinks.
- Growth re-rasterizes every cached glyph at about 5 µs each (3–6 ms at 800–1,200 glyphs); M4's prewarm keeps a growth out of interaction frames, and the cold-start row of w01 §10 reports it.
- Colour glyphs, CJK fallback and `ColorMode::Web` were not exercised; M2's fixture judges them before the colour convention is fixed.
- The acceptance game's damage-numbers row reads "per label"; the per-glyph version is the T2 row's first criterion. W2 R3 leaves the Phase 6 table.

## D-127 — DPI model: logical UI units, text laid out in logical units and rasterized at physical size, hinting off; `display.scale_mode` leaves 1.0 (W1 glyph gate)
**Amended by D-184:** the scale factor only; `ScaleFactor::get` stays the OS scale times the player's UI scale unless a game sets a reference height, which makes the base the larger of the OS scale and the window's height over it, or a minimum viewport, which holds the factor down so the window never lays out smaller than it; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-06
**Decision:** UI coordinates are logical units with a top-left origin, converted to physical pixels once at extraction with one rounding rule that primitives and glyph clips share (w01 §6). The scale factor, the OS scale times a user `ui_scale` setting that W11b persists, lives in a core resource that W1 M0b adds and the bridge updates on `ScaleFactorChanged`. Text is laid out in logical units and drawn with glyphon's `TextArea.scale`, so glyphs rasterize at physical size and a scale change relayouts nothing; hinting stays off, as today. `display.scale_mode` (`Stretch` | `Integer`), parsed and never applied, leaves `tungsten.json` and `DisplayConfig` before the freeze as a break-ledger row for W4b's sweep. Integer scene scaling is a 1.x render feature; when it comes it scales the scene only and leaves the UI at native resolution.

**Why:** Settled at the glyph gate (implementation plan §11) on the prototype's DPI strip (`perf-runs/20261005-glyph-path-prototype/captures/dpi-*.png`), with the owner taking the default. Twelve-pixel text laid out logically and drawn at `TextArea.scale` 2.0 equals a 24 px physical layout byte for byte, and 1.5 equals 18 px, in both packaged fonts: the scale enters glyphon's cache key, so fork (a) of w01 §2 loses nothing against unhinted physical layout, and the only thing fork (b) adds is hinting, which changes 3,073 px of a 12 px line and cuts the distinct glyph keys 48 → 36 at the price of physical-pixel layout and a relayout of every label on a scale change. The owner read the strip and kept today's rendering. No 1.0 game needs integer scene scaling, and a dead field in a file format the stability policy promises (`D-103`) would be frozen with it.

**Consequences:**
- The known-issues row for `display.scale_mode` closes with its removal; W1 M0b adds the scale-factor resource; M2 applies the conversion and the rounding rule.
- Hinting returns only with a physical-layout mode, a 1.x question; nothing in the model blocks it.
- `ui_scale` is one of the settings W11b persists (criteria question 15); a text-scale setting apart from it is M4's accessibility item (w01 §14).
- Amends no decision.

## D-128 — Frame-loop decision set, part 1: a `Schedule` of five closed stages with named systems and plugins in `tungsten-core` (W15a)
**Amended by D-155:** the kit's constraints only; a kit system's constraints on the engine's names and across the kit's plugins are if-present, as the engine's cross-plugin couplings are, while a plugin's own chains and a game's constraints stay required; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-06
**Decision:** `tungsten_core::schedule` holds a `Schedule` of five closed stages, `startup`, `pre_update`, `fixed_update`, `update` and `post_update`, in that order; the app's own steps (the exit check, the command flush of `D-039`, event rotation, hot reload, the post-AA request, extract, render, audio, input frame boundary and telemetry) run between and after them and take no systems. A system is `system(name, f)` with required `before`/`after` constraints on names in its own stage and `before_if_present`/`after_if_present` forms; a stable topological sort per stage resolves them with ties in registration order, and `resolve` fails at startup for a duplicate name, an unknown name (saying where the system is when it sits in another stage) or a cycle, each error naming the systems. `Plugin::build(&self, &mut Schedule, &mut World)` registers systems, events (`World::register_event`, idempotent; `World::flush_events` rotates every queue once per frame) and inspector rows (`InspectRegistry`, a `World` resource); `PluginSet::with`/`without` add and remove plugins by type; `App::new` is `DefaultPlugins`, `App::with_plugins` takes a subset or the empty set, and `App::add_system_to` reaches a stage other than `update`. Engine systems have public name constants; the resolved schedule's text is a snapshot test, and the systems overlay lists it in run order. The engine's cross-plugin couplings use the if-present forms; a plugin's own chains and a game's or the kit's constraints are required, so a typo fails at startup. `WindowSize` moves to core, which lets every kit item resolve to core types. `App` keeps inserting the engine's resources whichever plugins run. `02_bench` builds from an explicit plugin set, wires engine systems in its own order as today, and registers the events its hand-wired systems send. `PhysicsPlugin` lives in core, in the kit's shape; the display, debug, state, particle, tween, game-feel and camera plugins are the umbrella's. Extract contributions stay the umbrella's (W15c), since render types cannot enter core (`D-007`, `D-016`).

**Why:** Settled at the frame-loop gate (implementation plan §11) on the stage-and-plugin spike of 2026-10-05 (`perf-runs/20261005-stage-plugin-design/`, machine-local), with the owner taking each item's default. The spike put all of it in core with no render type, replaced the systems `App::new` registered and the two untimed particle and tween steps with eight plugins, left the template one plugin with a startup system that names no engine system, and kept the bench's order and every digest: every workspace test, smoke and the visual fixture passed, and a six-suite sitting read 0 regressed on every direct pair with digests matching 8 of 8, `ecs` `update` p50 10.27 → 10.27 ms and `physics_step` 6.10 → 6.10. The `particles` row's `unattributed` 2.19 → 0.00 ms is the particle stage relocated into three named `post_update` systems with `total` unchanged, so the owned metric loses its meaning and W15a re-owns the row. `fixed_update` sits before `update`, as Unity, Godot and Bevy order it and as W3b's accumulator wants: a seeded player under the old wiring moves on the first frame after a press, with its input in `update` on the second, and with its input in `fixed_update` before `physics_step` on the first again, so gameplay that drives bodies opts into `fixed_update` and the guide says so. A required `after(PHYSICS_SYNC)` failed startup once a game left `PhysicsPlugin` out, hence the if-present form. Plugins owning their resources would leave a game that drops `DebugPlugin` without a `DebugHud` to read and asks the extract to tolerate absent resources, for no 1.0 user.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-018` as its marker line says: user systems run in the five named stages that `Schedule` resolves, and the extract still reads a `&World` after them. Amends `D-040` as its marker line says: event registration and rotation live on `World`.
- `D-039`'s flush, `D-054`'s closed-enum reasoning (the `Stage` enum is closed; no custom stages in 1.0) and the two-thread rule stand. A `Last` stage is a one-variant addition if W13b's propagation must see the frame's deferred spawns.
- W15a lands it with the migration of example 01's input and contact systems into `fixed_update`, the bench's explicit set, the `particles` row's new owned metric (a benchmark-schema change the compare reports), the public name constants, the snapshot test and `just api`. Core gains about 62 public items, the umbrella `plugins` and six `App` methods; nothing is removed.
- Not decided here: additive extracts (W15c), W16a's component registry, running `fixed_update` more than once (`D-129`).

## D-129 — Frame-loop decision set, part 2: `Time`, a fixed step with interpolation on by default, timers and the clocks (W3a, W3b; Q7, Q13)
**Date:** 2026-10-06
**Decision:** A `Time` resource in core, inserted by `App`, carries the real clock (the frame's dt capped as `D-088` caps it, pinned in smoke runs, written as given by the harness), the game clock (real dt times `scale`, zero while paused), elapsed time on both, the frame index and a fixed-step accumulator with `fixed_step` (default 1/60 s), `max_steps_per_frame` (default 2), the steps run this frame, the dropped time and `alpha`. Each frame the app advances the clock, then runs `fixed_update` as many times as the accumulator holds whole steps, at most the bound, dropping whole steps beyond it and keeping the fraction; around each step `DeltaTime.dt` and `Time::delta()` read the fixed step and `InputState` answers edge queries from its fixed view (edges since the last step, so a press reaches exactly one step at every frame rate), and outside the steps they read the game dt and the frame view. A paused game runs no fixed step: it skips the physics stage rather than stepping it by zero. `Timer` is a plain value (duration, once or repeating; `tick(dt)` returns how many times it finished), ticked with whichever clock its owner passes; no timer system or event queue. Interpolation is on by default: `PrevPosition` is spawned with the body (W15b's `RigidBodyBundle` and the kit's spawn helpers include it, never an engine-side adoption pass), `physics_prev_snapshot` copies `Position` into it before each step, and `physics_sync` in `post_update` writes `Transform.position = prev + (cur − prev) × alpha` for bodies that carry it while `Time::interpolate()` is on, `Position` for every other body; the `D-018` seam, the extract and `Position` as the true state (`D-033`) are untouched. A teleport writes `Position` and `PrevPosition` together. The clocks (question 13): `physics_step` and gameplay that opts into `fixed_update` run on the fixed step; tweens, particles, game feel, the camera and game systems in `update` read the game dt, so a pause freezes them and slow motion slows them; state transitions, the HUD, overlays, the inspector, UI animation tracks, navigation repeat and double-click timing read real time; a "frame" stays the render frame for the HUD and telemetry, with the fixed steps a count on it and each system's runs summed under its name in the `systems:` line.

**Why:** Settled at the frame-loop gate (implementation plan §11) on the W3 design spike of 2026-10-05 (`perf-runs/20261005-w3-stage-map/`, machine-local), with the owner taking each item's default. Every test, smoke, the physics release tests (hash `0x088ec07a73c1b168` unchanged) and the visual fixture passed; a seven-suite sitting read the clock stage and the empty fixed loop at no cost with digests 8 of 8, and `physics_step` through `fixed_update` with the snapshot `unchanged` (6.15 → 6.15 ms aligned). The probes decide the defaults: a fixed-stage reader of per-frame edges misses 56% of presses at 144 Hz and sees each twice at 30 Hz, the fixed view sees each once; a 600 px/s body renders 0 or 10 px a frame at 144 Hz without history and a steady 4.17 px with it, one step (16.7 ms) behind game time, a lag the owner accepted for smooth motion on displays above 60 Hz; input in `fixed_update` before the step moves the body on the frame of the press; at a 0.1 s stall the bound of 2 runs two steps and drops 67 ms, which is `D-094`'s 1/30 s of simulated time a frame, so below 30 FPS the game runs slow instead of spiralling, the behaviour `D-094` chose and the owner kept (a game raises the bound in `tungsten.json`'s `[time]` section). `integrated` with its AI left in `update` after the step read 5.8% slower on a drifted workload, and with the AI opted into `fixed_update` before the step the digest and the cost were the baseline's, which is why gameplay that drives bodies opts in (`D-128`). Transitions on real time let a pause menu's fade run over a frozen world (criteria §6). Inserting `PrevPosition` on a live body would move it to another archetype and reorder the solve (`D-066`), so the component is spawned, never adopted.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-088` as its marker line says (`Time` replaces the single dt; the cap, its constant and the smoke pin stand), `D-094` (the loop's step bound is `max_steps_per_frame`; `max_step_dt` stays as the step's own invariant for direct callers and is never reached from the loop at the default step) and `D-093` (transitions advance on the real clock).
- W3a lands `Time` and `Timer` and deprecates `DeltaTime` (removed at the freeze, the ledger row); W3b lands the accumulator, the fixed edge view, `PrevPosition`, the snapshot and the interpolating sync, `FrameTimings::fixed_steps`, and decides the `[time]` section's fields (`fixed_step_hz`, `max_steps_per_frame`, `interpolate`). W15b's bundle carries `PrevPosition`; the kit's helpers and the guide carry the teleport rule.
- At the pinned 1/60 s a frame runs one step, `alpha` is 0 and a body with history renders the previous step's position: invisible to the digests, which hash `Position`, and to the owned metrics; a pixel test of a moving body sees it.
- Cursor and scroll deltas stay per frame; a fixed-step reader of them is a W3b follow-up if one turns up. `sync_position_to_transform` is five times slower than the optional-column query on the same world (52.6 against 10.7 µs at 12,000 bodies); W3b takes the win in `physics_sync`.
- Not decided here: replays, a per-system step rate, W2 workers inside a step.

## D-130 — Tuple queries and bundles: `World::query`/`query_mut` over query-data tuples through `get_disjoint_mut`, no `unsafe`; the arity-named functions deprecated to the freeze (W15b)
**Amended by D-135:** W15b landed at M40: the panicking resource accessor (`World::resource`/`resource_mut`) and `query_entities` join the scope, so eleven functions are deprecated since 0.54.0; the lone mutable column's cost is recorded, not removed; the default extract's hot read takes the slice form; `RigidBodyBundle` lands without `PrevPosition`, which W3b adds; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-06
**Decision:** A query names its data as a type: `&A`, `&mut A`, `Option<&A>`, `Option<&mut A>` or `Entity`, alone or in a tuple of up to eight, and its filter as a second type parameter, `With<T>`, `Without<T>`, `()` or a tuple of up to four, checked against an archetype's type key and never per row. `World::query::<Q>()` and `query_filtered::<Q, F>()` read over `&self`; `query_mut` and `query_mut_filtered` write over `&mut self`; `query_slices`, `query_mut_slices` and their filtered forms yield `(rows, slices)` per matching archetype. Every mutable column of an archetype is borrowed through one `slice::get_disjoint_mut` call, with an access check that panics with the query's type name when two items name one component, so the engine needs no `unsafe`; the traits are sealed and the column types private. `query::<T>()` and `query_mut::<T>()` therefore take query data instead of a component type at W15b, a break the ledger carries (callers write `&T`); the numbered arity functions stay, `#[deprecated]`, until the freeze removes them. Bundles: `world.spawn_with((A, B, C))` and a command-buffer form over a `Bundle` trait for tuples and game types, one archetype move per spawn, with `RigidBodyBundle` carrying `PrevPosition` (`D-129`). No `Send` or `Sync` bound anywhere (`D-131`). W15b budgets one step for the lone mutable column's loop layout, measured with `tuple_query_bench`'s `single_mut` and `entity_mut` shapes with and without LLVM's alignment flags, and records the cost in its evidence if no layout removes it.

**Why:** Settled at the frame-loop gate (implementation plan §11) on the tuple-query spike of 2026-10-05 (`perf-runs/20261005-w15-tuple-queries/`, machine-local), with the owner choosing the conventional names over the spike's `fetch`. Twelve tests read each arity-named shape row for row on a sixteen-archetype world; the `ecs` benchmark's fourteen systems and `churn`'s scan rewritten on the tuple API left every digest unchanged and `ecs` `update` p50 10.39 → 10.43 ms (`unchanged`, +0.04 [−0.01, +0.09]) in plain and block-aligned pairs; the optional, filtered and three-column shapes read 6–20% faster per row in criterion and the rest equal. One shape costs: a lone mutable column with a trivial body, `bounds_wrap`, 0.25 → 0.30 ms (+0.22 ns a row), because LLVM lays the loop over a lone `IterMut` out with one more taken jump and a re-zeroed register than the index loop it builds for the arity function's `Zip`, at native flags and aligned alike; W15b's done-when on `ecs` fails on that row as the patch stands, hence the budgeted step. The R1 spike's hand-written `query3_opt2_slices` is the slice form. A 1.0 API whose query is called `fetch` because two deprecated functions hold the name would carry that choice for all of 1.x.

**Consequences:**
- `D-005` stands: no external ECS. Amends no decision.
- Two break-ledger rows for W15b (`query`, `query_mut`); the arity-named functions' removal and the bundle-insert API already have theirs.
- W2 R1, should it return from 1.x, builds on the slice forms and needs no query of its own.
- W3b's `physics_sync` and the physics gather move onto the tuple forms where they win; `query2_mut`'s two-column shape is checked at W15b (`D-129`'s sync finding).

## D-131 — Multi-core scope for 1.0: R2 only; the fork-join extract (R1) leaves 1.0; no worker mechanism, no `Send`/`Sync` bounds, the two-thread rule stands (W2; Q5, Q6)
**Amended by D-220:** the two-thread clause only; the engine runs three background threads, the third `gilrs`'s backend thread behind the default `gamepad` feature; no worker mechanism and no `Send`/`Sync` bounds stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-06
**Decision:** W2's 1.0 scope is R0 (interned IDs and culling, landed in 0.45) and R2, the late-acquire split. The fork-join default and tilemap extracts (R1) leave 1.0 after their spike and join the 1.x backlog; they come back when C1 shows an extract-bound game row on a machine whose CPU has the memory bus to itself, or when the bytes-per-instance work the spike points at (fewer bytes per instance, or instances written once into the upload buffer, a C2 candidate) leaves an extract CPU-bound. No 1.0 candidate spawns a worker, so question 5 is moot for 1.0; if a fork-join stage ever lands, its mechanism is a rayon `ThreadPool` owned by `App` (`D-015` rule 3), not `std::thread::scope` and not rayon's global pool. Components and resources take no `Send` or `Sync` bound (question 6): a parallel path puts `T: Sync` at its own call site on the types it slices, and a parallel system scheduler, if 1.x wants one, is opt-in per system over `Sync` types. `AGENTS.md`'s two-background-thread rule (the `cpal` callback and the `notify` watcher) stands unchanged, and `D-067`'s serial physics step with it.

**Why:** Settled at the frame-loop gate (implementation plan §11) on the W2 R1 spike of 2026-10-05 (`perf-runs/20261005-w2-r1-spike/`, machine-local), with the owner taking each item's default. Pass 1 of the default extract over archetype column slices on scoped workers with per-worker buffers and a serial merge was bit-identical to the serial path at 1, 2, 3, 4 and 6 workers and did not pay on the reference machine: `gpu-throughput` `extract` p50 read `regressed` at every count (12.07 → 12.51 / 12.90 / 13.24 / 13.22 ms at 1 / 2 / 4 / 6 workers), because the row is bound by memory bandwidth the CPU shares with the integrated GPU (the same instructions take 3.4× the cycles at 6 workers, IPC 1.64 → 0.49 with clocks held; the serial extract halves when the GPU's fill work is cut to a quarter; headless the same pass scales 2.7× at 6 workers until the GPU row runs beside it) and the single-batch path's record concatenation, this path's whole merge, costs 1.37–4.02 ms; `particles` gained 0.15 ms of `extract` while its serial sort and pass 2 (61% of the extract) stayed and its owned `unattributed` and `animate_sprites` read `regressed` by 0.03–0.22 ms. One worker costs 0.35–0.44 ms of pass 1 on both rows, ten times the 22 µs spawn, so a persistent pool would not have rescued the design either; a `std::thread::scope` fork costs 21–71 µs a frame against 4 µs for a rayon pool, which decides the mechanism for any later stage. Requiring `Send + Sync` on every component now would break every game that holds an `Rc` or a `RefCell` for a scheduler no 1.0 row needs, and the tuple queries (`D-130`) carry no bound.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-102` as its marker line says: the multi-core clause reads R2 only. The threading rule, the worker mechanism and the `Send`/`Sync` policy that criteria §5.4 and §11 listed are answered here; `AGENTS.md` is unchanged.
- The break ledger's `Send`/`Sync` row closes as not a break. The column-slice query is `D-130`'s `query_slices` forms, which carry no `Sync` bound of their own.
- Track C drops W2 R1; the capture-rule extension for worker threads (criteria §12) waits until a candidate spawns one. The `gpu-throughput` row keeps its bytes-per-instance finding for C1 and C2.
- W2 R3 leaves 1.0 with the glyph gate's T1b (`D-126`) and R4 stays in 1.x (`D-102`).

## D-132 — W6 and W13 tiers for 1.0 from the acceptance game's feature map; the tilemap builder, world labels and audio pause placed (Q10, Q21)
**Superseded by D-215:** the acceptance-game map as the tier source; 1.0's scope is the roadmap redo's §5.7 and its phases.
**Date:** 2026-10-06
**Decision:** From the acceptance game's mechanics rows (`docs/plans/1.0/acceptance-game.md`), W6 is Must for kinematic bodies with contact events against dynamic bodies, sensors with enter events per pair, nearest and overlap shape queries, shape and ray casts against bodies and tile layers with the hit point and normal, layers and masks, audio buses and a voice limit per sound; Should for voice handles and fades; audio pause moves to 1.x. Every W13 item is Must with the game as its user, except `cursor_to_world`, the platformer controller and the path follower, whose only user is example 01 and which are Must only if example 01 moves onto the kit by the freeze (W12b), under the kit's admission rule. Physics bodies live on root entities only for 1.0 (question 21): W13b's hierarchy propagates transforms after `physics_sync`, a body on a child is refused, and the game places its orbiting blades each fixed step from the player's position. The generated arena's tilemap builder, a public `TilemapData` constructor with tile setters, is W16b's. World-anchored text leaves the 1.x backlog as W1 WL, a level B step after M3 in Phase 6. W6a's plan probes a crowd pressing on a kinematic player from every side, with several spawn orders and frame dt as a parameter, before the kit's mover lands.

**Why:** Settled at the frame-loop gate (implementation plan §11), with the owner confirming the tiers proposed from the spec agreed on 2026-10-05 and taking each placement's default. The game is the cut line (`D-102`): every Must row names its user in the game (the mover and the blades for kinematic bodies, bolts, blades and gems for sensors, the wand, the aura and the magnet for queries, the mover and bouncing bolts for casts, every sensor and bolts that pass over walls for masks, the volume sliders and the boss's music for buses, bursts of hits for the voice limit), fades have "a cut works" as their fallback, and nothing uses audio pause (music plays through pause and level-up, and effects are short). Bodies on roots keep one propagation point and the physics gather's archetype order (`D-066`) simple; the blades cost the game one position write per step. Only `TilemapData`'s public fields and `TilemapRegistry::insert` build a tilemap in code today, and W4's audit could close them, so a candidate had to own the builder; W16b already touches the tilemap loader. The damage numbers are the first 1.0 user of world-anchored text, and a step of its own keeps M2 at its size.

**Consequences:**
- Question 10 is answered with the W6 table and question 21 with "roots only"; W6a, W6b with W6c, W13a with W13b, W13c with W13d and W16b carry the rows in their cards (implementation plan §4). W6's decisions still amend `D-033` and touch `D-064` and `D-065` when W6a lands; the audio policy for `D-034`'s ring comes with W6c.
- The 1.x backlog gains audio pause and loses world-anchored text. The example-01-only kit items are decided at the freeze by W12b's state.
- Amends no decision; fulfils `D-102`'s consequence that the tiers were due at this gate.

## D-133 — W15a landed (M38): the `particles` row re-owned on its three named systems, plain engine system names, `WindowSize` in `tungsten_core::display`, `InspectorState`'s row API deprecated for `InspectRegistry`, example 01's stage assignment as the opt-in rule's worked example
**Amended by D-155:** clause (2) only; the constants are defined in `tungsten_core::schedule::names` and re-exported unchanged at `tungsten::plugins`; the other clauses stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-06
**Decision:** The five choices `D-128` left to W15a. (1) The `particles` benchmark row owns `system.particle_count_refresh`, `system.particle_emit`, `system.particle_tick` and `system.animate_sprites`, each at p50 and p95, with `system.particle_tick` its declared bottleneck; `stage.unattributed` is owned by no row, and `workload_version` stays 1, since the row's work and digest are unchanged. (2) The engine's system names are plain public constants in `tungsten::plugins`, without the spike's `__` prefix: `physics_debug_toggle`, `systems_overlay_toggle`, `inspector_toggle`, `inspector_pick`, `hud_toggle`, `display_input` and `state_dispatcher` in `pre_update`; `physics_step` in `fixed_update`; `physics_sync`, `particle_count_refresh`, `particle_emit`, `particle_tick`, `tween_tick`, `squash_stretch_trigger`, `squash_stretch_tick`, `shake_tick` and `camera_update` in `post_update`. (3) `WindowSize` lives at `tungsten_core::display::WindowSize`, re-exported at `tungsten_core::WindowSize`, `tungsten::WindowSize` and `tungsten::app::WindowSize`, so no caller changes and the move is not a break. (4) `InspectorState::new_with_defaults`, `register` and `registered_len` are `#[deprecated(since = "0.52.0")]` shims that keep their behaviour: the constructor registers its six rows on the state, and the overlay prints the `InspectRegistry` resource's rows first, then the state's own; `App::new` fills the registry from `tungsten::inspector::default_inspect_registry`, and W4b removes the three with the other deprecated items (the break ledger's row). (5) Example 01 is the `fixed_update` opt-in rule's worked example: its order is kept system for system with the stage boundaries drawn through it. `platformer_bindings` in `pre_update`; the twelve systems that ran before `physics_step` (its input, spawners and forces, and the three riders `update_text_display`, `rainbow_ball_hue_system` and `tick_ball_fire`) in `fixed_update` before `PHYSICS_STEP`; the five systems that ran after it (`ground_detection`, `small_ball_impacts`, `hazard_contacts`, `fireball_flight_system`, `spread_ball_fire`) after `PHYSICS_STEP` in that order; six in `update`; and `ball_fire_particles`, `orbit_lights_system`, `scene_effects` and `platformer_camera_base_zoom` as one `post_update` group `after(PHYSICS_SYNC).before(PARTICLE_COUNT_REFRESH)`, ahead of the engine's whole chain.

**Why:** Owned metrics change only by decision (the profiling workflow's regression policy), and the particle stage became three named systems: in the milestone's sitting of 2026-10-06 (`perf-runs/20261006-m38-schedule/`, machine-local; six suites of every tracked row at `--repeat 5`, the untouched `764ef4e` tree against this one, both direct pairs) `particles` `stage.unattributed` p50 read 2.19 → 0.00 ms (`improved` by 2.18 and 2.19 ms), the three new systems' medians (`particle_count_refresh` 0.60/0.81, `particle_emit` 0.10/0.13, `particle_tick` 1.46/1.53 ms p50/p95) sum to it, `total` read `unchanged` (p50 4.42 → 4.37 and 4.41 → 4.43 ms) and every compare matched digests 8 of 8, so the metric lost its meaning while the work stayed; every other row read 0 `regressed`. The `__` prefix marked engine rows in a flat list, which the stages now do, and a public constant the freeze covers should read like one; the baseline's `systems:` rows differ in those seven names, which compare lists as missing on one side (no verdict, no owner). `WindowSize` is a `Copy` pair of `u32`s that core's `display.rs` holds beside the display's other value types with no umbrella dependency, and the kit needs it to resolve to core types (`D-128`). Removing the three `InspectorState` functions would have contradicted `D-128`'s "nothing is removed" and W15's criterion, and a replaced item stays deprecated to the freeze (`D-103`); no example or template calls them. Example 01's assignment follows its data: `scene_effects` reads `p.age == 0.0` to colour newborn particles before `particle_tick` advances it, `platformer_camera_base_zoom` writes `CameraState.zoom` before `camera_update_system` reads it, `fireball_flight_system` precedes `spread_ball_fire` so a blast's ignitions spread in the same frame, and `tick_ball_fire` precedes both so an expiring source is out before it could ignite a neighbour; with `player_input` in `fixed_update` before the step a press moves the player on the step after it (`a_press_moves_the_player_on_the_next_step`), and with it in `update` the same test fails (`x` unchanged after the press's frame).

**Consequences:**
- Amends no decision: `D-128` deferred these five to this milestone, and its "nothing is removed" holds, since the three functions stay to W4b. `D-129`'s accumulator is not here: `fixed_update` runs once a frame.
- Compare reports the `particles` row's owned-metric change as a schema change: captures before 0.52 lack the three `system.*` metrics and captures from 0.52 lack `stage.unattributed` as an owned metric, each without a verdict, so the row's baseline restarts at 0.52 (step 3's `tree-suite`). `unattributed` in `particles` now holds the event flush alone.
- Pinned by `tests/plugins.rs`'s `DefaultPlugins` snapshot (the names), example 01's `runtime_schedule_matches_expected_pipeline` and `a_press_moves_the_player_on_the_next_step`, the template's and examples 03 and 04's schedule snapshots, and the `api/` snapshots (`just api-check`). The ledger carries the deprecation's removal at W4b; the benchmarks guide's T1 proposal closes, since the stage is timed under its names and `FrameTimings` gains no `particles_ms`.
- Not decided here: W3b may move the three riders to `update` when `fixed_update` runs per step (`D-129`); `CameraPlugin`, `GameFeelPlugin` and `TweensPlugin` into core (W13's plan); the hand-written test loops of known issues, left to the Phase 5 QA pass.

## D-134 — W3a landed (M39): `Time` with the real and game clocks and `Timer` in core, their edge rules, engine systems on `Time` alone, transitions on the real clock, `DeltaTime` deprecated since 0.53.0
**Date:** 2026-10-06
**Decision:** The clock half of `D-129`'s `Time`, with the choices it left to W3a. (1) `tungsten_core::time::Time`, re-exported at the crate root with `Timer` and `TimerMode`, has private fields and `new`/`Default`: both clocks at zero, scale 1, running, frame 0. `advance_frame(real_dt)` is public: the app's clock stage calls it once a frame, and a world driven by hand calls it in that stage's place.
