**Why:** W11b's scope and owner question 15's default (`D-150`): a corrupt user file resets to defaults, and the engine persists the display settings, `ui_scale`, the master volume and the bindings. A file the game wrote never stops it, while a broken shipped file stays a developer's error (`D-008`, `D-045`). Saving only the difference lets a game update's new defaults reach every action the player left alone. A code-set folder beats the environment so the template's tests stay pinned in a developer's shell (they used to read `~/.local/state/tungsten-template-basic`), and smoke mode beats both, so captures read no user file (RC-S3). A set vsync clears the present mode because a concrete present mode wins over vsync in the renderer. The plan's critique shaped the runtime apply: computing it from the shipped baseline lets an unset value come back and makes a runtime apply agree with a restart, editing the queued request lets the last apply before a frame win over a hotkey's, and re-applying the overrides only to a loaded config keeps startup and runtime on one rule.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-008`, `D-045` and `D-119` as their marker lines say. `D-022`, `D-127` and `D-144` stand.
- Not persisted: the present mode, frame latency and frame-rate cap stay developer settings in `tungsten.json`; the F9 and F11 hotkeys persist nothing, and `apply_settings` replaces their changes to the persisted fields. A window resized by hand returns to the set or shipped resolution at the next `apply_settings`, since a resize persists nothing.
- A save's skipped bindings are logged when the save runs, a logger existing by then; `ActionMap::take_load_warnings` holds the user layer's load warnings, which `App::new` logs.
- The API changes and additions are in the W4 break ledger; `ActionMapError` gains no variant (W4b's sweep can add a serialize variant).
- Template: a `toggle_mute` action on M that sets or unsets the master volume, applies and saves; `log` in its manifest; its tests set `UserFolder::Off`, and `mute_survives_a_restart` uses a temp folder.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/{persist,settings,config,display}.rs`, the action map's and `key_serde`'s, `crates/tungsten/src/tests/{user_dir,settings,app}.rs` and the template's `tests/game.rs`.
- Open: volume per bus joins `Settings` in W6c; the settings screen and its capture-next-input mode are W1 M4's; gamepad bindings W5a's; the user files are not hot-reloaded.

## D-160 — W11c landed (M53): save slots hold a game's own serde types in a versioned envelope in the user folder's `saves`, migrated by the game's own code; slot names follow `game.id`'s rule in lowercase; every failure is a typed error
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Decision:** `tungsten_core::save` adds `SaveData`, a trait over the game's `Serialize + DeserializeOwned` type with a `SCHEMA: u32` and a `migrate(from, Value) -> Result<Value, Box<dyn Error + Send + Sync>>` that by default refuses every older schema, and `SaveSlots`, a `World` resource `App::new` inserts over the user folder's `saves` (`D-119`). A slot is `<saves>/<slot>.json`, written through `write_atomic` (`D-159`): `{ "envelope": 1, "slot", "schema", "engine_version", "saved_at_ms", "data" }`, the engine version core's, the time Unix milliseconds or 0 for a clock before 1970; the folder is created on the first save. `load` reads envelope 1 only (`UnknownEnvelope` else), deserializes data saved under `T::SCHEMA`, calls `T::migrate` once for an older schema and then deserializes (`Migration` on either failure), and refuses a newer one (`NewerSchema`); a failed load never panics and never changes the file. `list` reads the header of every `*.json` that is not a dotfile (the write's temp files are), sorted by name, and skips an unreadable one with a line in `SlotList::warnings`; `delete` removes a slot. A slot name is 1–64 of `a`–`z`, `0`–`9`, `.`, `_` and `-`, starting with a letter or digit and not a Windows device name: `game.id`'s rule, lowercase only. `SaveError` (`#[non_exhaustive]`) has `NoFolder`, `InvalidName`, `NotFound`, `Corrupt`, `Serialize`, `UnknownEnvelope`, `NewerSchema`, `Migration`, `Io` and `Write`; without a folder (smoke mode, `UserFolder::Off`, no `game.id`) every call is `NoFolder`. `tungsten_core::save::Value` re-exports `serde_json::Value`, so a game writes a migration without a `serde_json` dependency of its own.

**Why:** Owner question 16 made save slots Must for 1.0 (`D-102`), and the acceptance game's unlocks row needs them. The game owns its types and migrations because only it knows what changed; the engine owns the envelope, so it can list slots and refuse a file it cannot read without knowing the game's types. Lowercase names keep two slots from sharing one file on a case-insensitive file system, and names may come from a player, so a bad one is a runtime error (`D-022`). Refusing a newer schema and never rewriting a failed slot keep a newer build's save safe from an older build, which is why the template's autosave turns read-only for the run when its load fails (the plan's critique finding 1).

**Consequences:**
- Narrows DESIGN's "Save / load" non-commitment to World snapshots: saving the `World` by reflection stays a backlog row (saves by reflection).
- No break: the module, its re-exports at the core root (`SaveData`, `SaveSlots`) and the resource are additions for the freeze (the W4 break ledger's new-surface row).
- Template: gameplay continues from slot `autosave` (`SaveGame { player }`, schema 1), pause saves it, and a slot that fails to load other than as missing is never overwritten that run; its tests `continue_from_the_autosave` and `a_slot_that_fails_to_load_is_never_overwritten`.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/save.rs`, the `SaveData` doctest and `crates/tungsten/src/tests/save_slots.rs` (a schema bump through the game's migration across two `App`s on one folder, a failed migration).
- Open: no slot metadata beyond the header (a thumbnail, a play time) and no cloud sync; a save is written on the calling thread, which suits the small saves 1.0 targets.

## D-161 — W1 WL landed (M54): world labels are pushed each frame to a core `WorldLabels` resource, projected, culled, capped and scale-snapped by the umbrella's extract, and drawn as one glyphon batch below the paint list on the shared layout cache, counted under the trim rule
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Decision:** `tungsten_core::text` adds `WorldLabel` (`#[non_exhaustive]`: text, a world `anchor`, an `offset` in UI units, a `pivot` in the label's box, default (0.5, 1.0), font ID, size and line height in UI units, sRGB colour and its own `scale`) and `WorldLabels`, a `World` resource `App::new` inserts: immediate mode, pushed during the frame and drained by every extract, drawn or not. `CameraState::world_to_screen` inverts `screen_to_world`, rotation included. The umbrella's extract (`extract_world_labels`, after the paint list) projects each anchor onto the viewport render draws at (the surface's size, else `WindowSize`), adds the offset times `ScaleFactor::get()`, culls a point outside the viewport grown by the cull margin (64 UI units by default) or a drawn scale that snaps to 0, keeps the first `cap` (512 by default) survivors in push order and counts the latest pushes past it as dropped, and snaps `label.scale × ScaleFactor` to the nearest 1/8. While `UiDebug::hide_game_layers` is set it drains and keeps nothing. The runs go to the renderer as plain `WorldLabelRun`s (`tungsten_render`, `#[non_exhaustive]`) through `Renderer::update_world_labels`, which keeps a copy for the next render, and to the harness's `FrameDraw::world_labels`; the counts go to `RenderCounts` (`world_labels`, `world_labels_culled`, `world_labels_dropped`). `TextEngine` keeps the labels as a second prepared group beside the sections on the one layout map: a label lays out unbounded on one line under its layout key and draws as one `TextArea` at `scale` with `left = x − width × scale × pivot.x` and `top = y − height × scale × pivot.y`, bounded by the viewport, so each label pops and scales as a whole (`D-126`). `TextPipeline` gives the group its own `TextRenderer` on the shared atlas; a non-empty group counts as one batch for the trim rule, prepares on every full frame and whenever its runs or the viewport changed, and is not drawn after an `AtlasFull` prepare, which forces the next frame full. The overlay pass draws the labels first, then the paint list's items, then the sections, so the UI's game layers cover labels. An empty group prepares and draws nothing. `TextStats` gains `labels`, `label_glyphs` and `layouts`, which reach `RenderCounts::text_label_glyphs` and `text_layouts` and the HUD's `ui` row.

**Why:** The acceptance game's damage numbers and the name tags of example 02 need text at world points (w01 §10–§11, §14). Projecting at extract keeps render free of the `World` and the camera's anchor in one place (`D-018`), and immediate mode needs no retained IDs for text that lives one frame. One batch below the paint list is the glyph gate's T1b shape (`D-126`): the labels share the atlas and the content-keyed layout cache (`D-085`), so equal damage numbers shape once, and a game-layer panel covers them as a HUD panel should. glyphon keys a rasterized glyph by its physical size, so a continuously animated scale would rasterize new glyphs every frame; eighths bound a pop to a few sizes. Counting the group as a batch stops `integrated`, which has no UI, from trimming the atlas on every changed frame now that its tags are labels (Q19). Touching both groups' layouts whenever the counter advances keeps a static group's layouts alive while the other changes every frame, which a per-group count would have aged out.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-085` and `D-147` as their marker lines say; extends `D-126` (a batch with whole-label scale and position).
- Example 02's name tags push `WorldLabel`s with their offset and scale divided by `ScaleFactor`, so they keep the benchmark's physical size; `integrated` stays at `workload_version` 1.
- Tests: core `world_to_screen_inverts_screen_to_world` and `world_label_builders_set_their_fields`; the umbrella's extract tests (projection, cull, cap, one-frame life, hide, snap); render `label_layouts_plateau_under_unique_text`, `labels_share_layouts_with_sections`, `label_pivot_places_the_box` and `labels_count_as_a_batch`; example 05's `labels` fixture against `ui-labels.png` and its direct and capture paths.
- Breaks for the freeze: `RenderCounts` and `TextStats` gain fields (struct literals); the rest is additive.
- Open: per-glyph effects stay the T2 backlog row; `push` takes an owned `String` (a pooled push is a backlog row); labels have no depth against sprites and draw over the whole scene.

## D-162 — W1 BC landed (M55): checkboxes and horizontal sliders are the closed leaf kinds `Toggle` and `Slider`, valued in node data, changed by the router and reported as `UiEvent::ValueChanged`; their indicator paints in the kind's text colour
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Decision:** `WidgetKind` gains `Toggle` and `Slider`, focusable, interactive leaves with the roles `CheckBox` and `Slider` (`Role` gains both), built by `UiTree::toggle(parent, on)` and `UiTree::slider(parent, range, value)`. A toggle holds a `bool`; a slider holds an `f32` in a `SliderRange` (`tungsten_core::ui::control`, re-exported from `tungsten_core::ui`): `new(min, max)` is continuous and panics on a bound that is not finite or `min > max`; `with_step(step)` panics on a negative or non-finite step and allows `min + k·step` for whole `k` up to `max`, and `max` itself. Snapping clamps to the bounds and takes the nearest allowed value, the lower on a tie, with `max` a candidate of its own; values compute in `f64` from `min` and a whole `k`, never accumulated, and store `f32` clamped to the bounds. A build value that is not finite starts at `min`; a set one changes nothing. `toggled`, `slider_value` and `slider_range` return `None` for any other widget; `set_toggled`, `set_slider_value` and `set_slider_range` clamp and snap, act only on a change (the first two return it), mark paint and send no event. Neither kind holds text: a caption is a sibling `Label` and the accessible name is the one the game sets. An `Auto` axis takes a natural size from the resolved line height, a toggle one line square and a slider ten lines by one; a `Px`, `Percent` or `Fill` axis wins. `KindStyles` gains `toggle` and `slider`, and the theme file the `toggle` and `slider` kinds with the same five looks: a toggle's box takes the background, border and radius and its mark the text colour; a slider's track takes the background, its fill and thumb the text colour, the thumb the border colour, width and radius; the built-in looks draw from the tokens. A control's resolved text change relayouts its root only when it measures differently; a colour-only change marks paint. Paint (`Rect` commands only): a toggle paints the generic box, then, when on, a mark inset on every side by a quarter of its shorter side with the box's radius less the inset, not below 0; a slider paints no box but a track `h / 4` thick across its rect on the centre line with radius `min(r, h / 8)`, a fill from its start edge to the thumb's centre, and a square thumb of side `t = min(h, w)` centred at `start + t / 2 + fraction · (w − t)`. Routing: an activation, a primary release over a toggle or `ui_accept` while it holds focus keys act on, flips it and sends `ValueChanged { widget, value: UiValue::Bool }` right after its `Activated`, before a `DoubleClicked`; a primary press on a slider, after its capture and focus, and each move while that press holds it set the value at the pointer, the fraction `(x − (start + t / 2)) / (w − t)` clamped to 0–1 between exact ends and snapped, off the slider and past its ends too, with no grab offset; the release and a cancel change nothing; `ui_left` and `ui_right` step a slider that holds focus keys act on to the next allowed value that way (a tenth of the span when continuous; when that value stores as the current `f32`, the first allowed value at or past the adjacent `f32`, else the end) instead of moving focus, on the navigation repeat, and at an end take the press and change nothing; `ui_up`, `ui_down`, `ui_next` and `ui_prev` move focus as before. `ValueChanged` follows each change of the stored value by the router, one per change, after the press's own events; `UiValue` is `#[non_exhaustive]` with `Bool`, `Number`, `as_bool` and `as_number`; `EventQueue::<UiEvent>::value_changed(widget)` returns the last value of the current window.

**Why:** M4's settings screen (display mode, the scales, the volume, one rebind) needs a checkbox and a slider, which the definition gate put in 1.0 (criteria question 8, `D-102`; w01 §8 and §11). Closed leaf kinds keep the kind match and the style model as they are: two more `KindStyle`s with the same five looks and the text colour as the indicator's, so no style property or state joins and a theme recolours a control as it recolours a label, while a leaf passes its text style to nothing. Holding no text node, a control has nothing to re-set for a colour, so the slider's hover and press looks repaint without a relayout. The router alone reports changes, so a game shows stored settings through the setters without echoing them back; one activation path keeps a click and `ui_accept` one rule, and `Activated` still follows, so `D-149`'s pointer contract holds for every interactive widget. Left and right stepping a focused slider leave up, down and Tab to walk a vertical settings list, and the first key after pointer use stays the game's. `f64` arithmetic from `min` and a whole number of steps keeps `-f32::MAX..=f32::MAX` finite and repeated steps exact; `max` as its own candidate keeps it reachable when the step index loses precision past 2^53 steps (M55's API critique); the adjacent-`f32` rule keeps every press moving when a step is finer than the `f32` spacing (M55's plan critique).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-125`, `D-147` and `D-149` as their marker lines say; extends `D-040` (`value_changed`) and `D-148` (two theme kinds).
- Breaks for the freeze: `KindStyles` gains two fields, so a struct literal needs them or `KindStyles::for_tokens`; the rest is additive (w04's ledger).
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/ui/{control,paint,route,style,theme_file}.rs`, the harness tests in `crates/tungsten/src/tests/ui_route.rs` and example 05's `tests/screens.rs`. Example 05's interactive run builds a "Settings" window; its captured fixtures are unchanged.
- Settles `D-149`'s open limits: the drag threshold stays with M5's movable windows; popups and click-outside dismissal join progress bars, collapsible sections, tooltips and window resizing in the backlog row "Broader controls beyond checkboxes and sliders".
- Open: a caption that activates its control, the whole settings row as the focus target, the wheel, Home, End and the page keys on a slider and an analog stick's reach (M4 decides); vertical sliders and sprite-skinned controls.

## D-163 — W1 M4 landed (M56): state-owned UI roots that hide or freeze while covered and fade with the transition; paint-only opacity, translation and scale with a root fade and frozen roots; UI animation tracks on the real clock; `ui_route` before the dispatcher; a label's colour-only change repaints; disabled ancestors' look; content-box text; text scale; prewarm
**Amended by D-167:** the text scale clause only; the text scale applies to `Game` and `GamePopup` roots, and debug labels and controls skip it; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-188:** the text scale's range only; `TEXT_SCALE_RANGE` is 0.5..=2.0; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-15
**Decision:** Gameplay screens build on the core UI with nine additions, plain data and pure functions in core, the umbrella mapping roots to states and bridging the prewarm to the renderer, which reads no `World` (`D-007`, `D-018`).

- **Paint-only properties.** `UiTree::set_opacity` (0..=1, NaN ignored), `set_translation` (UI units, non-finite ignored) and `set_scale` (1/64..=64, a value not finite or not positive ignored), with getters, mark paint only. Roots take `set_fade` (0..=1) and `set_frozen`. A node's alpha is the product of the opacities on its path times its root's fade; a node maps its subtree by `p ↦ c + s·(p − c) + t`, `c` its layout centre and `t` its translation rounded to whole physical pixels at the last paint's scale, composed down the tree. A text run draws at the composed scale rounded down to a multiple of 1/32 (unsnapped below 1/32) in its exact box shrunk to match about its centre, so it never outgrows its box or its clips. `UiTree::visual_rect` is where a widget paints and is hit.
- **Frozen roots.** A frozen root keeps its look, is hit and blocks; its widgets take no press, activation, value change or focus move, a press one held is cancelled at the next routing, no `ui_*` action acts in it, and as a modal root it still takes every key press, so an Escape during a fade neither cancels nor reaches gameplay.
- **Text and style.** A colour-only text style change repaints and re-sets nothing; a label measures and commits at its content box (UI F4); a widget under a disabled ancestor applies its `disabled` variant and `set_enabled` marks the subtree (UI F2).
- **Animation tracks.** `UiTrack` (`#[non_exhaustive]`) moves one `UiProperty` (opacity, translation, scale, background, border, text colour) with core's `Easing`, `TweenRepeat` and `TweenDirection` (`D-054`) and a `u32` tag. `UiTree::animate` writes the start value at once and replaces a track on the same property silently; `stop_animation` keeps the value; removal drops tracks; a non-finite value or a non-positive scale is refused with a warning; a duration that cannot run finishes on the next tick. `ui_animate_system` ticks on `Time::real_delta()` (`D-129`), the remainder carried across cycles, and sends `UiEvent::AnimationFinished { widget, property, tag }` for `Once` and `Times`, read with `EventQueue::<UiEvent>::animation_finished`. Colour tracks write the node's `StyleOverrides`; none relayouts.
- **State-owned roots.** `StateStack::own_root(root, RootCover)` gives a root to the state being dispatched, by its stack index (two instances of one `StateId` keep their own), or else to the top state. Covered, a `Hide` root hides and a `Show` root stays shown and frozen; uncovered, it takes back the visibility it had and thaws; the owner's pop or replace removes it after `on_exit`. While a transition is queued or active every owned root is frozen with `fade = 1 − transition_cover()`. The sync runs at the dispatcher's end and in `UiPlugin`'s `ui_state_roots` before `ui_route`, and returns at once with no owned roots.
- **The schedule.** `pre_update: …, display_input, ui_state_roots, ui_route, ui_animate, state_dispatcher`; `UI_STATE_ROOTS` and `UI_ANIMATE` join core's names, and `ui_route` and `ui_animate` take `before_if_present(STATE_DISPATCHER)`, so the order holds whichever plugin registers first. The bench, without `UiPlugin`, keeps its order.
- **Text scale.** `UiTree::set_text_scale` (0.25..=4.0) multiplies label size and line height where a text node is set and a control's natural size, resolved styles unscaled; `Settings::text_scale` (`TEXT_SCALE_RANGE` 0.75..=2.0) persists it.
- **Allocation-free text.** `UiTree::set_plain_text(id, &str)` compares a one-span label without allocating.
- **Prewarm.** `UiTree::prewarm(root)` asks for a one-shot raster of the root's runs, shown or hidden, once laid out; `paint_prewarm` paints them; the app hands the list to `Renderer::prewarm_ui_text` before the frame's prepare (`FrameDraw::ui_prewarm` records it), which prepares the runs on a renderer of their own on the shared atlas, never drawn, returns `TextPrewarm` and logs `UI prewarm: <runs> text runs, <glyphs> glyphs in <ms> ms`.

**Why:** The acceptance game's title, pause, level-up, results and settings screens all wait on W1 M4. Owning roots by stack index puts a screen's UI under its state's lifetime without `SceneEntity`'s shared-`StateId` limit, and fading every owned root with the cover keeps the UI, which draws after the transition pass (`D-093`), in step with the scene. Freezing rather than hiding keeps a covered game screen visible under a pause menu and makes a screen that is fading in or out inert. Routing before the dispatcher lets a state's `update` read `activated()` and `RoutedInput` for this frame, where the old order failed silently; the second sync before `ui_route` freezes roots for a transition an `Update` system queued after the dispatcher (the plan's second critique round). Paint-only properties and colour tracks repaint without relayout, and the unbaked style colour turns a fade into a batch re-prepare rather than a reshape. Rounding the text scale down to a 32nd bounds the raster sizes a pop rasterizes and keeps text inside its box (the plan's first critique round). The prewarm takes a menu's cold glyph cost out of the frame that opens it.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-125`, `D-147`, `D-149` and `D-159` as their marker lines say; extends `D-040` (`AnimationFinished`), `D-046` (state-owned roots), `D-093` (owned roots fade with the cover) and `D-126` (the prewarm prepares on the shared atlas under the trim rule; an `AtlasFull` counts as a batch's).
- Breaks for the freeze: `PaintCommand::Text` gains `scale` and its `rect` is the content box; behaviour: `ui_route` runs before the dispatcher, a colour-only change repaints, a widget under a disabled container looks disabled, a padded label measures at its content width (w04's ledger).
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/ui/{paint,anim,prompt,layout,style,route}.rs`, `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/settings.rs`, `crates/tungsten-render/src/tests/{text_batches,text_nodes}.rs`, `crates/tungsten/src/tests/{state,plugins,ui_route,settings,app,testing_ui}.rs` and example 05's `tests/screens.rs`; smoke's `UI screens` row runs the `menus` fixture.
- Measured at M56's sitting (2026-10-15, `perf-runs/20261015-m56-gameplay-screens/`, five-run suites of every row against 0.75 with an aligned pair): every owned metric of every row not `regressed` in both direct pairs, but `ecs` `bounds_wrap` p50 (0.316 → 0.362 and 0.364 ms), which the aligned pair reads `unchanged` and the symbol's move from 16 to 48 mod 64 explains (`D-083`), and `gpu-throughput` `render_encode` p95 in one pair, a drift metric whose rerun reads `noisy`; first-run digests equal in all eight rows. The 100-widget menu adds 0.020 ms of frame-work p95 at 1080p (budget 0.25 ms); a first open's `render_encode_pre_acquire` excess is +2.690 ms cold and +0.030 ms after a prewarm of 48 runs and 543 glyphs in 2.7–3.2 ms.
- Open limits: a span with its own colour does not fade; interaction-state easing, size tracks and reduced motion are backlog rows; frozen is per root; prewarmed glyphs stay until the atlas needs their room after a full frame's trim, and the atlas's byte size stays unreadable (the T2 row); `SceneEntity` keeps its shared-`StateId` limit.

## D-164 — Rebinding (M56): the router's capture-next-input mode, and binding labels and prompts resolved after each routing against the action map and the last device
**Date:** 2026-10-15
**Decision:** `UiRouter::capture_next_input(widget)` starts a capture from the next routing; `cancel_capture()` ends it and `capturing()` names it. While it is pending every key, mouse button, pad button and stick direction press is the UI's (`wants_keyboard` holds) and none navigates or repeats; the first that is not an OS repeat ends it with `UiEvent::InputCaptured { widget, binding }` (`EventQueue::<UiEvent>::input_captured`), its release staying the UI's and a stick's axis withheld until it. Escape is captured like any key (Q9); the wheel is taken, neither captured nor routed (Q10); a key with no `input.json` name is reported and `D-159`'s persist skips it. Window focus loss, `cancel_capture`, a new capture, or the widget removed, hidden, disabled or frozen ends it with `UiEvent::CaptureCancelled`. `Binding::label` names a binding for a prompt: the physical key's US label (`W`, `Space`, `Esc`, `Left Shift`, `Page Up`, `Key <n>`), `Left Mouse`, `Wheel Up`, Xbox-style pad buttons (`A` for South, `LB`, `D-Pad Up`) and stick directions (`Left Stick Up`), with tables beside the `input.json` names. `UiTree::set_prompt(id, template)` gives a label or a button's label a template whose `{action:NAME}` placeholders show, for the last device, the first binding of its kind (keys and pointer: key, mouse, wheel; pad: button, stick), else the first of any, else `Unbound`; every other brace is literal and `set_text` clears it. `ui_route_system` resolves prompts after each routing, rebuilding one only when the device kind or an action's bindings differ from its last resolution, so a reload's whole-map replacement and `replace_bindings` both show, and an unchanged prompt costs slice compares.

**Why:** M4's settings screen needs one rebind and its screens need prompts that follow the pad. Capturing in the router, not in a game system reading raw input, keeps the press and its release from the routed view and from `engine_exit`, so neither gameplay nor the exit check sees half a press. Comparing bindings rather than keeping a revision survives the reload, which replaces the `ActionMap` resource whole (A13). Xbox letters follow SDL's reference layout and the common PC pad; physical US key labels are what winit 0.30 offers without a keymap query.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-149` as its marker line says; extends `D-045` (labels beside the names) and `D-040` (`InputCaptured`, `CaptureCancelled`).
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/input/key_serde.rs`, `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/ui/{prompt,route}.rs` and the harness tests in `crates/tungsten/src/tests/ui_route.rs`; example 05's settings screen rebinds `jump`, replacing only the captured kind's bindings and persisting through the user layer.
- Open limits: an AZERTY player sees US labels and a Nintendo pad swapped ones; prompt icons and keymap-aware names are backlog rows; the owner's gamepad playthrough waits for the backend's re-land.

## D-165 — W1 M5 landed (M57): the engine's debug views are windows on the core UI's debug layer, on scroll panels, drag handles and a mesh paint command; the log console on the engine logger's `Tee`; the inspector's pick on the routed input
**Amended by D-168:** the views' scale, places, look, chrome and rows only; they draw at the OS scale times a zoom and a step, dock at fixed corners without overlap or keep a saved place in OS-logical units, take one palette, the engine font's SemiBold and 15-unit text, and gain a title bar's key, collapse and close buttons, a fixed header, one-line cells, the HUD's graph block, the systems overlay's stage groups, the inspector's component rows and the console's columns, filters, pause and clear; the core additions, the console's `Tee` and the pick stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-15
**Decision:** The HUD, the system timing overlay, the entity inspector, the log console (F8, `engine_toggle_log_console`) and the UI inspector (F7, `engine_toggle_ui_inspector`) draw as windows: a root on `UiLayer::Debug` holding a title bar, its drag handle, and a body of label columns sized by layout, every node setting every style and text override so a game's theme changes nothing. Core gains what they need, as plain data:

- **Mesh.** `WidgetKind::Mesh`, a leaf with the panel's kind style and role `Image`; `UiTree::mesh`, `set_mesh` (whole triangles of finite `UiVertex` positions in box fractions, a trailing partial one dropped; marks paint) and `mesh_vertices`. `PaintCommand::Mesh { rect, first, count, clip }` indexes `PaintList::vertices` (physical pixels, colours times the node's alpha), its `rect` the vertices' bounding box; the overlay draws it in paint order through a second pipeline (`vs_mesh`, `fs_mesh`) and a vertex buffer that grows and caps at a whole triangle.
- **Scroll panels.** `UiTree::scroll_panel` (vertical, clips its children), `set_scroll`, `scroll`, `scroll_max`, `scroll_into_view`. Taffy's `overflow.y = Hidden` and shrink 1 for an `Auto` main size; the maximum is how far the lowest child's box, margin included, and the bottom padding reach past the box. Offsets are paint-only, clamped where used, rounded to whole physical pixels; an offset at or past the end follows the end through later layouts; a thumb paints after the children in the panel's text colour at half its alpha.
- **Windows.** `UiTree::set_drag_handle` and `drag_handle`; `set_window_position` (roots and finite positions, the whole root inside the last viewport once measured, else its top-left 48 units inside) and `window_position`; `UiRouteSettings::scroll_line` (40) and `drag_threshold` (4); `UiEvent::Scrolled` and `WindowMoved`; `WindowPlacements` (`D-166`).
- **The views.** `debug_views` builds, places and removes the windows after the schedule and before the UI layout, timed into `extract`: a saved place (`D-166`) or the view's corner, stacked below or above the views opened earlier there, clamped again after a first layout or a viewport change; only places persist. The HUD adds a frame-time graph, a mesh of the last 240 intervals scaled against the larger of 33.3 ms and the tallest, amber over 16.7 ms, redrawn every frame it shows. The refresh throttles count `FrameTimings::interval_ms`, and the fps row reads `1000 / EWMA(interval)` beside `EWMA(total)`. `font_id` names a UI font family: `engine_mono`, over the engine face, joins the families at startup, load and reload unless the manifest declares it. Each view resource gains `background` and `window()`; `outline_color` and `outline_px` are the window's border.
- **The log console.** The logger's `Tee` (`D-119`) is the target whenever stderr or the file is on; it writes stderr through `eprint!` in a debug build without a file (the test harness captures it) and with `write_all` otherwise, and copies each record into a 1,024-record `sync_channel`, counting a full or closed channel's drops. `LogConsole` drains it every frame, shown or not, into 512 lines; its window shows `N lines · D dropped` (or `no engine logger`) over the lines, `ERROR` red and `WARN` amber, following its end until the wheel moves it off.
- **The UI inspector.** While shown, an `engine_inspector_pick` press in the raw input picks `UiTree::hit_test` at the press, on any layer; `UiTree::widget_dirty` (`WidgetDirty`) shows pending style, text and layout, and its rows add the rects, look, text style, state, flags and the run's text batch in the app's last paint list.
- **The pick.** `inspector_pick` runs after `ui_route` when it is installed, which `App::resolve_schedule` records, and reads `engine_inspector_pick` in `RoutedInput` at the press's position, so a press the UI took never picks; without routing it reads the raw input. `pre_update: physics_debug_toggle, systems_overlay_toggle, inspector_toggle, hud_toggle, ui_debug_toggle, ui_inspector_toggle, log_console_toggle, display_input, ui_state_roots, ui_route, inspector_pick, ui_animate, state_dispatcher`.

**Why:** The text overlays (`D-044`, `D-047`) sat above every UI root, measured rows by a monospace heuristic and picked through the UI; windows on the debug layer stack, move and stay out of the game's way, and the acceptance game's debugging needs the console and the widget panel. A mesh draws the graph in one command where 240 rectangles would crowd the primitive batches, and its `rect` bounds the triangles, not the box, so a later mesh cannot draw under earlier text (the plan's first critique round). Reading the routed input fixes a press over a window that then moved into the world in the same frame (round one). The console's sink sits in the `Tee`, as `D-119` reserved, not a second logger.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-147` and `D-149` as their marker lines say; extends `D-044`, `D-047` (the views are windows, no text sections), `D-119` (the `Tee` and its console sink), `D-123` (the engine font is a UI family) and `D-159` (`D-166`'s file).
- Breaks for the freeze: `PaintList` gains `vertices`; the three views draw as windows (no text sections, `font_id` a family, the outline the border, sizes in UI units, refresh and fps on the interval); `inspector_pick` runs after `ui_route` on the routed input; the default map and `DefaultPlugins` gain two actions and two systems (w04's ledger).
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/ui/{paint,scroll,window,tree}.rs`, `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/input/action_map.rs`, `crates/tungsten-render/src/tests/ui.rs`, `crates/tungsten/src/tests/{debug_views,debug_hud,systems_overlay,inspector,engine_font,asset_loader,logging,plugins,ui_route}.rs`; smoke's `Debug views` row runs the UI lab's `debug` fixture, and the template row forbids `Unknown font`.
- Measured at M57's sitting (2026-10-15, `perf-runs/20261015-m57-debug-views/`, five-run suites of every row against 0.76): no owned metric `regressed` in either direct pair, every row's `interval` p50 not `regressed`, first-run digests equal in all eight rows; the only `improved` readings, `ecs` `bounds_wrap` p50 in both pairs, are placement. The first sitting's base A/A tripped twice on `particles` `particle_count_refresh`, a documented drift, and the sitting ran again clean.
- Open limits: scrollbar dragging, horizontal scroll panels, raising a window on click and saved open state are backlog rows; the UI inspector picks the deepest widget, a button's label; physics debug's controls are not in the UI; `env_logger` strips its colours on the `Tee`, a pipe, so a debug build without a log file prints plain level labels, as a build with one already did.

## D-166 — Debug window placements persist in the user folder's settings as `debug_windows.json`
**Amended by D-168:** the file's units only; places are OS-logical units, a debug-unit place times the player's UI scale and the debug scale, and the file keeps an optional `zoom` beside `windows`, still version 1, so a 0.77 file's places read the same at UI scale 1; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-15
**Decision:** `tungsten_core::ui::WindowPlacements` keeps named windows' top-left positions in UI units in a JSON file, `{"version": 1, "windows": {"<name>": [x, y]}}`. `load` never fails: a missing file loads empty; a corrupt or invalid one loads empty and is renamed `<file>.bad` beside itself, as `Settings::load` does, and one that cannot be read is left in place, each with one warning; a newer version loads empty, leaves the file alone and warns; an entry that is not two finite numbers is dropped with a warning. `set` refuses a non-finite position; `save` writes through `persist::write_atomic`, and without a path writes nothing. `App::new` loads `<settings>/debug_windows.json` beside `Settings` and logs its warnings; with no user folder (smoke mode, `UserFolder::Off`, no `game.id`) there is no file. A debug view's window opens at the place saved under its name (`hud`, `systems`, `inspector`, `log`, `ui_inspector`), and the end of a drag saves it; a failed save warns once per run and keeps the place in memory. Which views are open is not saved.

**Why:** A developer who moves a view expects it back there next run. The user folder's settings (`D-159`) keep a shipped game from writing beside its executable, and its atomic write and quarantine are already proven there. Positions only: startup code decides which views open, and a pixel fixture must never show a view a developer left open.

**Consequences:**
- Extends `D-159`.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/ui/window.rs` (round trip, `.bad`, newer version, dropped entries) and `crates/tungsten/src/tests/debug_views.rs` (a drag saved through `UserFolder::At` and reopened there; a place saved at (1800, 900) reopening inside 800×600).
- Open limits: saved open state is a backlog row; a place saved on a larger screen reopens clamped, and the clamp is not written back until the next drag.

## D-167 — The core UI's debug layers take a scale of their own; a press raises a window; nodes cast shadows as meshes; the measure double keeps a line limit; window placements keep a zoom (M58)
**Date:** 2026-10-15
**Decision:** Core gains what M58's debug views need, as plain data:

- **The debug scale.** `UiTree::set_debug_scale` and `debug_scale`: how many of the tree's UI units one unit of a `Debug` or `DebugPopup` root spans, 1 on a new tree, clamped to 1/64..=64 and otherwise kept as given, not rounded; a value that is not finite or not positive is ignored; a change marks the debug roots for layout and the tree for paint, an equal value nothing. A debug root lays out in the viewport divided by it, and `UiTree::layer_viewport` reports each layer's viewport at the last layout. Paint, hit testing, `visual_rect` and the window clamp read the debug scale of the last layout, so rects and maps agree between a set and the next layout: a debug root's units paint at the paint scale times it, its translations and scroll offsets round to whole physical pixels at that product, and its text runs draw at it exactly times the node scales stepped to 32nds (`D-163`), so the layer's scale never shrinks a run. `PaintList::scale` stays the tree's; `rect` is in the root's units and `visual_rect` in the tree's; `set_window_position` clamps a root inside its layer's viewport.
- **The text scale** applies to `Game` and `GamePopup` roots: debug labels and controls skip it in `prepare_text` and the control measure, and `set_text_scale` marks only game-layer labels and roots.
- **Routing.** `UiTree::raise` moves a root after the other roots of its layer, marking paint; the router raises the root under a primary press the UI takes when its role is `Role::Window` and it is not frozen, before handling the press, so the raised window takes it. A debug root is hit in its own units; a drag moves it by the pointer's motion divided by the debug scale, and a pixel wheel delta over its scroll panels is divided by it too, while a line stays `scroll_line` units of the panel.
- **Shadows.** `UiShadow` (`#[non_exhaustive]`; `color`, `blur`, `offset`; `UiShadow::new`), `UiTree::set_shadow` (marks paint; an equal value marks nothing) and `shadow`. A node paints its shadow before its box as one `PaintCommand::Mesh` of nine quads, 54 vertices: its box moved by `offset` in the colour, ringed `blur` wide by a band fading to transparent, the alpha times the node's, under the node's map and in the clip its box paints in; nothing when `blur` is not finite and positive, the offset is not finite or the colour's alpha is 0. The mesh pipeline draws it (`D-165`); the shader's reserved shadow fields stay reserved, and hit testing ignores shadows.
- **The measure double.** `FixedAdvanceMeasure` keeps the first `n` lines of each paragraph, at least one, at a definite width under `TextOverflow::Ellipsis { lines: Some(n), .. }`, as the engine does; min- and max-content, other overflow and an ellipsis without a limit keep every line.
- **The placements' zoom.** `WindowPlacements::zoom` and `set_zoom` (finite and positive, else nothing kept): an optional `"zoom"` number beside `"windows"`, written when kept, the file still version 1; a zoom that is not a finite positive number loads as none with one warning.

**Why:** M57's views laid out at `ScaleFactor::get()`, the OS scale times the player's UI scale, under the player's text scale, so at the owner's OS scale of 1.75 their 28-unit text drew about 49 px tall and a larger UI or text setting grew them further. A scale of the debug layers' own lets the umbrella cancel the player's scales exactly; one rounded to 32nds could not (the plan's first critique round: 21/32 at a UI scale of 1.5 drew at 0.984), which is why the layer's scale never steps a text run. Raising on press is the backlog's row. A shadow as a core mesh needs no render change, where the shader's reserved fields would, and a shadow child in the umbrella would need a root that does not clip. The double's line limit lets headless tests hold a one-line ellipsized label to one line, as the engine does.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-147`, `D-149` and `D-163` as their marker lines say; extends `D-124`, `D-125` (each layer's viewport; the measure double) and `D-127` (a debug root's units at the paint scale times the debug scale).
- Game-layer roots lay out, paint and hit as before: the core UI tests and `just visual` (four targets) read unchanged.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/ui/{tree,layout,paint,hit,window,route,measure}.rs`.
- Open limits: directional focus compares layout rects, a debug root's in its own units, so moving focus between a game root and a debug root is approximate, and no debug view takes focus. `UiHarness::click` and `rect` read layout rects, so a test that clicks a debug root at a scale other than 1 maps its `visual_rect` itself.

## D-168 — The debug views draw on the OS scale with a zoom and a step down, dock without overlap, take one palette and the engine font's SemiBold, and gain window chrome and new columns (M58)
**Date:** 2026-10-15
**Decision:** The views of `D-165`, as the owner settled them on 2026-10-15:

- **Scale.** Every frame, before its early return, `debug_views` sets the tree's debug scale (`D-167`) to `zoom × step / ScaleFactor::ui_scale()`, so the views draw at the OS scale times zoom and step whatever the player's UI and text scales. The step is the largest of 1, 7/8 and 3/4 at which the OS-logical viewport (`WindowSize` over `ScaleFactor::os()`) divided by it holds 960×540 units, else 3/4. The zoom, 1/2 to 2 in eighths and 1 by default, moves on Ctrl+= (Shift allowed), Ctrl+- and Ctrl+0, each press counted, OS repeats included, read while a view is shown; it is kept with the places and saved at once, a failed save warning once a run, and the keys still reach the game.
- **Places.** `debug_windows.json` keeps places in OS-logical units, a debug-unit place times `ui_scale × debug_scale`, and the zoom (`D-166`'s file, still version 1). A saved window is placed again from its logical place when the debug scale moves, clamped with the last layout's size every frame, and snapped onto a margin line its edge lies within 16 units of; a drag's end saves the snapped place.
- **Docking.** A window with no saved place docks: the HUD top left, the systems overlay top right, the inspector bottom left, the UI inspector bottom right, the log console a strip along its corner's horizontal edge, the bottom by default, as wide as the viewport less its margins and at most 25% of its height. Before each layout every docked window takes a fixed height from what its content asked for at the last layout: a strip pushes its edge's windows away, and a side's windows stack from its top corner down and its bottom corner up in update order, each at most 60% of the height and what the windows before it left, less six lines and a margin for each window still to dock on that side. So docked windows never overlap at a debug viewport of 960×540 units or more. A corner window is at most 45% of the viewport's width; one margin, the largest docked `padding_px` (8 by default), spaces them.
- **Look.** One palette in `debug_views`, its saturated hues each carrying one meaning in a view: a near-opaque panel (alpha 252), faint title and stripe veils (alpha 5 and 2), a grey border of `outline_px` physical pixels (1) that brightens while the pointer is over the window or a press holds its title bar, a soft shadow; values and titles off-white, labels grey; the budget ramp (green, yellow, orange, red past 1, 1.5 and 2 times a 60 Hz frame, or a tenth of one for systems); level, value-kind and stage hues. Every text colour keeps 4.5:1 against the panel, the title bar, a striped row and its level badge, composited in linear light as the overlay does, over black and over white: 4.99:1 at worst, and M57's panel alpha of 245 fails at 3.81:1. Body text 15 units on 20; titles 17 on 22; titles, headers and key numbers at weight 600, which `engine_mono`'s SemiBold face draws (`D-123`).
- **Windows.** A title bar holding the bold title, the view's toggle key in grey and collapse and close buttons that do not take focus; a fixed header that never scrolls (the HUD's graph block, the inspectors' header lines, the console's controls); a scrolling body holding the table. Close turns the view off as its key does; collapse leaves the title bar and is not saved. Table cells wrap by glyph to one line ending in `…`, padded 6 units a side with no column gap; data rows stripe every other one, header rows are strong and unstriped. A `Fill` column is an `Auto` column capped at what the window's cap leaves after the other columns at the last layout: Taffy sizes an auto-width window by its children's flex bases, so a basis-0 column added only its min-content.
- **Rows.** The HUD: a 240-unit graph four lines tall with guide lines at 16.7 and 33.3 ms and its least, mean, most and 1% low beside it; the fps row's CPU time on the ramp; `ui` split into `ui`, `text` and `labels`; the averages start from the first interval, the values read `–` before it, and the first refresh after it runs at once. The systems overlay: a group per stage in frame order and `other`, each with its total, each system's average, its most over the last four refreshes, its share of the frame's average and a bar of that share; the title carries the total; `App::resolve_schedule` records each name's stage (`SystemStages`). The entity inspector: a header row per component, its fields indented, each value in its kind's hue read from its text. The log console: raw records in the ring, read at row build by `logging::record_fields` (`env_logger`'s format) into time, level badge, short target and message; level filters, pause and clear (`LogConsole::clear`, public).

**Why:** At the owner's OS scale of 1.75 M57's 26–32-unit views covered one another at 1280×720 and cut rows at the window's edge (known issues' M57 row). The owner settled sizes, places, look and columns on 2026-10-15 (the plan's §2.1). The plan's critique moved the contrast check to linear-light compositing, since a sRGB estimate claimed 4.9:1 where the overlay gave 2.5:1, and set docked heights before the layout, since caps from the last layout let a growing window overlap for a frame.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-123`, `D-165` and `D-166` as their marker lines say.
- Breaks for the freeze (w04's ledger): the views' default corners, sizes, colours, `outline_px` in physical pixels, the log strip, the HUD's split `ui` row, the inspector's two columns and the console's four, the places file's units and zoom.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten/src/tests/{debug_views,debug_hud,systems_overlay,inspector,engine_font,logging}.rs`; smoke's `Debug views` row runs the UI lab's `debug` fixture with an `ERROR`, a `WARN` and an `INFO` record and a 120-character HUD row; the template docks its HUD top right, clear of its title.
- Measured at M58's sitting (2026-10-15, `perf-runs/20261015-m58-debug-views/`, five-run suites of every row against 0.77): direct pair B read no owned metric `regressed`; direct pair A's one, `ecs` `follow` p50, a per-run-mode row, read `noisy` in its row's rerun pair; every row's `interval` p50 not `regressed`; first-run digests equal in all eight rows.
- Open limits: the UI inspector's outline, breadcrumbs and swatches and a debug menu are backlog rows; a growing window scrolls for a frame before its dock takes the new height; a dragged window may overlap a docked one; the HUD's and UI inspector's rows refresh on 500 ms of frame interval, so an unpaced capture at frame 60 shows their first values.

## D-169 — A window resized by hand keeps its size at `apply_settings` until the set or shipped resolution changes (M59)
**Date:** 2026-10-16
**Decision:** `apply_settings` keeps a memory of the last windowed target: the resolution startup resolved, then each set or shipped resolution a windowed apply sent. In windowed mode it sends the target resolution only when the target differs from that memory, and then updates the memory; otherwise the request keeps the resolution it already holds, a request queued earlier in the frame its own and with none queued the window's current size, and that kept size never enters the memory. In fullscreen the request carries the target as before, which the fullscreen path ignores, and the memory stays, so a resolution set while fullscreen is sent by the first apply back in windowed mode, while leaving fullscreen with nothing outstanding asks for no size and the platform restores the one the window had. The memory is a crate-private resource beside `ShippedConfig`, set where `App::with_plugins` resolves the startup display. A world built by hand, with no shipped config, keeps `D-159`'s rule.

**Why:** M59 step 2 reproduced the owner's windowed-mode glitch with the UI lab's `display` fixture at an OS scale of 1.75 under KWin on X11: a window at 1600×900 returned to the shipped 1280×720 at the next volume step, whose `apply_settings` copied the shipped resolution into a request (`D-159`'s consequence "A window resized by hand returns to the set or shipped resolution at the next `apply_settings`"), and the X11 sample agreed. Any settings change resized the window, so a hand size could not survive a settings screen. Remembering what was last sent separates a resolution the player chose from one the window took, without persisting a hand size, which stays a non-setting as `D-159` says. The plan's critique added the fullscreen case (a target cached while fullscreen would otherwise never be sent) and the queued-request case (two applies in a frame must not drop the first one's size).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-159` as its marker line says; a hand size is still not saved, and `D-043`'s frame-boundary apply stands.
- KWin keeps a maximized window's size against a size request, so the maximize run kept its size before and after the change; the rule removes the request rather than relying on the window manager.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten/src/tests/settings.rs`: `a_window_resized_by_hand_keeps_its_size_at_the_next_apply`, and the guards `a_resolution_set_while_fullscreen_applies_when_windowed` and `two_applies_before_a_frame_keep_the_queued_resolution`; the earlier settings tests stand unchanged.
- A behaviour change in w04's break ledger; no public item changes.

## D-170 — W13e landed (M60): `EventCursor`, a reader's place in an `EventQueue` by sequence that reads each event once from any stage and counts what the queue dropped; spawn inheritance registered on the World and applied at the command flush to a prefab queued from a source; `StatePlugin` registers `SceneEntity`, so the kit's spawns leave with their state; the kit's readers on cursors
**Date:** 2026-10-16
**Decision:** Two parts in core, and their users in the umbrella and the kit:

- **The event cursor.** Every `EventQueue<T>` numbers what it keeps: its events are a contiguous tail of all it was sent (the held frames oldest first, then `previous`, then `current`), so two private counters, the sequence of its oldest event and the count of its held events, give each event a `u64` sequence, and an `Arc<()>` allocated when the queue is built gives the queue an identity. `EventCursor<T>` (`new`, `Default`, `Clone` without `T: Clone`, `Debug`) holds the next sequence it has not read and, from its first read, a `Weak` of its queue's identity. `read(&queue)` returns an `EventRead<'_, T>`, `#[must_use]`, an exact-size, fused iterator of `&T` whose `Debug` prints its length and `missed`, over the events from the cursor's place or the queue's oldest, whichever is later, oldest first; the cursor moves past all of them when `read` returns. `missed()` counts the events past the cursor that flushes or the 256-frame hold cap dropped before the read. A new cursor starts at the oldest event the queue holds and misses nothing, and a cursor whose queue has another identity, after `insert_resource` or an assignment replaced it, starts at the new queue's oldest event, missing nothing. The cursor ignores `D-137`'s step views and reads held frames like any other event; `send`, `iter`, `iter_current`, `len`, `is_empty`, `flush` and the holds are unchanged. Both types are exported at `tungsten_core` and `tungsten_core::ecs`.
- **Spawn inheritance.** `World::inherit_from_source::<T: Clone>()` and `World::derive_from_source::<T>(fn(&World, Entity) -> Option<T>)` register what a spawn from a source takes when it lacks a `T`: a clone of the source's `T`, or what the function returns. Each is idempotent per type, the first registration of either form standing, and returns whether `T` was new. `CommandBuffer::spawn_prefab_from(source, prepared)` records `spawn_prefab`'s spawn and run, then a crate-private `Command::Derive { source, target }`; `Command` stays 24 bytes. At the flush a dead target does nothing, and a source an earlier command despawned despawns the target, so nothing of the spawn remains; otherwise each registered type the target lacks is staged in registration order and applied in one archetype move after the prefab's own fields and `with` bundles, whose values win, so a sourced prefab shares the prefab's archetype chain and adds one archetype per derived type at its end. `PreparedPrefab`, `spawn` and `spawn_prefab` are unchanged, and no spawn from a source is made at once.
- **Users.** `StatePlugin` calls `world.inherit_from_source::<SceneEntity>()` beside the dispatcher. The kit's `Spawner` queues each spawn with `spawn_prefab_from(spawner, prepared)`, so under `StatePlugin` its spawns take the spawner's `SceneEntity` and leave with its state, and a spawner despawned earlier in the frame's commands, as by its state's exit, spawns nothing. `SoundOnEvent` keeps an `EventCursor<E>` in place of its handled count and `TriggerZone`'s system an `EventCursor<SensorEntered>` in place of `iter_current`; their plugins, names, stages and behaviour are unchanged. The template's `AGENTS.md` and the getting-started guide name `spawn_prefab_from`; the template's code is unchanged.

**Why:** The kit catalogue's finalizing review (`docs/plans/1.0/w13-kit.md` §1, at `0732305`) found two gaps under the kit's base. R1: events had no exactly-once reader. A reader chose `iter` or `iter_current` by where it ran against its senders and the stage, and `SoundOnEvent`'s handled count held only for one run a frame outside the fixed steps, while the catalogue adds about ten reactive readers in both stages. A sequence makes what a reader has not read one comparison, whatever the windows, step views and holds did; `D-139` declined such a cursor for one reader at the 0.57 health pass and left it open, and the catalogue's readers make it worth a public type. The identity answers the plan's round-2 critique: a replaced queue starts at sequence 0, so a place alone cannot tell it from the old one, and the kit's installed readers keep their cursors in system closures that cannot be handed fresh ones; a `Weak` keeps its allocation, so no later queue shares the address, and a moved queue keeps its `Arc`. R7: kit spawns outlived their state. `SceneEntity` is the umbrella's, so a kit on core alone could not copy it (`D-155`), and a spawner due in its state's exit frame spawned after the dispatcher had listed the state's entities. Inheritance at the flush answers both: the umbrella registers its own marker, the kit only names the source, and a spawn queued in the exit frame derives behind the dispatcher's despawns, so its dead source drops it. A spawn made at once would take the marker after the listing and outlive the state (round 1's critique), so there is none. Derived types land after the run because ahead of it each prefab shape's whole archetype chain would be copied (round 1). The milestone's sitting (0.79 against the candidate twice in one sitting, five runs a side, on `physics`, `physics-sparse`, `ecs`, `churn`, `particles` and `integrated`) read no owned metric `regressed` or `improved` in either A/A and none `regressed` in either direct pair, every suite valid on its first attempt and the first-run digests matching in all six rows of all four compares: `churn` `flush` p50 2.438 → 2.450 and 2.420 → 2.456 ms (`unchanged`, `noisy`), `ecs` `update` p50 10.416 → 10.520 and 10.468 → 10.518 ms, `integrated` `total` p50 `unchanged` in both pairs, with 43 of 44 hot functions moved modulo 64 (`perf-runs/20261016-m60-events-ownership/`, machine-local).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-040`, `D-155` and `D-157` as their marker lines say, and takes up the reader cursor `D-139` left open; `D-137`'s step views and `D-139`'s holds stand for `iter` and `iter_current`.
- Additions only for the freeze: `EventCursor`, `EventRead`, `World::inherit_from_source`, `World::derive_from_source` and `CommandBuffer::spawn_prefab_from` (`api/tungsten-core.txt` 76 lines added, none removed); the umbrella's, the kit's and the render snapshots are unchanged. Two break-ledger rows: the additions, and the `Spawner`'s spawns from its spawner, a behaviour change and not a compile break.
- No new dependency, thread, system, stage or schedule change: `DefaultPlugins`' and the template's resolved schedules are unchanged. The determinism hash `0x088e_c07a_73c1_b168`, the containment hash `0xaee2_72e0_1ffc_4e4c` and the six CPU rows' first-run digests are unchanged.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/ecs/event_queue.rs` (ten cursor tests: reads across flushes, a reader before its sender, step views and held frames against `iter`, drops before a read, the hold cap, a new cursor, a replaced queue whose end lies below, at and above the old place, a moved queue, two cursors, a read's length), `tests/ecs/command_buffer.rs` (`Command`'s 24 bytes, one archetype per derived type), `tests/assets/prefab.rs` (eight inheritance tests), the umbrella's `testing::fixed_step::a_cursor_reads_each_event_once_at_any_frame_rate_and_counts_what_was_dropped` (four frame rates, a pause, a 0.001 time scale), the kit's `a_replaced_queue_plays_its_events_once` and `a_spawn_takes_what_its_spawner_passes_on_and_a_despawned_spawner_spawns_nothing`, and the harness's `a_spawners_entities_leave_with_its_state`, `a_spawner_due_in_its_states_exit_frame_leaves_nothing` and `a_spawn_queued_in_on_exit_from_a_states_entity_leaves_nothing`. The earlier `SoundOnEvent`, `TriggerZone`, event-queue and prefab tests stand unchanged.
- Recorded limit (known issues): an entity that takes a state's marker in its exit frame after the dispatcher's listing, ahead of its despawns, outlives the state: a spawn queued from a state's entity by a `pre_update` system ordered before `state_dispatcher`, or one spawned at once in `on_exit` with the marker through `with`. A sweep at the flush by state ID would also take the entities of a state that replaces itself, so a fix belongs to the state stack.
- Open: a spawn from a source that is not a prefab, a bundle form on `CommandBuffer`, when a game asks; the umbrella's own readers (game feel, tweens, particles, UI) stay on `iter` and `iter_current`. W13g derives `Source` through `derive_from_source` and reads zone entries through a cursor; W13h's cues and trace read through cursors.
- The plan, its two critique rounds and step 1's API critique are recorded in `docs/plans/archive/1.0/phase6-milestone-60-kit-core-events-ownership.md`.

## D-171 — W13f landed (M61): the manifest's `data` section of shared definitions by kind and ID in a core `DataRegistry` that plugins register kinds into; `DataRef<T>`, a field's reference to an entry by ID or written in place; `World::check_prefab_id`; a two-phase load of prefabs and data entries (`StagedLoad`) whose commits check every entry loaded from a file and keep all or none
**Date:** 2026-10-16
**Decision:** Four parts in core, and the load and reload in the umbrella:

- **The section.** A manifest's `data` holds kinds, each an object of entries by ID, each `{ "path": … }` (`DataEntry`, `ResolvedDataEntry`, both `#[non_exhaustive]`; `RawManifest::data` and `ResolvedManifest::data`, `BTreeMap`s by kind, then ID). A kind or an ID repeated in one manifest is a parse error and a missing file `ManifestError::MissingDataFile { kind, id, path }`; across roots an ID is unique within its kind, a repeat failing as `DuplicateId` naming `data.<kind>.<id>`, and two kinds, or a kind and `prefabs`, may share an ID. One file backs at most one `prefabs` or `data` entry, in a root or across roots, `ManifestError::SharedFile { path, first, second }` naming the smallest pair, checked on the merged graph with the reference checks (`D-089`). The manifest stays World-free: kinds are not checked at the parse.
- **The registry.** `DataRegistry`, a `World` resource that `App` inserts: `register::<T>(kind)` and `register_with_assets::<T>(kind, assets)`, the second with a check of the IDs a definition names against a World, `Fn(&T, &World) -> Result<(), MissingAsset>`; the World doors `register_data_kind` and `register_data_kind_with_assets` insert the registry when the World has none. Kind names are `&'static str` matching `[a-z][a-z0-9_]*`; one type per kind and one kind per type, the same pair again a no-op returning `false`, a clash panicking with both names. Entries are type-erased and read back typed: `get::<T>(id)`, `ids(kind)` and `kinds()` sorted, `kind_of::<T>()`, `contains_kind`, `entry_for_path`, `len`, `is_empty`; `insert::<T>(id, value)` takes a value as given and drops the file the ID had. Neither the registry nor `StagedLoad` is `Send` or `Sync`. `World::check_prefab_id(field, id)` is the asset check of a field that names a prefab, a `MissingAsset` of kind `"prefab"` when the World's `PrefabRegistry` lacks the ID.
- **`DataRef<T>`.** A field's reference to an entry of `T`'s kind: `by_id`, `inline`, `id`, `as_inline`; `resolve(&DataRegistry)` reads the definition in place or the entry the registry holds now; `check(&World, field)` is a `MissingAsset` of `T`'s kind for an ID the registry lacks, runs the kind's check on a definition in place with each field prefixed `field.`, and names an unregistered `T` as an unknown `data kind`. In a file a string is an ID and an object or an array is `T`, read from the same deserializer, so a mistake reports `T`'s own field path; another value fails. Serializing writes the same forms, and an inline definition that does not serialize as an object or an array fails. `Clone`, `Debug`, `PartialEq` and `Eq` when `T` has them.
- **The two-phase load.** `StagedLoad::add_prefab(id, path)` reads and parses a prefab file with no World check (`PrefabError::{Io, Parse}`); `add_data(world, kind, id, path)` reads a data file through the strict reader that refuses a key repeated at any depth and deserializes it as the kind's type (`DataError::{UnknownKind, Io, Parse, Invalid}`); each keeps the file's canonical path when it resolves, and a second add of an entry replaces the first. `commit(world)` first checks each staged entry's kind against the World's registry by type (`DataError::UnknownKind`, `MismatchedKind`), then puts every staged entry in place of the World's entry of that ID, each taking its file from whichever entry held it, and checks every entry the two registries hold from a file, staged or not, with the staging in place: data entries by kind, then ID, through their kind's check (`DataError::MissingAsset`), then prefabs by ID through `PrefabData::load`'s checks. The first failure (`StagedLoadError::{Prefab, Data}`, its source boxed) puts back every staged entry's previous state, a replaced entry with its file, an added one removed, and a file's former holder with its file. Entries without a file are taken as given; live entities are never touched; a map's objects keep their own checks at their map's load and reload. A missing prefab or data ID, and an unknown kind, end with "; did you mean `<name>`?" when one is at most max(1, length / 3) edits away. `PrefabData::load` keeps its signature; `PrefabRegistry::insert` and `insert_with_path` drop an ID's old file.
- **The umbrella.** `load_prefabs(manifest, world)` stages every data entry by kind, then ID, then every prefab by ID, and commits once, adding over the World's registries; `load_all` calls it where it did. `reload_prefab` and the new `reload_data(kind, id, path, world)` stage one file and commit, a failure logged with the previous kept. The manifest reload's block (`reload_prefabs_and_data`) stages every prefab and data entry of the new manifest, each file read again, commits once and keeps every previous entry on a failure, removed entries warning and staying. The hot-reload `json` arm collects a batch's files: themes, animations and particle configs reload as before, and the batch's prefab and data files commit together, a batch whose manifest reloaded leaving them to the manifest's block; a failure's log names the entry and says that saving the manifest reloads every prefab and data file together. Kinds register in a plugin's `build` through the World; `App` has no door of its own.

**Why:** The kit catalogue's review (`docs/plans/1.0/w13-kit.md` §7, R2) found that assets could not name each other at load: `load_prefabs` checked each prefab against the World before the registry it built was inserted, so no prefab could name a prefab, and nothing named shared definitions, which every later kit milestone needs (W13g's `tags` and `spawner`, W13h's `stats` and `cue_rules`, W13j's `loot_tables`, W13k's `effects`, `abilities`, `curves` and `offers`) and a game's own kinds use the same door. Checking after every file is in place lets any entry name any other whatever the order. A commit checks every file-backed entry, staged or not, because a changed definition can break an unchanged one that reads it (the plan's round-1 critique); edits that need each other then fail one at a time, so a batch commits together and a manifest save reads every file again (round 2). One file backs one entry because an edit routes to one (round 1), and a staged entry takes its file from a removed entry that kept it (round 2); `insert` drops a file so a value built in code is never routed or checked (round 2). Kind names are `&'static str` because `MissingAsset.kind` is, and widening it breaks `ComponentError::MissingAsset`'s patterns. A string is always an ID, so an inline definition is an object or an array, and one that would save as a string fails rather than read back as an ID (round 1). Step 1's API critique added the commit's kind check, since a value staged against one World could be dropped or stored under another type in another (C.1), and the canonical paths, since the app routes canonical watcher paths (C.2).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-017`, `D-052` and `D-157` as their marker lines say. `D-053`'s reload matrix gains a Data entry row and its Prefab row the staging; `D-103` covers the section's format and data files' shapes, which are the game's types'; `D-153`'s strict reader reads data files.
- Breaks, each a W13f row in the break ledger: the manifest types gain the section and `ManifestError` its two variants, a `data` key that was ignored now parsing and two prefabs sharing one file now failing the load; the load and reloads change behaviour (both sections in `load_prefabs`, adding over the registries, every file-backed entry checked at each commit, a batch's prefab and data edits committed together, a manifest reload reading every prefab and data file again and keeping all or none, `insert` and `insert_with_path` dropping an ID's old file, a suggestion in a prefab's validation message). The rest is additions for the freeze: `api/tungsten-core.txt` 526 lines added and none removed, `api/tungsten.txt` one (`reload_data`); the kit and render snapshots are unchanged.
- No new dependency, thread, system, stage or schedule change, and nothing per frame: `DefaultPlugins`' and the template's resolved schedules are unchanged, and no shipped manifest has a `data` key. The determinism hash `0x088e_c07a_73c1_b168`, the containment hash `0xaee2_72e0_1ffc_4e4c` and the row digests are unchanged.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/assets/data.rs` (24: registration and its panics, the registry, `DataRef`'s forms, paths and round trips, `resolve`, `check`, `check_prefab_id`, the commit in either order, its rollback and order, an unchanged dependent refused, entries without a file, a file moved and given back, phase one's errors, a second add, a bare World, the kind check, canonical paths, the nearest name), `tests/assets/manifest.rs` (the section, its parse errors, `MissingDataFile`, IDs within a kind, `SharedFile`), `tests/assets/prefab.rs` (a replaced path, the suggestion), `tests/manifests.rs` (`every_data_file_parses`), the umbrella's `tests/prefabs.rs` (twelve: loads naming each other, failures with their files and fields, a data edit by path, an unchanged prefab refused, a batch, the manifest's block, a moved file, a file given to a data entry, an unregistered kind), and `scripts/test-check-repo.py` (the coverage check two levels deep).
- Recorded limits (known issues): edits that need each other load only when saved in one batch or followed by a manifest save; a failed manifest reload retries only at the next manifest save; a reader's copy outlives a reload; a prefab or data reload does not check maps' objects again; Layer 1 parses data files as JSON only; suggestions in prefab and data files only.
- Open: nearest names for scenes, component names, particle configs and tilemaps (W13g or the Phase 6 QA pass); W14b's `tungsten check` driving `StagedLoad` against a World with the game's plugins built; prefab variants and nested prefabs (1.x); routes of other removed assets (the Phase 6 QA pass).
- The plan, its two critique rounds and step 1's API critique are recorded in `docs/plans/archive/1.0/phase6-milestone-61-kit-core-data-assets.md`.

## D-172 — W13g landed (M62): the kit's foundations: domain subfolders behind one public path per item, `prelude` and `KitPlugins`; seven phase anchors every kit system joins, the lifecycle items on the fixed step; `Tags` and `TagFilter` declared in the `tags` kind or at plugin build and checked at load; `Source` derived through spawn inheritance; `Zone`, `ActionInput` with `Intent`, `CameraTarget`; a scene component per item; inspector rows, `KitDebugPlugin` and field descriptions snapshotted as `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt` under `just api-check`; recipes in two genres as the admission rule; the kit determinism hash pinned
**Amended by D-173:** the set, the debug plugin, the descriptions, the phase table and the hash only; `KitPlugins::set()` ends with stats, counters, cooldown, spawn children, cues and then the debug drawing, `KitDebugPlugin` also inserts the `KitTrace` and its step count and logs the trace while `KitDebug` is on, `KitDescriptions` holds shapes beside the components and kinds in the components snapshot, stats and cooldowns join `Tick`, child spawns, cues and the trace's step count `React`, labels `Present`, and the kit hash's pin is `0xd2a0_6495_3ac2_b9c1`; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-174:** the one within-phase edge and the phase table's members only; `path_follow` is one of the kit's within-phase edges, each named on one side `_if_present` where an item reads or adds to another's output in the same step (`D-174`'s order), and the phases hold M's members (`aim` in `Intent`; the linear motion, oscillator, steering, follow, orbit, knockback and speed limit in `Move`; the speed limit's settle, wrap and stay in view in `Overlap`; the oscillator's rotation and scale, facing, the character animator and depth sort in `Present`); the rest stands.
**Amended by D-175:** the set, the phase table's members and the lifecycle items' despawns only; `KitPlugins::set()` adds the hit, contact damage, projectile and pickup plugins after depth sort and before the debug drawing; the phases hold C's members (`shield_tick` in `Tick`, `pickup_magnet` in `Move`, `hit_grid`, `hitbox_tick`, `contact_damage`, `projectile_tick` and `pickup_tick` in `Overlap` before `despawn_outside`, `damage_resolve` in `Resolve`, `kit_despawn` in `React` after the spawner, the child spawns and the cues); a `Lifetime` that ends and an entity outside its `despawn_outside` region are listed for `kit_despawn`, which marks each with its descendants and queues one `despawn_recursive` after the step's spawns from it; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-177:** the set and the phase table's members only; `KitPlugins::set()` adds the effects, abilities and level plugins after the pickup plugin and before the debug drawing; the phases hold E's members (`effect_tick` and `ailment_tick` in `Tick` after `stats_apply`, `ability_tick` in `Intent` after `action_input` and `aim`, `ailment_feed` and `effect_resolve` in `Resolve` after `damage_resolve`, `level_tick` in `React` before the cues); the rest stands.
**Amended by D-185:** the `top_down_mover` scene component and the mover's step only; the component takes optional `acceleration` and `deceleration` (each above 0), and with either set the mover keeps a velocity it moves toward its target at that rate, its inspector rows gaining `acceleration`, `deceleration` and `velocity`; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-215:** the admission rule only; a Phase 7 item enters when example 01 or the template uses it and a recipe exists in the other genre, an item whose job is one genre's on that use alone, and a Phase 8 item on a paper design's or 01's need with recipes and sketches; the reference genres become `platformer`, `top_down`, `rpg` and `shooter` at step 7.10; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-218:** the reference genres only; `arpg` joins `platformer`, `top_down`, `rpg` and `shooter` at step 7.10; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-16
**Decision:** The kit's foundations, so that the catalogue's later milestones add items and nothing else:

- **Layout.** `crates/tungsten-kit/src/` is eight private domain folders (`foundation`, `intent`, `motion`, `lifecycle`, `overlap`, `damage`, `present`, `transform`); the root re-exports every item at the path it had, so `api/tungsten-kit.txt` changes by additions, and `prelude` re-exports the plugins, components, events and helpers. `KitPlugins::set()` is every plugin that takes no game type, in the kit's order, as a `PluginSet` a game builds after `DefaultPlugins`; `SoundOnEvent`, `TriggerZone` and `StateMachinePlugin` take a game type, so a game adds them itself.
- **Phases.** Seven anchors, no-op systems that `ensure_phases` adds once (every kit plugin calls it first, a game's plugin that joins a phase too): `kit_tick`, `kit_intent`, `kit_move`, `kit_overlap`, `kit_resolve` and `kit_react` in `FixedUpdate`, the first after `physics_prev_snapshot`, `kit_move` before `physics_step` and `kit_overlap` after it, and `kit_present` in `PostUpdate` after `physics_sync`, `transform_propagate_late` and `tween_tick` and before `camera_update`, each constraint when the engine's system is registered. `Phase::join(desc)` orders a system after its anchor and before the next of its stage, a `Move` member also before `physics_step` and a `Present` member before `camera_update`, since the resolver takes the earliest-registered ready system. Inside a phase, registration order; the one within-phase edge stays explicit, `path_follow` before the controllers, so a rider reads its platform's velocity whatever the plugins' order. `health_tick` and `lifetime_tick` are `Tick`; `action_input` and a state machine `Intent`; `path_follow` and the controllers `Move`; `despawn_outside`, `zone_tick` and a trigger zone `Overlap`; `spawner_tick` `React`; `animation_tick`, `hit_flash_tick`, `camera_target`, `kit_debug_draw` and a sound plugin `Present`; the hierarchy's two passes keep their places against the engine's names. The lifecycle items leave `PostUpdate`: a lifetime ends on a step, a spawner spawns on one and a despawn-outside judges the last frame's camera view, so the kit's timers and spawns are frame-rate-free; `despawn_outside` and `spawner_tick` read `Position` when present, else `Transform.position`. With `DefaultPlugins` then `KitPlugins::set()`, the stages resolve to `fixed_update: physics_prev_snapshot, kit_tick, lifetime_tick, health_tick, kit_intent, action_input, kit_move, path_follow, platformer_controller, top_down_mover, physics_step, kit_overlap, despawn_outside, zone_tick, kit_resolve, kit_react, spawner_tick` and `post_update: physics_sync, transform_propagate, particle_count_refresh, particle_emit, particle_tick, tween_tick, squash_stretch_trigger, squash_stretch_tick, shake_tick, transform_propagate_late, kit_present, animation_tick, hit_flash_tick, camera_target, kit_debug_draw, camera_update`.
- **Tags and `Source`.** `Tags`, sorted and deduplicated dotted names (`[a-z][a-z0-9_]*` segments) that carry their ancestors (`has("team")` holds for `team.enemy`), and `TagFilter` (`all`, `any`, `none`; an empty filter matches everything, an entity without `Tags` a filter with no `all` and no `any`), both data, with private fields behind constructors and `with`-less builders (`all`, `any`, `none`). `TagsPlugin` registers the `tags` scene component (an array of names) and the `tags` data kind (a JSON array of names); a tag is declared by the kind's entries or by `TagsPlugin::with_tags` at build, a declared name declaring its ancestors, and a scene `tags` list or a zone filter naming an undeclared tag fails the load with the field as written (`0`, `filter.any.1`). `Source(Entity)`, the spawn chain's origin, is derived at the flush from a spawn's source (`D-170`): the source's own `Source`, else the source; `SourcePlugin` registers the derivation and nothing per frame.
