- **Zone, action input, camera target.** `Zone { filter }` on a sensor body; `ZonePlugin`'s `zone_tick` reads `SensorEntered` through an `EventCursor` and sends `ZoneEntered { zone, entity, position }` (`#[non_exhaustive]`, `new`) for a body whose `Tags` pass the filter; a tile and a bodiless entity never enter. `ActionInput` (two or four movement actions, four optional aim actions, named slots) and `Intent` (a movement of length at most 1, an aim, slots held or just pressed), both data and both validated at deserialization; `action_input` in `kit_intent` writes every `Intent` from its `ActionInput` through the `ActionMap`, from `RoutedInput` when the resource exists and `InputState` otherwise, and the load checks every named action against the map (`move.1`, `slots.jump`), a World without the map passing. `TopDownMover` takes the intent's movement as its direction and `PlatformerController` its `x` as the run and the `jump` slot as the jump, each when the entity has an `Intent`, their setters standing without one; `TopDownMover::direction()` reads what it moved by last. A `TopDownMover` without a `RigidBody` moves its `Position` by direction, speed and the step with no cast, writing a `Velocity` when it has one. `CameraTarget`, a marker: `camera_target` in `kit_present` sets `CameraController.mode` to `Follow` of the lowest-indexed live one when the mode is `Free` or its own earlier `Follow`, and back to `Free` when none is left and the mode is still its own; a mode the game set stands.
- **Scene components.** One per item, registered in its plugin's `build` under its bare name with a private DTO under `deny_unknown_fields`, numbers checked at deserialization (`lifetime { seconds >= 0 }`, `despawn_outside` with exactly one of `region` and `view`, `spawner { prefab, interval > 0, count }` with the prefab ID checked, `health { max > 0, invulnerability >= 0 = 0 }`, `hit_flash { color = [1, 1, 1, 1], seconds > 0 = 0.1 }`, `path_follower { points as offsets from the spawn, speed > 0, mode = stop, easing = linear }`, `top_down_mover { speed > 0 }`, which spawns `Position`, `PrevPosition` and `Velocity` at the spawn point so a prefab without a `rigid_body` moves bodiless, `platformer_controller` with the builder's defaults, `tags`, `zone`, `action_input` (spawning `Intent::default()` beside it) and `camera_target: null`). `Health` is data: it deserializes through a check of its four fields.
- **Seeing the kit.** Every kit component implements `Inspectable` and its plugin registers rows under the type's name in the `InspectRegistry`, inserting one into a bare World (an `AnimationState` row by ID and frame, a state machine's `elapsed` under its name). `KitDebugPlugin` inserts `KitDebug`, off, and `kit_debug_draw` in `kit_present` draws a path follower's polyline and target, a `DespawnOutside` region, a zone's collider, a spawner's mark and a camera target's cross through core's `DebugDraw` while it is on. Each plugin describes what it registers in the `KitDescriptions` resource: `ComponentDescription` (summary, `FieldDescription`s with a `FieldType` and a default, events, genres, a minimal sample and a round-trip hook over the DTO) and a kind line per data kind; `render()` is `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt`, written by the kit's `components` example from `scripts/public-api.sh` and compared by `just api-check`. `FieldType` is `#[non_exhaustive]` with a `Bound` enum for numeric limits, so later kinds are additions. The test holds each description to its DTO: the sample round-trips to the described defaults, each enum variant deserializes and each numeric field takes its extremes and refuses beyond. `serde_json` joins the kit's runtime dependencies for the hooks.
- **Recipes, the hash and the bench.** The admission rule is recipes in two of the four reference genres, in place of "has a user": `crates/tungsten/tests/fixtures/kit/{platformer,top_down,shmup,grid}/`, each a manifest of prefabs, `data.tags`, animations and the flash material where used, and `crates/tungsten/src/tests/kit_recipes/`, each genre's script on the harness at dt 1/60 and 1/30 through `KitPlugins::set()`, the moving-platform recipe with the controller registered before the follower. The coverage test fails when a registered scene name or data kind appears in fewer than two fixtures or a fixture names an unregistered one. The determinism test runs each genre's fixed script for 300 frames at 1/60 s, hashes every entity's index, point, health and tags in entity order with FNV-1a, combines the four and pins `0x2b64_8d77_6dff_9170`, equal across two in-process runs; the App flushes commands once a frame, so the hash is a one-step-per-frame claim. `crates/tungsten-kit/benches/kit.rs` (criterion 0.8) holds `lifetime_tick`, `health_tick`, `action_input`, the bodiless `top_down_mover` and `TagFilter::matches` at 1,000 and 10,000 entities, built and run once in test mode; W13i owns the first measured run.
- **The harness and the template.** `Harness::load_manifest(path)` loads a manifest's headless sections (animations, UI themes, each material's metadata without an upload, sounds, tilemaps, particle configs, then the prefabs and data entries in two phases), so a game's tests and the recipes load their manifest as a window's startup does. The template's player is a prefab of the kit's components (`tags`, `action_input`, `top_down_mover`, `health`, `hit_flash`, `camera_target`) beside its own `player` marker, with `KitPlugins::set()` added before the game's plugin, the tags declared in `assets/data/tags.json`, the flash shader and material in its manifest, a camera dead zone so the player's movement shows in an empty world, and no game Rust for movement, health or the camera; its pause pauses the game clock and saves the body's `Position` on the first frame that runs no step. `templates/basic/AGENTS.md` carries the growth rule for a game that passes three components or two features.

**Why:** The catalogue (`docs/plans/1.0/w13-kit.md` §2) wants every later item data-first, phased, visible and tested, which no single item could bring: the phases replace `D-158`'s pairwise chain, which grew by a constraint per pair, with one table and one explicit edge; tags let an item filter without naming a game type; `Source` lets credit and reactions follow a spawn chain; `Intent` lets input and AI share the movers; scene components and descriptions make data the API, with the snapshot the guard that the private DTOs' Rust surface cannot give (P7); recipes replace "has a user" with evidence a first game would give, in four genres so an item proves generic before the freeze. The plan's critique (§9) moved `kit_move`'s members before `physics_step` explicitly, since the resolver's tie-break would otherwise run the engine's earlier-registered step first; recorded the child spawner's lag and the per-frame flush as limits; and had `CameraTarget` remember its target so removal frees the camera. The step 1 API critique (C.1–C.4) made `Intent` and `Health` validate at deserialization, the template save the authoritative position once the clock has stopped, and the template's camera take a dead zone. The lifecycle items moved to the fixed step so the hash is frame-rate-free (Q1); the harness's two-steps-a-frame bound means a 0.05 s frame owes three and runs two, which the kit's harness tests now count. `serde_json` is a workspace runtime dependency of core already, a well-specified data format under `D-015` rule 2, so no new third-party crate.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-155` (the admission rule and the stage rule), `D-158` (the chain onto the anchors) and `D-107` (the second snapshot file) as their marker lines say; `D-170`'s inheritance derives `Source`, `D-171`'s kinds hold `tags`, `D-153` and `D-157` gain a scene component per item.
- Additions for the freeze, no compile break: `api/tungsten-kit.txt` 1,460 lines added and none removed, `api/tungsten.txt` one (`Harness::load_manifest`); the W4 ledger lists them. Behaviour changes a game may notice: `lifetime_tick`, `despawn_outside` and `spawner_tick` run on the fixed step (a lifetime of 0.05 s ends on its third step, and a 0.05 s frame runs two), the movers read `Intent` when the entity has one, `Health`, `Intent` and `ActionInput` refuse invalid data, and a kit plugin built twice registers once.
- Limits, in the known issues: a spawner that is a child of a moving parent carries a `Transform` only, so its spawns land one frame behind the parent; commands flush once a frame, so a despawn or spawn queued in a frame's first step is seen by the next frame, and the hash and recipes are one-step-per-frame claims (a flush per step is a core question); a bodiless mover is stopped by no wall and enters no zone, since the physics never sees it, until C's grid.
- Open, in the plan's follow-ups: a hotkey or console command for `KitDebug`; the inspector showing a field's description and `tungsten check` printing them (W14b); nearest-name suggestions in `MissingAsset`; `Lifetime` and `Spawner` serde once core's `Timer` has it; an immediate-spawner component beside `Source`.
- Pinned: the kit hash `0x2b64_8d77_6dff_9170`; `DefaultPlugins`' schedule unchanged; the umbrella's and the template's resolved-schedule snapshots take the phases.

## D-173 — W13h landed (M63): `Stats` with ordered modifiers, which the movers, health and cooldowns read; cues, the kit's one way to react to events in data (an entity's `cues`, the `cue_rules` kind and `CuesOnEvent<E>`), with nine actions, a fixed order and a closed boundary; the `label` action's timed world labels; `Cooldown`, `Counters`, `Flags`, `KitRng` and `SpawnChildren`; `KitTrace` and its log view; described shapes; the kit hash moved
**Amended by D-174:** `stats_apply`'s items only; `move_speed` also writes a `Steering`'s top speed, 0 stopping it, a sixth column of its query; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-175:** the cue vocabulary's events and actions and `stats_apply`'s items only; the kit's events add `hit` (the hitting entity, its target the other), `healed` (the heal's source the other), `picked_up` (the pickup, its collector the other) and `expired` (a projectile's end), and `damaged` and `killed` take the request's source as their other entity and its position when it has one; the actions add `roll`, which draws a `loot_tables` table's rolls from `KitRng` and spawns each prefab drawn at the event's position from the subject; a cue's `despawn` marks its target's descendants too; `damage` writes a `Hitbox`'s and a `ContactDamage`'s damage, its query's seventh and eighth columns; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-176:** the cue vocabulary only; the kit's events add `damaged_part` (one per part of a hit, its source the other, its amount and kinds), read before `damaged` so each pass runs every part before any total; a rule takes a `kind` filter over a damage event's kinds beside its tag filter, on `damaged`, `killed` and `damaged_part` only; the `label` action takes `{}` on `damaged_part`, floating the part in its type's style; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-177:** the cue vocabulary only; the kit's events add `effect_applied` (the effect's source the other, the stacks the amount), `effect_expired`, `ability_used` (the ability's target the other, at the use's position) and `level_up` (the level the amount); the actions add `effect`, which queues an effect on the subject from the other or on the other from the subject for the next `effect_resolve`, and `ability`, which uses or refills the subject's or the other's ability at once; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-197:** `KitRng` only; it serializes whole, its generator's words through `Pcg32`'s serde, so a save restores the stream at any draw count; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-200:** the labels only; a game floats an amount in a `LabelStyle` from its code with `float_label`, as the `label` action does, a non-finite amount or anchor floating nothing; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-17
**Decision:** The kit catalogue's A (`docs/plans/1.0/w13-kit.md` §7), on core alone with no new dependency:

- **Stats.** `Stats` holds named values (`[a-z][a-z0-9_]*`, sorted, read by binary search; names, not interned IDs, since a load's checks see `&World` only), each a finite base, a clamp and `Modifier`s (`Add`, `Pct`, `Mul`, `Override`, `#[non_exhaustive]`) keyed by a source string in add order. A value is `((base + Σadd) × (1 + Σpct) × Πmul)`, replaced by the last-added override, clamped to its bounds and to `±f32::MAX`, computed in `f64` with a zero factor making the product 0, recomputed on each change so `get` never computes; every value is finite. The well-known stats (`move_speed`, `max_health`, `damage`, `cooldown_rate`, `area`, `pickup_radius`, `projectile_speed`) are bounded `[0, ∞)`, `max_health` `[1, ∞)`; a game's stat is declared by `StatsPlugin::with_stat` or the `stats` data kind (`{ "armor": { "min": 0, "max": 10 } }`), the first declaration found giving its bounds. `stats_apply` in `kit_tick`, after `health_tick` by registration, writes each well-known stat an entity has into its item when they differ: `move_speed` into `TopDownMover`'s speed and `PlatformerController`'s run speed, `max_health` into `Health`'s max with the health left kept as a fraction, `cooldown_rate` into `Cooldown`'s rate; in `kit_tick`, not the catalogue's `kit_resolve`, so a spawn moves at its stats from its first step. The scene `stats` holds bases; the load refuses an undeclared name or a base outside its bounds.
- **Cues.** One way to react in data: an entity's `cues` (an action list per event), shared `cue_rules` entries (`{ "on", "filter", "do" }`, a list per entry) and `CuesOnEvent<E>`, which brings a game's event in under a name (`[a-z][a-z0-9_]*`, not the kit's, once) through a map to a `CueEvent { entity, position, amount, other }`. The kit's events at A: `damaged` and `killed` (after its `damaged`) from `Damaged`, `zone_entered` from `ZoneEntered` (the zone the subject, the entrant the other) and `cooldown_elapsed`. The actions: `prefab` (spawned at the event's position from the subject, so it inherits), `counter` and `flag` (on the subject, the other or the game's own `Counters` and `Flags`, inserted when missing), `despawn` (the subject or the other, with its children), `sound` (a manifest sound at most once per `min_interval` game seconds per ID), `shake`, `squash`, `flash` and `label`. The `cues` system, last in `kit_react`, reads each source once through its own `EventCursor`, the kit's in that order and then each `CuesOnEvent` in build order; skips an occurrence whose subject is dead or marked despawning, with a trace entry; runs every occurrence's own cues, then every occurrence's matching rules (entry-ID order, then file order, each list in written order, a rule matching on its event and its tag filter over the subject's `Tags`); applies counters and flags at once, queues spawns as met and the step's despawns after its spawns, marking each target so a frame's second step reacts to it no more. The boundary: no conditions, variables or sequences, no filter but the rule's, and `label` shows the event's own amount; a reaction that needs more is a game's Rust. Every ID and name is checked at load, prefabs, sounds when the World has a `SoundRegistry`, tags, events, a `label` on an event with an amount and a `target: other` on one with another entity (a game event is taken to carry both); a label's font is not, since core's `FontRegistry` exists only after a window loads fonts.
- **Labels.** `label` floats the event's amount (`3`, else `1.5`) as a `LabelStyle` (a manifest face, size, linear colour encoded to sRGB, seconds, rise, pop) for its seconds of game time: `label_tick` in `kit_present` pushes each live label to core's one-frame `WorldLabels` every frame (`D-161`), aged on the game clock and held while paused; it rises over its seconds, scales from its pop to 1 over the first fifth and fades over the second half; at most 512 live, the oldest dropped.
- **State items.** `Cooldown` repeats on the game clock at its rate and sends `CooldownElapsed` once per elapse, at most 16 a step (the rest of the step dropped, the next cycle whole); a jitter draws each cycle's extra in `[0, jitter]` from `KitRng` before the step's time decides whether another elapse fits, jittered cooldowns drawing in `Entity::id` order; `count` stops it. `KitRng`, a World resource over core's `Pcg32` at a fixed default seed apart from the particles' `WorldRngSeed`, with its seed and draw count. `Counters` (`i64`, saturating) and `Flags`, named by the tag rule and undeclared, are components and World resources that `CountersPlugin` inserts. `SpawnChildren` spawns its prefabs once at its first step from the parent, at the parent's `Transform.position` plus each offset, through the command buffer, each child marked and adopted into the parent's `Children` in queued order at the flush; a child's prefab may not hold a body (`D-132`), refused at load. `SoundOnEvent::with_min_interval` gives the per-event sound the same interval on real time.
- **The trace.** `KitTrace`, a ring of 256 `TraceEntry`s (`frame`, `step`, `entity`, `TraceKind`: an event, a cue run, a skip with its reason, a spawn queued or failed, a despawn) that `KitDebugPlugin` inserts with `kit_trace_step` counting ended steps; `damage`, `zone_tick`, `cooldown_tick`, the cues, `spawn_children`, `spawner_tick`, `lifetime_tick` (once, at its end) and `despawn_outside` (once a frame) push to it, and while `KitDebug` is on `kit_debug_draw` logs each new entry once at `info` under `tungsten_kit::trace`, which the log console shows.
- **Descriptions and stages.** `FieldType` gains `I64` and `Shape(name)`; `KitDescriptions` gains shapes (`add_shape`, `shapes`, `get_shape`), each a `ComponentDescription` that may be `one_of` (exactly one field), rendered `shape <name>: …` between the components and the kinds and held by the same tests. With `DefaultPlugins` then `KitPlugins::set()` the stages resolve to `fixed_update: physics_prev_snapshot, kit_tick, lifetime_tick, health_tick, stats_apply, cooldown_tick, kit_intent, action_input, kit_move, path_follow, platformer_controller, top_down_mover, physics_step, kit_overlap, despawn_outside, zone_tick, kit_resolve, kit_react, spawner_tick, spawn_children, cues, kit_trace_step` and `post_update: …, kit_present, animation_tick, hit_flash_tick, camera_target, label_tick, kit_debug_draw, camera_update`.

**Why:** The catalogue orders A before M, C and E because C's top-down recipe (counting kills, a burst, damage numbers and a drop through cues alone) and E's effects stand on cues, counters and stats. A closed vocabulary with one order keeps a reaction readable from its data and lets the load check every name; anything beyond a tag filter is Rust by design, so the vocabulary cannot grow into a script. Stats write into the items' own fields rather than having every item read stats, so the items stay unchanged and an entity without stats runs exactly as before, which the kit hash held through steps 1–2. The plan's critiques shaped it: each jittered cycle drawn before the next counts and at most 16 elapses a step (round 1), finite stats under overflow (round 1), a label's font as a face that the load cannot check (rounds 1 and 2), and a `KitRng::range` computed in `f64` so a full-width range stays finite (the step 1 API critique, C.2).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-155`, `D-158` and `D-172` as their marker lines say; `D-161`'s labels, `D-170`'s cursors and inheritance and `D-171`'s kinds carry it.
- Additions for the freeze, no compile break: `api/tungsten-kit.txt` 1,107 lines added and none removed, `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt` 86 (six components, ten shapes, two kinds); the W4 ledger lists them. Behaviour a game may notice: `KitPlugins::set()` registers `stats_apply`, `cooldown_tick`, `spawn_children`, `cues`, `kit_trace_step` and `label_tick` in its resolved schedule; the movers, health and cooldowns follow `Stats` when present; `SoundOnEvent` is unchanged without an interval.
- Pinned: the kit hash moves to `0xd2a0_6495_3ac2_b9c1`, its script spawning A's prefabs after the existing ones and landing hits at fixed frames, its rows adding each entity's counters, flags and stat values and each genre the game's counters and flags; the physics hashes, `DefaultPlugins`' schedule and the core, render and umbrella snapshots unchanged.
- Limits, in the known issues: a label's font is a face ID the load does not check, and world labels resolve faces only though `WorldLabel`'s doc allows a family; a child draws one frame at its queue point before its first propagation; a `prefab` cue on a subject another system despawned earlier in the frame spawns nothing; labels outlive a state's exit by at most their seconds; a cue reacts one step later to an event sent outside the fixed step; a command flush per fixed step stays the Phase 6 QA pass's question.
- Open, in the plan's follow-ups: `spawned` and `expired` with C's projectile; a `label_styles` kind; `KitRng` and `KitTrace` serde; nearest-name suggestions; a `KitDebug` hotkey; a core list of font faces for the label check; the motion review of the template's cue in W13i's hazard; the spawner's unbounded catch-up.

## D-174 — W13i landed (M64): the kit's motion and facing, thirteen items with `Target` and `knock_back` under one motion write rule (movers move roots before the step, a kinematic body has one driver, constraints correct after the step, presentation writes once a frame); the within-phase edges M needs; `Steering` in `stats_apply`; the kit bench's 23 rows within a per-entity bound at their first measured run; the kit hash moved
**Amended by D-212:** `CharacterAnimator` only; a platformer grounded on a kinematic body with a `Velocity`, its controller's `ground`, counts its own velocity, the body's taken off, for its state and its side, so a rider standing still on a moving deck idles; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-17
**Decision:** The kit catalogue's M (`docs/plans/1.0/w13-kit.md` §7), on core alone with no new dependency:

- **The motion write rule.** A mover writes a bodiless or static entity's `Position` by an increment, with its `Velocity` when it has one, or a kinematic or dynamic body's `Velocity`; never the `Transform.position` that the physics sync or the hierarchy writes, and an entity with a `Parent` is skipped with one warning. A kinematic body has one kit driver that writes its velocity every step, zero while it holds (the path follower, a controller, `LinearMotion`, `Steering`, `Follow` or `Orbit`); the adders add after it (the oscillator's position after a path follower or a linear motion, a knockback after every driver), the oscillator drives a body with no driver, a knockback writes its slide when nothing else does and zero on the step after its push ends, and beside a controller, a top-down mover or a steering, which set the velocity after it, the oscillator warns and leaves the body. A dynamic body keeps its velocity: the setters skip it, `Steering` steers it and `knock_back` adds once. The placers (`Follow`, `Orbit`) and the path follower fold their entity's oscillator displacement into their goal; the path follower folds it at the oscillator's clock, so the wave lands whichever runs first, and beside a placer, which edges put after it, the oscillator adds nothing. Constraints correct after the step: `Wrap` is a teleport writing `PrevPosition` with `Position`, `StayInView` a clamp keeping it; `SpeedLimit` caps last before the step and a dynamic body again after the solver. Presentation writes once a frame in `kit_present`, one kit writer per channel: rotation (`Facing` or an oscillator), scale (an oscillator), `Sprite.z_order` (`DepthSort`), the `AnimationState` and a new clip's first frame (`CharacterAnimator`, after `animation_tick`); gameplay reads no presentation. `motion/drive.rs` holds the body kinds, the driver test, the writes and the warnings.
- **Targets.** `Target`: the entity's `Source`, the nearest other entity whose `Tags` pass a `TagFilter` that names an `all` or an `any` tag (refused at load and panicking in `Target::nearest` otherwise, since an untagged entity passes a filter of `none` alone), within a range when given, ties to the lower index, or a world point; data, resolved again each step, no entity form since `Entity` is no data. A system run gathers each distinct filter's matches once, sorted by index, and each seeker scans its filter's; a placer takes a kinematic or dynamic target at `Position + Velocity·dt`, else at its `Position` as its movers left it.
- **The items.** `LinearMotion` (`Δ = (v + a·dt/2)·dt`, exact for a constant acceleration); `Oscillator` (position, rotation and scale channels on a fixed-step clock, the position's offset `amplitude·(sin(2π(t/period + phase)) − sin(2π·phase))` so it starts where it is, rotation and scale about the first frame's values at the clock drawn as the sync draws positions); `Follow` (`1 − e^(−dt/lag)` of the gap a step, a top speed); `Orbit` (`centre + offset + radius·(cos θ, sin θ)` on its own clock, its first point without a target, the last centre when one goes); `Steering` (`seek`, `flee`, `arrive` with a bounded acceleration, `arrive` braking in time and never further than the distance left, an emergency stop on that last approach); `Knockback` with `knock_back(world, entity, velocity) -> bool` (a controller launched with its input locked, a dynamic body kicked once, otherwise a push falling linearly to 0 over its seconds, a kinematic body sliding by a cast from where its driver's velocity ends); `SpeedLimit` (the length, then each axis); `Wrap` (out past one side, in by the area's extent plus its size from the other) and `StayInView` (clamped, the outward speed stopped), on a region or the last frame's camera view without its shake; `Aim` (the intent's aim, the cursor, the movement or a target, the last direction kept); `Facing` (motion, intent or aim, snapped free, horizontal, four or eight ways, held below a speed, turning when asked, flipping nothing); `CharacterAnimator` with `Clips` (idle, move, rise and fall, a clip or a set by facing, every ID checked at load); `DepthSort` (`base + round(y + offset)`, clamped). Each is a component with private fields, constructors that panic outside the data's bounds, serde through checked fields, a scene component under its bare name that spawns `Position` and `PrevPosition` for a mover, a description, inspector rows and rustdoc with a behaviour doctest; `kit_debug_draw` adds an orbit's circle, a follower's goal, the regions and an aim's ray.
- **Order.** Each edge named on one side with `_if_present`: `aim` after `action_input`; `linear_motion` before both controllers; the oscillator after `linear_motion` and before both controllers; `follow` and `orbit` after `path_follow`, `linear_motion`, the oscillator, both controllers and `steering`; `knockback` after `path_follow`, `linear_motion`, the oscillator, `top_down_mover`, `steering`, `follow` and `orbit`; `speed_limit` after all of those and `knockback`; `character_animator` after `facing` and `animation_tick`. A test builds each edge's two plugins alone in both orders and the set with M's plugins reversed. With `DefaultPlugins` then `KitPlugins::set()` the stages resolve to `fixed_update: physics_prev_snapshot, kit_tick, lifetime_tick, health_tick, stats_apply, cooldown_tick, kit_intent, action_input, aim, kit_move, path_follow, linear_motion, oscillator, platformer_controller, top_down_mover, steering, follow, orbit, knockback, speed_limit, physics_step, kit_overlap, despawn_outside, zone_tick, speed_limit_settle, wrap, stay_in_view, kit_resolve, kit_react, spawner_tick, spawn_children, cues, kit_trace_step` and `post_update: …, kit_present, animation_tick, hit_flash_tick, camera_target, label_tick, oscillator_present, facing, character_animator, depth_sort, kit_debug_draw, camera_update`.
- **Stats.** `stats_apply` writes `move_speed` into a `Steering`'s top speed too, 0 stopping it; a saved `Steering` holds 0, the constructors and the scene keep it above 0.
- **The bench.** `crates/tungsten-kit/benches/kit.rs` holds 23 rows at 1,000 and 10,000 entities, fifteen of them M's; the bound is each row's median per entity at 10,000 at most twice its median per entity at 1,000. The first measured run (2026-10-17, one quiet sitting in two blocking parts) read every ratio between 0.56 (`health_tick`) and 1.09 (`facing`); `perf-runs/20261017-m64-kit-criterion/summary.txt` holds the numbers.
- **The template.** The hazard, `prefabs/hazard.json`: the player's sprite tinted red, `team.hazard`, an `oscillator` patrolling a line below the player's start and wobbling, and the game's `hazard` component (checked fields), touched through the game's `hazard_touch` in `Phase::Overlap` (`damage`, then on `Hit::Hurt` a `knock_back` away), so the player's window paces the hits; the player gains `knockback`; a player whose health runs out starts gameplay over.

**Why:** The catalogue gathers here the movers, constraints and presentation that a shmup's bullet, a top-down enemy or an orbiting blade needs without a physics body, since motion by `Position` on the fixed step is drawn smooth by the sync at no cost (w13-kit R5, §2 rule 6). Thirteen items composing on one entity need one rule for who writes what: the physics step keeps a kinematic body's velocity, so adders accumulate unless one driver writes it each step; the path follower corrects to its point each step, so a placer must fold a wave rather than be added to; the solver adds gravity and impulses inside the step, so a pre-step cap cannot bound a dynamic body alone; `animation_tick` advances a fresh state before showing it, so a clip swap must come after it. The plan's two critique rounds found those seams (13 findings) and the step 1 API critique four more on the template (a checked `Hazard`, a death that restarts gameplay, the window held by contact; the empty step 1 systems rejected as the plan's staging). Stats write into `Steering` as into the other movers, so the item reads no stats (`D-173`'s rule). Nothing the perf suite runs links the kit, so the evidence is criterion's: no row grows faster than its entities.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-172` and `D-173` as their marker lines say; `D-132`'s bodies on roots, `D-158`'s movers and `D-170`'s `Source` carry it.
- Additions for the freeze, no compile break: `api/tungsten-kit.txt` 1,293 lines added and none removed, `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt` 106 (thirteen components, four shapes); the W4 ledger lists them. Behaviour a game may notice: `KitPlugins::set()`'s resolved schedule; thirteen more reserved scene names; `stats_apply` writing `Steering`; `kit_debug_draw`'s new shapes.
- Pinned: the kit hash moves to `0xd651_23fe_e583_03ee`, its script spawning M's prefabs after the existing ones and pushing at fixed frames, its rows adding each entity's rotation, sprite depth and clip; the physics hashes, `DefaultPlugins`' schedule and the core, render and umbrella snapshots unchanged.
- Limits, in the known issues: a bodiless mover is stopped by no wall; no sprite flip in place; a rider on an orbiting platform reads it a step late; a placer is a step behind a target that a later item corrects, and in a chain of placers; a kinematic body a game drives takes no kit adder; a dynamic body moves past its cap inside a step under gravity and contacts, bounded only in free fall; `arrive`'s last approach may brake harder than its acceleration, and with a radius over a step's travel it settles rather than lands exactly; one kit writer per channel, the kit's write hiding a tween's or a squash's; the nearest search's cost over many matches; `Wrap`'s and `StayInView`'s unshaken view against `despawn_outside`'s shaken one; a child's aim a frame behind; the hazard's touch in Rust; the template's camera shake moves nothing, its `shake_max_offset` zero.
- Open, in the plan's follow-ups: a flip in place; constant spin; a rider on an orbiting platform in the same step; a `knockback` cue action (C); the nearest search over many matches (C's grid); `despawn_outside` on the unshaken view; `hazard_touch` onto C's contact damage; placers tracking a later correction; a way for a game to name its own kinematic driver; a `nearest` among untagged entities; the template's shake offset.

## D-175 — W13j landed (M65): the kit's overlap and damage: hit shapes on a kit grid tested over the step's relative motion; one damage pipeline whose requests carry a source, damage tags, a position, a knockback and a crit flag and resolve once a step in sorted order through resistances, a shield and health, with heals and a death policy; contact damage, projectiles walled by tiles and static colliders, pickups with a magnet; the `loot_tables` kind and the `roll` cue action; one end-of-step despawn for the kit; the kit hash moved
**Amended by D-176:** the damage pipeline's resolution and the hit items' damage only; a request is a list of typed parts, each converted, penetrated and resisted on its own (a modifier's factor of 0 dropping a part as an immunity does) before one fall through the shield and health, and one `DamagedPart` per part goes out and into the trace before the hit's `Damaged`, which carries the parts' kinds; `Resist` takes modifiers by source and loses `From<Resist> for BTreeMap<String, f32>`; `Hitbox`'s and `ContactDamage`'s `damage` also takes a map of damage types, with `pen` and `convert`; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-177:** the window guard and the hitbox only; a periodic request (`with_periodic`) passes the target's invulnerability window, starts none and no flash, and `Damaged` and `DamagedPart` carry the flag; `Hitbox::with_window` makes a hitbox that starts disarmed and hits only while `arm` or an ability's `hitbox` payload keeps its window open, counted down by `hitbox_tick` after its run; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-201:** the hitbox's and the contact damage's targets only; their saved forms still leave entities out, and a game names them again after a load, `Hitbox::set_hit_targets` an activation's targets so it hits none twice and `ContactDamage::set_touches` each touch's seconds since its hit and since its meeting (`ContactTouch`), which keeps its schedule; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-202:** the projectile's walls only; a projectile chooses by a wall's physics layers, turning at one in its `bounce_layers` (every layer by default) while bounces are left, passing one in its `pass_layers` (none by default) and ending at the rest, with `ProjectileBounced`, `ProjectilePassed` and the wall on `ProjectileExpired`; walls are still tiles and static colliders, today's behaviour is the default, and the rest stand.
**Amended by D-208:** the contact damage's knockback only; `ContactDamage::with_push_always` (scene `push_always`) lands a touch's push on a target inside its invulnerability window too, the resolution pushing a request it ignores for the window, once a step, with no damage, flash or `Damaged`; the default contact pushes as before, and the rest stands.
**Amended by D-212:** the hitbox's debug shape only; `kit_debug_draw` draws a hitbox only while it is armed (`Hitbox::is_armed`), in a deep red, `[0.8, 0.02, 0.06, 1.0]`, 1.5 px wide; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-17
**Decision:** The kit catalogue's C (`docs/plans/1.0/w13-kit.md` §7), on core alone with no new dependency:

- **Hit shapes and the grid.** `HitShape` (a circle or a box, sizes in (0, 16384] as a `rigid_body`'s, written `{ "circle": r }` or `{ "aabb": [x, y] }`), `Hitbox` (shape, offset, damage, damage tags, filter, knockback, and the targets of its activation, not saved, which `rearm` clears), `Hurtbox` and the `Struck` event, the cue event `hit`, under `HitPlugin`. `hit_grid`, first of C's overlap systems, gathers each hurtbox whose entity is alive, not dead by its `Health` and not being despawned, with its swept bounds, into 64-px cells rebuilt each step; an entry past 64 cells goes on an oversized list that every query tests, and a query past `max(4 × entries, 64)` cells within the occupied bounds tests every entry, as core's index bounds its own. A shape stands at its entity's point plus its offset: a root with a `Position` sweeps from its `PrevPosition` to its `Position`, the step's motion, and an entity without one, or a child, stands at its `Transform.position`. Two shapes meet at the least `t` in `[0, 1]` at which the moving one overlaps the other with penetration above 0, a ray of their relative motion against their Minkowski sum, 0 when they start overlapping and never for a graze, so a bolt faster than a hurtbox per step and two paths crossing inside a step meet exactly, without substeps. A hitting shape's candidates land in (`t`, index) order; a hit spares the hitting entity, the entity it was spawned from (a crate-private `Emitter` derived at the flush) and its spawn chain's origin, passes its filter by the target's tags and lands once per activation; it sends `Struck` and, with damage above 0, queues a `DamageRequest` of its damage and tags from its `Source` or itself, at the target's centre, pushing the target away from it by its knockback.
- **The damage pipeline.** `DamageRequest` applies now (`apply`) or queues (`queue`) for `damage_resolve` in `Resolve`, which takes the step's requests stable-sorted by target index, then source index with none first, and then the heals the same way; a request queued outside the fixed step, a paused clock included, waits for the next step, since the queue is a resource and not an event. Resolution: an amount of 0, a target that is gone, being despawned, without `Health`, dead or inside its window is `Hit::Ignored` with no side effect; a `Resist` factor of 0 for a damage tag the request carries, ancestors included, is immunity, checked before any product, the matching factors otherwise multiplying the amount in `f64` clamped to `f32::MAX`, and a result of 0 is ignored too; a `Shield` takes what it can and waits its delay again before recharging; health falls by the rest; the window and the flash start; `Damaged`, still `Copy`, gains the request's source, position and crit; the outcome is `Killed`, `Hurt` or the new `Hit::Absorbed`, the shield's whole, and on the last two the request's knockback goes through `knock_back`. `damage(world, target, amount)` is `DamageRequest::new(target, amount).apply(world)`, so a plain hit keeps its outcome and its `Damaged::new(..)`. `HealRequest`, `heal` and `Healed` restore up to max, never on the dead and never into a shield, which `Shield::restore` fills. `Health::with_despawn_on_death(seconds)` (scene `despawn_on_death`) starts a delay at a kill that `health_tick` counts from the step after it, so the cue system has read the kill before the despawn.
- **One end-of-step despawn.** `kit_despawn`, in `React` after the spawner, the child spawns and the cues: each entity a step's items ended (a death past its delay, an ended projectile, a collected pickup, a finished `Lifetime`, an entity outside its `despawn_outside` region) is marked `Despawning` with every descendant and queued for `despawn_recursive` after the step's spawns from it, traced once with the item that ended it; `Lifetime` and `despawn_outside` list their ends here instead of queueing at once, and a cue's `despawn` marks its target's descendants too. A frame's later step skips what the kit ended: a hitting entity, a hurtbox, a pickup, a collector or a cue subject so marked does nothing.
- **The items.** `ContactDamage` (its own shape, an aura, or its entity's hurtbox's; a target met is hit at once and again each interval of the item's stepped seconds while met, and one unmet for a whole interval starts over); `Projectile` (pierce, range and bounces; its hitbox's hits in sweep order up to its first wall or its range, the hit past its pierce ending it; walls are tile layers, by a tiles-only `cast_shape`, and static colliders, found by an `overlap` of the sweep's bounds and met by the swept test with the face's normal, a start inside one met only moving in; kinematic and dynamic bodies are never walls; a bounce places it at the contact and reflects its movers' velocities, dropping the rest of the step; an end places it, stops its movers, marks it spent, sends `ProjectileExpired`, the cue event `expired`, and lists it for the despawn; `hitbox_tick` leaves a projectile's hitbox to it); `Pickup` (collected once by the first hurtbox its sweep meets whose tags pass its filter, sending `PickedUp`, the cue event `picked_up`, with the collector as the other entity; a magnet moving it toward the nearest collector in reach of that collector's `pickup_radius` stat, by `Write::Add` after the oscillator, the collectors binned per filter in cells as wide as the largest radius; a corpse kept by the default policy draws nothing). Each is a component with private fields, constructors that panic outside the data's bounds, serde through checked fields, a scene component under its bare name, a description, inspector rows, its debug shape and rustdoc with an example.
- **Drops.** `WeightedTable` and the `loot_tables` kind (`CuesPlugin`'s, its load checking every entry's prefab), and the cue action `roll`, a `loot_tables` ID or a table in place (`DataRef`), which draws its rolls from `KitRng`, now inserted by `CuesPlugin` as well, in the cue system's order, and spawns each prefab drawn at the occurrence's position from the subject; `FieldType::Data` describes the reference. The vocabulary's boundary stands: no conditions, and `roll` draws only from the table it names.
- **Cues.** Four kit events after A's: `hit` (the hitting entity, the target the other, its amount), `healed` (the heal's source the other), `picked_up` (the pickup, the collector the other) and `expired`; `damaged` and `killed` take the request's source as their other entity and its position when it has one.
- **Stats.** `stats_apply` writes `damage` into a `Hitbox` and a `ContactDamage`, its query's seventh and eighth columns; the magnet reads its collector's `pickup_radius`, the one stat an item reads from another entity; `projectile_speed` and `area` wait for W13k.
- **Order.** C's overlap systems join `Overlap` after `hit_grid` and before `despawn_outside`, `wrap` and `stay_in_view`, each `_if_present`, so they read the step's motion before a wrap rewrites it and an outside region judges a projectile where its wall left it; `pickup_magnet` joins `Move` after the oscillator and before the speed limit; `kit_despawn` follows the spawner, the child spawns and the cues, and `kit_trace_step` follows it. A test builds each of the 23 edges with its two plugins alone in both orders, and the card's recipe holds with the health, cue and overlap plugins built last in reverse. With `DefaultPlugins` then `KitPlugins::set()`, `fixed_update` resolves to `physics_prev_snapshot, kit_tick, lifetime_tick, health_tick, shield_tick, stats_apply, cooldown_tick, kit_intent, action_input, aim, kit_move, path_follow, linear_motion, oscillator, platformer_controller, top_down_mover, steering, follow, orbit, knockback, pickup_magnet, speed_limit, physics_step, kit_overlap, zone_tick, speed_limit_settle, hit_grid, hitbox_tick, contact_damage, projectile_tick, pickup_tick, despawn_outside, wrap, stay_in_view, kit_resolve, damage_resolve, kit_react, spawner_tick, spawn_children, cues, kit_despawn, kit_trace_step`; `post_update` is unchanged.
- **The bench.** `crates/tungsten-kit/benches/kit.rs` holds 31 rows at 1,000 and 10,000 entities, eight of them C's, each at one density with its area growing with its count: `hit_grid` (moving hurtboxes), `hitbox_tick` (each hitbox over one hurtbox it has already hit), `contact_damage` (each over one target, hit again each interval), `projectile_tick` (beside one static body, so the wall queries run), `pickup_tick` (no collector reached), `pickup_magnet` (one collector per ten pickups), `damage_resolve` (one request a step per target through its resistance and shield) and `shield_tick` (recharging). M65's run (2026-10-17, one quiet sitting in three blocking parts, the first rerun once) read every ratio within `D-174`'s bound, between 0.65 (`health_tick`) and 1.33 (`linear_motion`); `perf-runs/20261017-m65-kit-criterion/summary.txt` holds the numbers. `health_tick` costs 1.47 / 0.96 ns per entity at 1,000 / 10,000 against M64's 0.87 / 0.49 (1.69× / 1.96×), past the plan's 1.5× guard on the rows steps 1–2 touched, and is accepted as the death policy's cost: `Health` grew from 16 to 32 bytes (`despawn_on_death` and its countdown) and the tick reads each entity's death delay; 10,000 entities still tick in under 10 µs a step. `stats_apply` (1.38× / 1.36×) and `cues` (1.00× / 0.98×) stay within the guard.
- **The template.** The hazard's touch moves into data: its `hazard` component and `game::hazard_touch` go, and `prefabs/hazard.json` takes `contact_damage { damage 1, interval 0.5, knockback 420, filter team.player, shape circle 20 }` over the player's new `hurtbox { circle 8 }`, so a touch lands below 28 px between their points as before; the player's `stats` hold `pickup_radius` 96, and gameplay spawns three gems (`prefabs/gem.json`: a `pickup` with a magnet, an `oscillator` bob and a `picked_up` cue counting `gems` on the game's `Counters`). The template keeps no game Rust for either, and its text is unchanged.

**Why:** C stands on A's cues, counters, stats and `KitRng` (`D-173`) and on M's movers, `Steering` and `knock_back` (`D-174`), and W13k's effects and abilities queue through this pipeline and spawn these projectiles and hitboxes. A kit grid finds what the physics never sees (bodiless bolts and hurtboxes, w13-kit Q1), and testing each step's relative motion is exact and frame-rate-free where an overlap at the step's end lets a fast bolt pass. One pipeline gives hitboxes, contact damage, projectiles and a game's Rust one order and one set of defences, while `damage` stays the immediate path with no break (Q6). The plan's two critique rounds found nine seams the plan had not cited: `Source` names a chain's origin, not its shooter (the `Emitter`); an earlier despawn command drops a spawn from the despawned entity, so every kit despawn moved after the cues; a marked parent's unmarked child acted in a frame's second step (the subtree's mark); a zero-delay death listed in the killing step could despawn before the cue system read a late kill (counted from the step after); a resolution without `damage`'s zero guard; an `f32` product of finite factors that overflows to infinity and then to NaN against an immunity; a magnet that chose collectors collection refused, a scan per pickup over every collector, and an unbounded cell walk. The step 1 API critique found no concerns. `Damaged` stays `Copy` and gains no tags, since `Tags` holds a `Vec` (Q1); walls come from core's queries as they are, with no core change (Q2), and end projectiles only (Q3). Nothing the perf suite runs links the kit, so the evidence is criterion's: no row grows faster than its entities, and `health_tick`'s growth past the plan's guard is a larger component, not a scan.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-158`, `D-172` and `D-173` as their marker lines say; `D-154`'s queries, `D-170`'s spawn inheritance, `D-171`'s `DataRef` and `D-174`'s movers carry it.
- Additions for the freeze, no compile break: `api/tungsten-kit.txt` 994 lines added and none removed, `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt` 71 added (seven components, three shapes, one kind, the four cue events' fields and `roll`) and one changed, `health`'s events gaining `Healed`; the W4 ledger lists them. Behaviour a game may notice: `KitPlugins::set()`'s resolved schedule; seven more reserved scene names and the `loot_tables` kind; `picked_up`, `hit`, `healed` and `expired` reserved as kit cue events, so a `CuesOnEvent` of one of those names panics at build; `damage` ignoring a target the kit is despawning; `Lifetime`'s and `despawn_outside`'s despawns queued after the step's cue spawns, and the kit's despawns marking descendants, which a frame's later step then skips; `damaged` and `killed` occurrences with the source as their other entity.
- Pinned: the kit hash moves to `0x5a73_d4a5_51f4_0bfa`, its script spawning C's prefabs after the rest and its rows adding each entity's shield; the physics hashes, `DefaultPlugins`' schedule and the core, render and umbrella snapshots unchanged.
- Limits, in the known issues: hit shapes on a child stand at its last propagation with no sweep; a homer's steering moves it from rest after its end for the rest of its frame's steps; a bounce drops the rest of its step's travel; walls end projectiles only, and only tiles and static colliders; zones and the nearest search still see bodies or scan, off the grid; a bodiless mover is still stopped by no wall; a game's or the state stack's despawn earlier in the frame still drops the spawns from its entity.
- Open, in the plan's follow-ups: damage kinds on `Damaged` through a `Copy` handle; a static-only or layered wall query in core with W6b, and walls for plain hitboxes; a `spawned` cue event and `expired` from `Lifetime`; drop scatter, crit chances and a grid cell size per game; a child's shape swept from its root's motion; zones and the nearest search on the grid; HUD text for counters.

## D-176 — W13 damage types landed (M66): a hit as typed parts, resolved through conversion, penetration and `Resist` with modifiers and then once through the shield and health; the game's `damage_types` kind and its World's type table, no type named by the kit; `DamageKinds` on `Damaged` and on a new `DamagedPart`; the `damaged_part` cue event, a rule's `kind` filter and labels in a type's style; the kit bench's 33 rows; the kit hash moved
**Amended by D-177:** a `damage_types` definition's fields only; an entry takes `ailments`, a ladder of rungs inherited down the dotted tree as `label_style` is, which `ailment_feed` climbs from the top down for each typed, non-periodic part against the target's `ailment_threshold`; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-200:** `DamagedPart` and the type labels only; a part carries its request's `crit`, and `DamageKinds::label_style` reads the style a style-less `label` floats a part of those kinds in; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-18
**Decision:** The kit catalogue's DT (`docs/plans/1.0/w13-kit.md` §7), on core alone with no new dependency:

- **Parts.** `DamagePart` (an amount and its damage types, a `Tags`) and `DamageRequest::from_parts`; `DamageRequest::new` is one untyped part and `with_tags` gives the types each part without its own takes, so 0.85's request is a one-part request; `with_penetration` and `with_conversion` give the hit's own; `amount()` is the parts' sum before resistances, clamped.
- **The resolution**, at once or queued. Each part takes its own types, else the request's, and a part of 0 drops before any arithmetic; no part left, or a target gone, despawning, without `Health`, dead or in its window, is `Hit::Ignored` with no side effect. Conversion: the hit's own rules, then the source's `Conversion` (the request's source, a spawned bolt's chain origin), in one pass, each share a new part of its rule's type alone, a part's matching shares past 1 normalized to sum to 1 and never converted again; parts of equal types then merge in first-appearance order, in `f64`, clamped. Penetration: a part's points are the hit's and the source's matching `Penetration` entries summed, capped at 1. `Resist`, per part: a matching factor of 0, a base or a modifier's, drops the part before any product; a factor in (0, 1) gains the part's points, capped at 1; the factors' product runs in `f64`, clamped, and a result of 0 drops the part. The parts' total then falls once through the shield and health, with one window and one flash; one `DamagedPart` per part goes out in part order, then one `Damaged` with the total and the parts' kinds together, and the trace records each part, the hit and any kill. A one-part request with no conversion, no penetration and no modifier computes M65's arithmetic exactly.
- **The `damage_types` kind** (`HealthPlugin`'s). Entries keyed by declared tags, each a `damage_type` definition whose fields inherit down the dotted tree (`label_style` now, W13k's `ailments` later). A type that two entries define fails the load, naming both, and so does room past 64: when the types the World's table lacks outnumber its bits left, every checked entry holding one fails, naming its first, so the failure never hangs on which name sorts past 64.
- **The type table and `DamageKinds`.** The table, a crate-private World resource, takes its types' sorted names as bits at its first merge, appends what a later merge finds, and keeps a removed type's bit, so a mask queued before a reload keeps its meaning; every path that builds or reads a mask merges first (each `apply`, each `damage_resolve` run with requests, each cue run with occurrences), with no core file; a name past 64 that no check saw gets no bit and one warning. `DamageKinds` is a `Copy` `u64` with `NONE`, `is_empty`, `has(world, name)` and `names(world)`; a part's kinds are every table type its types are or lie under, found by searching each type's dotted prefixes.
- **`Resist`'s modifiers.** `add_modifier(tag, source, Modifier)`, `remove_modifiers(source)` and `base(tag)`; a factor is `Stats`' formula, one crate-private function both now call, over its base (1 for a tag a modifier alone lists), clamped to [0, 1000], and a base of 0 stays immune whatever its modifiers. Its save reads and writes 0.85's bare map for factors without modifiers and `{ "base", "modifiers" }` for the rest; `From<Resist> for BTreeMap<String, f32>` goes, since it would drop modifiers in silence.
- **Typed hit items.** `Hitbox` and `ContactDamage` take `damage` as a number or a map of declared damage types to amounts, in name order (a hand-written visitor keeps the field path; an all-zero map and `tags` beside a map fail the load), with `pen` and `convert`; `with_parts`, `with_penetration`, `with_conversion`, `parts`, `penetration` and `conversion`. A map's parts are base parts that no stat writes: each hit splits the item's damage, which the `damage` stat writes as before, among them by their proportions, 0 a trigger. A save adds `parts`, `pen` and `convert`. `strike` builds the typed request; a projectile's hits take its hitbox's parts; the `penetration` and `conversion` scene components sit on a hit's source.
- **Cues.** The kit event `damaged_part` (the entity, its source the other, the part's amount and kinds), read before `damaged`, so in each of the reactor's passes every part runs before the step's totals and `KitRng` draws in that order; a rule's `kind` filter (`{ "all", "any", "none" }` of declared damage types or their ancestors, read as a tag filter reads tags) on `damaged`, `killed` (the hit's kinds) and `damaged_part` only; `CueAction::type_label()`, `{ "label": {} }`, a `damaged_part` cue's only, floats the part in its definition's style (its deepest type, the first by name at one depth, else that type's nearest ancestor that sets one, from the current entries), and a part whose types set none skips its label with its trace reason.
- **Descriptions.** `FieldType::Damage`; the components `penetration` and `conversion`, the shapes `conversion_rule` and `damage_type`, the kind `damage_types`.
- **The bench.** `damage_resolve_typed` (two typed parts, half of one converted, with penetration, through two resistances, one under a modifier) and `cues_typed` (one `damaged_part` occurrence per entity with its kinds, an entity cue and one rule's kind filter) join, 33 rows.
- **The template.** No change: its hazard stays an untyped `contact_damage`, and its test's `Damaged::new(entity, 1.0, false)` still equals what an untyped hit sends.

**Why:** The owner asked on 2026-10-17 for damage types between C and E, and W13k's ailment ladder reads their kinds and feeds its buckets from `DamagedPart` amounts. The types live in the game's data (the recipes declare a fantasy and a sci-fi set), and a scan of the kit's sources with a negative control finds none of them named there, so the kit stays genre-free. A `Copy` mask keeps `Damaged` `Copy`, and per-part resolution before one fall keeps one window and one `Damaged` per hit. The step 1 API critique raised no concerns. The criterion sitting read every row's per-entity ratio at 0.61–1.25; `damage_resolve` reads 1.65× and 1.67× M65's per-entity medians, the cost of a request's parts, its `DamagedPart` and its mask, under the plan's 2.0× bound, and `hitbox_tick`, `contact_damage`, `projectile_tick` and `cues` read 0.91–1.09×.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-175` and `D-173` as their marker lines say; `D-171`'s data kinds and staged loads and `D-172`'s tags, recipes and coverage test carry it.
- For the freeze: `api/tungsten-kit.txt` 299 lines added and 4 removed, the two of `From<Resist> for BTreeMap<String, f32>` at both of `Resist`'s paths, the one break; `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt` 31 added and 8 changed (`health`'s events, `hitbox`'s and `contact_damage`'s `damage` and `tags`, `resist`'s summary and field, `cue_action`'s `label`); the W4 ledger lists them. Behaviour a game may notice: every hit sends a `DamagedPart` and traces `damaged_part` before its `damaged`; `damaged_part` is a kit cue event, so a `CuesOnEvent` of that name panics at build; `damage_types`, `penetration` and `conversion` are reserved names.
- Pinned: the kit hash moves to `0x2288_2ee7_97d4_aba9`, its script spawning a golem under a spellblade and a mainframe under a hacker's payloads after the rest and adding resistance modifiers at frame 100, its rows adding each resistance as its inspector row (the umbrella has no `serde_json`); the physics hashes, `DefaultPlugins`' and `KitPlugins::set()`'s resolved schedules and the core, render and umbrella snapshots unchanged.
- Limits, in the known issues: a World holds 64 damage types, and a type past them that no check saw gets no bit; a source despawned before its request resolves lends no penetration or conversion; `Struck` and the `hit` cue carry no kinds.
- Open, in the plan's follow-ups: kinds on `Struck` and the `hit` cue; a `label_styles` kind; the descriptions' placeholder tag `damage.fire`.

## D-177 — W13k landed (M67): effects, abilities, curves, offers and levels as data kinds and scene components, every kit payload queued for one `effect_resolve` after the damage; a periodic flag that passes the invulnerability window and starts none; the ailment ladder on `damage_types` entries, fed by typed parts and climbed from the top down; the hitbox window; the cue events `effect_applied`, `effect_expired`, `ability_used` and `level_up` and the actions `effect` and `ability`; the kit bench's 39 rows; the kit hash moved
**Amended by D-197:** the effects' payloads and holds only; an effect gains `restore` (a stat's base raised by an amount up to another stat's value, `{ "stat", "amount", "max" }`, its names checked at parse) and `suppressed_by` (tags that hold a running instance: its time and period wait, no payload lands); the rest stands.
**Amended by D-200:** an effect's source and a rung's fields only; `Effects::set_source` names a loaded instance's source again, the saved form still leaving it out; a rung takes `blocked_by`, tags whose carrier skips it while its bucket fills on, and a grant pending in the step counts as carried, by ancestor, for `requires` and `blocked_by` alike; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-18
**Decision:** The kit catalogue's E (`docs/plans/1.0/w13-kit.md` §7), on core alone with no new dependency:

- **Curves.** `Curve`: `(x, y)` points, at least one, every number finite, the `x`s strictly increasing; `at(x)` is linear between points and held at the ends. The `curves` kind and the `curve` shape, registered by whichever of `EffectsPlugin` and `LevelPlugin` builds first; a `DataRef<Curve>` by ID or in place. A curve that scales an effect has every `y` at least 0, refused at the effect's load, and what it scales runs in `f64` clamped to `±f32::MAX`.
- **Effects.** `Effect`, the `effects` kind's entry or inline: `duration` (absent infinite, 0 instant), `period`, a payload (`damage`, a number or a map of declared types, and `heal`), `stack` (`limit`, `refresh` as `Refresh::{Refresh, Keep, Extend}`, `expire` as `Expire::{Clear, RemoveOne}`), `modifiers` on the target's `Stats` and `resist` on its `Resist`, per stack, under the source `effect#<serial>`, `grant` and `blocked_by` tags, `scale` (a curve at the request's scale, a factor on what `Scaled` names: the duration, the periodic payload, the `add` and `pct` modifiers) and `on_expire`; `with_damage(amount)` and `with_typed_damage(kind, amount)` build the payload, an amount of 0 removing the type. `Effects`, scene name `effects`: entries applied at the entity's first `effect_tick` with no source, and the instances (`EffectInstance`: the effect, its source, stacks, time left, factor and serial); its save holds the instances, the next serial and the granted-tag table, never a source. `EffectRequest::new(target, effect)` with `with_source` and `with_scale`, `apply` at once returning `Applied::{Applied, Stacked, Blocked, Ignored}`, or `queue`; `end_effect` ends an instance early, its `EffectExpired` marked `ended`.
- **The resolution** (`effect_resolve`, in `kit_resolve` after `damage_resolve` and `ailment_feed`): the step's `on_expire` requests first, in the order the tick queued them, then the rest sorted by target then source, none first; every kit path queues (a fresh list, an expiry's transition, the cue action, the ladder, an ability's payload, a chosen offer), so no kit payload lands in `kit_tick`, a lethal one starts its death delay where a hit does whatever the plugins' order, and `apply` is the game's immediate exception, as `DamageRequest::apply` is. A target gone, despawning or dead is `Ignored`; one carrying a `blocked_by` tag is `Blocked`; both traced. A first application makes the instance with `left` the duration times the factor (the curve's value at the request's scale, 1 without one), places its modifiers scaled in `f64` and clamped, grants its tags into `Tags` (inserted if absent), the table counting each tag's live granters and whether the entity carried it before, lands a periodless payload once (`DamageRequest::apply` with `with_periodic(true)` from the instance's source, or `HealRequest::apply`) and sends `EffectApplied` with the stacks; a repeat stacks below the limit, keeps the count at it, follows `refresh`, takes the request's factor and rebuilds the modifiers as `stacks` copies. An effect whose scaled duration is 0 lands its payload, queues its `on_expire` and keeps no instance, so its modifiers and tags do nothing. What an instance placed stays as placed under a reload (its granted list, its modifiers, its duration); its period's payload reads the current definition.
- **The tick** (`effect_tick`, in `kit_tick` after `stats_apply`): a fresh `Effects`' entries are queued at its first run; each instance's `left` counts down by the step and its period by `min(dt, left)`, each elapse queueing the payload (`DamageRequest::from_parts` of the map times the factor, periodic, from the source, no position, knockback or crit; a `HealRequest` for `heal`), at most 16 a step, a capped instance starting its next period whole; at 0, `clear` removes the instance, its modifiers by source and its granted tags (a tag leaves at count 0 unless the entity carried it before), sends `EffectExpired` and queues `on_expire`; `remove_one` drops a stack, rebuilds the modifiers and restarts, ending as `clear` at none. A modifier placed in step N reaches the movers, health and cooldowns through step N+1's `stats_apply`, and an expiry comes after its step's, so an effect of k steps moves its consumer for k.
- **The periodic flag.** `DamageRequest::with_periodic` and `is_periodic`: a periodic request passes the window guard, `Health::hurt` starts no window for it and `resolve` no flash; `Damaged::periodic` and `DamagedPart::periodic` with `with_periodic`, false from `new`, so an untyped one-part hit's `Damaged` still equals `Damaged::new(entity, amount, killed)`.
- **Abilities.** `Ability`, the `abilities` kind's entry or inline: a `Trigger::{Manual, Action(slot), Interval(seconds)}`, a cost in a stat's base, a cooldown counted at the entity's `cooldown_rate`, charges (`max`, `refill`, `all`), an `AbilityTarget::{Itself, Aim, Nearest { filter, range }, Random { filter, range }}` and payloads in order (`Payload::{Spawn { prefab, speed, offset }, Effect, Impulse, Hitbox}`); the load checks a spawn's prefab ID and, with a speed above 0, that the prefab holds a `linear_motion`. `Abilities`, scene name `abilities`: entries named by ID or `#<index>`, unique in one component (a duplicate ID fails the load, `add` of an owned name replaces); `names`, `ready`, `cooldown_left`, `charges` and `refill`. `use_ability(world, entity, name) -> Used::{Used, NotReady, NoCharge, NoCost, NoTarget, NoAbility}`: the target first, `nearest` and `random` over the eligible matches only (alive, not despawning, not dead by a `Health`), the direction from the entity's `Aim`, else its `Intent`'s aim; no target, or no direction for a payload that needs one, spends nothing; then the cost, a charge and the cooldown are spent, the payloads run (`spawn_prefab_from` the entity with a `LinearMotion` along the direction when `speed` is above 0; an `EffectRequest` on the target or the entity, from the entity, queued; `knock_back`; the entity's own `Hitbox` armed for the seconds) and `AbilityUsed { entity, name, target, position }` goes out. `ability_tick` (in `kit_intent` after `action_input` and `aim`): per entry, the cooldown, the refill (one charge per `refill` seconds, or all of them together with `all`) and the interval; an `action` trigger uses on `Intent::pressed`, an `interval` at most once a step; `KitRng` draws in entity order.
- **The hitbox window.** `Hitbox::with_window(seconds)`: a windowed hitbox starts disarmed; `arm` rearms it (every target may be hit once more) and opens its window; `is_armed` and `window` read it. `hitbox_tick` skips a disarmed hitbox and counts the window down after its run, so a window of N steps hits for N; a projectile's hitbox runs as before. Scene field `window`; the save adds `window` and `armed_left`.
- **Levels and offers.** `Level`, scene name `level`: a counter, the entity's `Counters` or the game's, a curve from level to the total count that reaches it (its last `x`, floored, the top) and optional `Offers` (`count` distinct entries drawn by weight without replacement, each an `Offer` giving an effect or an ability); `level` (1 at the start), `offers`, `choose(index)` and `chosen`. `level_tick` (in `kit_react` before `cues`): a choice made since the last tick applies first (an effect queued on the entity with no source; an ability added to its `Abilities`, inserted if absent, or an owned one refilled with its cooldown cleared), then at most 16 level-ups a step, the rest carried, each raising the level, sending `LevelUp { entity, level }` and drawing offers that replace the pending ones.
- **Ailments.** `ailments` on a `damage_types` definition: rungs `{ effect, needs, requires, chance, window }` written from the lowest up (`AilmentRung`), `needs` a fraction of the threshold that requires `window`, the ladder inherited down the dotted tree as `label_style` is. `AilmentThreshold`, scene name `ailment_threshold`: `(flat + of_max_health × Health::max) × ailment_threshold_mult`, the stat read when present and floored at 0, 1 without it, not well-known; `AilmentBuckets`, a runtime component the feed inserts, saved by type name with inspector rows. `ailment_feed` (in `kit_resolve` after `damage_resolve`): each `DamagedPart` that is not periodic and has kinds fills the bucket of the entry whose ladder its deepest type resolves to, then walks the rungs from the last to the first: `needs` passes when the threshold is above 0 and the fill reaches `needs × threshold` (a threshold of 0, or no component, skips those rungs), `requires` when the target carries every tag, the tags of effects the feed queued for it earlier in the run counted, and `chance` draws `KitRng` only then, in event order; the first rung that passes queues its effect from the part's source at scale `fill / threshold` (1 without a threshold), empties the bucket and ends the walk. `ailment_tick` (in `kit_tick` after `stats_apply` and `effect_tick`): each bucket drains `threshold / window` a second, the window the ladder's first `needs` rung's (a ladder without one never drains), and an empty bucket leaves the map.
- **Cues.** The kit events `effect_applied` (the effect's source the other, the stacks the amount), `effect_expired`, `ability_used` (the ability's target the other, at the use's position) and `level_up` (the level the amount), read after the kit's earlier events and before each `CuesOnEvent`; the actions `effect` (`"burning"` or `{ "id", "target" }`, the definition in place allowed, queued on the subject from the other or on the other from the subject for the next `effect_resolve`, a missing other skipped with its reason) and `ability` (`"wand"` or `{ "name", "op": use|refill, "target" }`, run at once in `kit_react`); `CueAction::effect`, `ability` and `refill_ability`. The trace records each of the four events, the feed's trigger as `ailment` and a blocked or ignored request as a skip.
- **Descriptions.** The components `effects`, `abilities`, `level` and `ailment_threshold`; the shapes `curve`, `effect`, `effect_stack`, `effect_scale`, `stat_modifier`, `resist_modifier`, `modifier`, `effect_action`, `ability`, `ability_trigger`, `ability_cost`, `ability_charges`, `ability_target`, `ability_payload`, `spawn_payload`, `ability_action`, `offers`, `offer`, `offer_gives` and `ailment_rung`; the kinds `effects`, `abilities`, `curves` and `offers`; `damage_type`'s `ailments` and `hitbox`'s `window`.
- **The set and the edges.** `KitPlugins::set()` adds `EffectsPlugin` (which registers the ladder), `AbilitiesPlugin` and `LevelPlugin` after the pickup plugin and before `KitDebugPlugin`. Seven within-phase edges, each `if_present`: `effect_tick` and `ailment_tick` after `stats_apply`, `ability_tick` after `action_input` and `aim`, `ailment_feed` and `effect_resolve` after `damage_resolve`, `level_tick` before `cues`; `ailment_tick` after `effect_tick` and `effect_resolve` after `ailment_feed` inside `EffectsPlugin`; the umbrella pins each edge with its two plugins alone in either order and the set reversed.
- **The bench.** `effect_tick`, `effect_resolve`, `ailment_tick`, `ailment_feed`, `ability_tick` and `level_tick` join, 39 rows.
- **The template.** The hazard's `hit` cue applies `slowed` to the player, a 1.5 s effect in the manifest's `data.effects` that takes half its `move_speed`, which the player's `stats` now declares at 240 so the mover follows the stat.

**Why:** E is the catalogue's last candidate before the freeze and the largest API of the program (`w13-kit.md` §9): with it a game composes status effects, abilities and progression in data, and the four fixtures prove twelve recipes (a burning slime, a wand on an interval, a volley with ammo, gems into levels and an offer, a chill-then-freeze ladder with a thaw immunity, a poison by chance, a corrupting wither, a cannon that reloads, a pulse once per window, a regen, a haste, an inline effect and a level that grants an ability) with no game Rust. The plan's two critique rounds set the model's shape: the ladder climbs from the top so a full bucket freezes what a half-full one chilled; the granted-tag table counts live granters so overlapping grants release cleanly; every kit payload resolves after the damage so plugin order never moves a death; `effect_tick` after `stats_apply` so a one-step effect moves its consumer for one step; the period counts inside the lifetime so no payload lands after the end. The step 1 API critique found two save inconsistencies and a scaled-to-zero path, each fixed before step 2, and no shape tied to one genre; the coverage scan still finds no fixture damage type in the kit's sources. The criterion sitting read every row's per-entity ratio at 0.59–1.23 over 39 rows, the six new rows at 0.96–1.05; `damage_resolve`, `damage_resolve_typed`, `hitbox_tick` and `health_tick` read 1.00, 0.99, 1.02 and 0.99 of M66's per-entity medians, so the periodic flag and the armed check cost the kept rows at most 2 %.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-172`, `D-173`, `D-175` and `D-176` as their marker lines say; `D-171`'s data kinds and staged loads and `D-174`'s `Aim` and `knock_back` carry it.
- For the freeze: `api/tungsten-kit.txt` 1,488 lines added and none removed; `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt` 146 added and none removed (four components, twenty shapes, four kinds, the four cue events' fields, the two actions, `damage_type`'s `ailments` and `hitbox`'s `window`); the W4 ledger lists them, no break: `Damaged`, `DamagedPart` and `TraceKind` are `#[non_exhaustive]` and `Hitbox` grows behind builders. Behaviour a game may notice: `KitPlugins::set()`'s resolved schedule gains `effect_tick` and `ailment_tick` after `cooldown_tick`, `ability_tick` after `aim`, `ailment_feed` and `effect_resolve` after `damage_resolve` and `level_tick` before `cues`; `effects`, `abilities`, `level` and `ailment_threshold`, and the kinds `effects`, `abilities`, `curves` and `offers`, are reserved names (`D-172`); `effect_applied`, `effect_expired`, `ability_used` and `level_up` are kit cue events, so a `CuesOnEvent` of one of those names panics at build; a `damage_types` entry with a malformed `ailments` fails the load; a `Hitbox` save may hold `window` and `armed_left`.
- Pinned: the kit hash moves to `0x6fbd_8f24_2114_15a5`, its script pressing `attack` and `fire`, spawning an ammo orb and sending a cold hit on the golem and a digital one on the mainframe, its rows adding each entity's effects, buckets, level and abilities; the physics hashes, `DefaultPlugins`' resolved schedule and the core, render and umbrella snapshots unchanged.
- Limits, in the known issues: a periodic hit ignores the window and starts none; an effect's source is not saved; the `effect` action on `hit` runs for a hit the window ignored; an inline ability is `#<index>`; a level-up replaces pending offers, and a chosen offer's effect lands a step later; a bucket drains the step after its fill.
- Open, in the plan's follow-ups and the backlog: an `apply` list on hit items; an `event` form on an ability's trigger; kinds on `Struck` and the `hit` cue; a saved effect source; a `jump_speed` stat for the platformer controller.

## D-178 — W6b landed (M68): collision layers and masks on `RigidBody`; two bodies touch only when each one's mask holds a layer the other is in; a sensor and a spatial filter see what their own mask holds; tiles in layer 0 meeting every layer; a changed layer value wakes as a moved body or support does
**Date:** 2026-10-19
**Decision:** `RigidBody` gains two public fields, `layers: u32` (the layers the body is in, one bit per layer, 0 to 31) and `mask: u32` (the layers it meets), with `RigidBody::DEFAULT_LAYERS` (`1`, layer 0) and `RigidBody::ALL_LAYERS` (`u32::MAX`). Every constructor sets `layers: DEFAULT_LAYERS, mask: ALL_LAYERS`; `with_layers` and `with_mask` set them, as `RigidBodyBundle::with_layers` and `with_mask` do, and `RigidBody::meets` states the rule. A collider without a `RigidBody` and every solid tile are in layer 0 and meet every layer, so layer 0 is the world: a body whose mask lacks it passes tiles and every body left at the defaults.

- **Contacts.** Two bodies touch only when `a.layers & b.mask != 0 && b.layers & a.mask != 0`. A pair that does not meet is never admitted by the pair build or a repair, and the safety-net sweep and the arrival pass skip it, so it makes no contact, `CollisionEvent`, island, wake or clamp; `D-064`'s and `D-092`'s passes keep their rules over the pairs that meet.
- **Sensors** report a body or a tile in a layer their own mask holds, whatever the other's mask, since a sensor pushes nothing; sensors still detect no sensor, and a sensor's own `layers` matter to queries alone. A sensor whose mask comes to hold a body's layer reports that body at that step if they overlap.
- **Queries.** `SpatialFilter::with_mask(u32)` keeps the bodies, sensors and tiles in a layer the mask holds, replacing an earlier mask; the default holds every layer, so today's filters see what they saw, and sensors still need `with_sensors(true)`.
- **Changes wake.** A sleeper whose `layers` or `mask` changed wakes its island at the next step, as an external `Position` write does, and a static or tile support whose layers or mask changed counts as a changed support (`D-156`), so the sleepers near it wake: a door that stops meeting the bodies on it lets them fall. Both compare the values recorded at sleep and at support time.
- **The fast path.** The gather writes each proxy's layers and mask into an array beside the proxies, as `pair_flags` is (`D-080`), so `Proxy` keeps its 80 bytes, and notes whether any proxy or sensor is off the defaults. Only then do the pair build and repair, the sweep and the arrival pass test the rule, so a world without layers runs the same loops with one predictable branch; the sensor pass and the queries test their mask always, one AND per candidate.
- **Data.** The `rigid_body` scene component takes `layers` and `mask`, lists of layer numbers (`{ "kind": "sensor", "shape": …, "layers": [3], "mask": [2] }`): a missing `layers` is `[0]`, a missing `mask` every layer, an empty list none, and a number above 31 or a non-integer fails the load at its field's path (`layers[0]`).

**Why:** `D-132` made layers and masks a Must for the acceptance game: every sensor (a bolt that reports only enemies, a gem only the player), the mover's casts and bolts that pass over walls, and `D-154` left them to W6b. The plan's open questions set the shape (`docs/plans/archive/1.0/phase6-milestone-68-layers-audio-buses.md` §4). The fields go on the body component (Q1): a separate component would split archetypes when a game adds it at runtime, reordering the solve (`D-066`), and a scene entry would need a second component for one setting of its body. Contacts take both masks, Box2D's rule (Q2), because a contact pushes both bodies, while a sensor and a query use their own mask alone. Tiles are fixed at layer 0 (Q3) and layers are numbers in data (Q4), names joining later as strings in the same lists without a break. A changed value wakes (Q16) because a sleeper frozen against a body it no longer meets would hang in the air, the failure `D-156` fixed for supports. The milestone's sitting (0.87 against the candidate twice in one sitting, five runs a side; `perf-runs/20261019-m68-layers-audio/`, a first sitting set aside because a stuck editor file watcher held a core through it) reads no owned metric of any row `regressed` in either direct pair once one `particles` drift metric's rerun cleared, and neither A/A pair reads one `regressed` or `improved`: `physics` `physics_step` p50 6.150 → 6.248 and 6.174 → 6.214 ms, `physics-sparse` 3.682 → 3.730 and 3.698 → 3.732 ms, all `unchanged`, with the first-run digests matching on all eight rows. A world at the defaults pays the gather's layer array and a branch per admitted pair, 0.04–0.10 ms on `physics`'s 8,000 balls against the plan's estimate of 0.01–0.03 ms.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-033`, `D-065`, `D-154` and `D-156` as their marker lines say. `D-064`, `D-066`, `D-080`, `D-082` and `D-092` stand over the pairs that meet.
