- Break, a W6b row in the break ledger: `RigidBody` gains two public fields, so a struct literal outside its constructors needs them or `..RigidBody::dynamic()`; the workspace's three moved to the constructors (example 01's two to `RigidBody::dynamic().with_restitution(BALL_RESTITUTION)`), so `D-154`'s "without a break" did not hold. `RigidBody` stays exhaustive until W4b's sweep (Q5).
- Unchanged: the determinism hash `0x088e_c07a_73c1_b168`, the containment hash `0xaee2_72e0_1ffc_4e4c`, the kit hash `0x6fbd_8f24_2114_15a5`, `Proxy`'s 80 bytes (a test pins it), `DefaultPlugins`' schedule and the kit's scene shapes; no new system, stage or dependency.
- Tests: the 15 `layers_` tests in `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/physics/{step,query,plugin}.rs`, among them the pair oracle over random masks (`layers_persistent_pairs_match_fresh_contacts_with_masks`), a fall through tiles and a static box at a speed the safety net sweeps, the arrival pass's clamps against a wall met and not met, a kinematic press that wakes only what it meets, and the sleeper and support wakes.
- Limits, in `docs/known-issues.md` ("Collision layers and masks"): a tile layer has no layers of its own; layers in data are numbers; a pair that does not meet still costs its broadphase candidate test in a layered world.
- Open, in the backlog: a tile layer's own layers (M68 Q3) and layer names in data (M68 Q4). The kit's movers mask their casts through `with_filter` in code; a data field waits for a prefab that needs one.

## D-179 — W6c landed (M68): every sound plays on the `Music` or `Effects` bus, whose volumes the player sets in `Settings`; a play returns a `Voice` that fades, sets or stops; a voice limit per sound; linear gain ramps, a clamp and stereo channel mapping in a mixer that never allocates; a 1,024-command ring whose overflow waits in order, with a latch that resynchronizes a stalled callback and rebuilds a lost device
**Amended by D-184:** the bus volumes' defaults only; `apply_settings` sends a bus's set volume, else the one `tungsten.json`'s `audio.bus_volumes` ships, 1.0 when it ships none, and startup queues each that is set or shipped below 1; the rest stand.
**Superseded by D-193:** the listening host only; the UI lab is deleted, and the owner's listening and device-switch checks run in the template's settings; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-19
**Decision:** Core holds the vocabulary, the data and the settings; the umbrella holds the ring, the mixer, the limits' cap, the latch and the rebuild, so core gains no `cpal` or `rtrb` type (`D-007`, `D-018`). No new dependency, thread, system or stage.

- **Buses and voices** (`tungsten_core::audio`, re-exported at the crate root). `AudioBus`, `#[non_exhaustive]`: `Music` and `Effects`, the default; `AudioBus::ALL`; `name()` gives `"music"` and `"effects"`, the serde names. `Voice`: one playing instance, `Copy`, `Eq`, `Hash`, opaque; an `AudioCommands` numbers its voices from 1 and never reuses one, and a command naming a voice that ended, was dropped or never started does nothing. `MAX_VOICES` is 64. `AudioCommand` turns `#[non_exhaustive]` and derives `PartialEq`; `Play` turns `#[non_exhaustive]` and gains `voice` and `fade` (seconds from 0 to its volume); `StopVoice { voice, fade }`, `SetVoiceVolume { voice, volume, fade }` and `SetBusVolume { bus, volume }` join, each `#[non_exhaustive]`, so code outside core builds commands through `AudioCommands`. `play`, `play_looping` and `play_with` return the `Voice` they start; `play_faded` carries its fade on one `Play`, so a fade-in never comes apart from its start; `stop_voice`, `set_voice_volume` and `set_bus_volume` join. A volume or a number of seconds that is not finite or is negative is 0, and a bus volume is capped at 1.
- **Data and settings.** A manifest sound takes `bus` (`"music"` or `"effects"`, effects by default) and `max_voices` (1 to 64, 64 by default); an unknown bus or a limit outside the range fails the load with the sound's ID. `SoundEntry` and `ResolvedSound` gain both and turn `#[non_exhaustive]`; `SoundRegistry` keeps them per sound (`set_bus`, `get_bus`, `set_max_voices` clamped to 1..=64, `get_max_voices`; a sound registered in code is on effects with 64), and `load_sounds` sets them. `Settings::bus_volume` and `set_bus_volume` (0..=1, a value that is not finite ignored); the file holds `"bus_volumes": { "music": 0.5 }` with only the set buses, an unknown bus name ignored whatever its value and a known one outside 0..=1 or not a number making the file invalid. `apply_settings` sends every bus's volume, a set one or 1.0, and startup queues the set ones beside the master volume.
- **The mixer**, built on the main thread and owned by the callback: the sounds as stereo PCM at the device's rate with their bus and limit, 64 voice slots, the two buses' gains and the master gain; nothing in the callback allocates. A voice plays at its volume × its bus's volume × the master volume. Gains ramp linearly per frame: a fade-in from 0 over its `fade`, a voice's volume change over its fade and at least 10 ms, the master's and a bus's over 10 ms, a stop to 0 over its fade and at least 10 ms, after which the voice ends and frees its slot (`Stop` and `StopAll` take the 10 ms ramp); a stopping voice takes no command and counts toward no limit. At its sound's limit a play ends the sound's oldest one-shot at once, or its oldest loop when none of its one-shots plays; then it takes a free slot, else a stopping voice's, else the oldest one-shot's of any sound, and with 64 playing loops it is dropped. A sound with no samples plays nothing. The mix is clamped to [-1, 1]; stereo goes to a wider device's first two channels, the rest silent, and a mono device takes the mean.
- **The ring and its policy** (`D-034`). The ring holds 1,024 commands, allocated once. The audio stage keeps at most a sound's `max_voices` plays of it per frame, the last ones, and the harness's capture list takes the same commands. A command that does not fit the ring waits, in order, ahead of newer ones; `AudioSystem::flush`, public and non-blocking, moves waiting commands as far as the ring has room, `send` flushes first, and the stage flushes every frame. While 4,096 or more wait, a new one-shot play is dropped and counted; every other command still waits. Past 65,536 waiting, the outbox clears the queue and queues `StopAll`, then its latch's master and bus volumes and its loops from their start under their own voices, the waiting one-shots counted as dropped; the counts are logged at most once a second. A callback takes at most 1,024 commands before it renders.
- **The latch** applies the mixer's rules to the commands the outbox admits, in order: a stop or `StopAll` removes loops, a play of a sound whose latched loops fill its limit removes the oldest, a loop's play with 64 latched is dropped, a one-shot is never latched, and the master, bus and voice volumes keep their last values. Under the mixer's limits no one-shot's end decides a loop's fate, so the latch's loops are the mixer's playing loops, with no channel back from the callback.
- **The lost device.** The error callback only sets flags: `DeviceNotAvailable` and `StreamInvalidated` mark the stream lost, and every other kind (an ALSA underrun among them) adds to a count the stage logs at most once a second. On a lost stream the stage logs once, clears the waiting queue, drops the stream, so one callback thread runs at a time, opens the default device again with `AudioSystem::init` and replays the latch into the new ring; while the stream is lost, commands change the latch and nothing waits, and a failed rebuild tries again every two seconds.
- **The listening host.** The UI lab (example 05) plays `music_main` faded in over a second on entering play and out over half a second on leaving it; its settings screen gains Music and Effects sliders, applied live and saved by Back, an effects change playing `sfx_blip`; `audio_toggle_music` (M) crossfades the music into a new voice from its start over two seconds. The root manifest puts `music_main` on music and `sfx_blip` on effects.

**Why:** `D-132` made audio buses (the settings screen's music and effects volumes, the boss's music change) and a voice limit per sound (bursts of hits) Musts for the acceptance game, voice handles and fades a Should, and left the audio policy for `D-034`'s ring to W6c; `D-159` left the volume per bus to it. The known issues gave it the `f32` and channel-mapping P3, a `Play` that allocated in the callback, full rings that dropped commands, the lost-device P2 and the clamp the owner had left open: the clamp is a guard against wrapping on fixed-point backends, not an effect, so `D-029`'s no-DSP rule stands otherwise (Q9). The plan's two critique rounds shaped the policy (`docs/plans/archive/1.0/phase6-milestone-68-layers-audio-buses.md` §9): a callback cannot tell the stage when a one-shot ends, so the limit ends one-shots before loops and makes the loops' fate decidable from the commands; an admitted command is never dropped, since a dropped `StopAll` would part the latch from the mixer, so the queue's hard bound resynchronizes instead; a producer that keeps refilling the ring cannot hold the output back; a fade travels on its `Play`; and a deferred send needs a public pump. The same sitting covers the frame loop's audio stage, which now caps, sends and flushes every frame: `integrated` `total` p50 8.040 → 8.184 and 8.040 → 8.192 ms and p95 8.452 → 8.592 and 8.450 → 8.600 ms, `unchanged`, and no owned metric of any row reads `regressed` after the one drift rerun.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-029`, `D-034` and `D-159` as their marker lines say. `D-027`, `D-028` and `D-053` stand: sounds stay decoded at load and session-static.
- Breaks, W6c rows in the break ledger: `AudioCommand` and its `Play` turn `#[non_exhaustive]`, `Play` gaining `voice` and `fade` and the enum three variants (the workspace's four exhaustive `Play` patterns, the kit's doctest among them, end with `..`); `play`, `play_looping` and `play_with` return `Voice`, which every call site, a statement, compiles unchanged; `SoundEntry` and `ResolvedSound` gain `bus` and `max_voices` and turn `#[non_exhaustive]`. `AudioSystem::send` waits instead of dropping, and `flush` joins it.
- Outcomes: the lost-device rebuild landed (plan step 4); switching the output device while music plays is the owner's check at the ship sitting, not run. The UI lab carries the listening check (plan step 5), not run either.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/{audio,settings}.rs` and `tests/assets/{audio,manifest}.rs`; the mixer, outbox, latch and lost-stream tests in `crates/tungsten/src/tests/audio.rs`, among them the latch against the mixer over every six-play pattern of a two-voice sound with one-shots ending before and after the next play, a resync past 65,536 waiting and a callback under a producer that refills the ring; `crates/tungsten/src/tests/{settings,asset_loader,app}.rs`; and the UI lab's music, bus and crossfade tests in `examples/05_ui_lab/tests/screens.rs`.
- Limits, in `docs/known-issues.md` ("Audio buses, voices and fades"): two fixed buses; a bus's volume is the player's and does not fade; a replaced `AudioCommands` resource numbers its voices from 1 again and can address a voice the old one started; a resync or a rebuild restarts loops from their start and drops the one-shots that waited; a device whose output format is not `f32` stays silent.
- Open: buses a game declares (M68 Q6) and bus fades, ducking (M68 Q7), in the backlog; audio pause stays 1.x (`D-132`).

## D-180 — M69's engine part: `request_exit` and the window's close button end the run after one last frame that runs with `Exiting`; `engine_exit` keeps its immediate end; the player's frame-rate limit (`FrameRateCap`) joins `Settings`
**Date:** 2026-10-19
**Decision:** Two parts, the exit in the umbrella and the limit in core, so core gains no `winit` type (`D-007`). No new dependency, system or stage.

- **The exit.** `tungsten::request_exit(world)`, a function, sets a crate-private `PendingExit`. The end of `run_frame`, after the frame's work, turns it into the resource `Exiting { reason: ExitReason::Requested }`, so the next frame is the run's last; a frame that ran with `Exiting` ends as the crate-private `FrameEnd::Last`, unless its render failed, and the window loop exits after presenting it. `ExitReason` (`Requested`, `CloseRequested`) and `Exiting` (a public `reason`) are `#[non_exhaustive]`. A second call, or one during the last frame, changes nothing; called between frames, the frame after the next is the last.
- **The close.** `WindowEvent::CloseRequested` inserts `Exiting { reason: CloseRequested }`, keeping one already there, runs one frame at once from the event on the wall clock, and exits whatever it returns, so a minimized window that gets no redraw still exits; with no renderer yet it exits at once, as before. A redraw or a second close after the loop began exiting runs nothing.
- **Unchanged.** `engine_exit` still ends its frame after `Update` with no last frame: it is the developer's hatch, which `set_exit_on_escape(false)` turns off, and a game that keeps it gets no exit save. `TUNGSTEN_SMOKE_FRAMES` exits as before, and a render failure ends the run at once (`D-142`).
- **The harness.** `Harness::request_close` inserts the close's `Exiting`, so the next `step` runs the last frame; `step` keeps that frame's draw, audio and count, then `exit_requested` reads true and no frame runs, as after `engine_exit`.
- **The limit.** `tungsten_core::FrameRateCap` (`Uncapped`, `Fps(NonZeroU32)`) with `Settings::frame_rate_cap` and `set_frame_rate_cap`. The file holds `"display": { "frame_rate_cap": 60 }` or `"uncapped"`, absent when unset, and any other value (0, a negative, a float, another string or type) makes the file invalid, set aside as `.bad` with defaults under `D-159`'s rule; `null` is unset, as for the other keys. `Config::apply_settings` writes a set limit over `display.frame_rate_cap` (`Uncapped` clears it, so the resolve is uncapped; `Fps(n)` is `n`), so startup layers it as the other display fields and `TUNGSTEN_DISPLAY_FRAME_RATE_CAP` stays last; `apply_settings` copies the resolved cap into the display request in both of its branches, and the request changes the frame budget at runtime as it did.

**Why:** The template shell (M69, the owner's request of 2026-10-19) needs a menu Quit and a save on close. A game could end the run only through the `engine_exit` action while `exit_on_escape` held, read after `Update`, and the close button exited at once, with no frame to save in (`docs/plans/archive/1.0/phase6-milestone-69-template-shell.md` A7, A8). One last frame through the whole loop, render included, lets a save system read one resource and a toast draw, and both ends share it, so a game writes one exit save. `engine_exit` keeps its immediate end because it is the developer's hatch and a test pins its frame (Q2). The close runs its frame from the event, not from a redraw, so a minimized window that receives none still saves. The harness keeps the last frame's draw and audio because the plan's first sketch returned the old `ExitRequested`, whose branch drops them (critique round 1, finding 6). `DisplayState` already carried a runtime cap that a display request changes, but `Settings` could not persist one (A9), and the shell's Display tab offers it beside vsync, so it is layered and applied as the other display fields.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-159` as its marker line says. `D-142` stands: a render failure in the last frame still ends the run with its error.
- Additive: no break-ledger row; `api/tungsten.txt` and `api/tungsten-core.txt` gain the items.
- The frame end gains a check, two resource lookups a frame; the M69 perf sitting judges it.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten/src/tests/testing.rs` (`exiting_*`: the frame after a request or a close with every stage in it, the last frame's draw, audio and count, a second request and one in the last frame, 600 ordinary frames, `engine_exit` with no last frame), `crates/tungsten/src/tests/settings.rs` and `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/{settings,config}.rs` (`player_frame_cap_*`); `a_pressed_engine_exit_binding_ends_the_frame_after_update` still passes.
- Open: the F9 and F11 hotkeys still persist nothing (known issues).

## D-181 — M69's template part: `templates/basic` is a shell every game copies unchanged and a sample game it deletes, joined through a six-name seam, inside a line budget; a dark, square theme; gameplay options in their own store; four save slots with fixed triggers, each read-only for the run once it fails to load
**Amended by D-187:** the theme's one family and its fades, the four slots, the seam's six names, the sample's scope and the budget only; three families, quick motion with a run curtain, eleven slots, a seventh seam name, a sample that shoots and sounds, and caps of 2,400 and 2,900; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-189:** pause's Gameplay Options only; the gameplay options sit in Settings' Gameplay tab alone; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-192:** the confirm, the unfocused pause and the open F9 and F11 item only; a confirm takes its safe answer in the exit's last frame, a focus loss during a screen change pauses once gameplay is back on top, and the shell keeps the F11 and F9 keys' changes in `Settings`; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-194:** the autosave's triggers and the game's calls only; the run's first autosave, the exit's and one the game asks for through `shell::request_autosave` stay pending until a capture succeeds, where a failed capture dropped them; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-197:** the autosave capture only; `shell_save` captures the due autosaves at the start of `PreUpdate`, before the state dispatcher and after the previous frame's command flush, so a save holds a cast's cost and the spawn it queued; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-199:** the autosave, the options and the budget only; three autosaves written in turn over the oldest while an Autosave option is on, a Bloom option, and caps of 2,900 and 3,400; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-204:** the read-only rule for autosaves only; an autosave of a schema the game refuses is written after the empty ones, a damaged one only when no other autosave slot can be, a newer build's never; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-208:** the title's difficulty picker only; New Game shows a card for each difficulty, Normal focused, each holding its name, its rank's three squares filled to its place and its note's facts as lines; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-19
**Decision:** The template holds two parts and a seam. No engine or kit code; no new dependency.

- **The shell** (`src/shell/`), copied unchanged into each new game: a title (the game's name and version from `GameInfo`, Continue, New Game through a difficulty picker, Load, Settings, Quit), pause (Resume, Save, Load, Gameplay Options, Settings, Quit to Title, Quit Game), game over (Retry, Load, Quit to Title), a save and load screen over four slots, tabbed settings (Display, Audio, Interface, Controls, Gameplay), keybindings, a modal confirm with an optional real-clock countdown, toasts on a root no state owns, and its theme, widgets and input helpers. Every screen is a modal root a state owns (`D-149`, `D-163`), acts only when no transition runs, and gives focus back to the button that opened the screen above. A change of run goes through `shell::goto`, which a screen's `update` stores and the `shell_flow` system applies after the dispatcher with a plain pop for each state above the bottom one and a replace of the bottom one, since a state's `update` runs off the stack. `shell::apply_settings` is every shell apply, keeping the focus mute.
- **The seam**: the shell calls `game::GAMEPLAY`, `game::gameplay(RunStart)`, `game::BINDABLE_ACTIONS`, `save::SaveGame`, `save::capture` and `save::summary`, and nothing else outside `src/shell/`; the game calls `shell::game_over` and `shell::run`. A new game deletes `src/sample/`, writes its features under `src/features/<name>/` and edits those names.
- **The sample** (`src/sample/`): the player, the hazard and three gems from the kit's prefabs, its HUD and its difficulty (the `max_health` stat's base at spawn: 8, 5 or 3). It keeps the entities it spawned in `SampleRun`, which its death check, its HUD and `capture` read, so a replaced run's entities, despawned at the frame's end, never count.
- **Saves.** `autosave` and `slot_1` to `slot_3` in `SaveSlots`. The autosave is written on pause's first frame with no fixed step, at Quit to Title, in the exit's last frame (`D-180`) and on a run's first frame. A slot that fails to load (a newer schema or envelope, a damaged file) is never loaded or overwritten for the rest of the program's run, and can be deleted after a confirm. `RunState::checkpointed`, set by a save that succeeded and cleared by the next fixed step, spares a load from pause its question; `RunState::retry_from` names the slot the run last loaded or saved successfully, which Retry loads at the run's difficulty, or Retry starts fresh. `SaveGame` is schema 2 (the run's `RunMeta`, the player's position and `Health`, the gems left and the count) with a migration from schema 1.
- **Options.** `GameOptions` (mute and pause when unfocused, screen shake, damage numbers), a `SaveData` of schema 1 with `serde(default)`, live in their own store, the slot `options` of `SaveSlots::new(<user folder>/options)`; a damaged file logs, toasts and falls back to the defaults. Damage numbers are a cue rule that floats a hit's label only while the player wears the tag `show.damage_numbers`.
- **The theme**, `assets/ui/themes/shell.json`: dark, every radius 0, text and accent at 4.5:1 or more and borders at 3:1 against their surface, computed from opaque sRGB values; Inter Regular throughout, headings larger, not heavier; fades of 0.08 s and slides of 0.12 s over 8 units with no overshoot.
- **The budget**: `src/shell/` at most 2,000 lines of code and `src/` at most 2,600, as `tokei` counts code; tests are not counted. A feature past it belongs in the acceptance game.

**Why:** The owner's request of 2026-10-19 (M69): every game rebuilt the acceptance game's title, settings and pause (acceptance game §Screens), and W12b moves examples 01, 03 and 04 onto the template next, so the screens are written once, in the template, where a game copies them. The engine or the kit would freeze them before two games show what stays identical, so they move there in 1.x if they do. A seam of six names keeps the copy unchanged; a budget keeps the springboard readable and pushes a growing feature to the acceptance game. The plan's two critique rounds shaped the run rules (`docs/plans/archive/1.0/phase6-milestone-69-template-shell.md` §9): a failed write keeps the old file, so a load asks unless the run was checkpointed and Retry never loads another run's autosave; a replace despawns at the frame's end while a prefab spawns at once, so the sample tracks its own handles; a state's `update` runs off the stack, so `goto` defers the stack change; the difficulty is a stat base set before the first step, since an effect placed in a step reaches health a step later.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-123` as its marker line says. `D-149` and `D-163` stand: the shell is modal throughout, so the first key after a click acts there.
- The template's schedule snapshot moves (`shell_start`, `shell_frame`, `shell_flow`, `shell_checkpoint`, `sample_frame`) and its save schema goes from 1 to 2 with a migration; `set_exit_on_escape(false)` in `main.rs` frees Escape, which opens pause.
- Tests: `templates/basic/tests/game/` (`sample`, `shell`, `saves`, `settings`, `keys`, `fit`, and the title's pixel test in `visual`).
- Open: the monitor's size, a theme danger token, game summaries in slot headers, per-action labels in `input.json`, game-defined `Settings` fields, stick deadzone and sensitivity and startup warnings for games are backlog rows; F9 and F11 persist nothing (known issues); the template audit follows as its own stop.

## D-182 — W14b landed (M70): the `tungsten` command line, `tungsten-cli` in `tools/cli/`, with `new`, `check` and `package`; `check` loads every file a game reads inside the game's own build, behind `TUNGSTEN_CHECK`, and reports every error; `package` writes a Linux x86-64 players' archive and a debug archive
**Amended by D-186:** the consequence that `release.yml`, `release.py` and `D-072`'s levels stay the examples' only; the release archives ship the template beside the examples, and `tungsten package` stays as it is; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-19
**Decision:** The package `tungsten-cli` in `tools/cli/`, a workspace member at the workspace's version with `publish = false`, builds the binary `tungsten` (`doc = false`). It depends on `tungsten-core`, `serde_json` and `anyhow`, and parses its arguments by hand. Q20's tiers: `check` and `package` Must, `new` Should.

- **The promise** (`D-103`): the commands `new <path>`, `check` and `package`, and the options `-h`/`--help` and `-V`/`--version`, anywhere on the line. A usage error prints the usage to stderr and exits 2; a command exits 0 when it succeeds and 1 when it fails or finds errors. `api/tungsten-cli.txt` holds the help (`D-183`).
- **Version coupling.** `check` and `package` read `cargo metadata --format-version 1` in the game's folder and need exactly one package named `tungsten`, at the CLI's own version; otherwise they exit 1, naming both versions and the `cargo install` line for the game's. `new` pins the tag `v<version>`.
- **`check`, the config stage**, in the CLI: `Config::load("tungsten.json")` and `ActionMap::load("input.json")`, a missing file no error (`D-008`); an error ends the command after this stage.
- **`check`, the content stage**, in the game's build: the CLI runs `cargo run --quiet` in the game's folder with `TUNGSTEN_CHECK=<report file>`. `App::new` then resolves no user folder, as in smoke mode, and `App::run` checks the game's files in place of the schedule, the watcher and the event loop: no window, renderer, audio device, startup hook or frame. In the harness's order, each registry inserted before what checks against it: the manifest roots through `ResolvedManifest::load_for_check`, every root and entry held inside the game's folder; each sprite with its normal map and emissive mask, registered with a placeholder handle (`TextureHandle(u32::MAX)`) so a game's own asset check finds it; animations, themes, materials, sounds, tilemaps and particle configs with their references; data entries and prefabs through `StagedLoad::commit_for_check`; each map's objects; and scenes, every `.json` file under a root's folder that no manifest names. It writes one `<file>\t<message>` line per error and exits 1 when there is one. The handshake is not promised, since the CLI and the engine it builds share a version.
- **Every error**: one per file that does not read, parse or pass its loader's checks, its loader's first message, and one per reference to a missing file or ID. Only an entry whose file does not read, parse or decode cascades into the files that name it; one that fails a check stays in place. The CLI prints `<file>: <message>` lines sorted by file, paths relative to the game's folder, then one summary line.
- **Core** gains `ManifestIssue`, `ManifestError::OutsideFolder`, `ResolvedManifest::load_for_check` and `StagedLoad::commit_for_check`, which share one body with the first-error loaders through a sink that those callers stop at.
- **`package`**, on Linux x86-64 hosts only, refusing a set `RUSTFLAGS` or `CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTFLAGS`: the config stage; `cargo build --release` of the package's binary with `RUSTFLAGS="-C target-cpu=x86-64 -C link-arg=-Wl,--build-id"`, which replaces every config file's flags; a stage of the binary, `tungsten.json`, `input.json`, `assets/` and the game's root `LICENSE*`, `COPYING*` and `NOTICE*` files, links followed; the debug split as `D-120` makes it, the build-id and `.debug_line` read back; the staged binary's content check run from the stage, so a file outside it fails; the notices by `D-122`'s rules ported to Rust, the engine's four crates under its root `LICENSE`, a game's `licenses/clarifications.json` after the engine's; then `tar -czf` into `dist/<name>-<version>-<target>.tar.gz` and `dist/<name>-<version>-<target>-debug.tar.gz`, the debug file under the same top folder.
- **`new <path>`** makes the folder from `templates/basic`, embedded at the CLI's build: the name is the folder's, 1–64 lowercase letters, digits, `-` and `_` starting with a letter, a valid game ID, no Rust keyword, no Cargo folder name and no package of the template's or the engine's; the template's names become the game's; both engine lines pin the Git tag; the workspace's `[profile.*]` tables and its `Cargo.lock`, its template entry renamed, follow. It never overwrites and makes no Git repository.

**Why:** W14b (criteria Q20; RC-A2 and RC-A7). Only a game's code holds its registered components and data kinds (`D-153`'s open limit, w14 follow-up 2), so the content check runs in the game's own build after every plugin has built, and no registry file can drift from the game. Every loader stopped at its first error, so core gains reporting forms on the same bodies, which keeps the two paths from drifting. Cargo's precedence, probed on 2026-10-20 with a scratch crate whose config set `target-cpu=native`: a `--config` rustflags array was joined after the config's flags, so a config's `target-feature` would reach the build, while a set `RUSTFLAGS` dropped both, so `package` sets it as `release.yml` does and refuses one already set. The plan's critique found that checking the source folder passes a manifest path outside `assets/`, or an absolute one, that the archive leaves out; checking the stage, with every path bound to its folder, fails both. No `D-015` rule admits an argument-parsing crate for three commands and two options, and `tar` and `objcopy` are the platform's tools the release's publish job already runs (`D-120`).

**Consequences:**
- Extends `D-120` and `D-122` to a game's archive; `release.yml`, `release.py` and `D-072`'s CPU levels stay the examples'. Settles `D-153`'s open limit on the check's registered names.
- One public-API break, `ManifestError::OutsideFolder`, in the break ledger with the additions for the freeze.
- The notice rules live twice, in `release.py` and in the CLI, held together by a drift test on `PREFERENCE`, `MARKERS` and `EMBEDDED_WORKS`; one implementation, and `package` on Windows and macOS hosts, are backlog rows.
- Not checked: IDs written in Rust, sprites, fonts or shaders a game registers in code, WGSL, font file contents, the schedule and the player's files.
- Tests: `tools/cli/src/tests/` (`args`, `check`, `notices`, `elf`, `package`, `new`), `tools/cli/tests/cli.rs`, `crates/tungsten/src/tests/check.rs` (the seeded set, its clean twin and the asset-checked lookup), `crates/tungsten/src/tests/app.rs` (`run_check_…`), the `for_check` tests in `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/assets/`, and `just template-check` (`D-183`).

## D-183 — The tools around the command line: `just template-check` runs on `new`'s output with `check` and `package`; `api/tungsten-cli.txt` snapshots the help; the template's `AGENTS.md` joins `just ctx`
**Superseded by D-224:** the two entries outside the stage only; `check` refuses both, `../outside.png` and an absolute path, as `package` does; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-19
**Decision:** `scripts/template-check.sh` builds `tungsten-cli`, makes the outside copy with `tungsten new`, checks that both engine lines pin `v<workspace version>`, then points them at this checkout's `crates/tungsten` and builds offline into this checkout's target folder. In the copy it runs `tungsten check` (clean), the build, tests and 3-frame smoke run, `tungsten package` into `target/package-check` with `RUSTFLAGS` unset, the archives' listings and the extracted binary's 3-frame smoke run, two entries outside the stage that `check` passes and `package` refuses, five seeded content errors and two seeded config errors. Its free-space floor's default rises from 3 to 6 GiB. `scripts/public-api.sh` writes `cargo run -q --locked -p tungsten-cli -- --help` to `api/tungsten-cli.txt` under the same `cmp` and `--check` rule as the kit's components file. `scripts/check-agent-context.py` budgets `templates/basic/AGENTS.md` at 12,288 B and 100 lines and lists `templates/basic/CLAUDE.md` as import-only.

**Why:** RC-A7 needs `new`, `check` and `package` together on a game outside the workspace, and the outside check runs at every release. The help is the CLI's promised surface (`D-103`), and `D-107`'s snapshots are where a release sees a break. The template's `AGENTS.md` (11,951 B, 58 lines at M70's start) is read at the start of every session in every game and had no budget (a known issue naming W14b); the index's 12 KiB figure leaves room for the CLI's two lines.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-106`, `D-107` and `D-123` as their marker lines say.
- The pins are rewritten to a path and the build runs offline, so fetching the engine from its tag stays untested until W9b's final pass or C3 (known issues).
- The portable release build keeps its own folder, `target/package-check`, so `just perf`'s `target/release` is never rebuilt with other flags.

## D-184 — The template polish's engine part: the monitor's size and modes as `MonitorInfo`; volumes `tungsten.json` ships; a UI scale that can follow the window's height under a floor; a `danger` colour token
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** Four additions, each behind an opt-in, so a game that sets none behaves as at 0.90. Core gains no `winit` type (`D-007`, `D-018`); no new dependency, thread, system, stage or per-frame lookup.

- **The monitor.** `VideoMode` (`#[non_exhaustive]`; `size` in physical pixels, `refresh_millihertz`; `new`) and `MonitorInfo` (`#[non_exhaustive]`; the current mode's `size`, its `refresh_millihertz` when the platform reports one, and `modes`, largest first, each once), in `tungsten_core::display` and at the crate root. `MonitorInfo::new` returns `None` for a size with a 0 side, as a Wayland output reports before its first mode; `window_sizes` lists each distinct mode size no wider and no taller than the current one, and the current one, smallest first. `App` inserts it as a `World` resource at `resumed` from the window's `current_monitor()`, else the first of `available_monitors()` (Wayland has no primary), rebuilds it on `Moved`, `ScaleFactorChanged`, and `Resized` while none is known, removes it when no monitor answers or its size is 0×0, and logs `Monitor <w>×<h> at <hz> Hz, <n> modes` at each change. It is absent headless and in the harness.
- **Shipped volumes.** `Config` gains `audio: AudioConfig` (`#[non_exhaustive]`, `deny_unknown_fields`): `master_volume` and `bus_volumes` by bus name, each 0 to 1, 1 when left out; `bus_volume(bus)`; `validate`. `Config::load` and `App::new` refuse a volume that is not finite or outside 0..=1 with `ConfigError::InvalidValue` (`audio.master_volume`, `audio.bus_volumes`); an unknown key or bus name is a parse error, so `tungsten check` names it. `tungsten::shipped_volume(world, bus)` reads the master (`None`) or a bus from `App`'s shipped config, 1.0 without one. `apply_settings` sends the set volume, else the shipped one, for the master and each bus, and `App::new` queues each resolved volume that is set or shipped below 1.
- **The window-relative UI scale.** `ScaleFactor` gains `set_reference_height` and `reference_height`, `set_min_viewport` and `min_viewport`, and `set_window_size`; a value that is not finite or not positive is ignored, and a 0×0 window size, the default, leaves both terms out. `get()` keeps its signature and returns a value cached when an input changes: the base times the UI scale, the base being the OS scale or, with a reference height and a window size, the larger of the OS scale and the window's height over the reference; then, with a floor and a window size, at most the window's width over the floor's and its height over the floor's. `App::new` and both display syncs set the window size wherever they set `WindowSize`, so the factor follows resizes with no per-frame work; `UiHarness` sets it before each frame and each layout. The debug views draw at `zoom × step × os / get()`, M58's factor when nothing is set, so a game's policy never resizes them, and their saved places stay in OS-logical units.
- **The `danger` token.** `ColorTokens.danger`, `[255, 107, 107, 255]` by default and the theme file's `colors.danger`: destructive actions and warnings, such as a Delete button's text or a low-health bar. No built-in kind uses it.

**Why:** The owner's template notes of 2026-10-20 (the template polish pass, items 2, 7, 12, 13 and 14). A 3840×2160 window at OS scale 1 laid the template's UI out in 3840×2160 units, a small box mid-screen; the Audio tab could not show the 80 % the template wants; the window-size list ended at 2560×1440 on a 3840×2160 monitor, since the engine read no monitor; a Delete button had no theme colour to say it destroys. The base is the larger of the two scales (the pass's Q1), so the UI never draws smaller than the OS asks and fills the same share of any window at least 720 logical pixels tall; the floor keeps M69's smallest layout, 731×411 units, at every UI scale (Q2). Caching the factor where its inputs change adds nothing per frame, so the pass needed no perf sitting (Q16). winit 0.30.13 on Wayland reports an output of 0×0 before its first mode and lists only the modes the compositor sends, often the current one alone, so `new` refuses a 0 side and a one-mode monitor is the game's to pad.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-127`, `D-159` and `D-179` as their marker lines say; `D-144` and `D-180` stand.
- One break: `ColorTokens.danger` breaks a struct literal outside core (none in the workspace), a W4 ledger row; the additions are in the ledger for the freeze. `Config` and `ScaleFactor` have private fields, so their additions break nothing.
- Tests: core `tests/display.rs`, `tests/config.rs` and `tests/ui/theme_file.rs`; the umbrella's `tests/settings.rs`, `tests/app.rs`, `tests/display.rs`, `tests/debug_views.rs` and `tests/testing_ui.rs`; the template's fit matrix.
- Open: a Wayland window moved between outputs of one scale keeps the old monitor until a scale change (known issues); exclusive fullscreen at runtime stays backlog row 71's.

## D-185 — The template polish's kit part: `top_down_mover` accelerates and decelerates at optional rates
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** `TopDownMover::with_acceleration(rate)` and `with_deceleration(rate)` (px/s², each panicking with `#[track_caller]` when the rate is not finite and above 0), and the scene form's optional `"acceleration"` and `"deceleration"` (each above 0, absent by default; the field descriptions say `= null`). Each step the target velocity is the direction times the speed. With a direction the mover's kept velocity moves toward the target by the acceleration times the step, with none toward 0 by the deceleration times the step; an unset rate jumps to the target, as before. On a kinematic body the kept velocity is the slid one, so a wall absorbs it; a bodiless mover keeps the velocity it moved by. The inspector rows gain `acceleration`, `deceleration` and `velocity` only when a rate is set.

**Why:** The owner's template notes of 2026-10-20 (item 20): the sample's player reached full speed at once and stopped at once. A rate per direction lets a game ease in and slide to a stop in data, and a `move_speed` stat that changes the speed is approached at the acceleration, not jumped to. Rows that appear only with a rate keep the kit hash (`0x6fbd_8f24_2114_15a5`), whose fixtures set none.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-172` as its marker line says.
- Additions only, in the W4 ledger for the freeze; `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt` gains the two field lines.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-kit/src/tests/top_down.rs` (the ramp up and down, an unset rate, a turn, a lower speed stat, a wall, the refused rates in the constructor and the scene form).

## D-186 — The template ships in the examples' release archives: its builds beside theirs, its files under its own folder, its launcher running it from there
**Amended by D-196:** the layout, PDB and debug-file clauses only; the template ships as `Template`, its builds under `data/bin/<level>/` and its files under `data/templates/basic/`, the launcher's `FOLDERS` key is `Template`, and no debug file ships; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** `release.yml` builds the template with the examples at every CPU level and uploads its binaries, and on Windows its PDB, beside theirs. `scripts/release.py` reads the `templates/*` workspace members as it reads `examples/*`, through one member reader (`templates(root)`). `package` puts each template's builds under `bin/<level>/` with the examples', a launcher copy named after it at the archive root, and its `tungsten.json`, `input.json` and `assets/` under its own folder, `templates/basic/`; its debug files join the debug archive (the Linux split, the Windows PDB matched to its CodeView record); `shipped_packages` adds it, so the notices cover its crates; README.txt names its launcher, the folder it runs from and its log folder from that folder's `game.id`. The launcher holds a `FOLDERS` table, `tungsten-template-basic` → `templates/basic`: a launcher whose file stem is listed runs its build with that folder of the archive as the working directory, any other from the archive root. The template keeps its own version, 0.1.0; the archive is named by the engine's tag.

**Why:** The owner's template notes of 2026-10-20 (the template polish pass, item 26): a player who downloads a release plays the template as it ships, beside the examples, without building it. The template reads its own `tungsten.json`, `input.json` and `assets/`, which differ from the ones the examples share at the root, so it runs from its own folder. A table in the launcher, held to `release.py` by a test, keeps the launcher on the standard library with no side file to read (the pass's Q14).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-072` and `D-182` as their marker lines say; extends `D-120` and `D-122` to the template's builds.
- `tungsten package` (`D-182`) stays Linux-only and one-level for a game's own archive; the template in the examples' archive is the release workflow's.
- The archives grow by the template's builds at each level and its assets, two font faces and eight sounds.
- Tests: `scripts/test-release.py` (the template's Linux and Windows layouts, its debug files, its README lines, the shipped packages and the launcher's table held to `templates(root)`) and the launcher's `a_listed_build_runs_from_its_folder` and `an_example_runs_from_the_root`.

## D-187 — The template polish's template part: three font families, scrims and cards, quick motion and a run curtain, a UI that holds its layout at every scale, readouts and a frame-rate slider, shipped 80 % volumes, monitor sizes, eleven slots, quit questions, a seventh seam name and no mute hotkey; a sample that eases, shoots, mends a dummy, takes pickups, frames its play space and sounds; schema 3; the line caps at 2,400 and 2,900
**Amended by D-189:** the sample's tinted sprites and its hazard, the run's music under game over, the window mode and size applying at once, the title's size and spacing, and game over's look and sound only; own sprites with hits their size, four red enemies that return, game over's sting and music, Apply, a larger title under a cap; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-191:** the frame-rate limit's floor and the UI scale's top only; the frame-rate slider runs 30 to 480 fps and the UI scale slider 50 % to 200 %; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-199:** the clear colour, the UI scale's default, the frame-rate row, the slots and the caps only; the template's window clears to black, an unset UI scale applies as 75 %, the frame-rate limit runs from 60 fps beside an Uncapped toggle, thirteen slots (three autosaves and ten), and caps of 2,900 and 3,400; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-208:** the seventh seam name's note only; `game::difficulty_note`'s facts, joined by " · ", fill its difficulty's card a line each, under the name and the rank (`theme::CARD_PADDING`, `theme::PIP`), where it sat beside the difficulty's button; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** `templates/basic` takes the owner's notes of 2026-10-20 on the engine's `D-184` and the kit's `D-185`.

- **Look.** Three font families: body text in `sans` (Inter), headings and the title in `serif` (Source Serif 4 Regular, copied with its license), the shell's numbers in `engine_mono` (the engine's JetBrains Mono), which the theme's `fonts` name. The window clears to the theme's `background` (`render.clear_color`). The title is one serif line above the button column, the version and "Saving is off" small at the bottom left. Every screen's root is a scrim filling the viewport, the theme's background at alpha 184, owned with `RootCover::Show`, so the screen below stays drawn and frozen; its one child, the card, is a vertical scroll panel `min(design width, 100 %)` wide that scrolls when its content is taller than the viewport. The theme gains `danger`, `#ff6b6b`, for Delete buttons and the HUD's health bar at a third of max or less.
- **Motion.** A push fades and slides the new card in over 0.10 s and acts on its first frame; a pop fades and slides the card out over 0.08 s, then pops; a change of run (New Game, Continue, Load, Retry, Quit to Title) draws a curtain in the theme's background over every screen but the toasts, 0.10 s in, the stack changing under it, 0.14 s out. `start_run` stages the incoming `RunState` in the flow, inserted at the swap, so an exit save during the curtain holds the old run or none and one after it the new run. One change runs at a time: `push`, `pop`, `goto` and `start_run` do nothing while one waits, and `shell::transitioning` covers them, the engine's transitions included.
- **Scale and fit.** `ShellPlugin` sets `ScaleFactor`'s reference height, 720, and minimum viewport, 731 × 411 units (`D-184`). Captions take what their row leaves and wrap, choices share the row at least 170 units wide, sliders are 170, settings tabs sit in a rail beside the rows, keybinding cells share their row, and the keybindings card has no height cap. `tests/game/fit.rs` checks every screen at six windows and scale extremes: every root inside the viewport, every box inside its parent's (vertically unless a scroll panel scrolls it), and the title on one line.
- **Settings.** Every slider shows its value in a mono readout, a held scale slider too. The frame-rate limit is a slider from 10 to 480 fps in steps of 5 beside an Unlimited toggle; a stored cap off that grid applies and reads as stored until the player moves the slider. `tungsten.json` ships the master, music and effects volumes at 80 %, which the Audio tab shows unset and Reset tab returns to. Window sizes are the monitor's modes inside its current size, from 1280 × 720 up; a monitor listing one mode adds the old fixed sizes that fit; none gives them alone.
- **Saves and quit.** Eleven slots, the autosave and `slot_1` to `slot_10`, in a scrolling card. Pause's Quit to Title and Quit Game autosave, then ask once, naming lost progress only when the autosave failed; the title's Quit asks; game over's Quit to Title asks nothing; the close button keeps `D-180`'s last frame. Continue names its slot and the save's age. The seam's seventh name, `game::difficulty_note`, gives the New Game picker's note beside each difficulty. The mute hotkey is gone; "Mute when unfocused" stays.
- **The sample.** The player accelerates at 1,800 and decelerates at 2,400 px/s² (`D-185`), aims at the cursor and shoots a bolt on the left mouse button through the `shot` ability; a target dummy mends each hit and floats its damage; a heal and a speed pickup; the camera zooms by the window's height over 720 and starts centred on the play space. `SaveGame::SCHEMA` is 3, adding the pickups left; schema 1 migrates through schema 2's step.
- **Sound.** `tools/sounds.py`, standard library only, synthesizes every sound as 16-bit mono WAV at 22,050 Hz, and `--check` compares every byte, each loop's length and its seam (`just script-test`). The shell plays `ui_move` on a focus move a navigation press made, `ui_select` on an activation and `ui_back` on a cancel or Back; the prefabs' cues play `shoot`, `hit` and `pickup`; the sample plays its run's loop on the Music bus and, while pause is on top, silences it under pause's loop.
- **The budget.** `src/shell/` stays within 2,400 lines of code and `src/` within 2,900, as `tokei` counts them.

**Why:** The owner played M69's template and asked for 26 changes on 2026-10-20: a 3840 × 2160 window laid the UI out as a small box mid-screen, text at 200 % overflowed the settings, every menu change faded the frame to black, the window's grey did not match the theme, Quit Game ended a run without a question, and the sample had no feel and no sound. The scrim keeps the screen below in view, so a question reads in place over its menu; the curtain hides a run change, which swaps every screen, and staging the run state keeps a save during it whole. The pass measured `src/shell/` at 2,202 lines and `src/` at 2,654, so the caps sit about 9 % over them.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-181` as its marker line says; `D-180` stands.
- `tests/game/` holds 115 tests, the fit matrix, the run curtain's timing and saves, the quit questions, the readouts and frame-rate limit, the shipped volumes, the monitor's sizes, the slots, the sample's ease, shot, dummy, pickups, camera and save schema, and the menu sounds and music among them; `the_schedule_matches_the_snapshot` is unchanged, since no system was added.
- The title takes its card's width, and Continue's line and the picker's notes keep one line (`TextWrap::None`), around the known issue of a label box sized to its own text (`core/ui/layout/taffy.rs`, `place`); `fit.rs` checks the title's one line.
- The run's music plays on at 0 under the pause loop, so it resumes where it has got to, not where it stopped; a voice pause stays backlog row 34's. Gamepad aim stays a backlog row: the sample aims at the cursor alone.

## D-188 — The player's text scale reaches 50 %
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** `Settings::TEXT_SCALE_RANGE` is `0.5..=2.0`: `set_text_scale` clamps to it, and a settings file's `text_scale` outside it is refused as invalid, as before. The UI tree's own clamp, 0.25..=4.0, is unchanged.

**Why:** The owner's playtest of the template polish (2026-10-20) asked for text down to 50 %, which the UI scale already reaches; the template's fit matrix now lays every screen out at UI 50 % with text 50 %.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-163` as its marker line says.
- The API snapshot holds the constant's type, not its value, so `just api` moves no line. A settings file at a text scale under 0.75, written by this build, fails an older build's check, which falls back to defaults beside a `.bad` backup (`D-159`).
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/settings.rs` clamps to 0.5; the template's `shell_values_clamp_and_ignore_non_finite_input` and fit matrix.
- The UI lab's own text scale slider (example 05) keeps 0.75..=2.

## D-189 — The template's playtest pass: own sprites with hits their size, four red enemies that return, a hurt sound, a red game over with its sting and music, Apply for the window mode and size, a larger title under a text-scale cap, and no Gameplay Options on pause
**Amended by D-190:** the shapes' places, the shot's start, the confirm's layout and Apply's place only; every shape and the aim on a 32 × 32 sprite's middle at `[16, 16]`, the shot from the player's middle, a menu-wide confirm with stacked answers and a small Apply at the Display tab's foot; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-191:** the title's buttons and the shot's speed and range only; smaller title buttons 40 units below the name with a song of its own, and a bolt at 640 px/s for 760 px that bursts into sparks; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-199:** game over's look and the second orbiting enemy only; "YOU DIED" in spaced crimson serif on a dark band while the world pixelates and dims for 1.6 s, then the buttons; an `enemy_still` at the second orbiter's place; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** `templates/basic` takes the owner's playtest notes of 2026-10-20 on `D-187`.

- **Sprites.** `tools/sprites.py`, standard library only, draws `bolt`, `dummy`, `gem`, `heal_pickup`, `speed_pickup` and `enemy` as RGBA PNG from discs, rounded boxes and polygons sampled 4 × 4 per pixel; its `--check` compares decoded pixels, since deflate's bytes differ between zlib builds (`just script-test`). M36's `player.png` stays. Each prefab names its own sprite, untinted.
- **Hits.** A bolt's circle is 5 px and the dummy's 15, as their sprites draw them, and bolts hit `team.dummy` or `team.enemy`. The kit sweeps hits over each step already; the misses came from a 32-px bolt sprite over a 4-px circle and a 10-px circle inside a 32-px dummy.
- **Enemies.** Four red enemies, `ENEMIES`: the hazard, now an enemy that still slows, two `enemy_orbit` circling their places and an `enemy_patrol` across the top. Each takes three hits, despawns, and returns at its place `ENEMY_RETURN_SECS`, 4 game seconds, later, through `sample_frame`; they wear the damage numbers tag with the player and the dummy, and are not saved, so `SaveGame::SCHEMA` stays 3. The heal pickup moves to (304, 280), clear of the HUD at OS scale 1.75.
- **Sound.** `hurt` plays on the player's `damaged` cue. Game over plays the `game_over` sting once and loops `music_over`, a two-bar dirge at 72 BPM, faded over 1.2 s, while the sample silences the run's music under it as under pause.
- **Game over.** "You died" in the heading family at 44 units in the danger colour, fading in and falling from 1.6 times its size as the scrim deepens from clear to a dark red over 0.9 s.
- **Settings.** The window mode and size cycle a pick their rows show; Apply sends it and asks to keep it, reverting after 10 s; Reset tab and leaving drop it. VSync and the frame-rate limit still apply at once. Pause drops Gameplay Options; the Gameplay tab holds them.
- **Title.** The name at 56 on 64 units, its buttons 14 units apart. A display label's size follows the text scale only up to a cap, the title's 80 units and game over's 52, which `widgets::capped` sets and `shell_frame` applies again whenever the text scale moves, so the name keeps one line in the 731 × 411 floor at text 200 %.

**Why:** The owner played the polished template and asked on 2026-10-20 for better projectile hits, sprites of their own for the bolt, the dummy and the pickups, more moving enemies in red, an impact sound when the player is hurt, no Gameplay Options on pause, an Apply step so window sizes can be cycled, a larger title with more room between its options, and a game over with an effect, a sound, music and a red "You died". Enemies that return keep the arena busy with no save change; a cap keeps a larger title inside the floor, where text 200 % at 56 units would wrap.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-187` and `D-181` as their marker lines say; `D-188` lowers the text scale's floor alongside.
- Tests: 118 in `tests/game/`, among them `a_bolt_hits_what_it_visibly_touches`, which fails at the old circles, `enemies_move_take_three_hits_and_come_back`, `window_sizes_cycle_until_apply_sends_them`, `pause_reaches_the_gameplay_options_through_settings`, `game_over_plays_its_sting_and_music_over_the_silenced_run`, and game over's red heading and tint; the schedule snapshot is unchanged, since no system was added.
- `src/shell/` measures 2,303 lines of code and `src/` 2,804, under `D-187`'s 2,400 and 2,900; `assets/sounds/` grows to 938,050 bytes, the dirge 294,044 of them.

## D-190 — The template's kit shapes sit on their sprites' middles, a confirm is a menu's width with stacked answers, and Apply is small at the Display tab's foot
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** `templates/basic` takes the owner's last playtest notes of 2026-10-20 on `D-189`.

- **Shapes on the sprite's middle.** The engine draws a sprite's top-left corner at its entity's point, and the kit centres each shape on the point. `tools/sprites.py` now draws every sprite on a 32 × 32 canvas with its art centred, as M36's `player.png` is, and every prefab offsets its hurtbox, hitbox, contact shape and pickup shape, and the player its aim, by `[16, 16]`, the sprite's middle. The shot starts 18 px from the player's middle along the aim, and an enemy's circle is 13 px.
- **Damage numbers.** A hit's number floats from its target's shape, so the dummy's now rises from the dummy's middle.
- **Questions.** A confirm is a menu's width, its question centred and its two answers stacked under it, so the longest pair, Overwrite and Cancel, fits at text 200 % in the 731 × 411 floor.
- **Apply** is a plain button at the Display tab's foot, aligned with the values.

**Why:** The owner found shots still landing off and the dummy's number off its middle: with every shape on its sprite's top-left corner, a shot left the player's corner, aimed from the corner, and the hit and hurt circles sat 16 px up and left of what was drawn. The confirm stood wider than the menu it covered, and Apply spanned the tab.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-189` as its marker line says.
- Tests: 119 in `tests/game/`; `shots_land_where_the_cursor_points_at_every_window_size` aims at the dummy's middle through the camera at 1280 × 720 and 3840 × 2160 and checks the bolt's heading, the hit and where the number rises; the other two shot tests aim from the player's middle.
- `src/shell/` measures 2,311 lines of code and `src/` 2,812, under `D-187`'s caps.
- A sprite has no origin of its own: a game with other sprite sizes offsets its shapes by half its sprite (known issues).

## D-191 — The template's final playtest pass: a title song, smaller title buttons further below the name, window sizes with their aspect ratios, a spinning enemy, a faster, longer shot that bursts into sparks, bodies for the player, the dummy and the enemies, a harder hit shake, the UI scale offered to 200 % and a 30-fps floor
**Amended by D-199:** the frame-rate floor only; the limit runs 60 to 480 fps, and a stored cap under 60 applies as stored while its slider and readout read 60; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** `templates/basic` takes the owner's final playtest notes of 2026-10-20.

- **Title.** Its buttons sit in a 220-unit column with 3-unit vertical padding, 40 units below the name; the picker keeps a menu's width for its notes. `music_title`, an original four-bar theme at 112 BPM in C major from `tools/sounds.py`, loops while the title is on the stack, faded in and out over 1 s, so a run's start fades it out.
- **Settings.** A window size reads with its aspect ratio, the nearest common one within 3 % (`16:9`, `16:10`, `4:3`, `5:4`, `3:2`, `21:9`, `32:9`), else the size reduced (`settings::aspect`). The UI scale slider offers 50 % to 200 % (`UI_SCALES`), which `set_scale` clamps to inside the engine's range, and the frame-rate limit runs 30 to 480 fps.
- **Enemies.** The enemy turns through four frames, `enemy` and `enemy_1` to `enemy_3` from `tools/sprites.py`, its spikes an eighth of their spacing a frame and its body swelling, as the `enemy_spin` animation at 110 ms a frame.
- **Shot.** The bolt flies at 640 px/s for 760 px, and its `hit` cue spawns `spark`, a 0.5-s emitter of the `spark` particle config: 14 amber sparks of the 8-px `spark` sprite, drawn at its own size, bursting from where the bolt met its target.
- **Bodies.** The player is a kinematic body with a 24-px box, the enemies kinematic bodies with 12-px circles and the dummy a static body with a 14-px circle, each offset `[16, 16]` as the shapes are (`D-190`): the top-down mover stops at the enemies and slides along the dummy, so the player no longer walks through them, while the enemies' motion drives their bodies' velocity and their touch still lands, the player's hurt circle grown to 12 to meet it. The dummy's body is a pixel smaller than its hurtbox, so a bolt lands its hit before the static body ends it as a wall. F1, the engine's physics view, outlines every body.
- **Shake.** The player's `damaged` cue adds 0.6 trauma, not 0.25: the camera's offset goes as the trauma squared, so 0.25 moved it 1.5 px at most and 0.6 moves it about 9.

**Why:** The owner's final notes asked for smaller title buttons with more room under the name and a title song, aspect ratios beside the window sizes, an animated enemy, a quicker shot that goes further with an impact effect, collision boxes for the enemies, the player and the target, a screen shake on a hit, the UI scale clamped to 50–200 % and a 30-fps floor. The clamp stays in the template: narrowing the engine's range would make a settings file with a larger UI scale invalid, and an invalid file falls back whole to the defaults (`D-159`).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-187` and `D-189` as their marker lines say; extends `D-190`'s offsets to the bodies.
- Tests: 126 in `tests/game/`, among them `bodies_keep_the_player_out_of_the_dummy_and_the_enemies`, `a_bolt_hit_bursts_sparks_where_it_struck`, `enemies_turn_through_their_four_frames`, `a_shot_flies_at_its_speed_and_ends_at_its_range`, `the_title_plays_its_song_until_a_run_starts`, `the_frame_rate_limit_stops_at_thirty` and `window_sizes_show_their_aspect_ratio`; the schedule snapshot is unchanged.
- `src/shell/` measures 2,359 lines of code and `src/` 2,860, inside `D-187`'s caps; `assets/sounds/` grows to 1,316,078 bytes.
- A stored frame-rate cap under 30 or UI scale over 2, from an older build, applies as stored and reads exactly until its slider moves, as an off-grid cap does (`D-187`).

## D-192 — The template audit: the shell keeps the F11 and F9 keys' changes in `Settings`, pauses after a screen change that a focus loss interrupted, saves an open Settings screen at the exit after a confirm's safe answer, counts down on `Timer`, and its `AGENTS.md` names every kit item, which `just repo-check` holds to the kit's snapshot
**Amended by D-199:** the exit's save only; the exit's last frame writes the run's autosave only while the Autosave option is on, before an open Settings screen's changes; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** `templates/basic` takes the template audit's fixes (w12 follow-up 2, the roadmap's w12qa). No engine API changes: the rustdoc table of `tungsten::core::component_registry` gains `rigid_body`'s `layers` and `mask` (`D-178`), `CameraMode` and `camera_update_system` say what a game may write, and the content check's error reads "found 1 error" for one.

- **Display keys.** `shell_frame` reads the engine's `engine_toggle_fullscreen` (F11) and `engine_toggle_vsync` (F9) on the raw input and writes into `Settings` the value the engine's request toggles to, from the live `DisplayState`, which that request replaces at the next frame's start: `BorderlessFullscreen` or `Windowed`, and vsync flipped. It then applies them again, since an apply earlier in the frame, a slider's, would replace the engine's request, and saves them unless the Settings screen is open, which saves when it closes. The Display rows show the set value, so they read the keys' change when Settings next opens, and the next `apply_settings`, which a volume change sends, keeps them, as a restart does.
- **Focus loss.** A loss with "Pause when unfocused" on marks a pause due, and `shell_frame` pushes pause once gameplay is on top with no screen change running, so a loss under a run's curtain or a closing pause pauses after it; focus back drops a pause still due.
- **The exit and Settings.** `SettingsState` sets the shell's `settings_open` from its enter to its exit; while it is set, the exit's last frame saves `Settings` and the gameplay options after the run's autosave. A `ConfirmState` on top in that frame takes its safe answer first, so a window mode not yet kept reverts before the save and every other question cancels.
- **Deleting a slot.** A delete that fails for any reason but a missing file keeps the slot read-only and toasts that it could not be deleted.
- **Answers.** A confirm's first answer is final: it drops the other answer's callback, so the exit's safe answer during a kept question's fade-out reverts nothing.
- **Timers and queries.** The sample's fallen enemies (`Enemy::back`), the toasts, the confirm's countdown and a rebind's capture count down on `Timer`, as the template's `AGENTS.md` asks; the screen changes keep their seconds, which time motion, not a countdown. The sample tags whatever has `health` with `show.damage_numbers` through one query, and `GemSlot`, which only the tests read, is gone.
- **The catalogue.** The template's `AGENTS.md` names the kit items its prefabs use and every other scene component, data kind and cue event, and points to the engine's own components in the rustdoc of `tungsten::core::component_registry`. `check_kit_catalogue` in `scripts/check-repo.py` fails `just repo-check` when a template's `AGENTS.md` lacks a `component` or `kind` name, or a field of `component cues`, from `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt`, which `just api-check` holds to the kit's registrations (`D-172`); `just checks` recommends `just repo-check` when that snapshot moves, as the validation matrix's API row says.
- **The cold start.** A fresh session made a game, Ember Run, from `tungsten new`'s copy with the sample deleted, reading only the template, the guide, rustdoc and the paths they name; its findings reworded four of `AGENTS.md`'s rules (features live in `src/features/<name>/` from the start, a spawn that must be kept is made at once, the one-query rule is about entities collected from a query, the shell names the sample uses beyond the seam may change), said that the game acts on the gameplay options and gave the rustdoc command that documents `tungsten::core` and `tungsten::kit`. The guide gained what goes with the sample (its assets, each with its manifest entry, its schema count, the shell tests' helpers), a camera with no target, `KitRng` for a game's draws, a timer's stage, the difficulty note's room and `cargo fmt` after `new`; the tests read the game's title from `tungsten.json`, the title's song and game over's music are tested in `tests/game/shell.rs`, so they survive the sample, and the camera's modes and update are documented in rustdoc.

**Why:** The audit (2026-10-20) found that a focus loss during a screen change was dropped, so a run started under the curtain played on in the background; that an F11 fullscreen was undone by the next Settings change and lost at restart, the known issue the audit was asked to settle; that a window closed from the Settings screen lost that visit's changes; that a failed delete dropped the slot's read-only mark; and that `AGENTS.md` missed `conversion`, `penetration`, `damage_types` and `damaged_part` and listed `lifetime`, which `spark.json` uses, as unused. The shell persists the keys, not the engine, since an engine change would alter every game's hotkeys and is W1 M6's call; the exit saves only an open Settings screen, so a game that never opens it writes no file; the safe answer at the exit keeps a mode the player never kept from becoming the next start's.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-181` as its marker line says; `D-180`'s open F9 and F11 item stands for the engine (known issues), and `D-187`'s and `D-189`'s Settings rules stand.
- Tests: 132 in `tests/game/`, among them `a_close_while_keep_fades_out_keeps_the_display`, `losing_focus_during_a_screen_change_pauses_after_it`, `f11_and_f9_are_kept_in_settings_and_survive_a_restart`, `a_close_in_settings_keeps_the_changes_but_not_an_unkept_display` and `a_delete_that_fails_keeps_the_slot_read_only_and_says_so` (Unix only) and `game_over_plays_its_sting_and_music_until_the_run_ends`; `test_template_agents_names_every_kit_item` in `scripts/test-check-repo.py`; the schedule snapshot is unchanged.
- Open: `tungsten new`'s textual rename leaves sources that need `cargo fmt` (known issues), and a `spawner` with an area and a cap is a backlog row.
- `src/shell/` measures 2,375 lines of code and `src/` 2,872, inside `D-187`'s caps; the template's `AGENTS.md` is 12,255 B of `D-183`'s 12,288.

## D-193 — Examples 03, 04 and 05 become one test-only crate, `examples/03_fixtures`, with example 01's lighting fixture; the template's shell is the only UI reference
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** Examples `03_scene_state`, `04_shader_playground` and `05_ui_lab` stop being demos. Their fixtures, with example 01's lighting fixture, move into one test-only crate, `examples/03_fixtures` (package `example-03-fixtures`). It is not a reference, and no game copies it. Like `02_bench` it stays off the template layout, and it stays out of the release archives, which carry every other example. The template's shell (`D-181`) becomes the only UI reference. The three examples are deleted, and so are the showcase images and recipe they feed: `docs/showcase/` and the README's link to it.

The crate keeps:

- from 04: `tests/post_regression.rs` (5 tests) and the smoke rows M26 empty and all, M27 SMAA, M28 bloom, M30 game feel and M31 mesh trail;
- from 03: `tests/transition_regression.rs` (1 test over four effects), M31's four transition rows and the frame-cap row;
- from 05: `tests/ui_regression.rs` (6 tests, two of them against fixture PNGs at tolerance 0), the M56 menus row and the M57 debug-views row, and those of `tests/screens.rs`'s 19 tests that check engine behaviour rather than the lab's screens, less the ones the template's tests cover;
- from 01: the M29 lighting row (`TUNGSTEN_LIGHTING_FIXTURE`).

In all, 14 smoke rows beside the plain run every example gets, and three of `just visual`'s five suites.

**Why:** Two UI references would drift apart, and W12b shrinks to example 01 (`D-194`). Deleting the tests with the examples would leave post-processing, transitions and the UI overlay with no pixel tests just before W4b freezes them. Rejected: deleting the examples with their tests, for that loss; folding the fixtures into `02_bench`, which risks its digests-unchanged rule (workflow §5); keeping and migrating all three, which keeps two UI references.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-072` and supersedes `D-179`'s listening host, as their marker lines say: the release archives carry every example but this crate, and M68's listening and device-switch owner checks move from the UI lab to the template's settings.
- If the merged crate draws a different frame, the owner recaptures the two UI fixture PNGs on the reference machine, at its OS scale of 1.75 with the packaged fonts (`D-116`), as the UI lab's capture notes say.
- Root `assets/` keeps what `02_bench`, the fixtures crate and the engine's hot-reload copies need.
- `D-181`'s Why had W12b move examples 01, 03 and 04 onto the template; 01 alone moves now, rebuilt (`D-194`).
- The register's "Example fixtures" stop, level B, after the template audit (implementation plan §4 and §10, the catalog's `exfix`). Tests that older decisions name in the three examples keep their place in those entries; the fixtures stop's plan records where each went.

## D-194 — Example 01 is rebuilt from scratch as a sandbox on the template, in four Must stops before the feature gate; the template's requested autosaves wait until a capture succeeds, and the game requests one through `shell::request_autosave`
**Amended by D-199:** the requested autosave only; while the Autosave option is off a due or requested autosave is dropped, and while it is on each lands over the oldest of three autosaves; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-202:** stop (d) only; it landed as level C, with a kit change and its API critique, rather than re-tiered to 1.x, and with the mine as an open cut under the sky so lightning reaches the beetles; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-205:** the stops after (d) only; (e) landed widened by the owner's prompt of 2026-10-22 past the feedback plan's non-goals to core physics (`D-203`), core queries (`D-206`), the template's autosave (`D-204`) and the dev profile; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-207:** the stops after (d) only; the owner's notes of 2026-10-22 put two stops between (e) and (f), the quick fixes (0.99) and a level and title pass (0.100), and the quick fixes landed with a kit addition, `ImmunePart`, for the gap they found (feedback K15); the rest stand.
**Amended by D-208:** the stops after (d) only; the level and title pass (0.100) landed with two kit additions for the gaps it found (feedback K16 and K17), and (f) feel follows; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-209:** the stops after (d) only; (f) feel landed with no kit, engine, template or shell change, its gaps (feedback K2, K4 and K7) left as backlog rows, and (g) props and foes follows; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-210:** the stops after (d) only; (g) props and foes landed with no kit change, its three kit gaps (feedback K3, K10 and K11) kept as backlog rows with 01's code meanwhile, and (h) the look follows; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-211:** the stops after (d) only; (h) the look, the last of them, landed with no kit, engine, template or shell change, and the Phase 6 QA pass follows; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-212:** the stops after (d) only; the owner's notes of 2026-10-24 put three stops after (h), (i) fixes and tuning (0.104), (j) pushes and spells and (k) scenery, the aqueduct and the start, and (i) landed with its planned kit change (K24) and, at the owner's review of its frames, a second: the kit's hitboxes drawn only while armed; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-213:** the stops after (d) only; (j) pushes and spells landed with a kit addition for two of the gaps it met (feedback K7's shove and K18), the rest of K7 and K20, K21 and K28 left as backlog rows, and (k) follows; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-214:** the stops after (d) only; (k) scenery, the aqueduct and the start landed with no kit, engine, template or shell change, and (l) the tomes follows; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-215:** the acceptance clause only; example 01 is the acceptance game, accepted when the owner says it is, and its remaining stops (`e01quick2` to `e01flask`) are `ROADMAP.md` items; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** Example 01 is rebuilt from scratch as a sandbox on the template: no objective, no win and no boss, so it stays distinct from the acceptance game, an arena auto-shooter with XP, level-ups, waves and a boss. Its design is a plan, `docs/plans/1.0/example-01-design.md`.

- **Placement.** The whole overhaul lands in Phase 6, after the example fixtures (`D-193`) and before the Phase 6 QA pass, as four stops: 01 (a) rebuild, 01 (b) elements and weapons, 01 (c) bestiary and 01 (d) new level. The owner tiers all four Must, so the feature gate waits for them. 01 (a) carries W12b's Must scope and W1 M6.
- **Fallback.** 01 (d) is content with no public API: if Phase 6 runs long, the feature gate may re-tier it to 1.x without harming the freeze.
- **The template.** A requested autosave stays pending until a capture succeeds, where a failed capture dropped it: the run's first autosave, the exit's, and one the game asks for. The game asks through `shell::request_autosave`, a call back to the shell beside `shell::game_over`, `shell::run` and `shell::run_meta`, since `shell::autosave` saves at once and sits outside the seam the template's `AGENTS.md` names. The change stays within `D-187`'s line caps. The title loop asked for with it, `music_title`, which the shell plays on the title by fixed ID as it plays its `ui_*` sounds, is already there (`D-191`).
- **Not decided here.** 01 (b), (c) and (d) take no decision now. Each stop's milestone writes its own when it lands, as every milestone does, starting from the design file, which is a plan and may change while the stops are planned.

**Why:** `D-158` moves the platformer controller and the path follower to 1.x unless example 01 uses them by the freeze, and the kit's combat API should meet a real game's hardest cases before W4b locks it. C1 then takes 01 as a stable benchmark row. A sandbox keeps 01 apart from the acceptance game. The shell's `frame` clears the run's first-save flag before its capture can fail and drops `autosave`'s result, so a run whose first capture fails has no autosave until pause or a quit; 01's area autosaves need a request that waits the same way.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-181` as its marker line says. `D-158` stands: 01 (a) is the W12b move it names, with the `gate_step` corner widened. `D-187`'s caps and seam names and `D-191`'s title song stand.
- The register's W12b with W1 M6 row gives way to five rows, the example fixtures and 01 (a) to (d), and the catalog's `w12b` stop to five stops (`D-109`). The `D-108` pair ends: W1 M6 sits inside 01 (a), which is level C.
- The 1.x backlog gains line-of-sight sensing in the kit, stereo panning per voice, looping kit sound cues that keep their voice, and a same-step order that survives a load.
- The exit's request has only the exit's last frame (`D-180`), so a capture that fails there still writes nothing.
- Tests, with 01 (a): the template's pending-autosave test, in which the run's first autosave lands even when the first capture attempt returns `None`, and the shell-copy check in `just repo-check`.

## D-195 — Example 01's four stops run every session on Opus 5.5 at max, through a stop's own `tier` in the roadmap catalog
**Superseded by D-215:** the per-stop `tier` field only; the tier is `D-151`'s rule as amended (a session that generates art or sound on Opus max, a code session at xhigh); its reason stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** A stop in `docs/plans/1.0/roadmap.json` may carry `tier`, `{"model": "opus" | "fable", "effort": "high" | "xhigh" | "max"}`, which `tier(stop, key)` in `scripts/roadmap.py` returns for every scaled session stage of that stop (`plan`, `qa-plan`, `gate`, `run`, `experiment`, `rc-fix`) ahead of `D-151`'s rule; the fixed steps (`rc-checklist`, `release`, `verify`) keep theirs, and a malformed `tier` fails the catalog. Example 01's four stops, 01 (a) rebuild, 01 (b) elements and weapons, 01 (c) bestiary and 01 (d) new level (`e01a`–`e01d`, `D-194`), carry `{"model": "opus", "effort": "max"}`, so their plan and run sessions run on Opus 5.5 at max.

**Why:** The owner's request of 2026-10-20. Every one of these sessions generates assets: each plan draws a contact sheet for the owner's review, and each run draws sprites with their maps, icons and tiles and synthesizes sounds and music loops (`docs/plans/1.0/example-01-design.md`, Assets and Art review). In the owner's experience Opus 5.5 does that work better than Fable 5.1. `D-151`'s rule gave (a), (b) and (c), level C at complexity 3 and XL, Fable 5.1 at xhigh, and (d), level B, Opus 5.5 at xhigh. A field on the stop keeps the owner's choice beside the stop it covers, and leaves the ratings the rule reads describing the work.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-151` as its marker line says: its rule holds for every stop without `tier`. No remaining session stage runs on Fable 5.1.
- Eight session stages move to Opus 5.5 at max: six from Fable 5.1 at xhigh, (a)–(c)'s plans and runs, and two from Opus 5.5 at xhigh, (d)'s.
