Max spends more of the 5-hour usage window; `D-151`'s medians put an Opus max plan at 199,282 output tokens against 128,974 at xhigh, and a run at 270,066 against 251,466.
- The catalog hash changes, so the next sync rewrites `meta/catalog`, and the roadmap page shows Opus 5.5 max on the four stops. Recommendations stay advice.
- Tests in `scripts/test-roadmap.py`: `tier` returns a stop's own `tier` for every scaled stage, `recommend` keeps the fixed steps', and an unknown model or effort, a missing field or a non-object `tier` raises; the repository test reads a stop's own `tier` before `D-151`'s rule.

## D-196 — Release archives are zips for players: plain names, launchers under friendly names beside a `data/` folder, one plain-text notices file and no debug archive
**Amended by D-197:** the archive's game folders only; a folder game, a template or a shipped example whose folder holds its own `tungsten.json`, ships `tungsten.json`, `input.json` and `assets/` under `data/<folder>/` with its log folder in `README.txt`, and `FOLDERS` lists `Platformer` after `Template`; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** `scripts/release.py package` writes one zip per target, on Linux as on Windows, named `tungsten-examples-<version>-<platform>.zip`: the tag without its `v`, and `windows-x64` or `linux-x64` from the `PLATFORMS` table (another target keeps its triple), for example `tungsten-examples-0.93.0-windows-x64.zip`; its top folder has the archive's name. That folder holds one launcher per shipped build under the build's player-facing name from the `TITLES` table (`example-01-platformer` → `Platformer`, `example-02-bench` → `Benchmark`, `tungsten-template-basic` → `Template`; a package missing from it keeps its name), `README.txt`, `LICENSE.txt` (the repository's `LICENSE`) and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt`, and a `data/` folder with the builds under `data/bin/<level>/<title>` and every file they read: `tungsten.json`, `input.json`, `assets/`, the examples' asset folders and the template's files under `data/templates/basic/`. The launcher runs `data/bin/<level>/<its own file name>` with `data/` as the working directory, or the folder under it that its `FOLDERS` table names (`Template` → `templates/basic`); `DATA` in the launcher matches `release.py`'s. `README.txt` is written for players: what each program is (`TITLES`), how to start it, what it needs, where its logs go, the backend and CPU-level overrides, and the licenses. `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` ends with the standard library's notices, `COPYRIGHT-library.html` from the build job's license data converted to plain text by `plain_text` (stdlib `html.parser`: blocks on their own lines wrapped at 78 columns, each `<pre>` license text verbatim, nested boxes indented, an outside link's address after its text); no HTML file ships, and `release.py notices` writes the one file. No release ships a debug archive: `package` strips each Linux binary and launcher copy with `objcopy --strip-debug` (`strip_debug`, a parameter tests fake), and `release.yml` no longer uploads the PDBs. The build keeps the tuned profile's line tables and the Linux build-id, `split-debug`, `crash-report.py` and the build job's informational crash-report probe stand. `SHA256SUMS` covers the two zips.

**Why:** The owner's request of 2026-10-20: the release page and the archives are for end users who click an example, not for the engine's developers. v0.91.0 shipped four archives named by target triple, with `tungsten-debug-*` archives (187 MB on Windows, 264 MB on Linux) larger than the players' (80 MB and 95 MB) and read as debug builds; the archive's top folder mixed three launchers named after their packages with `bin/`, `assets/`, `examples/`, `templates/`, two JSON files, an extensionless `LICENSE` that Windows does not open, and two notices files, one of them HTML. Symbolizing a player's crash from an example is not worth a second download per platform: a crash file still carries the panic's message and location, and Linux function names from the symbol table that `--strip-debug` keeps. The standard library's notices must still ship with the binaries; their format need not, and the converted text keeps every license text and heading of the file (checked on 1.98.1's 1.5 MB file: all 267 `<pre>` texts and 142 crate headings present, no tag left). Python's `zipfile` keeps each file's mode, so `unzip` and the desktop extractors restore the launchers' executable bit. GitHub adds its own `Source code (zip)` and `Source code (tar.gz)` to every release; the tar cannot be turned off.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-071`, `D-072`, `D-120`, `D-122` and `D-186` as their marker lines say.
- The crash-report check on a released Linux build (`docs/releases.md`) is gone; `crash-report.py symbolize` still serves a local build and the probe's run. `tungsten package` (`D-182`) still writes a game's debug archive and its HTML standard-library notices; moving it to this layout is a follow-up.
- A shipped example or template needs a `TITLES` row: `scripts/test-release.py` fails when the table and the shipped packages differ, when two titles differ only in case or when a title is `data`, and when the launcher's `FOLDERS` or `DATA` disagree with `release.py`.
- Log and crash file names take the build's file name (`Platformer-<stamp>-<pid>.log`); the log folders, named by `game.id`, stay.
- Tests: `scripts/test-release.py` (both zips' names and layout, the executable bit in the Linux zip, every Linux copy stripped and no `.debug`, `.pdb` or `.html` entry, the README per platform with its log folders and the template's, the notices ending with the standard library's text, `plain_text` on the file's shapes) and the launcher's unit tests (the `data/` working directory and the `Template` folder).

## D-197 — Example 01 (a) landed on the template: it runs from its own folder with the template's shell byte for byte, its player on the kit's platformer controller with air jumps, mana on the kit's restore and suppression, saves of the kit's runtime state and `World::component_types` for their coverage, autosaves captured in `PreUpdate` after the last flush, and the archives' folder games
**Amended by D-199:** the shell copy only; `check_shell_copies` holds example 01's `assets/ui/themes/shell.json` to the template's, as it holds `src/shell/`; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-200:** the game's parts and its save only; `src/features/` adds `combat`, `dummy`, `lightning` and `gun`, and the save is schema 5, which migrates schema 4; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-20
**Decision:** M72 rebuilt example 01 as a game on `templates/basic`:
- **The game.** `examples/01_platformer` holds its own `tungsten.json`, `input.json` and `assets/` and runs from that folder, as the template does; `src/shell/` copies the template's shell byte for byte, which `check_shell_copies` in `just repo-check` holds; the game lives in `src/features/` (level, player, run, audio, toys, elements, spells) behind the seam's seven names. The generator writes the level's placements as object layers whose classes name generated prefabs, and copies `tools/content/` (prefabs and data) verbatim.
- **The controller.** The player moves on the kit's `PlatformerController`, which gains air jumps: `with_air_jumps`, `air_jumps` in data, `air_jumped` and `air_jumps_left`, a fresh press past coyote time, refilled by the ground.
- **Effects.** An effect gains the `restore` payload (a stat's base raised up to another stat's value, `{ "stat", "amount", "max" }`, every name checked at parse) and `suppressed_by` (tags that hold a running instance: its time and period wait, no payload lands). 01's mana is `mana_regen` (1.5 every 0.1 s up to `max_mana`) suppressed by the `casting` tag, which the eight spells' abilities grant for a second.
- **Saved runtime state.** `PlatformerController`, `PathFollower` and `KitRng` serialize their runtime state: the controller leaves out its filter and its first step, so a loaded controller takes a still first step; `KitRng` saves its generator's words through `Pcg32`'s serde, which refuses an even increment. `World::component_types` lists an entity's component types for a save's coverage test; no frame calls it.
- **Autosaves.** The template's due autosaves are captured by `shell_save` at the start of `PreUpdate`, before the state dispatcher and after the previous frame's command flush, so a save holds a cast's cost and the spawn it queued; a requested autosave waits until `capture` returns a save.
- **Archives.** A folder game is a template or a shipped example whose folder holds its own `tungsten.json`: it ships `tungsten.json`, `input.json` and `assets/` under `data/<folder>/`, its launcher runs it from there (`FOLDERS` gains `("Platformer", "examples/01_platformer")`), and its log folder joins `README.txt`; the other examples keep the shared root files.

**Why:** The rebuild is W12b's scope and the design's stop (a): the template as the starting point of a real game, the kit wherever it has the mechanic, and a save that loads back to itself. Each kit addition is what the game needed and two genres' recipes can use: the air jump for the platformer's controls, `restore` and `suppressed_by` for a resource that a use pauses (mana in the platformer recipe, stamina in the top-down one), and the saved forms for a load that keeps with the run it came from. Capturing autosaves in `PreUpdate` after the flush closes round 1's first finding: a capture in `Update` held a cast's cost without the missile its payload had queued. Measured: a capture of a busy run loads into a fresh harness and captures equal as JSON; a loaded run kept with the uninterrupted one for 3 s within 0.00 px in a fresh harness and in one that had spawned and despawned 10,000 entities; 12,000 balls saved in 1.78 MiB, captured and written in 4.9 ms (release).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-123`, `D-158`, `D-173`, `D-177`, `D-181` and `D-196` as their marker lines say; `D-194`'s pending autosave lands as written.
- Tests pin it: the kit's `ecs::world` and `rng` tests, the recipes `a_mages_mana_regenerates_to_its_max_and_a_cast_pauses_it`, the top-down stamina recipe and the platformer air jump, the template's `a_requested_autosave_waits_for_a_capture` and its schedule snapshot, 01's `saves::` (round trip, coverage by entity kind, the same-frame cast, the save during `casting`, divergence fresh and churned, the refused schemas, the full pit) and `playthrough::`, `check-repo.py`'s shell-copy check and `test-release.py`'s folder-game package test.
- Limits: a lit sprite shows no hit flash (Q6), so the courier flashes nothing; the ball record is one flat array, since the slots write pretty JSON; a loaded missile spawns at the load frame's flush; knockback's and contact damage's timers come back from the prefab. The kit's `place` payload, one-shot clips for the character animator, `Lifetime` in saves and the pad's aim wait in the backlog.

## D-198 — UI text takes letter spacing: `TextOverrides::letter_spacing` passes down the tree with the other text properties, a theme file sets it as `text.letter_spacing`, and the debug views pin it at 0
**Date:** 2026-10-21
**Decision:** `TextOverrides` gains `letter_spacing: Option<f32>`, space added after each glyph in em as `TextLayout::letter_spacing` takes it. It is an inherited text property: a kind's, a state's or a node's own value replaces the inherited one and passes to the node's children, and `apply` writes `style.layout.letter_spacing` only for a finite value, so a NaN or an infinity is ignored. A theme file's `text` object, in a kind's state or `normal`, takes `"letter_spacing": <number>`; `deny_unknown_fields` still refuses other names. A change re-lays the text, since `ResolvedStyle::text_measures_differently` compares the whole `TextLayout`. The debug views' `Look::text` sets `Some(0.0)`, so a game theme's spacing never reaches them (M58's isolation). Nothing upper-cases text: a game that wants caps writes them.

**Why:** M73's game-over heading is spaced serif caps, and a theme had no way to space UI text although the text layout and both measures already read the value (A29): the field was the missing link between the theme model and the layout. An inherited property matches the nine other text fields, so a button's spacing reaches its label as its weight does. Ignoring a non-finite value at apply keeps a hand-built override from reaching the text engine, and a JSON file cannot hold one.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-125` as its marker line says.
- A break for a `TextOverrides` struct literal that names every field (the break ledger's row); the workspace's two such literals, the debug views' look and their hostile-theme test, set the field. `api/tungsten-core.txt` gains two lines.
- Tests pin it: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/ui/style.rs` (`letter_spacing_inherits_and_a_labels_own_beats_it`), `theme_file.rs` (`text_fields_map_onto_the_text_overrides`), `layout.rs` (`letter_spacing_widens_a_label_by_its_em_per_glyph_and_ignores_a_non_finite_value`) and `crates/tungsten/src/tests/debug_views.rs` (the hostile theme's 0.5 em).

## D-199 — The template final pass: a grace for continued runs, toggles that take their first click, Uncapped from 60 fps, a 75 % UI by default, an outlined slider thumb, three rotating autosaves with an Autosave option, a question before an older version's save, a clearer save screen, a Bloom option, a game over band over a pixelating world, a black play area, a still enemy and a Windows icon; example 01's theme held to the template's; the line caps at 2,900 and 3,400
**Amended by D-204:** the autosaves' order only; empty, then an older schema's the game refuses, then the oldest, then a damaged one, never a newer build's; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-211:** the copy check's theme clause only; a shell example's `assets/ui/themes/shell.json` keeps the template's text with every `#rrggbb` or `#rrggbbaa` masked, its keys, values, order and layout, its colours its own; `src/shell/` stays held byte for byte; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-21
**Decision:** `templates/basic` takes the owner's request of 2026-10-20 (M73), and example 01 takes each shell change by copy.

- **The death loop.** A continued run's player spawns under `spawn_grace`, an effect that grants `status.spawning` for 2.5 s, which every enemy's `contact_damage` filter leaves out; a fresh run takes touches at once. It covers every save, those already on disk included, since every load spawns through `GameplayState::on_enter`.
- **Settings.** A toggle takes its first click: the rows refresh once after the routing's events, so its `ValueChanged` applies first. The frame-rate limit runs from 60 to 480 fps beside an Uncapped toggle; a stored cap under 60 applies as stored while its slider and readout read 60. An unset UI scale applies as `theme::UI_SCALE`, 75 %: `ShellPlugin` sets it on `ScaleFactor` and `shell::apply_settings` after the engine's apply; the setting stays unset until the slider moves, so Reset returns to it. A slider's line height is `theme::SLIDER_LINE`, 18 (an 18-unit thumb on a 4.5-unit track at text 100 %), and the theme outlines its thumb in `#0b0c10` at 1 unit, in the focus colour at 2 when focused.
- **Autosaves.** Three slots, `autosave_1` to `autosave_3` (`AUTOSAVES`), then `slot_1` to `slot_10`. An automatic or requested save rewrites the slot the run's last automatic save wrote while no fixed step has run since, else takes the first empty writable slot, else the oldest by its save time, equal times going to the slot this program wrote first, then to the lower number; read-only slots are skipped. The shell's start renames a 0.93 `autosave.json` to the first free slot, keeping its time; one it cannot move stays listed under its old name and is never written. `GameOptions::autosave`, on by default, is the Gameplay tab's Autosave toggle: off, no save is written at a run's start, pause, a quit, the exit or a request, a due one is dropped, and quitting a live run names lost progress. New Game asks first only with the option on and an autosave ready.
- **Older saves.** `shell::older` compares versions by their leading numbers, a suffix ignored, and calls one that does not parse older. A row names an older version ("Older version 0.0.9", "Unknown version"). Loading an older save from the Load screen or Continue asks first (`shell::load`), one question that adds the progress line when a live run is not checkpointed; Retry asks nothing.
- **The save screen.** "Autosaves" and "Slots" labels; each row a button holding the slot's name in the body family and its details in mono, smaller and muted, at the row's end; empty rows without fill; Delete muted until its row or itself has focus, then in danger, as tall as its row. Save mode leaves the autosaves out, with a note on when they are written or that Autosave is off.
- **Bloom.** `GameOptions::bloom`, on by default, is the Display tab's Bloom toggle. `shell_frame` makes the shell's one edit of `PostStack` a frame: with the option off it takes the game's Bloom passes out, each with its index, and puts them back when it comes on; a stack the game rebuilt drops what the shell kept; the curtain's swap gives the game its stack back as it built it before a change of run. A game builds its stack whole when its run starts. Each settings tab's Reset resets its own rows only. The sample's run inserts one Bloom pass (threshold 0.9, knee 0.1, intensity 3.0, radius 1.0) and the default stack at its end.
- **Game over.** Its own screen: a clear modal root, a full-width band (black at alpha 200) holding "YOU DIED" in the serif family at 64 on 72 units, capped at 96, letter spacing 0.2 em (`D-198`), in `#be1420`, and the buttons under it. Its entrance, 1.6 s on the real clock, is a screen change: the band fades in while the shell's post tail pixelates the world to 48 px and dims it to 0.8 under a near-black crimson; then the buttons fade in over 0.3 s and Retry takes focus. The tail leaves when game over does.
- **The rest.** The template's window clears to black; the curtain keeps the theme's background. The sample's second `enemy_orbit` becomes `enemy_still`, the same enemy without its orbit, at `[1100, 380]`. `tools/icon.py` draws the gem on the theme's background at 16, 32, 48 and 256 px into `windows/icon.ico` and `windows/icon.res`, and `build.rs` links the `.res` into the executables of a Windows MSVC build.
- **The copy check.** `check_shell_copies` holds each shell example's `assets/ui/themes/shell.json` to the template's, as it holds `src/shell/`.
- **The budget.** `src/shell/` stays within 2,900 lines of code and `src/` within 3,400, as `tokei` counts them.

**Why:** The owner's request of 2026-10-20, before 01 (b) builds example 01 on this shell: a save made in an enemy's reach died again on every load; a toggle took two clicks; the UI was large at the reference height and the slider thumbs big; one autosave that a quit overwrote; no warning before an older version's save; a save screen hard to scan; no way to turn bloom off; a game over that should land harder; a grey play area; an enemy that stands for aiming practice; and an icon for the Windows build. The grace is data on the kit's effects and tag filters, so it needs no kit API and covers saves already on disk, where a capture guard would drop pause's and quit's saves silently. An autosave coalesces while no fixed step runs, so a pause and the quit after it take one slot, not all three. The shell edits the game's `PostStack` rather than owning a pass, and passes carry no identity, so a game that sets its stack to what the shell left keeps its withheld Bloom, which a test pins. The caps are the next hundred at or above 105 % of the pass's counts, 2,718 and 3,227.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-181`, `D-187`, `D-189`, `D-191`, `D-192`, `D-194` and `D-197` as their marker lines say. `SaveGame::SCHEMA` stays 3 and `GameOptions::SCHEMA` 1, the new fields defaulting; the slot names move, a 0.93 folder reaching them through the rename.
- Tests: 161 in the template's `tests/game/` (among them `saves::a_continued_run_survives_its_first_two_seconds`, `settings::every_toggle_takes_its_first_click`, `settings::the_ui_scale_defaults_to_75_percent`, `saves::autosaves_rotate_through_three_slots_overwriting_the_oldest`, `shell::pause_then_quit_game_keeps_the_earlier_autosaves`, `saves::an_older_save_names_its_version_and_asks_before_it_loads`, `saves::the_load_screen_groups_autosaves_and_slots`, `settings::the_bloom_option_strips_and_restores_the_games_bloom` and `shell::game_over_pixelates_and_dims_and_removes_exactly_its_passes`), 107 in example 01's, the shell-copy check's theme cases and `tools/icon.py --check` in `just script-test`. The schedule snapshot is unchanged, since no system was added; `tests/fixtures/title.png` moved twice, for the 75 % UI and for the black clear.
- Open, in the M73 plan's follow-ups and `w12-template.md`: game over's crimson over a bright sprite under its band, a damage number frozen over game over, the HUD under the band at the floor, example 01's own grace, and the icon's other homes.

## D-200 — Example 01 (b) landed: its damage model on the kit's damage types with crits, its bodies' element states on 01's components and its targets' on the kit's effects, the endings in a saved cascade queue, lightning with rods and conduction, the gun, damage numbers, the training dummy with the summon slot and first-time hints; the kit gains `Effects::set_source`, `float_label`, `DamageKinds::label_style`, a rung's `blocked_by` and `DamagedPart::crit`
**Amended by D-201:** the save, the summon slot and the impacts only; schema 6 adds `enemies`, `throws` and `orbs`, `gone` takes the map's dead enemies and `migrate(5)` moves the IDs past the map's nine enemies; the summon slot also summons each enemy kind, six live at most beside the dummies' four; a thrown body's first impact credits its thrower at its damage factor, the player takes impact damage from thrown bodies alone and the crush spares the player for a thrown block; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-209:** the gun's magazine only; nine rounds 0.2 s apart, and a `reload` press refills a magazine short of its rounds after `RELOAD_SECS`, the trigger blocked and clicking meanwhile; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-210:** the cascade queue only; an entry credits whoever lit its chain, not only the run's player: `queue_from` and `thermal_shock_from` take a source save ID, every ending a sourced entry queues keeps it and the reactions queue by their part's source, a gone source crediting none; and a fourth kind of ending, `Detonation`, blows a powder keg or a goblin's bomb; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-21
**Decision:** M74 builds example 01's second stop on the kit:

- **The kit.** `Effects::set_source(serial, source)` names a loaded instance's source again, which the saved form still leaves out. `float_label(world, style, anchor, amount)` floats an amount in a `LabelStyle` from a game's code as the `label` cue does, a non-finite amount or anchor floating nothing. `DamageKinds::label_style(world)` reads the style a style-less `label` floats a part of those kinds in. An `AilmentRung` takes `blocked_by` (`with_blocked_by`, data `blocked_by`, each tag declared): a target carrying one skips the rung while its bucket fills on for the rungs above, and a grant an effect has pending in the step counts as carried, by ancestor as `Tags::has` reads a carried tag, for `requires` too. `DamagedPart` carries its request's `crit` (`with_crit`).
- **The damage model.** 01's types are `damage`, under it `damage.physical` (with `damage.physical.bleed`), `damage.fire`, `damage.cold` and `damage.shock`, each with a colour and a ladder: bleeding on flesh and shattered on the frozen, burning, chilled then frozen on the freezable, shocked then stunned. `combat::Hit` builds 01's requests: a hit from the run's player crits on one `KitRng` draw at 5 %, doubling every part, and the player takes half of a blast or a steam burst. Every request is built and queued in the intent, move or overlap phase, since no save holds the kit's queues.
- **Bodies and endings.** Balls, iron and scraps keep 01's components (burn, chill, a five-second `Frozen`, douse) and targets the kit's effects. Thermal shock, a shatter, a steam burst and a conduction jump each run through `Cascades`, a saved queue that `cascade_run` works through in the overlap phase: at most 48 due entries a step, by due step, distance from the chain's origin, then target save ID; one entry a kind per target and one jump per strike's target; an add past 16,384 dropped and counted.
- **Lightning,** slot 4: a bolt from the sky onto the aim point or the nearest conductive thing within 160 px, stopping at the first tile or static collider on its way down; 40 shock to the targets within 40 px, the caster spared, lighting nothing; one chain a strike, decided a jump a step, to the nearest conductor within 256 px not yet in it, ×0.7 a jump, six at most, none past ice.
- **The gun.** `shoot` held fires twelve rounds 0.12 s apart, all back 1.2 s after the last; a bullet is the kit's projectile with a trigger hitbox, and `gun_hits` makes one decision a step from fractions of its step's path: the kit's hurtbox hit (6 physical with a 120 px/s push, which can crit), its wall or range end, or the first ball (900 px/s over its mass, a frozen ball shattering) or iron block (a turn about its face, twice at most), each swept with its own motion. The kit's `charges.all` refill starts at the first spent charge, so the ability counts the rounds with an hour's refill, and 01 times the reload (`Reload`) and calls `Abilities::refill`.
- **Numbers, the dummy, the summon and hints.** Damage numbers float in their types' styles through `float_label`, while the Damage numbers option is on: a type's plain parts on one target merge while they arrive within 0.6 s, floated 0.6 s after the last or 1 s after the first, and a step's crit parts on one target float at once as one sum at 24 in the crit yellow. The training dummy (400 health, no window, unlit so the kit's flash shows) stands in the Lantern clearing through the map's last layer, `dummies`, and ends in one burst by its state. Slot 0 summons the type `summon_next` and `summon_prev` pick, four live at most, the oldest going, refused over a solid tile. The first freeze, shatter, steam burst and shock in a save toast a hint.
- **The save,** schema 5: `dummies`, `gone`, `cascades`, `bullets`, `hints` and `summon`, and the player's `buckets`, `sources` and `reload`. `migrate(4)` moves every save ID from schema 4's player's up by the map's one dummy and gives the player `move_speed` 560 and the `lightning`, `gun` and `summon` abilities ready; schemas 1 to 3 stay refused.

**Why:** The design's stop (b): the elements and weapons a real game needs, on the kit wherever it has the mechanic, and a save that keeps every chain. Each kit addition is one the game needed and a second genre's recipe uses: a source named after a load credits a burn in the top-down and shmup recipes, a rung a tag skips builds a frost through a held chill and an acid through a held eat, and a game's own numbers float crits gold from code. A saved queue makes each ending's order a property of the queue, not the frame rate, and its cap bounds a pile's chain. Measured: a full pit's chain ran 483 runs, its queue peaking at 808 with none dropped; the full pit saves in 2,034,937 bytes, under the 2 MiB bound; a loaded run kept with the uninterrupted one at 0.00 px, fresh and churned; the kit's rows read `damage_resolve` 1.04, `damage_resolve_typed` 1.06 and `ailment_feed` 1.00 against 0.94's. The step 1 API critique's one finding held (pending grants were matched exactly) and was fixed before the rest.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-173`, `D-176`, `D-177` and `D-197` as their marker lines say.
- For the freeze: `api/tungsten-kit.txt` 14 lines added and none removed, `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt` 1; no break. The kit hash moves to `0x3334_9665_de0c_501a`, its top-down fixture's chilled rung blocked by chilled.
- Tests: the kit's `set_source_restores_a_loaded_instances_source_by_serial`, `float_label_floats_a_games_number_in_its_style`, `label_style_follows_the_deepest_type_then_its_ancestors`, `a_steady_stream_climbs_past_a_blocked_rung`, `a_pending_grant_skips_a_blocked_rung`, `an_undeclared_blocked_by_tag_fails_the_load` and `a_crit_requests_parts_carry_the_flag`; six recipes in the umbrella's `kit_recipes`; 159 in example 01's `tests/game/` (`combat`, `cascades`, `lightning`, `gun`, `dummy`, `saves` among them).
- Limits, in the known issues: the kit's damage and effect queues are not saved; `charges.all` refills from the first spent charge; the dummy draws unlit, and (c) decides the lit sprites' flash.
- Open, in the backlog: a kit chain-reaction queue, impact damage by speed and mass, a conduction payload, and a public refill countdown for a reload sweep.

## D-201 — Example 01 (c) landed: five enemies on the kit's state machine, with line of sight, alerts and holds in 01's code, the difficulty as stat modifiers, throws credited to their thrower, drops, a combat layer and their sounds, save schema 6; the kit gains `StateMachine`'s and `TopDownMover`'s saved forms, `StateMachine::pending` and `set_held`, `Hitbox::set_hit_targets` and `ContactDamage::set_touches`
**Amended by D-202:** the save schema only; schema 7 marks 01 (d)'s map and every older schema, 4 to 6 included, is refused, `migrate(4)` and `migrate(5)` gone; `MAP_ENEMIES` is 13; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-209:** the troll's knockback and chill only; a kind whose prefab resists knockback whole takes no push from 01's hits and pushes, and the troll's two-stage chill becomes every freezable creature's graded chill under the same `deep_chill` source; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-210:** the bestiary, the troll and the alert's sting only; the goblin is a sixth kind; each kind keeps its own recovery (`KindInfo::recover`), the troll's 0.35 s, and the troll's slam tells for 0.8 s, with a swipe under 90 px; each fresh alert sounds the sting (`Sound::Alerted`), where the `spotted` cue rule did; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-21
**Decision:** M75 builds example 01's third stop on the kit:

- **The kit.** `StateMachine<S>` serializes (`S: Serialize + Copy`) its state, previous state, elapsed, pending `go`, whether its first visit ran and whether it is held: a loaded machine runs no enter hook again and enters a pending `go` at its first visit, the old state's exit hook first. `pending()` reads that `go`; `set_held(true)` holds a machine, whose visit is then skipped whole, so no hook runs and its elapsed stands; `is_held()` reads it. `Hitbox::hit_targets` and `set_hit_targets` read and name again an activation's targets, so a loaded activation hits none twice; `ContactDamage::touches` and `set_touches`, with the new `ContactTouch` (a target, the seconds since it was hurt and since they met), keep each touch's schedule through a load. `TopDownMover` serializes its speed, direction, rates and kept velocity, its filter and first-step flag left out, and a not-started kinematic mover keeps its kept velocity through its still first step, so a loaded mover resumes from it. The saved forms leave entities out, for the game to name again by its own IDs.
- **The enemies,** from prefabs: the fire wisp (roams 160 px about its home, burns by touch, lights the scene, drifts to and heals at burning balls, freezes), the frost crawler (walks its ledge, chills by touch, lunges after a 0.4 s tell, never into the lit lantern's light), the rust beetle (charges after a 0.8 s paw, its charge hurting enemies too; its front turns bullets as iron does and its rear takes every hit 01 builds whole, through physical penetration 1; a quarter of physical, half of fire, double shock, conductive; a brittle shatter rung; fast iron crushes it), the cave bat (roosts under the ceiling above its spawn, flashes 0.5 s, dives and climbs back; frozen, it falls and shatters on landing) and the cave troll (a slam after 1.2 s within 160 px of its fist; lifts an iron block, a bowling ball, or a frozen enemy or dummy and throws it at the player on a ballistic arc; regenerates unless burning; no knockback; slowed rather than pulled by the black hole; slowed in two stages, frozen briefly and stunned only past four times the others' needs; a bar over its head). Each thinks on the kit's state machine; 01 adds the sight (casts over the tiles and the slabs' layer), the alert passed to the enemies within 320 px of the one that saw, the hold of a dead, dormant (past 1,200 px), frozen or stunned enemy's machine, a frozen flyer's fall on a borrowed controller, and the difficulty's factor on health and damage as `Stats` modifiers. The map's last layer places nine; the summon slot summons each kind, six live at most, the oldest going.
- **Throws and impacts.** A held object keeps its entity and save ID, its body a sensor that 01 puts at the troll's hands each step and gives back at the throw or the drop; a troll that dies, freezes, is stunned or goes drops it. `Throws` credits a thrown body's first impact on a live target, the player among them, to its thrower while it lives, at an enemy's kind's mass and the thrower's damage factor kept from the throw; the player takes impact damage from thrown bodies alone, and the crush rule spares the player for a thrown block.
- **Drops, music and sounds.** Each enemy's `killed` cue rolls `enemy_drops` (a health orb 30, a mana orb 30, nothing 40; the troll's `troll_drops`, three rolls): bodiless pickups filtered to `team.player`, drawn by the player's new `pickup_radius` (96), fading after 20 s, whose pickup queues `orb_heal` (20) or `orb_mana` (25) in the overlap phase. `music_combat` fades in over 1.5 s while an awake enemy sees the player and out over 3 s after the last. Tells, attacks, the troll's steps and roar sound through `Sounded` events and their rules.
- **The frozen look.** A cube of ice fitted to each kind's hurtbox plus 2 px a side on a full frame, the size classes' cubes for the rest and a small cube for a frozen ball, in place of (b)'s flat block and round shell, with icy particles on a frozen target.
- **The save,** schema 6: `enemies` (each by its save ID, with its driver and a frozen flyer's mover set aside, its machine, what it knows, its health, stats, effects and buckets, its hitbox's targets and touches by save ID, and a troll's held object), `throws` and `orbs`, the map's dead enemies in `gone`. `migrate(5)` moves every save ID from schema 5's player's up by the map's nine enemies and gives the player `pickup_radius` 96, and `migrate(4)` runs through it; schemas 1 to 3 stay refused.

**Why:** The design's stop (c): the enemies a real game needs, on the kit wherever it has the mechanic. Each kit addition is one a load needed to resume exactly, and a second genre's recipe uses it: the umbrella's top-down guard saved mid-swing and shmup drone saved mid-volley resume with the same states and hits per step at three frame rates. Line of sight, the alert and the hold stay in 01: no kit item senses, and a game holds a machine for its own reasons. A throw keeps its thrower's save ID and damage factor rather than its entity, so a throw whose troll dies or leaves in flight keeps its damage and credits no one, through a load too. Measured: the kit's `top_down_mover_bodiless` medians read 20.83 and 20.36 ns per entity at 1,000 and 10,000 against 20.87 and 20.82, ratio 0.98; a loaded run with a bat mid-dive and a wisp mid-search kept with the uninterrupted one, states equal and within 4.28 px, fresh and churned; a full pit with the map's nine enemies saves in 2,064,662 bytes, under 2 MiB. The step 1 API critique raised no concerns.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-158`, `D-175` and `D-200` as their marker lines say.
- For the freeze: `api/tungsten-kit.txt` 63 lines added and none removed, `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt` unchanged; no break. The kit hash stays `0x3334_9665_de0c_501a`.
- Tests: ten in the kit (`tests/{state_machine,hit,projectile,contact,top_down}.rs`), the umbrella's `a_guard_saved_mid_swing_resumes_exactly` and `a_drone_saved_mid_volley_resumes_exactly`; 209 in example 01's `tests/game/` (`enemies` and `saves` among them).
- Limits, in the known issues: the enemies draw unlit through `damage_flash`, since a lit sprite's flash drops its material; a loaded top-down mover takes its still first step as a loaded controller does; the hazards' contact times stay unsaved; iron and a beetle's front end a shot the kit's pass ended later in the same step; a troll facing left draws its status overlays 20 px right of its hurtbox.
- Open, in the backlog: a lit sprite's flash (amending `D-061`), a kit hit that carries its angle to its target, and kit sensing with the alert passed to the entities near the one that saw.

## D-202 — Example 01 (d) landed: a new level of six areas and five transitions with new tile families, layered backgrounds, scenery, each area's light, far strip, night sky and ambience, the hollow's lava and the mine's iron; save schema 7 refuses the old map's saves; the kit's projectiles meet walls by their physics layers, turning, passing or ending, with `ProjectileBounced`, `ProjectilePassed` and the wall on `ProjectileExpired`
**Amended by D-205:** the save schema only; schema 8 migrates schema 7, appending the staff light's ability, and saves `lantern_guttered`; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-208:** the hollow's lava, the mine's open cut, the cave mouth and the save's IDs only; the lava lake is two pools round its stone and the shelf pool a column wider, each a surface only the player meets that bites, burns and throws, crusted by the ice spray; deck 4 is an elevator, the only way to an upper level with a fourth beetle; the hollow's mouth is drawn, the pool ending against fallen stones and the rising steam gone; the map's objects take save IDs only when a save names them (schema 10); the rest stand.
**Amended by D-210:** the map's enemies and loose objects only; seven goblins join its `enemies` layer (26 enemies, save IDs 28 to 34) and nine crates and five kegs its markers, which a fresh run sets down after the iron; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-22
**Decision:** M76 builds example 01's fourth stop, level C since the owner's reviews of 2026-10-22:

- **The kit.** `Projectile` gains `bounce_layers` (every layer by default) and `pass_layers` (none), with builders and readers. A wall's layers are a tile's layer 0 and a static collider's `RigidBody::layers` (layer 0 without a body), and a wall is in a mask when one of its layers is, passing first. In a step the sweep meets walls in order: each passed sends `ProjectilePassed` and the sweep goes on; the first not passed turns the projectile when it is in the bounce layers and bounces are left, sending `ProjectileBounced`, or ends it, sending `ProjectileExpired` with the wall. A wall it starts the step inside is behind it, and tiles passed are met where it enters terrain from open space, once a step. `WallContact` carries the wall's entity (none for a tile), normal and layers; `ProjectileExpired::wall` is `None` at a hit or the range's end, `new` keeps its signature and `at_wall` builds one with a wall. The scene form takes lists of layer numbers 0 to 31, the saved form the masks, left out at their defaults, so older saves load as before. `ProjectilePlugin` registers both queues; no cue event and no trace kind.
- **The level,** 430 × 72 tiles, kill row 71: the Lantern clearing with today's start and a 24 × 32 ball pit (the owner's last review halved its width), a free chute at each end, a rope bridge in two decks over its middle with a gap between them, and ten shelves zig-zagging up its middle to that gap; the Broken aqueduct on its arcade, the way on climbing ledges, up through a fallen span onto its channel and over two more fallen spans, the floor below a lower way over rubble with a lift up the third span; the Waterfall ravine, the channel falling over its rim into a pool, the way down seven rocks jutting into the shaft back and forth across the falls, a lift by its east ledges; the Ember hollow, a basalt cave with a lava lake round a stepping stone and a pool in a shelf; the Iron mine, an open cut with iron on its terraces' faces and wooden scaffolds; the Troll bridge on arches over a deep river, the map's east wall. Thirteen enemies; no spikes or roaming fireballs (the owner's last review took them out; the generator still places them from `hazards`); five moving decks; eight routes, every pair landing with at most one jump.
- **The art,** from the art review's sixth draw: masonry undersides, the pit's lining, the channel, columnar basalt, wet and mine rock by open-side mask in four variants seamed on both sides, iron cladding, borders between families and the bridge's deck; in the background layer the aqueduct's arcade (and the bridge's in front), piers, the parapet, the gorge's far wall, the cut's far terraces, cave backs and tunnel mouths; five far strips, `sky_black` on the palette's true black and three mist bands; props tiled or cut `_big_`; 21 looping clips (water, river, pool, falls, pour, lava, lavafall, campfire); six 6 s ambience loops at 22,050 Hz and `splinter`.
- **The generator.** A ground platform's style names its family; a mine cell is iron when it has open air on its left, right or below, or stands one cell behind such a face, and none above: iron leaves the collision layer for merged static boxes in physics layer 2, wooden decks' slabs take layer 3 and stone stays in 1. An indoor area's open cells under its roof take cave backs, `backs` spans fill the outdoor background, `river` fills the river's depths and `lava` basins become lava props and a hazard each, the lake's with its light. `validate_layout` holds the areas' columns, the tests' sky, at most 10 map lights in any 92 × 52 view, the bats' roofs, the fires' sweeps, the pit and its shell, iron under no open air, closed basins and outdoor backs.
- **The game.** `Area` gains `indoor`, `ambience`, `backdrop` and `night`; `level_light` eases an indoor area's ambient (0.30, 0.28, 0.32) and the moon to 0 over 1 s of real time and back outdoors, snapping on a run's first frame; the backdrop draws each outdoor area's far strip and the night sky by its share of the view, crossfading 5 tiles each side of a border; `Ambience` raises the current area's loop over 1.5 s and lowers the rest. `Hazard::lava` hurts as a fire does and is never doused. Sight and roosts meet iron and wood as slabs. The bullet turns twice off iron and passes wood; the missile banks once off iron; `gun_hits` takes the kit's turn as the step's stop and answers each meeting (sparks and `ricochet`, a puff and `splinter`, dust at a wall). A fresh run sets four loose iron blocks at the `iron` markers on the cut.
- **The save,** schema 7: `SaveGame` keeps its shape, and the trait's default `migrate` refuses every older schema.

**Why:** The design's stop (d), widened by the owner's reviews of 2026-10-22: more tile variants, background and scenery, areas flowing into each other, projectiles meeting the level, lightning reaching the beetles and a deep pass over the art, settled in six draws. Core puts every tile in physics layer 0 and a cast's hit carries no layer, so a wall that turns or passes shots must be a static body in a layer of its own; the kit chooses by layers, and a second genre's recipe takes it (the platformer's bolt off armour, the top-down's arrow through hedges, each saved mid-flight). Every map object takes a save ID in layer order, so a new map renumbers them all and moves every place; a migration would keep a sandbox's stats for little. Measured: `projectile_tick` medians 116.50 → 111.56 µs at 1,000 and 1.1693 → 1.1257 ms at 10,000, within 5 %; the kit hash unchanged; all 56 route pairs land on the controller; the playthrough walks `main` in 4,026 frames at full health; a full pit with 13 enemies saves in 2,079,145 bytes, under 2 MiB; C1's reading of a full pit with six enemies awake, release, headless: `total` p50 6.306 ms, p95 6.438 ms, on the 48 × 20 pit, owed again on the 24 × 32 one before C1 takes it. The step 1 API critique raised no concerns.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-175`, `D-194` and `D-201` as their marker lines say.
- For the freeze: `api/tungsten-kit.txt` 138 lines added and none removed, `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt` 4 added and the `projectile` summary and events lines changed; no break. The kit hash stays `0x3334_9665_de0c_501a`.
- Tests: five wall tests in the kit's `tests/projectile.rs`; the umbrella's `a_bolt_turns_off_armour_and_ends_at_a_tile_and_resumes_after_a_load` and `an_arrow_passes_hedges_and_ends_at_a_stone_wall_and_resumes_after_a_load`; 209 in example 01, the route test's 56 pairs, the areas' five crossings with ambience and light, the playthrough, the gun's wall test, the lava case and the refusal of schemas 1 to 6 among them, and the ignored capture `frame_time::a_full_pit_with_enemies_awake`.
- Limits, in the known issues: past 16 lights in view the renderer keeps them in query order, so the player's lantern can drop.
- Open, in the backlog: a cue event for a projectile's wall meetings, and materials on tiles.

## D-203 — Core physics keeps the tile collision proxies across steps: scanned when a map loads or changes, with their pair grid, their spatial-index entries and each unchanged sensor's tile pairs
**Date:** 2026-10-22
**Decision:** The physics step's cost of a tile map no longer grows with the map while the map stays as it is, and no query result, candidate order, event or digest changes (`D-080`'s rule):

- **The gather.** `PhysicsBuffers` keeps the collision layers' tile proxies (keys, face masks, sensor identities) with the maps they came from: each map's entity, origin bits, tile and map sizes, and every collision layer's index among the map's layers with its tiles. Each step compares the maps, in query order, with that record, the tile slices whole; a difference, or a map added, gone or reordered, scans every map again as before and bumps the cache's version. The gather appends the kept tiles after the entity proxies, so proxy order, keys and identities are a scan's.
- **The pair grid.** Entity proxies stage at every pair build; the tiles stage once per cache version, cell size and inflation (`2·linear_slop`) into a grid of their own, ids offset by the entity count, their inflated boxes kept for the pair arrays. While the tiles outnumber a build's moving proxies, every pair-grid query (the build, a repair, the arrival pass, the safety-net sweep) walks the two through `broadphase::for_each_in_both`, cell by cell, a cell's entity entries before its tiles, the order one grid staging every proxy in proxy order returns; otherwise the build stages the kept tile boxes into the entity grid after the entity proxies, that one grid, since each moving proxy's query pays the walk and each build the staging. The sweep's statics-only grid stages the entity statics and walks the same tile grid.
- **The substep.** Its passes whose bodies skip tiles (gravity, integration, travel, the tripped budgets, the sweep's outer loop) walk the entity proxies only.
- **The spatial index.** Its tile entries, their grid and the corners of their union are kept per cache version and cell size; the entity proxies are restaged each step, and sensors, a few and often large, are matched by their boxes. Each part gives every one of its ids when a query box spans more than four times its entries' cells, else its grid's; the bounds are the exact union of the entries' corners. Every query decides by its exact tests, ties by id, so these candidate sets give the same answers as one grid's.
- **The sensor pass.** A sensor whose identity, centre, shape and mask, and the tile cache's version, match what the previous step's pass saw reuses that pass's sorted tile pairs, none of which can be new, and tests only bodies; any other sensor tests every tile as before. The step's pairs merge sorted, and its events come in a full test's order (each sensor's bodies, then its tiles, in index order). A step without sensors forgets the kept pairs with the rest.

**Why:** The owner's measurement of 0.96 against 0.97 (2026-10-22; Windows, RX 7800 XT, 3840 × 2160): `physics_step` 0.34 → 1.93 ms a fixed step with one to five dynamic bodies, the map's collision tiles 1,820 → 9,803, and every seventh frame a spike. The engine's physics had not changed between the two (`git diff --stat v0.96.0 HEAD -- crates/` touches the kit's projectile alone). On the reference machine (release, a New Game standing at the spawn, 10,108 proxies, 5 bodies all asleep) the step took 1.878 ms of a 2.52 ms frame: the profile put 64 % of it in the sensor pass (six area sensors as tall as the map, each paired with every tile under it, those pairs tested, sorted and searched every step), 20 % in the pair build's grid (each 64 px tile inflated over 3 × 3 cells) and the rest in the gather's scan and the substep's passes. Kept, the step takes 0.153 ms there (0.96 read 0.46 ms) and the frame 0.780 ms; a full pit's idle frame 5.55 → 3.73 ms and C1's frame-time reading 5.745 → 3.897 ms `total` p50; windowed at the spawn, 1080p, 90 → 120 fps, the extra GPU time 0.97 showed there (a scene pass slow on 44 % of frames, the iGPU's clock ramping after the longer CPU frame, which 0.96 with a 1.7 ms busy-wait reproduced) gone to 1 %. The bench rows, against a `git archive` of `a2054ee` in one interleaved sitting, A/A clean: `integrated` `improved` (`total` p50 8.17 → 7.59 and 7.52 ms, `physics_step` 5.38 → 4.71), `physics` and `physics-sparse` `unchanged`, digests 3 of 3. Walking both grids for every query read `physics` `regressed` (`physics_step` +8.9 %, the visitor outlined over 8,000 queries a build), which the cost rule removed; example 01's differential probe (a full pit set alight, sprayed, struck and burning out over 1,710 frames, every body's place and velocity bits and every collision and sensor event hashed per frame) prints the same hashes as before, to the bit. Merged boxes, which change contacts and a tile's sensor identity, were not needed.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-066`, `D-080` and `D-154` as their marker lines say.
- Tests: core's `a_two_grid_walk_returns_one_grids_order` (both table layouts, exclusion, dedupe), `both_pair_grid_layouts_build_the_same_pairs` (the two grid choices, pair for pair), `the_tile_cache_matches_a_fresh_scan_after_every_change` (an edit, a move, a second map, a render layer before the collision layer, a despawn; no scan when nothing changed), `a_changed_linear_slop_restages_the_tile_grid` and `kept_sensor_tile_pairs_are_what_testing_every_tile_finds` (a brute-force oracle of the pairs and the new-pair events over 48 steps with a still, a moving and a masked sensor, a map edit and a step without sensors); `sweep_grid_returns_the_pair_grids_statics_in_order` now compares the two-grid walk with one grid holding the tiles; the determinism, containment and tunneling hashes unchanged.
- Memory: a copy of each collision layer's tiles (about 124 KB for example 01's map) and the kept proxies, boxes and index entries beside the per-step arrays.
- Each step still copies the kept tiles into its proxy arrays and compares the collision layers, about 31,000 tiles for example 01; the sleep pass's support comparison still walks the tiles while anything sleeps.

## D-204 — The template's autosave writes over an autosave of a schema the game refuses, after the empty ones, and over a damaged one only when no other autosave slot can be written; a newer build's never
**Date:** 2026-10-22
**Decision:** `SlotState` gains `Older(u64)`, a save of an older schema this build cannot read (`SaveError::Migration`), with when it was written; the save screen labels it "Saved by an older version". An autosave takes, in order, an empty autosave slot (by number), one an older schema left (the oldest by its header's time, then by number), the oldest readable one (as before), and a damaged one (the lowest number) only when none of those can be written, which then stops being read-only; a newer build's is never written. A manual slot of a refused older schema stays read-only for the run, as a damaged one does. The rotation loads the three autosaves alone, not every slot, though it still reads every slot's header through `SaveSlots::list` for their times; a slot without a user folder reads as off. Example 01 takes the shell by copy.

**Why:** Example 01's schema 7 (`D-202`) refuses every older save, and the shell marked a refused save Damaged and kept it for the run, so an upgrader whose three autosaves were 0.96's schema 6 never autosaved again ("No autosave slot can be written"), each attempt parsing all thirteen slots once more. `D-160` keeps a newer build's save from an older build, and `D-181` keeps a slot that fails to load for the run; both hold for what they protect. A refused older autosave can never load in this build or a later one, so the rotation may take it as it takes an empty one; a damaged autosave is kept as long as any other slot can take the save.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-181` (a slot that fails to load is kept for the run) and `D-199` (the rotation's order) as their marker lines say.
- Tests: the template's `older_autosaves_take_the_next_autosaves`, `a_newer_autosave_is_never_written` and `damaged_autosaves_give_way_only_when_nothing_else_can`; example 01's `schema_6_autosaves_get_an_autosave`, and `schemas_one_to_six_are_refused` reading them as `Older`. The shell is 2,742 lines of code of its 2,900 and the template's `src/` 3,251 of 3,400; `templates/basic/AGENTS.md` took the rule at 12,287 of its 12,288 bytes.

## D-205 — Example 01 (e) landed: the held spells' frame-time collapse and the full pit's per-ball scans fixed, the playtest's bugs and tuning, a staff light that burns mana and holds the crawlers off, save schema 8 migrating schema 7; widened past the feedback plan's non-goals to core physics, core queries and the template's autosave; the dev profile builds 01, the kit and the umbrella at opt-level 1
**Amended by D-207:** the staff light's lit start, the death's three seconds, the killing hit's hurt and the banner's face only; a new run starts with the light off and a load keeps its own, game over comes two game seconds after the death, the killing hit plays the death's sound, and the banner is Source Serif 4 Bold over no box; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-22
**Decision:** M77 builds example 01's fifth stop from the owner's playtest notes of 2026-10-21 (the feedback plan's (e) rows), widened by the owner's prompt of 2026-10-22 past that plan's non-goals:

- **Performance in 01.** The feedback's B1 and P1–P4 patches: a spray looks the world's maps up once for its traces (`SolidTiles`), not with a query and a registry lookup per sampled point, and gathers the bodies its rays can reach once (`traced_near`), not every body per trace; `fire_contacts`, `body_impacts` and `toys_bounds` test their cheap condition first and `ball_look` reads one query. P5 buckets only the unburnt balls within a touch of the burning ones; P6 tests each conditional scan's cheap condition first: the troll's lift and slam by reach, the cascade bin's ID map for its due targets only, the gun's swept bodies inside its shots' box, the hole's pull by reach before the body's response, iron's friction through a slot bitset, no hole particles without a hole, newborn sparks by age, one particle pass for every drained burst, lightning's conductors from query columns, and the crush's smash list read after the impact. Each keeps its results: 01's tests and the physics probe's hashes are unchanged.
- **The engine and the template.** Core physics keeps a tilemap's collision proxies across steps (`D-203`); the World keeps each query shape's matching archetypes (`D-206`, the feedback's X6, taken on its measurement); the template's autosave writes over an older schema's autosaves (`D-204`).
- **The GPU's share** of the new level: 0.97's GPU passes at the spawn were 0.96's, and its extra GPU time was the integrated GPU's clock ramp after a longer CPU frame, gone with `D-203` (slow scene frames 44 % → 1 %). 01's backdrop drops the quads that fully opaque tiles cover and the midnight sky under a full night of the black one, frame for frame the same in captures at eight spots.
- **The bugs.** B2: both readouts on one line, health rounded up while alive and mana down; B3: an area banner of 01's own, one name at a time, in the theme's heading face; B5, X5, T6: the troll's freeze at 1 × its threshold within its chill, `DEEP_CHILL` 0.5, a 3 s freeze, its stun at 3 ×, creatures frozen 7.5 s, iron's 5 s kept; B6: bloom 0.22 → 0.6, the halos and the moon's glow discs following the Bloom option; B7, F12: a hit's number floats at once, follow-ups within 0.25 s merging, burn and bleed ticks smaller; B8: the bolt keeps the cursor's x and falls to the first body, tile or static box; F13: game over three game seconds after the death, the body dropping straight down on its controller, its input locked; X1: the hurt's sound and shake on a blow or the killing hit, never a tick, through a `player_hurt` cue event; I1: the ball cap checked before the spend; I2: a held spell refused for mana stays off until the cast is released; I8, B4: the crawler reads the drawn glow's centre and reach and, held at its edge, shows a new scared clip with a hiss and a puff of frost breath; I16: a test of each Rust constant that restates data; I18: the zoom snaps to its 0.25 grid; I20: the README and the shatter hint.
- **Tuning.** Every spell's cost halved; regeneration 3.0 a tenth of a second; the bowling ball 0.3 s, the push 1 s, the black hole 0.5 s.
- **The staff light** (L5's first half): the `staff_light` ability costs 0.05 mana a step and its effect grants `staff.lit`; used each step the light is on, before the spells, its cost handed back at full mana, where the regeneration covers it; refused, the light gutters with a puff and a sound and relights at 10 mana; lit at a run's start; the `lantern` action labelled "Staff light".
- **The save,** schema 8: `migrate` appends the staff light's entry to a schema 7 save's abilities, editing its JSON through core's `Value`; `lantern_guttered` is saved, and a migrated save has none.
- **The dev profile** (P8): `tungsten`, `tungsten-kit` and `example-01-platformer` at opt-level 1, beside `tungsten-core` (`D-096`).

**Why:** The owner's notes named the held spells' collapse first, and the plan measured each step on this machine (`perf-runs/20261022-m77-example-01-fixes/`). From 0.97 to this release, p50, release / dev: the idle spawn 2.504 → 0.485 / 4.701 → 0.640 ms; an ice spray into air 13.740 → 0.569 / 45.663 → 0.734; the flamethrower 10.109 → 0.546 / 32.537 → 0.711; a full pit idle 5.937 → 3.425 / 14.843 → 4.555; ice beside a full pit 20.173 → 3.877 / 72.105 → 5.328; the frame-time harness (a full pit, six enemies awake) 6.091 → 3.547 release. P8 alone, dev: the idle spawn 2.627 → 0.974 ms and ice beside a full pit 15.188 → 5.549. P6 with the gun firing into a full pit: 32.0 → 29.3 ms. P7, a `Spent` marker for burnt-out balls, was measured first as the plan asked: the spent pit's walkers cost about 0.05 ms a release frame (`fire_tick` 0.014 ms, the extract 0.024 ms more, the rest under 0.01 ms each), under its 0.3 ms bar, so it moved to (f).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-194` (the stops after (d): (e)'s widened scope) and `D-202` (schema 8 migrates schema 7) as their marker lines say.
- Measured and kept: with P6 a full pit idle reads 0.10 ms more in the build pair that toggled it, all of it in `physics_step`, whose code P6 does not touch and whose hashes match; the X6 build carrying P6's code read 2.039 ms against 2.008 without it, so the cost follows the build's layout. Recorded, not tuned.
- Not met: step 4's "the hollow at least 3 ms lower": its view keeps a band of sky above the cave, so the view-sized sky and strips stay whole, and a textured quad cannot be clipped exactly; four areas gained 0.1 to 1.3 ms of scene pass at 2160p and none lost.
- Tests: 01's 223, with `hud::full_mana_reads_100_of_100_on_one_line`, `areas::a_new_area_replaces_the_banner`, `enemies::a_steady_ice_spray_freezes_the_troll`, `enemies::a_crawler_is_scared_at_the_drawn_light`, `combat::a_hit_floats_its_number_at_once`, `lightning::a_bolt_falls_to_the_first_body_under_the_cursor`, `player::the_dead_fall_for_three_seconds_before_game_over`, `hazards::a_burn_tick_plays_no_hurt_but_a_killing_one_does`, `camera::zoom_returns_to_one`, `spells::the_staff_light_burns_mana_and_gutters_out`, `saves::schema_7_autosaves_migrate_and_rotate`, `saves::a_guttered_staff_light_loads_guttered` and `data::every_restated_constant_matches_its_data`; the template's 164; the probe's hashes (the first burnout at frame 536, `0b1847b3fbaa98b5` through the frame before, `fe42acd1fa85b639` after 1,710 frames) at every step.
- Moved to (f): P7 with its numbers, and P2's rainbow tint table (0.26 ms a frame for 12,000 unburnt balls, which `tests/game/spawning.rs` pins). Recorded: the kit gaps the stop worked around (a cue filter for a tick against a blow, a draining effect) in the backlog; `World::has`'s linear scan, the clip name hashed twice a sprite, the spatial index's every-step rebuild of a sleeping pit and the autosave rotation's header reads in the known issues.
- Owner checks: a playthrough; the bloom's strength; the scared clip; the gutter's and the scared crawler's sounds.

## D-206 — World queries keep each query shape's matching archetypes, one bit an archetype, each archetype tested once per shape
**Date:** 2026-10-22
**Decision:** The archetype store keeps a bitset per query shape, one bit an archetype in archetype order, for the archetypes the shape's data (`QueryData::matches`) and filter (`QueryFilter::matches`) select. `Archetypes::matching` tests every archetype the first time a shape runs, and after that only the archetypes added since; archetypes are only ever added (`find_or_create`) and an archetype's types never change. A shape is keyed by the addresses of those two functions: a function the compiler copies into two places gets a second entry with the same bits, and two it folds into one share code, so their bits agree. `query_slices_filtered` and `query_mut_slices_filtered`, which every query form goes through, read one bit per archetype where they tested its type list, and collect the matching archetypes' slices into a vector before returning its iterator. Rows still come in archetype order, then row order. The cache is boxed behind a `RefCell` in the store, so `World` keeps its auto traits and no API snapshot changes.

**Why:** Example 01 builds 146 queries a frame over 255 to 271 archetypes, and each tested every archetype's type list: 1.76 µs a walk at the spawn and 1.85 µs over a full pit, in release, about 0.26 ms a frame, past the 0.1 ms M77's Q6 set for taking it (example 01 feedback X6 and K6: 5.3 µs a query in dev). An archetype's match never changes, so keeping it changes no result or order. A first version filtered the archetypes lazily through the bits inside the returned iterator: that longer chain kept the callers' row loops from optimizing, and the `ecs` bench row's update stage rose 17.7 % (`regen` 2.7×, `integrate` 2.2×) in both pairs; with the vector they read as before. Measured, the bench's CPU rows base against candidate in two interleaved pairs: `physics`, `physics-sparse`, `gpu-throughput`, `particles`, `churn` and `integrated` without a regressed owned metric, `ecs`'s update stage 2.3 % and 2.4 % lower, first-run digests 7 of 7; example 01's frame-time harness 3.947 → 3.547 ms and its idle spawn 0.792 → 0.485 ms in release.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-135` (the query cost model) as its marker line says.
- Justified regression: the `ecs` row's `system.faction_histogram` p50 rose 0.02 ms (8.1 % and 8.9 %, its p95 7.1 % and 9.4 %), one read loop over 250,000 rows compiling slightly differently over the vector's iterator, while `integrate`, `tint` and `age_phase` fell 0.03 to 0.04 ms and the update stage 0.25 ms.
- Tests: core's `a_query_shape_sees_the_archetypes_added_after_its_first_run`; every workspace test, `just physics-release` and example 01's probe hashes unchanged; `just api-check` current.
- One base suite's `integrated` row read invalid when the remote-desktop encoder started during its runs 4 and 5; its pair's other rows and the other pair stand.

## D-207 — Example 01's quick fixes landed: the kit's `ImmunePart`, so fire and lava blow a frozen wisp apart; a crawler lunge that lands and a frozen enemy that touches nothing; four bats and a leashed crawler; eight placed health orbs; glowing orbs, a two-second death with its clip and sound, quieter ambience, bold area titles, the staff light off at a run's start and closed cave ceilings; save schema 9
**Date:** 2026-10-22
**Decision:** M78 runs the owner's quick fixes of 2026-10-22 (the feedback plan's §3.1a, a stop between (e) and (f)), planned and run in one session, with the owner's review of in-game captures of every item before the test pass:

- **The kit** (gap K15): `ImmunePart`, an event for each part of a hit that the target's immunity drops (a resist factor of 0), in part order, whether the rest of the hit lands or the hit is ignored, with the part's amount before the resistances, its kinds, source, position and periodic and crit flags; `HealthPlugin` registers its queue. A part of 0, a part a non-zero factor shrinks to nothing and a target that takes no hits send none; it has no trace, cue event or `Damaged`, and the landed path's arithmetic is unchanged.
- **Fire on the frozen.** 01's reactions read `ImmunePart`, so a non-periodic fire part on a frozen target queues thermal shock as a landed one does: any fire, a missile, the flamethrower or a blast, blows a frozen wisp apart, and an unfrozen one still takes none. Each lava basin carries a child touch, 34 fire on what is frozen only, with no knockback (`ex10_lava_frost_<i>`); the player's touch is unchanged.
- **The crawler.** Its touch reaches 4 px past its body on each side and 2 px above and below, so a lunge that stops flush against the player lands; its wind-up asks for the player at most `LUNGE_RISE` 64 px above it; three clips, `crawler_windup`, `crawler_lunge` and `crawler_recover`. A frozen enemy's touch is set aside until the thaw (`HeldTouch`), so a frozen crawler chills nothing and a thrown one hurts by the throw. A placement may leash a patrol to its home (`enemy.leash`, in tiles).
- **The map.** Two bats under the aqueduct's channel (columns 98 and 141) and two in the ravine (189 and 198), each passing the generator's roof check, and the falls crawler on the pool bed at 181 with a leash of 5: eighteen enemies. Eight health orbs from `level.json`'s `pickups`, each 0.6 tiles over a support the placement step checks, at least one in each area and two off the main way, in a `pickups` layer after the enemies; they never fade, and one taken stays gone through a save.
- **Orbs.** A pulsing red or blue halo through `Glow` with `light` 0, which now spawns no light, so an orb takes none of the renderer's 16; a potion sound under the pickup's ID; a burst in the orb's colour.
- **Death.** `DEATH_SECS` 2.0; the courier's `player_death` clip (stagger, buckle, kneel, slump, rest) from the first dead step, held, facing as at death; the killing hit plays `player_died`, a death sound, with a 0.8 shake in place of the hurt.
- **Ambience** at half its volume, 0.15 to 0.2.
- **Area titles.** The banner's root panel's fill and border cleared; Source Serif 4 Bold, the repository's own copy, as `serif_bold`, the serif family's second face; the title at weight 700, 34 units capped at 52, letter-spaced 0.12 em, over a shadow of eight dark copies and a drop; eased fades of 0.8 s in and 1.2 s out over 4 s, a new name waiting for the shown one's 0.25 s fade out.
- **The staff light** off at a new run's start (`Lantern::default()`); a load keeps its saved light.
- **The caves' ceilings.** The generator puts a plain back tile behind each roof's, ceiling's and ledge's lowest cell, outside the random stream, and skips a mist tile only under wholly opaque terrain: 143 back tiles and 72 mist tiles added, no other tile changed.
- **The save,** schema 9: the thirteen new map objects take save IDs before the player's, so `migrate` moves every saved ID of a schema 8 save at or above its player's up by `SHIFT_9` (13), and takes schema 7 through 8; taken placed orbs join `gone`; the leash reads as none from older saves.

**Why:** The owner's notes named each item, and their review of the captures approved the run with no notes. Each cause was found before its fix. The crawler's touch was exactly its body's width and the two kinematic controllers stop 0.01 px apart (centres 44.01 px apart against a reach of 44), so a lunge into a still player overlapped nothing; its wind-up allowed 440 px of height against a lunge that reaches under 40 px up. The ceilings' cut-away undersides (0–12 px) had no back behind them, so the unlit backdrop showed through. The banner's box was its own root, a panel the theme fills. The wisps' fire dropped in the kit before any event, so no game could react to it (the feedback's cause (a)), and lava's touch was untyped and filtered to the player (c). `damage_resolve`'s four criterion rows read within noise of the step's baseline (241.66 µs +0.91 %, 2.4749 ms −0.12 %, 582.37 µs −1.19 %, 6.0734 ms −0.07 %).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-194` (the stops after (d)) and `D-205` (the staff light's lit start, the death's three seconds and its hurt, the banner's face) as their marker lines say.
- The kit's public API grows by `ImmunePart` alone, an addition in the break ledger: `api/tungsten-kit.txt` +60 lines, `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt` only `health`'s events.
- Found by the test pass: the wider touch let a thrown frozen crawler's own chill land before the throw's impact, credited to itself and spending the player's invulnerability; the touch set aside while frozen fixes it, and the throw test fails without it.
- Tests: the kit's `an_immune_part_goes_out_beside_the_landed_ones_and_only_when_struck` and `a_hit_whose_every_part_drops_is_ignored_with_its_immune_parts_alone`; 01's 230, with `enemies::a_frozen_wisp_blows_apart_under_a_fire_missile` and `…_in_the_lava`, the three `crawler_lunge_` tests, `saves::schema_8_saves_migrate_with_the_new_map_objects_ids_moved` on a schema 8 save captured from the 0.98 code, `saves::placed_health_orbs_never_fade_and_one_taken_stays_gone_through_a_load`, `player::the_dead_fall_for_two_seconds_before_game_over`, `hazards::a_burn_tick_plays_no_hurt_and_a_killing_one_plays_the_death`, and the lit-start tests lighting first. The wisps', the lunge's and the migration's tests fail without their fixes.
- Left: I9 (the orbs' fade and an appearing sound) and cold on a burning cold-immune target, which sends no steam (the gap's other half), to (f); the vertical edge at column 211 to 0.100's cave mouth; a dark light still takes one of the 16 light slots (known issues). The owner's defaults stand: a frozen wisp survives its own blast (16 of 40), placed orbs heal 20, the hollow's ambient and the aqueduct's crawlers unchanged.
- Owner checks: a playthrough from a dark start; the potion and death sounds; the titles over bright scenery.

## D-208 — Example 01's level and title pass landed: lava a surface the player never sinks below that bites, burns and throws toward the nearer bank, a crust under the ice spray, an elevator the only way to the mine's upper level and its fourth beetle, a beetle's wind-up and charge drawn, the hollow's cave mouth; the picker's cards in the template and 01; save IDs only for what a save names, schema 10; the kit's `ContactDamage::with_push_always` and `PlatformerController::ground`
**Superseded by D-222:** the save's map IDs in layer order only; each saved map object's save ID derives from its authored name; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-22
**Decision:** M79 runs the owner's level and title pass of 2026-10-22 (the feedback plan's §3.1b, the stop between the quick fixes and (f)), planned, critiqued and run in one session, with the owner's review of in-game captures of every item before the test pass and their notes on the cave mouth applied:

- **The kit** (gaps K16 and K17). `ContactDamage::with_push_always` and `push_always` (scene `push_always`, written in the definition, left out of the saved form at false): a touch whose request the resolution ignores for the target's invulnerability window still pushes it by the touch's knockback, away from the touch's box centre, once a step, with no damage, flash, `Damaged` or cue, and a dead target takes none. `PlatformerController::ground`: the body its last step ended standing on, a moving deck, a slab or another body, as its floor probe or its landing met it; none on tiles or in the air; not saved, so a loaded controller reports none until the step after its still first one.
- **Lava.** The lake is two pools, 230–234 and 236–240, round its stone at 234–236, the shelf pool 260–263. Each basin's surface is a static body at its skin in physics layer 4 (`LAVA_LAYER`) with no mask, so only the player's controller, whose filter keeps every layer, meets it, and an enemy's controller leaves layer 4 out (`walk_filter`, set at spawn and again after a load). Its touch, from 4 px over the skin to the bed, bites 16 fire with a knockback of 1,500 every 0.1 s, push-always, so the player is thrown up and toward the pool's nearer bank, burns, and never has its feet below the surface; a sizzle sounds at the bite.
- **The crust.** A cold ray that stops on a basin's surface chills it; 0.5 s of spray (draining as fast unsprayed) sets the whole basin: its touch and its frost child's held aside, its surface in layer 0 meeting every body, its glow out, steam along it and a hiss (`lava.rs`'s `Lava` and `Crust`). It holds 6 s, its cracks glowing in three stages over the last 1.5 s, one sprite a lava cell; the melt puts the touches back, so a player on it is thrown out and what stood on it falls in.
- **The mine.** An upper level, `mine_upper` (318–332, row 37), on three timber supports, out of every jump's reach (the nearest surface 278.6 px short over every air-jump and coyote time), with a fourth beetle: nineteen enemies. Deck 4 is its elevator (`ElevatorDef { rise 1024, speed 320 }`, `elevator.rs`): at rest in a one-row collar flush with the cut floor, it starts 0.3 s after the player steps on, rises on a fresh eased stop-mode path, and goes back down 4 s after its rider leaves the top; it moves only while each controller on it reports it as its ground and stands wholly over it with 12 px to spare, and holds otherwise, so a rider is never carried into the upper level's deck and a loaded rider's still first step finds no ground; `LiftStarted` and `LiftStopped` sound; the game draws its cable from the gantry's sheave to the cage. A beetle's paw and charge are drawn: a glint swelling at its horn, puffs at its front foot every 0.15 s, three streaks behind the charge and a dust trail every 0.06 s.
- **The cave mouth.** The 15 `steam_big` props go; the tunnel under the ravine's east wall (columns 203–210) is drawn as the hollow's own rock, 8 × 7 opaque lit tiles (`cave_mouth`), darkest at the opening, ember-veined toward the hollow, a jagged lip of the ravine's wall in its first column, with stalactites under the lintel and stones and bones on its floor. The owner's notes on the captures: the ravine's pool runs out against four fallen wet stones (`pool_shore`, 3 × 2 tiles) where its wall-edge tile ended it in the air; the cleft's steam and the hollow's heat columns go (`steam_small`, `heat`); vines, glowcaps, a hung fire crystal, a stalagmite, pebbles and a fire crystal are added.
- **The picker,** in the template's shell and 01's copy: New Game shows a card for each difficulty, Normal focused, each its name, its rank's three squares filled to its place and its note's facts as lines; the template's `title.png` is unchanged, the shell 2,790 of 2,900 lines and `src/` 3,299 of 3,400.
- **The save,** schema 10: the map's objects take save IDs only when a save names them (the basins, the decks with the elevator, the dummy, the enemies, the placed orbs), in layer order, so an art change moves no save: basins 0–2, decks 3–7, the dummy 8, enemies 9–27, orbs 28–35, the player 36. `basins` (chill and crust) and `elevators` (point, stop, boarding, idle, rider, hold and an optional follower) are saved. `migrate(9)` maps every saved ID through `remap_9` (schema 9's lava, decks, dummy, enemies and orbs to their places, the rest down by 850), drops the old ping-pong deck, so the elevator starts at rest, and sets a player over a lake no higher than its banks on the nearest bank or stone; schemas 7 and 8 go through 9 first. A glow with no save ID takes its pulse's phase from its column.

**Why:** The owner's notes named each item, and their review of the captures asked only for the cave mouth's water, scenery and the two columns. Lava had been a hazard touch the player sank into while its window ignored the next touches, so a fall held the player under the surface (K16); a surface only the player's controller meets keeps the feet above it, and a push that passes the window throws again. An elevator that starts on a step needs the body its rider stands on, which no reader gave (K17). Every art change since M76 moved the props' save IDs, so schema 9 shifted them; IDs for what a save names alone end that. The plan's critiques found the migration stranding a player under a new surface, a push skipped when another hit opened the window first in the same step, a rider overhanging the deck's edge, one centre throwing a split basin's player away from the nearer bank, a reach check that missed a late air jump and a release check deleting the tool it needs; each was fixed in the plan. The two kit rows the change passes through read within ±3 % of the code before it in one sitting, against a drifted baseline.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-158` (the controller's reads), `D-175` (the contact's knockback), `D-181` (the picker), `D-187` (the note's place), `D-194` (the stops after (d)) and `D-202` (the hollow, the cut, the mouth and the save's IDs) as their marker lines say.
- The kit's public API grows by additions alone, in the break ledger: three methods and `contact_damage`'s `push_always` field line; the kit hash stays `0x3334_9665_de0c_501a`.
- Tests: the kit's `contact_damage_with_push_always_throws_a_target_inside_its_window` and `the_controller_reports_the_kinematic_body_it_stands_on`; the template's `the_picker_shows_a_card_for_each_difficulty`; 01's 237, among them `hazards::lava_throws_the_player_up_and_out_and_never_lets_them_sink`, `the_ice_spray_crusts_a_basin_that_holds_the_player_and_melts_to_throw_them`, `level::the_elevator_sweep_clears_tiles_slabs_iron_and_a_riders_headroom`, `the_mines_upper_level_is_reached_only_by_its_elevator`, `saves::schema_9_saves_migrate_to_the_new_save_ids` on a schema 9 save captured from the 0.99 code, `a_crusted_basin_and_a_riding_elevator_load_back_to_themselves` and `a_player_saved_under_a_new_lava_surface_loads_on_its_bank`.
- Seen in the captures and left: the shelf pool's throw meets the hollow's roof before the player lands; a player shoved against the stone's wall goes straight up at the melt; the falls' plume, the same family as the removed columns, stays, as the notes named the mouth only.
