- Owner checks: a playthrough through the lava, the crust and the elevator; the lift's and the lava's sounds.

## D-209 — Example 01's feel landed: pushes and missiles that knock enemies back and a level's edge that takes them, three 6 s black holes that hum, collapse and sweep up small balls under the actors, weightier iron that knocks back, splats and refuses a blocked spot, a jump that shoves off what buries the courier, blood, burning, a graded chill and warned thaws, the sprays over the enemies, key bursts, a nine-round gun reloaded on R, the missing sounds and refusals; save schema 11
**Amended by D-210:** the pushes by mass only; the troll's slam and swipe kick each dynamic body in reach by its mass too, `min(650, 2,400 / mass)` out and 0.6 of it up, where every body took a small ball's kick; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-213:** the force push and the holes' pull on flyers only; the force push is a wave along the aim (`toys::wave`), `Ring::Push` and `warp_burst` gone and `radial_push` left to the blast; a hole holds a flyer on its own mover (`toys_hold`) rather than renewing a knockback each step, and a bowling ball takes `BOWLING_PULL`; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-23
**Decision:** M80 runs example 01's sixth feedback stop (the feedback plan's §3.2), planned, critiqued in two rounds and run in one session, with no kit, engine, template or shell change:

- **Pushes.** `toys::radial_push` moves each dynamic body within reach by distance over its mass and each live enemy through the kit's `knock_back` by its kind's `pull`, the hole's response; the force push and a missile's blast go through it, the push's ring in a new gravity purple (palette `Ж`–`Щ`) with a `warp_burst`. `KindInfo::resist` restates each prefab's knockback resist, held by `tests/game/data.rs`, and `enemies::stands_firm` skips a kind that resists whole (the troll): `radial_push` passes it, and `combat::Hit::request` leaves out a hit's push on it. `toys_bounds` culls escaped enemies, so a placed one is gone in the next save. `shared::body_centre` and `shared::surface_distance` replace nine copies.
- **Black holes.** At most three (`HOLE_CAP`), 6 s each; a fourth collapses the oldest. A hole shrinks over its last 0.5 s and closes with `HoleCollapsed` and a 360 px `Ring::Collapse`. Its layers draw in 01's sprite base between the tiles and the sprites at `z_norm` 1.0, its under-layers in the gravity purple; one shared hum voice follows the nearest hole, silent past 900 px and ducked as the wisps' is; `hole_broom` takes the small balls within 24 px of a hole's centre, 12 a hole a step; each hole's particles are a third of before.
- **Iron.** Mass 400, scraps 25. `iron_weight` adds 1,800 px/s² to a block falling past 30 px/s, never held or thrown. Iron's hits push along the closing line (0.6 × the closing speed, at most 900 px/s) and apply at once in `body_impacts`, so one that kills a `flesh` target marks it (`IronKills`) and its end bursts in gore. A block landing on a still body or a tile at 900 px/s or more raises dust, a fifth of a blast's shake and `IronLanded`. `toys::iron_fits` refuses a spot with a solid tile, a body that is not dynamic or another block before the spell spends anything; the cap's oldest poofs.
- **The jump's shove.** `player_unstick` kicks the dynamic bodies over the courier's head and shoulders (1,600 px/s over their mass) and beside it (600); a jump they blocked, the courier within 2 px of its press, is launched again at `player::JUMP_SPEED` the next step, which spends no jump.
- **Effects.** Blood on a physical blow to `flesh` or the courier, a spurt when bleeding starts, denser drips; the courier red for 0.12 s after a blow (`Moves::hurt`); burning's embers, flickering ember tints, flames at 0.55 of the side on three spots over a warm glow; the chill graded by `fill / threshold` on the climb from the chill, whose rung empties the bucket, to the freeze, a second slow of up to 35 % (`chill_grades`, on `troll_chill`'s `deep_chill` source, which it sheds each step) and a frost toward frozen's tint; every freeze sounding with its frost burst; a freeze's last 0.75 s flickering to a cracked cube, its thaw in glints with a crack; the sprays' particles at z 11 over the enemies; key bursts (`shared::spawn_key_burst`) for deaths, gore, summons, shatters and refusals, which take the oldest cosmetic burst's slot at the cap; a strike's sparks and snap, a bowling ball's thud, sparks at a shot's wall end, steam from cold a burning target's immunity drops.
- **The gun.** Nine rounds 0.2 s apart; `reload` on R and pad West tops a short magazine up after 1.2 s, the trigger blocked and clicking meanwhile; a warm muzzle light; the shot's 0.1 shake.
- **Sounds.** Fourteen new one-shots (`Sound` 40–53), the holes' and the small balls' loops, the enemies' death and the dummy's thump on their `killed` cues, the push synthesized again as a gravity warp and the shot heavier; refusals through `spells::systems::refuse_cast` (no mana, not ready, and no room with a fizzle), once a press; the air jump and a spell switch sounding; a dropped orb popping and blinking over its last 3 s; the loops fading over 0.08 s.
- **The save,** schema 11: `black_holes` replaces `black_hole`; `migrate(10)` moves the one hole into the list and keeps the player's `gun` to nine rounds, a running reload running on; schemas 7 to 9 go through 10 first.

**Why:** The owner's playtest notes of 2026-10-21 named each item (the feedback plan's rows T4, T7, T8, F1–F11, X2–X4, I1, I3–I6, I9, I11–I13, I17 and I19). The plan's two critique rounds found the shove's retry spending the air jump and its ceiling disabling it, a schema 10 save keeping twelve rounds, iron's pushed hits stopping the troll, the hole under the actors only under the CPU sort, the small balls' loop outliving a pause, an iron kill indistinguishable from an iron hurt, a saved `deep_chill` with nothing to remove it and a diving bat's speed override, each fixed or recorded in the plan. The run made two changes the plan did not name: a trace showed unkicked balls over the courier's shoulders blocking the kicked column, so the shove's box covers the shoulders too; and the kit's chill rung empties the cold bucket as it applies, so the grade runs on the climb from there to the freeze rather than from 0.3 of the threshold.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-194` (the stops after (d)), `D-200` (the gun's magazine) and `D-201` (the troll's knockback and chill) as their marker lines say.
- No public API change; the kit hash stays `0x3334_9665_de0c_501a`.
- Tests: 01's 267, among them `enemies::a_force_push_throws_enemies_by_distance_and_the_troll_stands`, `a_pushed_hit_leaves_the_troll_standing`, `an_enemy_thrown_out_of_the_level_is_gone_and_stays_gone_through_a_load`, `spells::three_black_holes_stay_open_and_a_fourth_collapses_the_oldest`, `an_open_hole_sweeps_small_balls_from_its_core_a_few_a_step`, `brick::gore_follows_only_the_blow_that_kills`, `player::a_jump_under_small_balls_shoves_them_off_and_rises_clear`, `ice::a_chill_slows_and_frosts_more_as_its_bucket_fills`, `ice::a_thaw_cracks_the_cube_first_and_ends_in_glints_and_a_crack`, `presentation::a_death_at_the_burst_cap_takes_a_cosmetic_bursts_slot`, `gun::r_tops_up_a_part_empty_magazine_and_an_empty_trigger_clicks`, `spells::a_refused_cast_says_why_and_spends_nothing`, and `saves::schema_10_saves_migrate_their_black_hole_into_the_list`, `a_schema_10_trolls_deep_chill_goes_at_its_first_step` and `schema_10_saves_keep_at_most_nine_rounds` on a schema 10 save captured from the 0.100 code.
- Left: the kit's `knock_back` still launches a controller that resists the push whole at zero and locks its input, so a kit hitbox's or touch's knockback stops the troll for 0.2 s (known issues); the kit's `damaged` cue fires on a killing blow too, so a kill plays the blow's hit under the death sound; the magazine's readout shows its rounds during an R reload, for (h); a particle config has no draw depth (K2), the chill shows by tint and speed with no slower clip (K4), and the controller never pushes the bodies it meets (K7), each a backlog row with 01's meanwhile; P2's tint table goes to the Phase 6 QA pass, P7's marker and I4 are dropped.
- Owner checks: a playthrough with the new sounds and looks; the art sheet in `perf-runs/20261023-e01f-art/`.

## D-210 — Example 01 (g) props and foes landed: pine crates that burn and fall in and powder kegs on a fuse that blow through a sourced detonation in the cascade queue, a goblin sapper that throws rocks that stay as bodies and one rolling bomb in five, stabs a courier too close and hops what it can, the troll's quicker slam with its ring, grunt and shake, a swipe up close and a kick by mass, alert and search marks with one sting an alert, crates, kegs and seven goblins on the map and in the summon slot; save schema 12
**Date:** 2026-10-23
**Decision:** M81 runs example 01's (g) stop (the feedback plan's §3.3), planned, critiqued in two rounds and run in one session, with no kit, engine, template or shell change; the owner's playtest of the cut's build, in the same session, added physical rocks, rolling bombs, a livelier goblin with a stab, and four more goblins:

- **Crates and kegs** (`src/features/props/`): dynamic boxes of mass 12 and 16 that join every place iron's code names iron: the level's bounds, wood's friction (900 px/s²), a hole's pull at 0.5 and 0.4, pushes by mass, the sprays, thermal shock and shatters, the gun (a bullet passes a crate as it passes wood and ends at a keg), lightning (a keg's hoops conduct) and the troll's lift (crates, never kegs). A crate's `Fuel` burns 8 s, lights the crates and kegs within 4 px of its box once it has burned 2 s and the small balls within 2 px at once, adds 12 fire to its impact on a live target, and falls in; cold, steam and a hole put it out, its fuel kept, and keep it from lighting 4 s. Whatever lights a crate lights a keg's 1.5 s fuse; a hard impact (closing at 600 px/s), a bullet, lightning or a blast sets a keg off at once; a frozen crate or keg never lights, and fire on it is thermal shock. The summon slot's types are an enum (`Summoned`): the dummy, the six kinds, the crate and the keg, 16 crates and 8 kegs at most, the map's counted, the oldest in a poof.
- **The sourced detonation** (feedback K10): `CascadeKind::Detonation` in the saved queue, reach 192 for a keg and 128 for a bomb, reach / 8 fire and physical, a push of 900, crates lit and kegs set off with its lighter, the flare for a step's first two and a shake by the courier's distance. `queue_from` and `thermal_shock_from` carry a source save ID through every ending a sourced entry queues, and the reactions queue by their part's source, so a chain credits whoever lit it and a gone lighter none; a detonation runs only on a live keg or bomb not yet spent in its run.
- **The goblin** (`EnemyKind::Goblin`, `goblin.rs`): 40 health at 110 px/s; it backs off inside 140 px across, closes past 420, winds up 0.45 s, throws for 0.25 s and recovers 0.9 s (`KindInfo::recover`); one `KitRng` draw at the tell, kept as `Brain::strike`, picks a bomb under 0.2. A rock is a stone body, a `PropKind::Rock` of mass 6, lobbed on the world's gravity with a flight of its distance over 650 held to 0.35–0.9 s; its first blow on a live target lands 8 physical at its thrower's factor, pushed 260 along its flight, credited through `Throws` to the goblin while it lives; then it bounces, rolls and stays, 24 at most, the oldest crumbling, and clacks on a hard landing. A bomb is a lit prop on the world's gravity, the troll's flight, that rolls, its fuse's emitters on its turned tip, blows after 1.5 s, credited to the goblin, and fizzles out when snuffed; a bomb or a rock turns by its travel over its radius and slows at 300 px/s² where wood slows at 900. A courier within 56 px across and 40 up or down gets a 0.2 s tell, a stab of 6 physical at its factor in a box ahead, pushed out and up, as 01's box hit for K3's reason, and a leap back. It hops a crate, a rock or a step no higher than 64 px, turns at a keg or a bomb, steps down a drop of 96 px or less, fidgets on patrol, a look back or a skip in place by its patrol's saved seconds, and at 30 % of its health or less runs from a courier within 260 px.
- **The troll** (T5, C2, I7, feedback K3): the slam tells for 0.8 s (`SLAM_TELL`, from 1.2) with a ground ring about its fist, a grunt and a shake of 0.5 within 120 px of the courier to none at 640; it recovers 0.35 s; within 90 px across and 80 up it swipes instead (a 0.3 s tell, a box 12–92 px ahead, 18 physical at its factor, a push of (560, −200)) as 01's box hit, since a kit `Hitbox` cannot follow its owner's facing; both kick each dynamic body in reach by `min(650, 2,400 / mass)`, 0.6 of it up.
- **Marks and the sting** (I10): "!" for 0.8 s from a fresh alert, kept as `Brain::alerted` since Alert ends sooner and a transition clears the machine's memory of it; "?" through a search past its first 0.4 s; `Sound::Alerted` from each fresh alert, one sting for the spotter and its neighbours within 0.5 s, the `spotted` rule gone.
- **Art and sound:** a pine ramp in the palette; `tools/props.py`'s crates, keg, char overlays and particle sprites; the goblin's rig and five clips, the troll's swipe clips and its wind-up retimed, the marks, icons and fitted cubes, from the plan session's review sheet; ten sounds, the fire's crackle one voice following the nearest burning crate.
- **The map** (`tools/level.json`): `crates` and `kegs`, each `{support, x}` with an optional `tier`, stood half a pixel over their support by `placement.py` and set down quietly by a fresh run after the iron: nine crates and five kegs at the clearing's west end, on the aqueduct's west channel, on the mine's upper level and at the east bank's end; seven goblins, on the clearing's meadow, over the aqueduct's ledges, between the upper level's kegs, on the cut's two floors and two on the troll bridge: 26 enemies.
- **The save,** schema 12: `props` (crates, kegs, bombs and rocks), a thrown rock's credit in `throws`; `migrate(11)` moves every save ID from schema 11's first placed orb's (28) up by `SHIFT_12` (7), past the goblins, through `map_ids`, which now walks `/black_holes` and `/props`; schemas 7 to 10 go through 11 first. A ball's array leaves its trailing `null`s off, a load reading six to ten values.

**Why:** The owner's notes (the feedback plan's rows C1–C4, T5, I7, I10 and I17's last note, its §3.3 and Q16–Q18). Kit first: the swipe (K3), the radial blast (K10) and the fuses' saved clocks (K11) stay 01 code, since the kit has no hitbox that follows a facing, no blast payload and no timer with an expiry event and a saved form, and fire on props is 01's body state as the balls' is, the kit's `burning` needing `Health`. The plan's two critique rounds found the bomb's arc on half the gravity that flies it, the alert mark outliving the machine's memory of it, a chain's credit reset to the player by thermal shock and by the reactions, two kegs queuing each other again in one run and the slam's ring away from its hit, each fixed in the plan. The run changed three things the plan did not foresee: the map's dummy stands where the clearing's crates were drawn, so the third stands on the other two (`tier`) and the keg past the dummy; a bomb at restitution 0.3 bounced off the courier and rolled out of its own reach, so it landed dead with wood's friction until the owner's playtest asked for it to roll, which it now does at 0.15, slowing at 300 px/s²; and the goblins and props took a full pit's save to 2,111,844 bytes, over its 2 MiB bound, which the trimmed ball arrays bring to 1,775,844, and 1,787,829 with the seven goblins. The owner's playtest of the cut's build asked for the thrown rocks to stay physical, the bombs to roll with their fuses, more movement and a small melee for the goblin, and goblins near the start, in the mine and on the troll bridge; the release commit waited for them. Measured: a keg chain saved after its second wave finishes the same after a load; a schema 11 save from the 0.101 code migrates with every ID from 28 up moved by 7; the frame reading's `total` p50 3.743 → 3.705 ms on the full pit with six enemies awake, the playtest's changes in, indicative (release, headless, the machine idle but for a remote-desktop encoder).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-194` (the stops after (d)), `D-200` (the cascade queue's credit and a fourth ending), `D-201` (a sixth kind, each kind's recovery, the troll's tell and the alert's sting), `D-202` (the map's goblins and placed props) and `D-209` (the kick by mass) as their marker lines say.
- No public API change; the kit hash stays `0x3334_9665_de0c_501a`.
- Tests: 01's 286, among them `props::a_crate_set_alight_burns_down_and_falls_in`, `a_bomb_rolls_with_its_fuse_and_a_rock_stays_a_body_that_rolls`, `a_lit_keg_blows_after_its_fuse_and_its_neighbours_follow`, `a_detonations_credit_passes_through_the_endings_it_sets_off`, `two_kegs_blowing_in_one_step_blow_once_each`, `spells::the_summon_slot_places_crates_and_kegs_where_they_fit`, `enemies::a_goblin_lobs_rocks_on_their_arc_and_one_throw_in_five_is_a_bomb`, `a_goblin_keeps_its_distance`, `a_goblins_stab_hurts_a_courier_too_close_and_it_leaps_back`, `a_goblin_hops_a_crate_in_its_way`, `a_rock_from_a_goblin_killed_in_flight_still_lands_at_its_factor`, `the_troll_swipes_what_is_close_and_slams_what_is_farther`, `the_troll_walks_at_a_courier_on_either_side_and_slams_it`, `presentation::an_alert_shows_its_mark_and_stings_once`, `saves::a_keg_chain_saved_mid_chain_finishes_the_same_after_a_load` and `schema_11_saves_move_their_ids_past_the_map_goblins` on a schema 11 save captured from the 0.101 code.
- Left: K3 and K10 are backlog rows and a keg chain joins K11's, with 01's code meanwhile; a bat that spots the player from its patrol skips Alert and shows no "!"; an older save's run has none of the new crates and kegs; a keg falling more than about 50 px blows on landing; the frame readings are indicative and C1's reading stays owed.
- Owner checks: a playthrough with the crates, kegs and goblins and the troll's new moves; the review sheet in `perf-runs/20261023-e01g-art-review/`.

## D-211 — Example 01 (h) the look landed: the wizard on a 96 px frame whose staff's orb is the staff light, with gestures, a hurt clip and a death on the ground; save schema 13 for the moved body point; one icon family on 36 px tiles; liquid bars in their own colours and an action bar with each slot's live key and the gun apart with its cartridges; the fire missile as a rocket; the sprays 30 % further, drawn as animated sprites with a light and bursts on what they hit; 01's theme in its own colours over the template's structure; a starry title in OLED black
**Date:** 2026-10-24
**Decision:** M82 runs example 01's (h) stop (the feedback plan's §3.4), planned, critiqued in two rounds, revised for the owner's three reviews of its art-review sheet, and run in one session with no kit, engine, template or shell change:

- **The wizard** (`tools/actors.py`): the courier's rig on a 96 × 96 frame, the body as the courier's (half 20 × 28) centred at (48, 65), the feet on row 93: a long indigo robe with bell sleeves, a mantle, a rope belt and pouch, brass trim and sewn stars, a big felt hat that bends back and flops, a white beard, and a gnarled staff whose crook holds an amber orb, plain glass, never emissive, the staff light. A frame's anchor is the drawn glass's centre, which the rig returns with the frame (the death's lying frames' moved with their crops) and the generator writes as the `lantern_anchors` entry, the scan and the `_cast` twins gone: 66 frames, 132 sprites with their `_left` twins, from 192. New clips: `hurt`, `bolt`, `push`, `conjure` and `staff`; `aim` is now the gun's pistol pose; the death falls back onto the ground in seven frames (910 ms).
- **The frame's move:** `BODY_OFFSET` (48, 65) and the prefab's body, aim, hurtbox and glow seed with it; the status overlays draw in a 64-unit square on the player's body (`OVERLAY_SIDE`); the camera keeps following the entity's point, so the body sits 16 px right and 32 px lower on screen. Save schema 13: `to_13` moves a save's player corner by (−16, −32), `S12_BODY_OFFSET` frozen for it and `to_10`, every older arm running it last.
- **Gestures** (`player::Gesture`, `Spell::gesture`): the bowling ball tosses, the missile and lightning bolt, the push and the hole push, iron and the summons conjure, each a one-shot for 0.3 s facing the aim; the held sprays and the small balls pour and a shot shoots, poses standing in the idle clip. A blow that is not a tick plays the hurt clip for 0.2 s. A spell flares the staff light (its halo × 1.35, core × 1.6, light × 1.5 over the prefab's glow kept in `StaffGlow`, its reach the prefab's, which the crawlers read); a shot does not.
- **The icon family** (`tools/effects.py`'s `icon_tile`): each 32 px subject on a 36 px tile with a bevelled frame in its element's colour, a backing tinted by the element with a glow, and the element's rim light; a new pistol; mana's blues `И Й З Ю` in the palette for the summons' frames and ring, the mana orb and the mana liquid.
- **The HUD:** bars of 168 × 10 texels at whole screen pixels, each fill clipping `hud_liquid_health` or `hud_liquid_mana` scrolled by the game clock (14 and 9 texels a second) with a bright edge, health's liquid pulsing and its track's border beating in the theme's danger at a third or less, the bars' root on whole pixels so the tiles meet seamless, readouts in mono at 14; each slot's tile with its key, read each frame from the `ActionMap` and shortened to three characters at most (`hud::short`), on a corner plate, the ring in the theme's focus, no labels row, the cooldown's veil and iron's pips, a white flash as a cooldown of 0.5 s or more ends and a danger flash on a press refused as not ready (`hud::refused` from `refuse_cast`); the summon's name over slot 0 for 1.2 s after a turn; the gun past a 26-unit gap and a divider with no key, its rounds as `hud_bullet` and `hud_bullet_spent` cartridges and their count, "reloading" through any reload, its tile greyed under the reload's sweep. No HUD widget reads the theme's accent; `Spell::school`, `SLOT_KEYS` and `STEPS_PER_SECOND` go with their readers, and `School` stays for the damage numbers' tests.
- **The fire missile:** its own clip, `ex10_fire_missile`, a rocket of fire facing +x with a white-hot nose, a lit body with a glowing band, swept fins and a licking plume, mirrored flying left; its prefab's drips gone, its trail kept; its icon the rocket turned 40°.
- **The sprays:** `SPRAY_RANGE` 9.1 tiles and `FIRE_SPRAY_RANGE` 4.55, newborns trimmed by their own spray's reach; drawn as animated, self-lit sprites (`toys/spray_look.rs`, `SprayLook`, `SprayPuff`): a muzzle flare and a point light at the flame, a stream of puffs (the fire's every 12 ms at 900 px/s, the ice's every 18 ms at 1,600) along lines within the cone that stop where they meet something, a looping splash at the contact, and the ice's core beam tiled to the cone's end; the particles stay as accents at caps of 110, 48 and 70 (ice) and 120, 70 and 12 (fire). Every second damage pulse from the first (`Spray::pulses`), each live target the cone damages bursts at its contact (`ex10_frost_hit`, `ex10_flame_hit`), a cosmetic burst under `BURST_CAP`; a surface keeps the splash alone.
- **01's theme:** `tools/generate.py`'s `THEME_COLORS` maps each colour of the template's theme to 01's, darker backgrounds, the accent `#a46cff` and the focus `#cbb0ff`, and writes 01's `shell.json` from the template's text, stopping on a template colour the map lacks; `check_shell_copies` holds the theme to the template's text with its colours masked, its message "differs … beyond its colours"; 01 holds its own contrast floors in `presentation::the_theme_reads_in_its_own_colours`.
- **The title** (`level/title.rs`): while no run is live, `TitleSky` runs its own clock; while the title is on top the camera holds the base zoom, unbounded, 80 rows above the start's view (`SKY_LIFT`), and the backdrop draws the black night sky whole, the moon, no clouds and the ridges, the clearing's far strip and the woodland at 0.45, 0.40 and 0.35 of their colour; 40 twinkles over the view's upper half and a shooting star every 4 s, owned by the title's state; their particles go the first frame a run is live.

**Why:** The owner's notes (the feedback plan's L1–L7, I1, I14 and F1, its §3.4 and Q7) and three reviews of the plan session's sheet in `perf-runs/20261023-e01h-art-review/`: the missile should look like a missile, the bars smaller with a fluid texture, a new gun with its rounds as bullets and set apart from the spells, darker backgrounds and a more vivid purple, the title in OLED black seen from low down, the death ending on the ground, and the sprays 30 % longer, redone with sprites and animation, with particles on contact so a hit shows. Kit first: everything here is 01's presentation over the kit's items, so nothing moved into the kit. The plan's two critique rounds found the flare raising the crawlers' reach through `Glow::reach`'s fallback, the title's particles outliving its emitters, regeneration before a step's last generator input, the migration tests refusing the shifted position, a schema 12 test with no schema 12 fixture, and the title star's size unregistered, each fixed in the plan. The run changed what real frames and the tree showed: the anchor is the drawn glass's centre, the sheet's death anchors up to 3 px off; the generator writes the anchors, since `placement.prepare`'s caller lay outside the step; a shot poses without a flare; the key plate's text is the mockup's 9.5 units, the shell's small size covering two thirds of a tile at the owner's scale; the bars' root sits on whole pixels after a seam showed where two liquid tiles met on a half pixel; and the title's lift is 80 rows, since the sheet's driver ran its title camera at the base zoom squared, where 90 set the treeline at the foot. Measured: every wizard frame, icon, missile frame, spray frame and HUD texture byte-equal to the sheet's; a schema 12 save from the 0.102 code keeps its body within 0.01 of where it stood; the sheet's light probe kept the staff light in the cull's 16 in the hollow with six missiles cast (A27).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-194` (the stops after (d)) and `D-199` (the copy check's theme clause) as their marker lines say.
- No public API change; the kit hash stays `0x3334_9665_de0c_501a`.
- Tests: 01's 296, among them `saves::a_schema_12_player_keeps_its_body_where_it_stood` on a schema 12 save captured from the 0.102 code, `presentation::the_wizards_overlays_sit_on_his_body`, `player::each_gesture_shows_its_clip_and_the_staff_flares`, `hud::each_slot_shows_its_live_key_and_a_rebind_at_once`, `the_bars_keep_their_own_colours_and_the_ring_takes_focus`, `a_slot_flashes_when_ready_and_red_when_refused`, `spells::the_sprays_reach_further_and_burst_on_what_they_hit`, `a_spray_draws_its_sprites_and_clears_them`, `presentation::the_theme_reads_in_its_own_colours` and `the_title_draws_its_sky_and_its_twinkles_leave_with_it`; `test-check-repo.py`'s theme cases (a theme in its own colours passes, a changed key or number fails).
- Left: the light cull's tie-break stays known issue P3; UI text outlines stay K5, the keys on plates meanwhile; a camera follow offset would keep today's framing for a larger frame; the spent cartridges read dim on dark stone; a shooting star's trail is dotted, one emitter place a frame.
- Owner checks: a playthrough of the look, the title and the sprays; the review sheet in `perf-runs/20261023-e01h-art-review/`.

## D-212 — Example 01 (i) fixes and tuning landed: a crawler lunge twice as long with a harder touch, a floor on body hits, an ice beam at half damage on a cold ladder whose gates halve, difficulty on the enemies alone, bats flush on their roosts, an elevator that turns back over the player and sets one out of its deck, drops that land clear of rock and fall, lava that takes what falls in, F1's hit boxes while a blow lands, a double jump that sounds like a jump and no hint toasts; from the owner's review, a darker theme, slow out-of-step stars of three sizes on the title and a quicker troll whose slam cools down; in the kit, the animator without a moving floor's carry and hitboxes drawn only while armed; save schema 14
**Date:** 2026-10-24
**Decision:** M83 runs example 01's (i) stop (the feedback plan's §3.5), planned, critiqued in two rounds and run in one session, with the owner's notes from its step 14 review of real frames and a sprite-sheet demo added:

- **The crawler** (N3): `LUNGE_SPEED` 640, `LUNGE_LIFT` 620, `LUNGE_RANGE` 300 and `LUNGE_LOCK` 0.45: a leap 53 px up and 224 px out, about 290 px with its skid; its touch and `damage` stat 15 cold (`crawler::DAMAGE`).
- **Body hits** (N11): `elements::body_hit`: a body lighter than `LIGHT_MASS` 4 counts `LIGHT_SHARE` 0.5 of its mass, and a hit under `BODY_HIT_FLOOR` 2, read before a thrower's factor, lands nothing, no fire and no push, its throw flying on; a goblin's rock keeps `ROCK_DAMAGE`.
- **The ice beam** (N14): `COLD_SPRAY_DPS` 15 beside `FIRE_SPRAY_DPS` 30; the cold ladder's `needs` 0.15, 0.5 and 0.5 and its windows 6 s, so the beam chills and freezes at the old times; `combat::FREEZE_NEEDS` 0.5 grades the chill; a crawler's touch now chills the player at once.
- **Difficulty** (N17): the player's `MAX_HEALTH` 100 at every difficulty; the card reads "enemy health ×f · enemy damage ×f".
- **Bats** (N7): each map bat flush under its roof, `level.py` rejecting any other, so the roost found is the point placed.
- **The elevator** (N8): a player inside its deck by more than `ON_TOP` is set out above or below, the other side when a tile or a wall fills the first; a falling deck whose box, swept down by its eased path's fastest step, meets the player turns back to the top and comes down again `RETURN_SECS` later.
- **Drops** (N12): `drops::fallen` records each killed enemy's body centre, since a dead enemy goes the step after its kill, before `adopt`, which centres its orb there and moves it clear of rock; `orb_fall` drops an orb no magnet reaches at `ORB_FALL` 120 px/s to the first tile, wall or lava surface; the player's reach 192 (`player::PICKUP_RADIUS`), the orbs' magnet 480.
- **Lava** (N13): `lava::take` takes the dynamic bodies with no health in an open basin's burn, in save-ID order: a keg or a bomb goes off, a frozen prop is left to the heat's thermal shock, anything else goes in steam and embers, one `Sound::LavaTook` a basin a step.
- **F1** (N16): `KitDebug` follows the colliders' overlay; 01's stab, swipe and slam draw in the kit's hitbox red at its width only while that blow's attack runs (`enemies::draw::debug_boxes`).
- **The double jump** (N10): 0.25 s, a breathy low puff over a soft thump into a rising whoosh, no chime and no flutter.
- **Hints** (N18): `Reaction`, `react`, `Hints` and `combat_hints` gone.
- **Save schema 14** (`to_14`): `hints` dropped; the player's maximum 100, its health keeping its share; its reach, each crawler's damage and each troll's walk at their new bases, their modifiers kept; each saved cold build-up halved with the gates.
- **The kit:** `CharacterAnimator` takes a grounded platformer's kinematic floor's `Velocity` off its own before picking its state and side (K24); `kit_debug_draw` draws a hitbox only while it is armed, in a deep red at 1.5 px.
- **The owner's review:** 01's theme background `#020306` and surface `#060812`; the title's 56 twinkles in three sizes (`ex10_title_twinkle_small`, `_medium`, `_large`, 60, 30 and 10 %), one at a time each over a random 5.6 to 14 s at a hashed first phase, scattered and thinning toward the treeline; the troll's walk 91 (`troll::MOVE_SPEED`, 30 % faster) and a 2 s slam cooldown kept in its saved brain (`SLAM_COOLDOWN`, `Brain::slam_cooldown`).

**Why:** The owner's third playtest of 2026-10-24 (the feedback plan's N3, N7, N8, N10 to N14 and N16 to N18, its Q24 to Q27, Q31 and Q32), and their notes on the run's review: F1's red boxes 1.5 px, a deeper red and shown only while a damage window is open; a darker UI theme; title stars less uniform, out of step, of different sizes and slower; the troll a small slam cooldown and 30 % more speed. Kit first: the animator's misreading of a carried rider and the debug drawing of an unarmed hitbox are the kit's own, so they were fixed there; K19, K22 and K23 stay gaps with 01's code meanwhile. The run found that a dead enemy goes the step after its kill, before its drop is adopted, so the kill records where it fell; that a lone small ball rebounds out of a hit's reach within its step; that a keg set off in lava blows inside that step; and that the longer lunge and the wider reach moved two older tests' setups (a spotter left past dormancy, a loaded player healed by an orb). Measured: the leap 53.33 px up and 224 px out; the troll frozen 2.2 s after its chill under the beam; the elevator turning about 10 px over the player's body; a slam every 2.85 s rather than 1.62 s with the cooldown; the `character_animator` row at 33.2 and 33.4 ns an entity for 1,000 and 10,000 (`D-174`'s bound holds at 1.007).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-174` (`CharacterAnimator`), `D-175` (the hitbox's debug shape) and `D-194` (the stops after (d)) as their marker lines say.
- No public API change (`just api-check`); the kit hash stays `0x3334_9665_de0c_501a`; 01's save schema moves 13 → 14, tested on a schema 13 save from the 0.103 code.
- Tests: 01's 307, among them `enemies::the_crawlers_lunge_leaps_about_220_px_and_53_up`, `a_light_body_needs_more_speed_to_hurt`, `the_maps_bats_hang_at_their_roosts_from_the_start`, `a_drop_lands_clear_of_rock_falls_and_is_drawn_in`, `the_trolls_slam_waits_out_its_cooldown`, `ice::the_cold_spray_lands_half_the_flames_damage`, `level::the_elevator_turns_back_over_a_player_and_sets_one_inside_out`, `a_still_rider_idles_on_the_rising_elevator`, `hazards::lava_takes_what_falls_in_and_sets_off_a_keg`, `presentation::f1_draws_the_hit_boxes_with_the_colliders_while_they_land`, `saves::a_reaction_toasts_nothing` and `a_schema_13_save_drops_its_hints_and_keeps_its_health_share`; the kit's `a_rider_on_a_moving_floor_idles_until_it_runs` and `the_hit_items_draw_their_shapes_at_their_points`.
- Left: K19 (a falling pickup, a magnet on bodies) and K22 (a `KitDebug` hotkey) as backlog rows and K23 (kinematic pairs that touch) as a known issue; a walker under the falling elevator is not turned back for; a lone small ball rarely hurts, since it rebounds; the hat's tip shows into the cage's floor at the elevator's turn.
- Owner checks: a playthrough of the tuning; the demo at https://claude.ai/artifact/SuUZs3DNu9YixZ3kFVTxkT and `perf-runs/20261024-e01i-tuning/`.

## D-213 — Example 01 (j) pushes and spells landed: the kit's platformer controller shoves light dynamic bodies and climbs low steps when set (`with_shove`, `with_step_height`), the wizard walking bodies aside and walkers stepping over the balls in their way; the force push as a wave toward the cursor that throws what its front meets, the troll a little; a black hole that lifts bowling balls, tears open over 0.3 s and holds bats and wisps about its core; a creature's frost in four white stages from the first point of cold
**Date:** 2026-10-25
**Decision:** M84 runs example 01's (j) stop (the feedback plan's §3.6), planned with an art-review sheet of real in-game loops, critiqued in two rounds and run in one session:

- **The kit** (feedback K7's shove, K18), additions only: `PlatformerController::with_shove(mass, share)`: of the dynamic bodies its horizontal move meets as walls, one of mass `m` up to `share` of `mass` takes the controller's horizontal velocity less `m / mass` of it, unless it already moves that way faster, and is woken; the controller stops at it that step, as at a wall, then follows at its pace. `with_step_height(height)`: on the ground, a move that meets a wall goes up by at most `height`, across by at least 0.15 of it (and 0.29 of a circle collider's radius more) and down again, landing on a floor that is not a kinematic body farther on; an accepted climb sets the body's `Position` at the landing and a zero `Velocity`, so it shoves nothing it climbs, and a kinematic body is never a step. Both off by default; the `platformer_controller` component's `shove_mass`, `shove_share` (0.5) and `step_height` (0); the saved form's `shove` and `step_height`, left out when off, so an older save loads with both off. `Slide` gains the first wall's body and normal and `slide::climb` the climb, crate-private.
- **01's shoves and steps** (N6, N19): the wizard shoves at 60 × 0.5, set at a spawn and a load (`player::shove_bodies`); each walker at its kind's mass up to `WALK_SHOVE_MOST` 8, a ball, a rock or a bomb but never a crate or a keg, with a `WALK_STEP` of 32 px (`enemies::walk_on_rock`); `solid` looks past dynamic bodies, and a walker's ledge is 32 px deeper, so a walker on a ball walks on.
- **The force push** (N4, N20) is `toys::wave`: a front from 24 px ahead of the wizard's centre along the aim at 1,200 px/s for 0.3 s, its sector widening from 15° to 30°, taking each body and enemy once where it meets the front (its velocity and half its smallest extent counted), throwing an enemy by `(aim × 500 + (0, −300)) × falloff × KindInfo::wave` through `knock_back`, a walker's input locked 0.35 s, the troll (resist 1) launched on its controller past its resist, and a body by `(aim × 1,800 + (0, −500)) × falloff` over its mass, the falloff 1 to 0.5 at 360 px. It draws as `push_wave`, a crescent of bent air turned to the aim and sampled linearly, with `push_dust` puffs and `push_grit` where it scrapes a floor, under a new whoosh. `Ring::Push`, `FORCE_PUSH_RADIUS`, `force_push`, `push` and `warp_burst` are gone; `radial_push` stays the blast's.
- **The black hole** (N9): a bowling ball takes `BOWLING_PULL` 0.625 of the pull, iron 0.2; a hole opens over `HOLE_OPEN_SECS` 0.3 s, all presentation, its layers growing in from 0.08 of their size, a `Ring::Open` of its photon ring art closing from 432 px to 24 and a `hole_open` rush of 72 sparks from 230 px in at 700 px/s (`OpeningOf`), under a generated `black_hole_open.wav` in `ex10_black_hole_sfx`'s place, `black_hole.ogg` deleted and `HAND_AUTHORED_SOUNDS` empty. A live flyer in reach is held on its own mover (`toys_hold`, before `top_down_mover`): steered toward a ring 40 px about the core at the hole's pull capped at its speed, eased within 48 px and circling there at 0.55 of its speed, blended with its own direction by the pull over its speed, its own direction written back after the movers (`toys_release`); the pull's knockback on top-down movers goes.
- **The frost** (N15): `combat::frost_level`, a quarter of the climb to the chill's rung (`CHILL_NEEDS` 0.15, held to the data) and the rest to the freeze's, its stages 1 to 4 (`frost_stage`) drawn as `frost_<kind>_<stage>` on the six fitted hurtboxes or `frost_<class>_<stage>`, white rime with no dark rim; a tint toward a whiter `FROST_TINT` before the chill and toward a pale `CHILL_DEEP` from it, frozen keeping `COLD_TINT`; flakes by stage (`chill_flakes_1` to `_4`, one `StatusEmitter` a stage); a left-facing enemy's overlays take its drawing's mirror shift back.

**Why:** The owner's third playtest of 2026-10-24 (the feedback plan's N4, N6, N9, N15, N19 and N20) and their review of the plan's art sheet, which asked for the troll pushed back "but just a little" and the chill's effects "more white / frosty, more subtle". Kit first: a controller that moves what it walks into and climbs a step is the kit's, so it landed there, off by default with the kit hash unmoved. The critique's two rounds found a climb too short to reach a round top at a slow walk, a climb written as velocity that would shove what it climbs, a front that misses a target running into it or crossing it on a slant, steering that would keep its correction on a bat's dive, and a circle controller's longer reach; each changed the design. The run found the kit's description census counting the new fields (its count moved by owner approval), two migration comparisons that see the shoves 01 sets at a load, the prototype's left-aimed dust set 48 px off, and a left-facing enemy drawn shifted only once its clip shows, which needs its mind. Measured: throws at 1.5 tiles of 157.5 px (crawler), 135.5 (goblin), 41.7 (beetle), 180 (bat), 157.5 (wisp) and 22.0 (troll); the wizard at 448 px/s behind a crate; a patrolling beetle up 29.7 px over a bowling ball, and a slow climb over a ball moving the ball 0.256 px, its settle, and nothing across; a bowling ball 180 px under a core rising 219.7 px within a second while one 230 px under and iron 120 px under stay; a bat and a wisp held within 58.0 and 58.4 px of the core.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-158` (the platformer controller's blocking), `D-194` (the stops after (d)) and `D-209` (the radial force push and the holes' pull on flyers) as their marker lines say.
- API: additions only (`api/tungsten-kit.txt` +4 lines, `api/tungsten-kit-components.txt` +3), with a ledger row in `w04-api-freeze.md`; the kit hash stays `0x3334_9665_de0c_501a`; 01's save schema stays 14, a 0.104 save shoving after its load.
- Tests: the kit's 16 new controller tests, among them `a_shove_moves_a_light_body_at_the_run_less_its_share`, `the_step_climbs_a_ball_at_a_slow_walk`, `a_round_controller_climbs_a_ball_too` and `a_save_keeps_the_shove_and_the_step_and_an_old_one_loads_them_off`; 01's 323, among them `player::the_wizard_walks_small_bodies_aside`, `enemies::a_walker_steps_over_balls_on_its_way`, `enemies::the_wave_throws_each_kind_along_the_aim_the_troll_a_little`, `spells::the_wave_meets_a_body_running_into_it`, `spells::a_hole_lifts_bowling_balls_within_200_px_and_leaves_iron`, `enemies::a_bat_diving_through_a_hole_dives_on_its_own_way_after`, `presentation::a_hole_opens_over_three_tenths_of_a_second`, `ice::the_frost_climbs_in_four_stages_from_the_first_point_of_cold` and `ice::a_left_facing_troll_s_frost_and_cube_fit_its_hurtbox`.
- Left: a controller pushed by bodies (K7's other half), outside velocity on the top-down mover (K20), an emitter's rate at run time (K21) and a hit that does not flash (K28, under the ice beam each pulse restarts the white hit flash) as backlog rows; walkers press against a crate or a keg they can neither shove nor climb, as before; a climb is a teleport no contact sees, so a body that moves into its path within the step is passed (known issues).
- Owner checks: a playthrough of the pushes and the spells; the review page at https://claude.ai/artifact/7zrhDsGmeU4CqZYp251JkW and `perf-runs/20261024-e01j-art-review/`.

## D-214 — Example 01 (k) scenery, aqueduct and start landed: a generator check that fails the build on any prop, group, stream, support or decoration that floats, props standing and hanging on the drawn terrain, a wall and a boundary at the start with save schema 15, the aqueduct's broken spans, pours and gargoyle, and lava that freezes and thaws by one front
**Date:** 2026-10-25
**Decision:** M85 runs example 01's (k) stop (the feedback plan's §3.7: N1, N2, N5 and N21), planned with an art-review sheet of real in-game loops, critiqued in two rounds and run in one session, in 01's code and data alone:

- **The floating check** (N1): `placement.floating(source, tiled, images)` lists each piece of the built level that floats, sinks or states no way it stands, as lines `kind name x y detail`. Solid is the drawn terrain layer (alpha 128 or more), liquid the river, pool, lava and channel-water props. A prop is judged by its `anchor`, else by its sprite's list in `scenery.py`: `ground` (and `LEVEL_PROPS`) has solid within `GAP` 2 px under `MOST` 60 % of its bottom two rows' columns and sinks no more than `SINK` 6 px; `under` (and `HUNG_PROPS`) has solid from 2 px over its top to `UNDER_TUCK` 6 px into it; `wall` touches solid within 3 px to its side; `back` lies wholly over the background layer; a stream's columns (`pour`, `pour_wide`, `pour_foot`, `falls_*`, `lavafall`) meet a source at their top, join segment to segment and meet solid or liquid at their foot, none a quarter inside solid; haze, plumes, far pieces, liquids and spills are exempt; anything else is `unjudged`. Groups are judged by their own pieces' composite, a piece inside two groups' boxes or a group with none failing; each column of timber supports ends on solid; decorations by their part (pier columns to a base over terrain or a cutwater over the river, springings beside a capital, arch tops on their springings, parapets standing, a background structure filling its bottom row over terrain). `generate.py --floating` prints the lines and a total; `build_level` raises on any, so `--check`, the write and the tests fail.
- **Standing on the drawn surface**: `build_level` makes the terrain tiles first, which read only the platforms, and `prepare` moves each supported prop and group from its support's edge by the smallest 0–12 px (down for `ground`, up for `under`) at which the check's own rule passes, so props meet the rock families' ragged tops and undersides and the weathered decks' planks; a group takes the pieces inside its box where `level.json` writes them and `prepare` refuses a piece inside two. By hand: the pours re-stacked under their sources and onto `aq_rubble_1`, the gargoyle `anchor: wall`, the fissure `anchor: back`, the lavafall from the fissure's drawn foot to the lava's skin, `bones_1` and `boulder_2` onto their floors, the ravine's vine groups each on its ledge's row, and the falls set on the channel's end at 177.
- **The start** (N21): a `ground` platform `start_wall` over columns 0–1 from row 21, its variants from a stream of its own (`"variants": "own"`) so no other tile moves; `level::BOUNDARY`, a static box from x −64 to 0 over the world's height in the slabs' stone layer, spawned by `level::spawn` and saved by none; the crates, keg and dummy moved right of the moving deck under the perch, the oak and its roots two columns right, the spawn at 6.5. Save schema 15: `to_15` sets each saved body whose box meets the wall's or lies left of the map at rest on the clearing's floor at the first spot from x 128 to the terrace's face at 1,024 that meets no other saved body and skips the moving deck's columns, the player first and at the spawn when no spot is clear; any other body with no room is dropped and logged, a map dummy or enemy into `gone`. The body boxes are frozen beside it (`S15_*`).
- **The aqueduct** (N2): each channel run's ends broken in three ragged variants a side (`channel_break_<side>_<k>`, by the run's place), a right lip over its pier's shaft and a left one over the haunch beside its pier; spills over each gap's lips (`spill_<side>_<k>`), 10 px pours (`pour_wide`) ending in splash feet (`pour_foot`) 4.5 px past each lip, three spouts (`spouts`) each with its pour; each gap's arcade tops cut along one broken profile (`arcade_gap_<g>_<row>_<col>`) onto wet piers (`pier_wet_<k>`); fallen voussoirs, lime drips under the coping, a two-tier ruined `far_arcade`, the gargoyle a horned beast in pale limestone as a 4-frame clip with its water over its tongue, and a fuller ambience (flow, patter, plops) at the same 0.175.
- **The lava's freeze and thaw** (N5): its look reads only the basin's chill and its crust's seconds left, which a save keeps. One front comes down through the whole basin, 60 % of its depth as the chill builds and the rest over the crust's first 0.3 s, each row showing the set frame above it, a partial at it (8 px apart) and nothing below; the crust's nine frames dull, hold and crack (two a stage); a second front comes down over the 0.4 s after it melts. `LavaChilled`, `LavaCracked` and `LavaMelted` join `Sound::ALL` (0.45, 0.5 and 0.35) with their cue rules; frost mist (`lava_mist`) as the front starts and at each further third of the depth, glints (`lava_frost`) at its start, sparks (`lava_sparks`) at each crack stage and spatter (`lava_spatter`) at the melt, a burst to each two columns, each over within a second and a new build's at most once a second, so a basin's bursts stay under `BURST_CAP`. The crust's touch, its 0.5 s set, 6 s hold, 1.5 s of cracks and its throw are unchanged.

**Why:** The owner's third playtest of 2026-10-24: props floating across the level (N1), a flat aqueduct (N2), a lava freeze with no build-up (N5) and a start that let things fly off the map's west edge (N21). Their reviews of the plan's sheet asked for one top-down front rather than freezing in layers, ragged broken ends with the water leaving past the lip, a gargoyle set apart from the masonry and broken ends that sit on their piers; the third draw answered each and the run ports it, its 100 lava frames, 90 aqueduct pieces and four sounds matching the sheet's byte for byte. Almost every floater had one cause: `prepare` stood props on the tile's edge while the wet and mine rock draw their tops 4–5 px lower and basalt's undersides end about 8 px higher; the check found 135 pieces at `1803bb7` and placing by its own rule left 11, the nine the plan fixed by hand and two it had not seen (the lavafall's top 17 px under the fissure, which its restack covers, and the falls' lip 12 px off the channel's end). The critique's two rounds moved the pour repairs into step 2, widened the migration to the boundary and the whole floor, gave the wall its own variant stream, bounded the bursts and added the stream-gap rule.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-194` (the stops after (d)) as its marker line says.
- No kit, engine, template, shell or public API change: the kit hash, the physics hashes, `api/` and 01's shell copy are unmoved. Save schema 15; a schema 7 to 14 save migrates through `to_15`. The generator's outputs grow from 4,290 to 4,815; `level.tmj`'s tile IDs move with the new sprites, and the map's save IDs do not.
- Tests: the generator's nine `test_floating_*` cases and `test_grounded_props_and_hanging_vegetation_meet_their_support` held to the drawn edge; 01's 327, among them `level::the_start_wall_and_its_boundary_keep_a_thrown_ball_in_the_map`, `saves::a_schema_13_save_moves_its_crates_out_of_the_start_wall`, `saves::to_15_sets_bodies_from_the_wall_and_the_boundary_on_the_floor` and `hazards::the_lava_freezes_and_thaws_through_its_frames_with_a_sound_at_each_change`, with the crust's own test unchanged.
- Left: the melt's look is presentation, so a load inside its 0.4 s shows the lava at once; the falls take the new break's lip by hand, so a later change to `BREAK_LIPS` moves them by the check's finding; the sheet's notes after the run's start go to the Phase 6 QA pass. The run added `src/features/audio/mod.rs` to step 5's files with the owner's approval, the plan's list having left out the sounds' cue arms.
- Owner checks: a playthrough of the start, the aqueduct, the hollow's lava and the mine; the review page at https://claude.ai/artifact/XszVhBV87spJFg3GYkReqT and `perf-runs/20261025-e01k-art-review/` (the run's captures in `shots/run/`).

## D-215 — The roadmap redo replaces the 1.0 roadmap after Phase 6: example 01 is the acceptance game, 1.0 promises a fully featured engine in four genres, and H1 freezes the 1.0 records behind `ROADMAP.md`
**Amended by D-216:** the Git bullet's push only; agents push the version branch after each commit, while tags, merges and every other push stay the owner's; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-218:** the genre promise only; 1.0 also promises action RPGs, a fifth genre beside platformer, top-down, story-driven RPG and shooter games; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-227:** the Git bullet's "until H2" clause only; a pushed tag publishes, and the release pull request, merged without squashing, publishes nothing; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-25
**Decision:** [docs/plans/roadmap-redo.md](docs/plans/roadmap-redo.md) is the plan of record from Phase 6's tail to 1.0: Phase 7 Seams, Phase 8 Genres and Phase 9 Release (its §3), with health in two parts, H1 now and H2 at step 7.4. 1.0 promises a fully featured engine, tooling and kit for platformer, top-down, story-driven RPG and shooter games, with no API stability promise at 1.0 (§5.7; the policy after it is step 9.3). Example 01 is the acceptance game, accepted when the owner says it is. H1 lands in this change, with its rules in [docs/operating-rules.md](docs/operating-rules.md), each naming the failure it prevents:

- **Status:** `ROADMAP.md` at the root (Now, Next, Later; Now holds one to three items) replaces the register (the implementation plan's §10), `roadmap.json`, the roadmap page and the 1.0 README's owned fields. Those are frozen behind a one-line banner naming it, and the checks and scripts that read them are retargeted or removed (`check_roadmap`, `check_summary`, `roadmap.py`, `release-cut`'s summary write, `tungsten-next`, `tungsten-milestone`).
- **Git:** agents commit at each logical step in Conventional Commits, checked by a `commit-msg` hook (`just hooks`), and stage only the paths they changed; a parallel session works in its own worktree; the owner pushes. Until H2, the release pull request stays the publish step, merged without squashing.
- **Plans:** small changes have none, medium ones are planned in the session, large ones keep one plan file deleted when done, with one critique round at xhigh. The 1.0 milestone route, level C and its API critique, the break ledger and the freeze's field rule are retired.
- **Decisions** only for choices that constrain later work (dependencies, formats, policy), short.
- **Tiers:** a session that generates art or sound runs on Opus 5.5 at max, a code session at xhigh.
- **Docs:** a `missing_docs` ratchet in the release checks, from 1,165 items.
- **Habits** (the owner's answer of 2026-10-23, toward agile by a gradient): a new idea is parked as one line in Later; the owner plays before the next thing is planned; an agent names scope creep and proposes the smallest version; the redo's order is intent, re-cut after each game finding.

**Why:** Example 01 measured the bottleneck as the cost of making a game, not engine speed or API stability (the redo's §1). M32–M85 each carried a plan, a decision, a changelog section, a register row and a catalog stop. M76–M85's sessions spent about 22 % of their tool calls and 20 % of their output tokens on bookkeeping, and each release touched about 12 record files (`perf-runs/20261025-overhead-baseline/`). Meanwhile 01's own Rust grew to 27k lines, much of it restating seams the engine and kit lack. The owner's answers of 2026-10-22, 2026-10-23 and 2026-10-25 are the redo's §2.

**Consequences:**
- Supersedes `D-102`, `D-103`, `D-105`, `D-108`, `D-109`, `D-118`, `D-132`, `D-152` and `D-195`, and amends `D-079`, `D-140`, `D-150`, `D-151`, `D-153`, `D-157`, `D-172` and `D-194`, each as its marker line says. `D-104`'s snapshot stays, as a free diff.
- The Phase 7 rewrite's owner answers of 2026-10-16 on the cutover's timing, the bench suite and the roadmap page, and its R-Q5 and R-Q7, are superseded; the rewrite is archived. Its other answers and defaults stand where the redo's §2 carries them.
- Frozen behind a banner, until step 9.7 deletes them: the implementation plan, `roadmap.json`, the 1.0 README's owned fields, `w04-api-freeze.md`'s break ledger, workflow §4's level C, the release checklist's RC-A1, RC-B2 and RC-B4 (RC-B1 now publishes step 9.3's policy), `acceptance-game.md` and w13 §14's field rule.
- Stops keep their IDs; milestone numbers end with the register at M85. 0.108 is the last release on the old pipeline, and agents commit from the next session.
- The overhead is retaken after the first example stops under H1 (the redo's §4), with the baseline's scripts.

## D-216 — Agents push the version branch after each commit; tags, merges and every other push stay the owner's
**Date:** 2026-10-26
**Decision:** After each commit, an agent pushes the version branch it works on with `git push origin 0.NNN`, or `git push -u origin 0.NNN` the first time, as the whole command: a fast-forward. Force pushes, `main`, `HEAD`, refspecs, `--all`, `--mirror`, `--delete`, bare and chained pushes stay the owner's, as do tags and merges. The owner's global `~/.claude/settings.json` holds it: a PreToolUse hook on the Bash tool, `~/.claude/hooks/git-push-guard.sh`, refuses every other command that runs a push, in place of the deny on all of `git push`.

**Why:** H1 (`D-215`) had agents commit and the owner push. On 2026-10-26, with `e01tomes`' plan committed and left unpushed, the owner asked for agents to push as well: until the owner pushed by hand, a session's commits sat on one machine, unseen by the remote, other clones and GitHub. A deny pattern cannot tell a version branch's fast-forward from a force push in a session that bypasses permission prompts, so the guard matches the one allowed form whole.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-215` as its marker line says.
- `docs/operating-rules.md` (Git), `AGENTS.md`, `docs/plans/README.md`, `docs/releases.md` and the `tungsten-milestone` and `tungsten-next` skills say so. The release block still has the owner push the tag with the branch, open the pull request and merge it.
- A refused push (the remote moved, while `git fetch` stays denied) is reported, never forced; the owner fetches and the session carries on.
- The guard catches mistakes, not evasion: as with the other denies, a push is never hidden from it through another command or API. A Bash command whose text names a push, such as a commit message or a heredoc, trips it too; reword it, or write the file with the editing tools.

## D-217 — A state command that would exit a state the same dispatch entered waits a frame
**Date:** 2026-10-28
**Decision:** `state_dispatcher_system` applies a frame's state commands in order, but a `Pop` or `Replace`, plain or a transition's boundary command, that would exit a state entered earlier in the same dispatch is held, with every command after it, and runs first on the next frame, ahead of the requests the hooks made. A push then a pop, or two replaces, in one frame give the entered state one frame of `update`.

**Why:** A state's exit despawns the live entities carrying its `SceneEntity`; spawns its `on_enter` queued through the `CommandBuffer` are not live until the flush, so a state entered and exited in one dispatch left them behind (known issues, W8b's queued transitions). A sweep at the flush by state ID would also take the entities of a state that replaces itself, and a flush inside the dispatcher would break `D-039`'s rule that systems never see a frame's commands.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-046` and `D-093` as their marker lines say; `D-039`'s flush stands.
- `tests/state.rs` pins a push then a pop, two replaces then a push, a pop then a push (both apply) and a boundary pop after a plain push.
- Two instances of one state ID still share cleanup: either's exit despawns both's entities. Per-instance ownership would change `SceneEntity` at every construction site; it is a recorded limit.

## D-218 — 1.0 promises a fifth genre, action RPGs
**Date:** 2026-10-28
**Decision:** 1.0 promises a fully featured engine, tooling and kit for action RPGs, beside platformer, top-down, story-driven RPG and shooter games (the roadmap redo's §5.7 and its Q18). An action RPG here has three parts:
- skills whose properties tag-scoped modifiers change;
- equipment from procedurally generated items, on the same modifiers;
- one rule set for players and enemies.

The kit's reference genres become `platformer`, `top_down`, `rpg`, `shooter` and `arpg` at step 7.10. Step 7.19's paper design scopes the work, and 7.7 waits for it. Step 8.7 builds what 7.10, 8.1 and 8.2 leave.

**Why:** The owner's answer of 2026-10-28 to the redo's Q18. The kit's survey that day found that the stat formula already matches flat, increased and more (`crates/tungsten-kit/src/foundation/stats.rs:96-123`). It also found what the kit lacks:
- modifiers scoped by tag;
- a caster's stats on its projectiles and its damage over time;
- tags on abilities and hits;
- crit chance and cast speed;
- chain and fork;
- affix data.

Steps 7.10, 8.1 and 8.2 build the systems those would sit on. Scoping them before 7.7 assigns those steps' rows saves reworking the steps later.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-215` and `D-172` as their marker lines say.
- 8.6's proof adds 7.19's weekly agent tasks and the `arpg` recipes and sketch. 9.1 adds 7.19's stress numbers.
- 1.0 moves later by 7.19 and 8.7, and by what the owner's triage of 7.19's register adds to 7.10, 8.1 and 8.2.
- The UI and camera steps (7.17, 7.18, 7.20) do not wait for 7.19.

## D-219 — A `game.id` may not end in `.`
**Date:** 2026-10-28
**Decision:** `game.id` keeps `D-119`'s rule and may not end in `.`; `Config::load`, `GameConfig::validate` and `App::new` refuse one that does, and save slot names, which follow the id's rule, do too.

**Why:** Windows drops trailing dots from paths that are not verbatim, so `mygame.` and `mygame` would share a user folder, settings and saves (core review F9).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-119` as its marker line says.
- `tests/config.rs` pins `mygame.` among the refused ids.

## D-220 — Gamepads: `gilrs` (`D-015` rule 1) behind a default `gamepad` feature, its backend thread the third background thread with force feedback off; pad buttons and sticks in core's input, the `gamepad_button` and `gamepad_axis` bindings, `ActionMap::value` and UI navigation from pads
**Amended by D-221:** the stick reading and the backend's filters only; `ActionMap::value` reads a stick through `InputState`'s `StickResponse`, a radial dead zone (0.15) and a sensitivity the player sets in `Settings`, no longer from 0.35, and the backend turns gilrs's default filters off and runs its d-pad and jitter filters itself; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-28
**Decision:** Satisfies `D-015` rule 1: `gilrs` 0.11 (Apache-2.0/MIT; `gilrs-core` 0.6, WGI on Windows) is the platform API for gamepads, an optional dependency of `tungsten` behind the `gamepad` cargo feature, on by default. The crate-private backend builds `GilrsBuilder::new().with_force_feedback(false).build()`: force feedback stays off because its server spawns a thread of its own, and gilrs's d-pad filter turns the d-pad axes into buttons. `App::run` starts it after the watcher, never `App::new`, so the harness spawns nothing; it reports the pads connected at start, logs `gamepad backend started: N connected` at info, and runs without pads after one warning when the build fails. `run_frame` drains it first, so pad events follow the window events that arrived before the frame, through `apply_pad_event` over a crate-private `PadEvent`: button and axis events drop while `InputState::focused()` is false, connections always apply, and smoke mode drops every event, so captures never read a pad. Positional buttons map by name, gilrs's second triggers (`LeftTrigger2`, `RightTrigger2`) to `LeftTrigger`/`RightTrigger` and its first to the shoulders; unmapped buttons and axes are skipped. Core (landed in 0.74, recorded here) adds `GamepadId`, `GamepadButton` (17 positional variants, `#[non_exhaustive]`), `GamepadAxis` (the two sticks, x right and y up, `#[non_exhaustive]`), `AxisDirection`, `STICK_PRESS` (0.5) and `STICK_RELEASE` (0.35). `InputState` keeps each connected pad's buttons, stick directions and axes with edges in both views (`D-146`), records `GamepadButton`, `GamepadAxis`, `GamepadStick` and `Gamepad` in the raw stream (a stick record right before the axis record of the move that crossed, and adjacent axis moves coalescing only when neither crossed), answers "any pad" queries for the action map and per-pad ones, releases a disconnected pad's input with edges, and treats focus loss as for keys and zeroes every axis. `Binding` turns `#[non_exhaustive]` and gains `gamepad_button` (`button`) and `gamepad_axis` (`axis`, `direction`), named by variant in `input.json`; an axis binding is held while its stick direction is pressed, and `ActionMap::value` returns an action's largest binding, a digital one 1.0 and a stick its reach rescaled from 0.35..1 to 0..1. The router keys its navigating presses by pad (`NavInput`), so each pad's presses and releases keep their owner, takes pad and stick presses as keys, repeats a held d-pad or stick direction, feeds `RoutedInput` a stick it took at 0 until that direction's release, and shows focus after a pad (`InputDevice::Gamepad`). The default map binds `ui_accept` to South, `ui_cancel` to East, `ui_next` and `ui_prev` to the shoulders and the directions to the d-pad and the left stick. The harness presses a pad binding on pad 0.

**Why:** No window read a pad: core's half landed in 0.74 (M54), but the backend was held back because `gilrs` 0.11.2 and `gilrs-core` 0.6.8 ship no licence file and `just notices` failed. A twin-stick shooter cannot ship without pads (P7-12). Owning evdev, XInput, WGI and IOKit backends is the work rule 1 exists to avoid, and SDL2 would ship a C library. With force feedback off every `gilrs` backend spawns one thread (`gilrs-core` 0.6.8): Linux watches a udev monitor (`platform/linux/gamepad.rs:180`) or, in Flatpak or under `GILRS_DISABLE_UDEV`, inotify (`:112`), the branches exclusive; macOS runs an IOKit run loop (`platform/macos/gamepad.rs:55`) and WGI polls (`platform/windows_wgi/gamepad.rs:137`); the force-feedback server would be a second (`gilrs` 0.11.2, `ff/server.rs:290`). Its events reach the main thread through `std::sync::mpsc`, the shape the root rule already allows for the watcher, so the rule names a third thread rather than banning pads. On Linux `libudev-sys` finds `libudev` through pkg-config at build time and links it, so builds need `libudev-dev` and binaries `libudev.so.1`; the feature lets a game leave all of it out. Positional names keep one `input.json` for every pad family. Keying presses by pad keeps pad A's release from matching pad B's UI-owned press, and the crossing records keep their values so a same-frame release and rebound still reaches a reader that derives edges from values.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-045`, `D-131` (its two-thread clause), `D-144` and `D-149` as their marker lines say.
- The root `AGENTS.md` names three background threads; DESIGN's rule-1 table gains `gilrs`; CI and the release workflow install `libudev-dev`; the getting-started guide and the archive README name libudev. `licenses/clarifications.json` gives both crates gilrs's upstream MIT text at the packaged commit and records SDL_GameControllerDB (zlib), which `gilrs` compiles in.
- Tests: core `src/tests/input.rs` (edges in both views, hysteresis, focus loss, disconnect, coalescing), `src/tests/input/action_map.rs` (JSON round trip, `value`, the pad defaults), `src/tests/ui/route.rs` (d-pad focus and South, stick repeat, a taken stick reading 0, focus shown, two pads, same-frame crossings), the harness's `press_action_presses_a_gamepad_binding`, and the umbrella's `src/tests/input_bridge.rs` (events applied, dropped while unfocused and in smoke mode, unmapped ones skipped).
- Open: the stick's dead zone is gilrs's per-axis filter until a radial one in core (P7-12); per-player action maps, analog triggers, rumble (P8-14) and in-game pad remapping are backlog rows; a pad without an SDL mapping reads only what gilrs's fallback maps; a stick held when focus returns reads 0 until it moves.

## D-221 — Sticks read through a radial dead zone and a sensitivity (`StickResponse` in `InputState`), the player's in `Settings`; gilrs's own dead zone off
**Date:** 2026-10-28
**Decision:** `ActionMap::value` reads a stick binding through the input's `StickResponse { dead_zone, sensitivity }` (core; 0.15 and 1.0 by default, kept clamped to 0..=0.9 and 0.25..=2, a value that is not finite taking the default's), over both axes of the stick together: below the dead zone the stick reads 0, and past it the vector is rescaled from the dead zone to 1 along its own direction, times the sensitivity, its length clamped to 1; the binding reads the part along its direction on the pad where that is largest. `InputState` holds the response (`stick_response`, `set_stick_response`), `begin_frame` and focus loss keep it, and each UI routing passes it to `RoutedInput`. The digital stick presses keep reading the raw value at `STICK_PRESS` and `STICK_RELEASE`, and `gamepad_axis` stays raw. `Settings` gains `stick_dead_zone` and `stick_sensitivity` (the file's keys of the same names; setters clamp to the ranges; a file value outside one sets the whole file aside, as the other fields do; a file without them reads the defaults); `Settings::stick_response` fills an unset one from `StickResponse::DEFAULT`, `App::new` sets the loaded response on the `InputState` it inserts, and `tungsten::apply_settings` sets it at run time. The gamepad backend builds `gilrs` with its default filters and automatic state updates off and runs the d-pad and jitter filters itself, updating gilrs's state after them, so gilrs's per-axis dead zone does not stack on this one.

**Why:** P7-12. A per-axis dead zone squares the stick: a diagonal past the zone in length but inside it on each axis reads 0, and a stick off an axis bends toward it as each axis rescales alone. Reading a stick from `STICK_RELEASE` (0.35) on, as `D-220` records, also threw away a third of its travel. The response lives in `InputState` because `ActionMap::value` takes only `&InputState` and `Config::apply_settings` runs before `InputState` exists, so the kit's `axis_value` needs no change; the routing passes it on because `RoutedInput` is a second `InputState`, which the kit reads when present. With automatic updates on, gilrs would update its state before the jitter filter run by hand, which compares a move against that state, and drop every move.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-159` and `D-220` as their marker lines say.
- Tests: core `value_reads_a_sticks_reach_past_the_dead_zone`, `a_diagonal_under_the_dead_zone_reads_zero_on_both_axes`, `a_stick_past_the_dead_zone_rescales_along_its_own_direction`, `sensitivity_two_clamps_the_length_to_one` and `the_stick_response_clamps_holds_across_frames_and_leaves_the_presses_raw` (`src/tests/input/action_map.rs`), `the_stick_response_saves_loads_clamps_and_falls_back` (`src/tests/settings.rs`), `each_routing_gives_the_routed_view_the_raw_inputs_stick_response` (`src/tests/ui/route.rs`); the umbrella's `apply_settings_sets_the_stick_response_an_action_reads` and `an_app_starts_with_the_stick_response_its_settings_file_sets` (`src/tests/settings.rs`).
- Open: the template's settings screen has no stick row (7.11 or 7.17); one response serves every pad and both sticks; the backend's hand-run filters are untested on a real pad.

## D-222 — Example 01's map objects take save IDs from unique authored names (save schema 23); runtime save IDs count below bit 31
**Date:** 2026-10-28
**Decision:** Every object in a layer of 01's level that a save names (`hazards`, `platforms`, `dummies`, `enemies`, `pickups`, `geysers`: `tools/level.py`'s `SAVED_LAYERS`) has a `name` in `tools/level.json`, unique across those layers and never `player`, which `validate_level` refuses otherwise and `build_objects` writes into the Tiled object's `name`. `level::spawn` reads each classed object's name beside its entity and gives each saved one `SaveId::authored(name)`, the name's 32-bit FNV-1a hash with bit 31 (`shared::AUTHORED`) set; the player takes `SaveId::authored("player")`; a saved object with no name, or one whose ID another's or the player's takes, stops the spawn. Whatever spawns during a run takes `SaveIds`' counter, from 0, which refuses to reach bit 31. A fresh run's crates and kegs carry `MapProp`, saved as `PropSave::map`, which the summon's caps skip, in place of a range counted off the player's ID. Save schema 23: `to_23` maps schema 22's IDs 0–56 through the frozen `S22_NAMES` (schema 22's spawn order, then the player), keeps every later ID and `next_id`, puts each list a map ID can sit in back in save-ID order, and marks the crates and kegs inside schema 22's map range (`S22_MAP_PROPS`, 61..75). 7.12's level format keeps the names.

**Why:** P7-9. Map objects took their save IDs in spawn order, so an enemy added to the map moved every later ID and cost a schema with a shift (`SHIFT_9`, `SHIFT_12`, `SHIFT_19`, `SHIFT_22`). An ID derived from the object's name makes the save independent of the map's order; a hash keeps no table in step with the map (Q3, the plan's default). Bit 31 keeps authored and runtime IDs apart with no shared counter, and 32 bits keep every ID exact for a JSON reader that reads numbers as doubles.

**Consequences:**
- Supersedes `D-208` as its marker line says.
- An object added to, removed from or moved within a saved layer moves no other save ID and needs no schema; renaming a saved object is a save change, a migration from the old name's ID to the new one's. Marker props (iron, crates, kegs, stones) keep counter IDs, and a load restores them from the save.
- What read the save ID as a number keeps its result: a crate's, rock's or stone's art takes the parity of the ID plus one (`props::art`), so a fresh run's map props keep the art schema 22's IDs gave them; a glow's pulse takes its phase from the ID's low ten bits, which an authored ID's size would push past an `f32`'s precision.
- Tests: `an_enemy_added_to_the_map_needs_no_schema_bump` and `a_migrated_schema_22_capture_keeps_its_maps_crates_outside_the_summons_caps` (`tests/game/saves.rs`), `schema_9_saves_migrate_to_the_new_save_ids` (holds `S22_NAMES` to the map), every schema fixture test through `schema_23_id`, `the_map_s_own_crates_and_kegs_stay_outside_the_summon_s_caps` (`tests/game/props.rs`); the relabel tests start from a capture taken back to schema 22 (`as_schema_22_capture`).
- Open: Tiled object `id`s stay generator-numbered and unused; 7.9's generic saves.

## D-223 — Example 01's accepted asset families are locked by each file's SHA-256 in `tools/locks.json`, which the generator enforces; an unlock is the owner's
**Date:** 2026-10-28
**Decision:** `tools/generate.py`'s `FAMILIES` splits 01's generated outputs into families: the player, the projectiles, the balls, the dummy, each enemy, the stones, scenery, props, terrain, backdrops, effects, tomes, sounds and music, which lock, and the level and `content` (the shell's copies, `content/`, shaders, materials, particle configs), which are source-driven and never lock. `tools/locks.json` holds, for each locked family, the date it was locked and every one of its files' SHA-256. A run, whole or by family, that would change a locked family's files (a byte changed, a file gained or lost) fails before it writes, naming the family and the files; `--check` also holds every locked file in the tree to its hash. `--lock NAME` records a family's hashes and refuses while the tree's files differ from what the family makes; `--unlock NAME` drops the entry, the owner's call. Every lockable family is locked at landing (the plan's Q1: each of 01's stops closed with the owner's acceptance).

**Why:** The roadmap redo's asset sessions (§6, `docs/operating-rules.md`'s Assets): accepted files are locked and code sessions leave `tools/` art alone. A hash per file makes a code session's accidental change to accepted art, through a shared helper, the palette or a relief prefix, fail at the run that makes it, naming each file, instead of reaching a review sheet.

**Consequences:**
- An asset session that redraws a family starts from the owner's `--unlock` and ends with `--lock` after the acceptance; a code session never unlocks, so a change to shared drawing code that moves a locked file waits for the owner.
- A new output in a locked family is a gained file: generated data beside an actor's sprites, such as its body, hurtbox and anchors, takes a family of its own.
- `locks.json` is source: only `--lock` and `--unlock` write it, and `just level-check` reads it.
- Tests: `test_a_locked_files_change_fails_the_run` (`tools/test_generate.py`).

## D-224 — A manifest entry's path stays inside its manifest's folder, at every load; `just template-check`'s two entries outside the stage become errors `check` and `package` report
**Date:** 2026-10-28
**Decision:** Every manifest load, a game's own start, `tungsten check` and `tungsten package`'s staged check, refuses an entry whose path is absolute or climbs out of its declaring manifest's folder with `..` (`ManifestError::PathEscapes`), lexically and before the file is looked for, wherever the file sits. `scripts/template-check.sh`'s two entries, `../outside.png` and an absolute path into the copy, both to files inside the game's folder, now fail `check` and `package` alike, each naming `assets/manifest.json` and the path.

**Why:** `qa6` (0.112, `e466fd51`) closed Physics F11: a path that left its manifest's folder made the watcher watch that folder's parent, which matters for manifests from untrusted sources such as mods. The release's `just template-check` then failed on its seeded case, whose clause in `D-183` the fix reversed without an entry.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-017` and supersedes `D-183`'s clause, as their marker lines say.
- A game keeps every file a manifest names under that manifest's folder; a file shared between roots is listed by the root that holds it.
- The template-check case no longer shows `package`'s staged check catching a file `check` passes; that check stands.
- Tests: `a_path_may_climb_only_within_its_manifests_folder` (`tungsten-core/src/tests/assets/manifest.rs`), `just template-check`.

## D-225 — Scripted runs: `tungsten run --script`, `render` and `describe` through handshakes in the game's own build; script and report JSON at version 1; a capture per script frame and loops
**Date:** 2026-10-29
**Decision:** Three commands join `D-182`'s, each on the check's handshake: the command line checks its input on core alone, then runs `cargo run` in the game's folder with variables naming files, which `App::new` and `App::run` read first. `tungsten run --script` sets `TUNGSTEN_SCRIPT`, `TUNGSTEN_REPORT` and `TUNGSTEN_USER_DIR`; `tungsten render` writes a script from its flags and runs it the same way; `tungsten describe` sets `TUNGSTEN_DESCRIBE`, a check run that writes the World's components, script hooks and asset IDs as JSON. The script and the report are JSON at version 1 (`tungsten_core::script`): a field added or changed bumps the version, and a script of another version is refused. A script captures every frame it names, and a `capture` with `until` writes a folder of `NNNN.png` frames, a loop in one run. A script's `camera` is drawn at extract only: the runner swaps it in before the UI layout and puts the game's camera back after render. Script mode pins dt at 60 Hz, drops the window's keyboard, mouse, cursor and focus-loss events, starts no watcher or gamepad backend, and takes its user folder from `TUNGSTEN_USER_DIR` alone, none without it. The umbrella takes `serde_json`, already in its graph through core.

**Why:** Redo 7.2: later steps' checks, 7.13's evaluations and the asset sessions need captures and probes without a scratch driver crate. A review round launched the game once per frame, about 1.4 s each (`e01flask`'s three rounds took some 1,400 frames, 35 minutes); a loop is now one run. The handshake keeps the command line on core alone and runs the game's own registered code, as `check` does. Drawing the override at extract only keeps the camera update from taking it as its base, as a driver writing `CameraState.zoom` drew at base².
